# Research sweep — 2026-08-23

The evidence base produced by this session's ultracode workflow: 13 parallel domain
finders plus 4 critic-directed follow-up finders, each running verified web research,
followed by adversarial fact-checking of every load-bearing claim. 188 findings total.
Companion editorial synthesis: `CONNECTIVE-LEAPS.md`. Project context: `HANDOFF.md`.

## How to read this document

- Every finding carries a **status**: `convergent` (both fields found it independently),
  `partial` (a fragment exists in ML), `absent` (no ML translation — a building site),
  `new-since-aug-2026` (newer than the Engram Loop sweeps), or `speculative` (a design
  proposal by the research agent, not a literature claim).
- **Load-bearing** findings (marked ⚑) were adversarially fact-checked by a second agent
  instructed to refute them: `CONFIRMED` (source located and verified), `PLAUSIBLE`
  (source real and consistent, a detail unverifiable in this environment — mostly because
  scholarly domains were egress-blocked mid-session), `REFUTED` (unlocatable or wrong).
- Verdicts appear per area under "Verification". Unverified areas are flagged.
- REFUTED material is retained below only as a struck record, never as evidence.

   (Qwen-2.5-0.5B retrofit numbers) — unlocatable; the KL slot-selection *idea* is
   mathematically sound but must not be cited to these papers. The verified anchor for
   sparse memory finetuning remains arXiv 2510.15103.
2. **Dropped:** arXiv 2605.09315 "Do Self-Evolving Agents Forget?" and arXiv 2608.05810
   "When Self-Evolution Backfires" — unlocatable, all their quantitative anchors void.
   The gate-before-commit recommendation stands on arXiv 2605.12978 (confirmed) alone.
3. **Corrected:** the Fisher-memory bound is Ganguli, **Huh**, Sompolinsky (PNAS 2008),
   not "Huerta"; total integrated Fisher memory ≤ N holds for **arbitrary** networks
   (normal networks have capacity exactly 1) — not "≤ N for normal dynamics".

## Gaps found by the completeness critic

- **Streaming-sketch and mergeable-summary theory (count-min sketch with conservative update, Bloom filters, invertible Bloom lookup tables, heavy hitters)** — REFA's banks ARE linear sketches, yet no sweep touches the theoretical-CS sketching literature that owns this object: capacity-math covers superposition SNR and coherence, but not (epsilon, delta) frequency-estimation guarantees with ONE-SIDED error (count-min only over-counts — a provable asymmetry directly usable by the support/oppose/unknown head), conservative update (write only where the current estimate is the minimum — a principled alternative to the delta rule's erase term), heavy-hitter guarantees for which facts dominate a bank, and above all MERGEABILITY theorems: sketches can be summed/offloaded/consolidated with preserved guarantees, which is exactly the math REFA's bank-residency VRAM/RAM offload and episodic-to-parametric consolidation need and currently lack. IBLTs additionally give exact key-value recovery below a load threshold — a formal model for REFA's audit/decoding path.
- **Rate-distortion / information-bottleneck theory of memory: memory as lossy compression under a capacity budget, with gist intrusions as the predicted distortion** — Every sweep treats capacity as 'how many facts fit'; none asks the prior question of WHAT to store at WHAT fidelity. The Bates-Jacobs and Nagy-Torok-Orban line derives forgetting and schema-based gist errors as OPTIMAL lossy coding (semantic compression: episodic traces are compressed against a learned generative prior, and DRM-style false memories are the rate-distortion optimum, not a bug). For REFA this gives (a) a principled objective for which value components the tanh-squashed writes should preserve per bank, (b) a per-bank bit budget that turns lambda/beta into rate-allocation variables, and (c) quantitative, testable predictions for the Engram Loop's Confabulation Battery (DRM gist intrusion rates as a function of prior strength) — the direct mathematical bridge between the two companion documents that neither the sweeps nor the docs supply.
- **Truth discovery and source-reliability estimation (Dawid-Skene latent-truth models, streaming truth discovery)** — REFA's roadmap names a 'provenance conflict' task and the Engram Loop types episodes by source (perceived/told/self), but every fusion mechanism the sweeps found (Dempster-Shafer, subjective-logic fusion, trusted multi-view) assumes source reliabilities are known or equal. The truth-discovery literature solves the missing joint problem: infer per-source reliability weights AND fact truth values simultaneously from conflicting assertions (Dawid-Skene EM, Bayesian latent truth models, with identifiability conditions and spectral initializations), including streaming/incremental variants. For REFA this is a small learnable per-source scalar that modulates write gain beta and evidence weight at read — turning the provenance-conflict task from a heuristic into an estimator with known convergence and identifiability theory, and giving 'unknown' a fourth cause: unreliable-source evidence, distinct from no-evidence and conflicting-evidence.
- **Neural algorithmic reasoning and the theory of length/size generalization (algorithmic alignment, CLRS-30, position-coupling and RASP-L accounts)** — Length extrapolation is a named roadmap item and the sweeps' only extrapolation math is eigenvalue expressivity (parity) and SITH timescale generalization — neither explains WHEN a trained model generalizes to longer evidence streams. The algorithmic-alignment framework (sample complexity is governed by how well the architecture's computation graph matches the target algorithm's structure) plus the 2023-2025 length-generalization theory for sequence models (position coupling, RASP-L characterizations of which tasks extrapolate, scratchpad/format effects) gives REFA a predictive account of which of its synthetic relational tasks its recurrent fast-weight structure should extrapolate on versus provably not, and CLRS-style hint-based intermediate supervision is a concrete training lever for the multi-hop/revision tasks. Absence would embarrass a synthesis that promises length-extrapolation experiments.
- **Region-based (box/cone) embeddings: facts as volumes, probability as measure, conjunction/negation as set operations** — The relational-structure sweep settled on point embeddings with unitary rotations, and math-frontiers audited (and rejected) hyperbolic — but nobody examined region embeddings, which are the one geometry where REFA's core semantics is native: P(fact) = normalized box volume (calibrated by construction), entailment = containment, conjunction = intersection, negation/opposition = complement or disjointness, and 'unknown' = large-volume (high-entropy) box. Gumbel-box training is stable at very low dimension (works at d=16-64, REFA scale), query2box/BetaE extend to multi-hop conjunctive queries with negation — exactly the contrastive-evidence and abstention tasks — and the value side of REFA's banks could store box parameters instead of point values at negligible cost, giving the evidential head a geometric substrate rather than a purely count-based one.
- **Schema-congruency-gated consolidation (Tse rapid schema assimilation; van Kesteren SLIMM model)** — The consolidation sweep's commit gates are all surprise-shaped (Bayesian surprise, KL slot-surprise, verification), but the flagship biological result runs the OPPOSITE direction: Tse et al. showed schema-CONGRUENT new facts consolidate into cortex within ~48 hours and rapidly become hippocampus-independent, and the SLIMM framework formalizes the U-shape — high congruency routes learning directly to slow cortical weights (mPFC-gated), high surprise routes to fast episodic storage, and the middle band learns worst. For REFA this is a missing routing policy for the episodic-to-parametric loop: a cheap congruency score (agreement between the new event and the current slow-weight prediction) that decides fast-bank write vs. direct small-step slow-weight update vs. both, with a testable U-shaped retention prediction the synthetic generator can audit exactly. No sweep, and neither companion document, carries this mechanism or its sign.

The first four received dedicated follow-up sweeps (sections below); the last two
(region-based box/cone embeddings; schema-congruency-gated consolidation) remain open
reading for a future session.

---

## Delta-rule lineage and parallelization mathematics
`fastweight-math`

REFA's core update M ← λM − βr_h k(k^T M) + βr_h·1[s=pol]·k v^T is, term for term, the gated delta rule of 2024-25 linear-attention research: one online SGD step on an associative-recall regression loss, with routing folded into the learning rate and stance into a value mask. This lineage delivers three load-bearing gifts. First, an exact (not approximate) chunkwise-parallel rewrite via the WY representation of generalized Householder products (Yang et al. NeurIPS 2024; gated case ICLR 2025), which REFA can adopt per (bank, stance) pair while preserving erase-then-write semantics, signed banks, and soft routing — the Python loop stays as a float64 oracle. Second, sharp stability/expressivity theory: the transition λI − βr_h kk^T has spectrum {λ − βr_h, λ}, and REFA's current sigmoid gate confines it to ≈[0,1), which provably blocks parity-class revision tracking (Sarrof, Grazzi); rescaling the gate to (0, 1+λ) unlocks negative eigenvalues at zero cost while keeping contraction and the same chunk kernel. Third, a unifying test-time-regression frame (Wang, Shi and Fox — not Wang and Li) that positions every upgrade path: implicit/proximal steps (Longhorn) for unconditional stability, RLS/mesa second-order reads whose ridge posterior variance is a principled "unknown" signal for the three-class head, momentum/window objectives (Titans/ATLAS), and multi-Householder steps (DeltaProduct) for state tracking. A cluster of 2026 papers unknown to prior sweeps — Gated DeltaNet-2 and Erase-then-Delta Attention (decoupled erase/write addresses), Preconditioned DeltaNet and OSDN (curvature-aware delta steps), and Sparse Delta Memory (sparse addressing scaling state orders of magnitude at isoFLOPs) — independently converge on exactly the mechanisms REFA's revision, conflict, and bank-offload roadmap needs. Production systems (Qwen3-Next, Kimi Linear/KDA, RWKV-7) confirm the family trains stably at scale, and MoM validates REFA's routed multi-bank design as a capacity multiplier.

**Verification** of load-bearing claims:

- **CONFIRMED** — REFA's update IS the (gated) delta rule: one SGD step of test-time regression, with exact capacity and crosstalk bounds
  - Both sources verified. arXiv:2501.12352 is 'Test-time regression: a unifying framework for designing sequence models with associative memory' by Ke Alexander Wang, Jiaxin Shi, Emily B. Fox (Jan 2025, v3 May 2025) — the claim's correction of the brief's 'Wang and Li' misattribution is right. Abstract confirms the unification: linear attention, SSMs, fast-weight programmers, online learners, and softmax attention arise as special cases of test-time regression defined by three design choices (regression weights, regressor function class, test-time optimization algorithm). arXiv:2102.11174 (Schlag, Irie, Schmidhuber, ICML 2021) abstract confirms the memory-capacity limitation of linearised attention and the delta-rule-like programming instruction replacing purely additive outer products. Math checked and correct: gradient of ½||M^T k − v||² is kk^T M − kv^T, so one SGD step gives M ← (I−βkk^T)M + βkv^T, matching REFA's update with β_eff = β_t·r_{h,t}; eig(I−βkk^T) = {1−β on span(k), 1 elsewhere}; Hebbian crosstalk for N random unit keys in R^R is Σ_{i≠j}(k_i^Tk_j)v_i with expected norm ~sqrt((N−1)/R)||v||; delta rule stores up to R orthonormal key-value pairs exactly; 8 banks × 2 stances × rank 16 = 256 is correct arithmetic. Caveat kept honest by the claim itself: the λ-decayed weighted-argmin ('M_t ≈ argmin') is an approximation — one SGD step per event, not the exact minimizer.
- **CONFIRMED** — Exact chunkwise-parallel training of REFA's loop via the WY representation of Householder products (DeltaNet algorithm)
  - arXiv:2406.06484 verified: 'Parallelizing Linear Transformers with the Delta Rule over Sequence Length' by Songlin Yang, Bailin Wang, Yu Zhang, Yikang Shen, Yoon Kim (NeurIPS 2024). Abstract confirms the hardware-efficient algorithm exploiting a memory-efficient representation for products of Householder matrices, scaling DeltaNet to 1.3B/100B-token training. The companion blog (sustcsonglin.github.io DeltaNet series, WY representation / UT transform) is real and matches the stated T = (I + tril(diag(β)KK^T, −1))^{−1} diag(β) solve; I verified the WY recursion w_t = β_t(k_t − Σ_{j<t}(k_j^Tk_t)w_j) by hand for C=2 — it is correct under DeltaNet's right-multiplication convention S_t = S_{t−1}(I−β_tk_tk_t^T)+…; for REFA's left-acting convention on an R×D memory the same formula holds after transposition (P = I − Σ_t k_t w_t^T). This is a notation nuance, not an error; the exactness argument (linearity in M ⇒ chunking is re-bracketing, unlike TTT minibatch approximations) is sound. Spectrum claim |1−β|≤1 for β∈[0,2] is correct. The '10-100x wallclock over per-step Python loops' figure is an unsourced engineering estimate — plausible for kernel-launch-bound loops but not a paper result; treat as expectation, not citation.
- **CONFIRMED** — Gated DeltaNet: REFA is its constant-gate special case; production-validated (Qwen3-Next, Kimi Linear/KDA)
  - Sources verified with one math correction. arXiv:2412.06464 'Gated Delta Networks: Improving Mamba2 with Delta Rule' (Songlin Yang, Jan Kautz, Ali Hatamizadeh; ICLR 2025) abstract confirms gating + delta rule and the parallel training algorithm. arXiv:2510.26692 (Kimi Linear, Oct 2025) abstract confirms KDA extends Gated DeltaNet with finer-grained gating via a specialized DPLR-variant chunkwise algorithm, and the numbers — but they are 'up to 75% KV-cache reduction and up to 6× decoding throughput at 1M context' (paper's headline TPOT figure is 6.3× vs MLA at 1M); the claim should carry the 'up to' qualifiers. Qwen3-Next interleaving Gated DeltaNet with attention is accurate (Sep 2025 release, 3:1 GDN:attention layers). REFA-as-constant-gate special case holds: λI − β_eff kk^T = α(I − β'kk^T) with α=λ, β'=β_eff/λ. CORRECTION to the stability line: for the GDN multiplicative form α(I−βkk^T) the eigenvalues are {α, α(1−β)}, so the spectral radius is max(α, α|1−β|), NOT max(α, |α−β|), and the stability condition is β ∈ [1−1/α, 1+1/α]; the stated bound max(λ, |λ−β_eff|) ≤ 1 ⇔ β_eff ∈ [0, 1+λ] is correct only for REFA's additive parameterization λI − β_eff kk^T. The two forms were conflated; the REFA-side conclusion survives.
- **CONFIRMED** — Eigenvalue-range expressivity: REFA's gates confine transition eigenvalues to ≈[0,1), provably blocking parity-class revision tracking; scaling β to (0, 1+λ) unlocks it free
  - All three cited papers verified: arXiv:2404.08819 'The Illusion of State in State-Space Models' (Merrill, Petty, Sabharwal; ICML 2024) — abstract confirms S4/Mamba-class SSMs are within TC0 and cannot solve permutation composition (the S5 word problem); arXiv:2405.17394 'The Expressive Capacity of State Space Models: A Formal Language Perspective' (Sarrof, Veitsman, Hahn; NeurIPS 2024) — confirms star-free characterization and the design-choice (nonnegative-eigenvalue) limitation blocking parity; arXiv:2411.12537 'Unlocking State-Tracking in Linear RNNs Through Negative Eigenvalues' (Grazzi, Siems et al.; ICLR 2025) — confirms extending eigenvalues to [−1,1] (DeltaNet: β∈(0,2)) enables one-layer parity and improves modular arithmetic/state tracking. The uncited supporting references also check out: arXiv:2506.10918 is 'Sequential-Parallel Duality in Prefix Scannable Models' (Morris Yau et al., Jun 2025), and RWKV-7's all-regular-languages result is from the RWKV-7 paper (arXiv:2503.14456). Math verified: spectrum of λI − β r kk^T (||k||=1) is {λ−βr, λ with multiplicity R−1}; with β=σ(·)∈(0,1), r<1, λ≈0.995 the key-direction eigenvalue is confined to (λ−1, λ) ≈ (−0.005, 0.995), effectively nonnegative; reparameterizing β=(1+λ)σ(·) reaches eigenvalue −1 while |λ−βr|≤1 holds for βr∈[0,1+λ]; parity ∈ TC0 is correct (parity is outside AC0, inside TC0), so no contradiction with the TC0 ceiling. One caution for synthesis: 'REFA cannot represent parity-type toggling' is an idealization — the negative sliver (down to λ−1 ≈ −0.005) is nonzero, so the correct statement is that practically reachable negativity is negligible, which is what the (1+λ) rescaling fixes.
- **CONFIRMED** — Mesa layer / RLS with Sherman-Morrison, MesaNet, and 2026 preconditioned delta rules: second-order reads whose ridge variance is a principled 'unknown' signal
  - All sources located, including both 2026 papers. arXiv:2309.05858 'Uncovering mesa-optimization algorithms in Transformers' (von Oswald, Schlegel, Meulemans, ..., Sacramento) verified — this is where the mesa layer is introduced. arXiv:2506.05233 'MesaNet: Sequence Modeling by Locally Optimal Test-Time Training' (von Oswald et al., Jun 2025) verified via the flash-linear-attention library (which ships a mesa_net layer); its read is exactly the stated ridge form o_t = G_t^T(H_t + diag(λ))^{−1}q_t, and the CG-at-read-time / chunkwise-parallel characterization matches. arXiv:2604.21100 'Preconditioned DeltaNet: Curvature-aware Sequence Modeling for Linear Recurrences' (Apr 2026) EXISTS — accepted at ICML 2026, with official code and fla-org kernel implementations (precond_gated_deltanet, precond_kda); independent notes confirm it derives an exact equivalence between linear attention and the delta rule under inverse-key-Gram preconditioning, matching the claim. arXiv:2605.13473 'OSDN: Improving Delta Rule with Provable Online Preconditioning in Linear Attention' (Zhou, Li, Liu, Lin, Ge, Ye; May 2026) verified — diagonal preconditioner via hypergradient feedback, preserves chunkwise pipeline, proves residual-contraction bounds. Math checked: Sherman-Morrison recursion is the standard form and correct; R²=256 flops/step at R=16 and Cholesky ≈ R³/3 ≈ 1.4k flops are right; q^T(G+Λ)^{−1}q is the standard Bayesian linear-regression predictive variance. One convention flag: the decayed Gram is written G_t = λ²G_{t−1}+β_tk_tk_t^T here but the TTR weighting in claim 1 uses λ^{t−i} (first power) — whether decay enters the regression weights as λ or λ² is a modeling choice that should be made consistent when implementing; SM/Cholesky work either way.

### REFA's update IS the (gated) delta rule: one SGD step of test-time regression, with exact capacity and crosstalk bounds ⚑
*status: convergent*

The per-(bank h, stance s) update in refa.py is algebraically the delta-rule fast-weight update introduced by Schlag, Irie and Schmidhuber (2021) to fix linear attention's capacity overflow, and unified by the test-time regression (TTR) framework of Wang, Shi and Fox (Jan 2025; NB the brief's 'Wang and Li' is a misattribution): every fast-weight/SSM/attention layer is an online regressor defined by (regression weights, function class, optimizer). Delta rule = online SGD; pure linear attention = Hebbian accumulation with crosstalk; softmax attention = nonparametric kernel regression; RLS/mesa = exact solver. Hebbian storage of N random unit keys in R^R incurs retrieval error ~sqrt(N/R); the delta rule stores up to R orthogonal pairs exactly and REPLACES rather than accumulates along revisited keys — precisely REFA's 'later evidence supersedes earlier' semantics. REFA's total addressable capacity is H*2 banks x rank R=16 = 256 exact associations, with routing partitioning key space to reduce interference.

**Math.** REFA: M_t = (λ_h I − β_t r_{h,t} k_t k_t^T) M_{t−1} + β_t r_{h,t} σ_t^s k_t v_t^T, ||k_t||=1, σ^s = 1[s=polarity]. Read loss ℓ(M) = ½||M^T k − v||²; ∇_M ℓ = k k^T M − k v^T; one SGD step of size β: M ← (I − β kk^T)M + β k v^T — identical with β_eff = β_t r_{h,t} and stance-masked value. TTR view: M_t ≈ argmin_M Σ_i γ_{t,i}||M^T k_i − σ_i v_i||², γ_{t,i} = λ^{t−i} β_i r_{h,i}. Hebbian crosstalk: reading k_j from S = Σ_i v_i k_i^T yields v_j + Σ_{i≠j}(k_i^T k_j)v_i, E||err|| ~ sqrt((N−1)/R)||v||. Delta rule: exact recall for ≤ R orthonormal keys; eig(I − βkk^T) = {1−β on span(k); 1 elsewhere}.

**REFA application.** Treat the Python loop as the reference semantics of a TTR layer with λ-decayed routed weights. Two immediate wins: (a) the implied per-event loss gradient norm ||k^T M − σ v^T|| is a free surprise signal to feed the abstention/unknown head; (b) the ≤R-orthogonal-keys capacity bound says frequently co-routed entity keys should be pushed toward per-bank orthogonality (soft orthogonality penalty) before spending VRAM on larger R.

Sources:
- Wang, Shi, Fox. Test-time regression: a unifying framework for designing sequence models with associative memory. arXiv:2501.12352 — <https://arxiv.org/abs/2501.12352>
- Schlag, Irie, Schmidhuber. Linear Transformers Are Secretly Fast Weight Programmers. ICML 2021. arXiv:2102.11174 — <https://arxiv.org/abs/2102.11174>

### Exact chunkwise-parallel training of REFA's loop via the WY representation of Householder products (DeltaNet algorithm) ⚑
*status: convergent*

Yang, Wang, Zhang, Shen and Kim (NeurIPS 2024) reparameterize the delta rule as a matrix RNN whose transition I − β_t k_t k_t^T is a generalized Householder matrix, then use the compact WY representation of Householder products to train chunkwise in matmuls without materializing per-step matrix states. Because REFA's update is LINEAR in M with exactly this transition structure, chunk-parallelization is EXACT — same algebra, different bracketing — unlike TTT-style shared-base-point minibatch approximations. This matches REFA's repo culture of float64 output+gradient equivalence tests: the loop remains the oracle, the chunk kernel must match to tolerance. Open-source kernels exist (flash-linear-attention library); 10-100x wallclock over per-step loops is typical since the loop is kernel-launch/latency bound, not FLOP bound, at REFA's sizes.

**Math.** Chunk of C steps: P = Π_{t=1..C}(I − β_t k_t k_t^T) = I − Σ_t w_t k_t^T (WY), with w_t = β_t(k_t − Σ_{j<t}(k_j^T k_t) w_j), i.e. W = T K where T = (I + tril(diag(β) K K^T, −1))^{−1} diag(β) — a unit-lower-triangular solve, O(C²R). State: M_C = P·M_0 + Σ_t k_t u_t^T with pseudo-values U = T(V − K M_0). Reads (REFA reads BEFORE write): inter-chunk term q_t^T M_0-propagated + intra-chunk causal quadratic attention with STRICTLY lower-triangular mask, O(C² max(R,D)) per chunk. Totals: O(T·C·R + T·C·D + T·R·D) flops all in matmuls; sequential depth T/C instead of T. Stability of kernel: identical spectrum to loop, |1−β|≤1 for β∈[0,2].

**REFA application.** Concrete port: run 16 independent recurrences (H=8 banks x 2 stances) as 'heads' of an FLA-style gated delta kernel with per-head effective rate β_eff = β_t·r_{h,t}·is_history_t and stance-masked values v'_t = σ_t^s v_t (erase hits both stances; write only one — the kernel doesn't care that v' is zeroed). C=32-64 fits an RTX 2060; expect ~10-50x on T≥512. Keep the loop as float64 oracle; test bit-tolerance equivalence.

Sources:
- Yang, Wang, Zhang, Shen, Kim. Parallelizing Linear Transformers with the Delta Rule over Sequence Length. NeurIPS 2024. arXiv:2406.06484 — <https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.06484>
- Songlin Yang, DeltaNet Explained Part II (WY representation, UT transform) — <https://sustcsonglin.github.io/blog/2024/deltanet-2/>

### Gated DeltaNet: REFA is its constant-gate special case; production-validated (Qwen3-Next, Kimi Linear/KDA) ⚑
*status: convergent*

Gated Delta Networks (Yang, Kautz, Hatamizadeh; ICLR 2025) combine Mamba-2's forgetting gate with the delta rule: S_t = α_t(I − β_t k_t k_t^T)S_{t−1} + β_t v_t k_t^T, and extend the WY chunkwise algorithm to the gated case. REFA's λ_h M − β r_h kk^T M is exactly this with α = λ_h CONSTANT per bank — the simplest gated case (decay factors are plain powers λ^t; no cumulative-product bookkeeping). The gated delta rule is now production infrastructure: Qwen3-Next interleaves Gated DeltaNet with attention; Kimi Linear's Kimi Delta Attention (KDA, Oct 2025) extends it with channel-wise (diagonal) gating via a specialized Diagonal-Plus-Low-Rank (DPLR) chunkwise algorithm, reporting ~6x decoding throughput and 75% KV-cache reduction at 1M context. Fine-grained (per-channel) gating consistently beats scalar gating on recall-intensive tasks — evidence that REFA's per-bank scalar λ_h is the weakest link in its forgetting model.

**Math.** GDN: S_t = α_t(I − β_t k_t k_t^T)S_{t−1} + β_t v_t k_t^T; chunkwise handled by scaling keys with cumulative decays γ_t = Π_{j≤t} α_j inside the WY/UT solve; REFA's constant λ_h gives γ_t = λ_h^t exactly. KDA: transition Diag(a_t) composed with (I − β k k^T)-type reflection, a_t ∈ (0,1)^d per-channel; chunkwise via restricted DPLR, cheaper than general DPLR. Stability: spectral radius ≤ max(α, |α − β|) ≤ 1 for α ≤ 1, β ∈ [0, 1+α].

**REFA application.** Upgrade path at zero serial cost: replace scalar λ_h with a per-slot decay VECTOR λ_h ∈ (0,1)^R (KDA-style diagonal gate) so some slots hold durable relational facts while others churn with recent evidence; the DPLR chunk algorithm stays exact. Keep λ input-independent — constant gates give the cheapest kernel and REFA's decay-as-persistence semantics don't need token-dependent forgetting yet.

Sources:
- Yang, Kautz, Hatamizadeh. Gated Delta Networks: Improving Mamba2 with Delta Rule. ICLR 2025. arXiv:2412.06464 — <https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.06464>
- Kimi Team. Kimi Linear: An Expressive, Efficient Attention Architecture (KDA). arXiv:2510.26692 — <https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.26692>

### Eigenvalue-range expressivity: REFA's gates confine transition eigenvalues to ≈[0,1), provably blocking parity-class revision tracking; scaling β to (0, 1+λ) unlocks it free ⚑
*status: convergent*

Three converging results. (1) Merrill, Petty, Sabharwal (ICML 2024): log-precision constant-depth SSMs (and transformers) lie in L-uniform TC0 — the 'illusion of state' — so NC1-hard state tracking (S5 permutation word problem) is out of reach at any width. (2) Sarrof, Veitsman, Hahn (NeurIPS 2024): finite-precision linear RNNs with nonnegative real eigenvalues express exactly the star-free regular languages; parity is impossible. (3) Grazzi, Siems et al. (ICLR 2025): extending the eigenvalue range to [−1,1] — for DeltaNet, allowing β ∈ (0,2) so I − βkk^T has eigenvalue 1−β ∈ [−1,1) — lets a single layer solve parity and improves modular arithmetic and state tracking with no language-modeling loss and UNCHANGED WY parallelizability. RWKV-7 goes further: its decoupled-transition products can recognize all regular languages in constant layers (beyond TC0 under TC0≠NC1), but exactly because those products don't compress — the parallelizability-expressivity tension made precise by prefix-scannable-model duality (arXiv:2506.10918).

**Math.** REFA transition λ_h I − β_t r_{h,t} k k^T has spectrum {λ_h − β_t r_{h,t} (on k); λ_h (mult. R−1)}. With β = σ(·) ∈ (0,1), r_h < 1, λ_h ≈ 0.995: the key-direction eigenvalue lies in (λ−1, λ) ≈ (−0.005, 0.995) — effectively nonnegative, so by Sarrof/Grazzi REFA cannot represent parity-type toggling (evidence whose net stance flips on each repetition). Contraction requires |λ − βr_h| ≤ 1 ⇔ βr_h ∈ [0, 1+λ]; reparameterize β = (1+λ_h)·σ(w^T x) to reach eigenvalue −1 while staying stable, same WY kernel. Ceiling: any exactly-chunk-parallel REFA remains in TC0; S5-type tracking needs DeltaProduct steps, depth, or sequential RWKV-7-style transitions. Parity ∈ TC0, so no contradiction.

**REFA application.** One-line change: multiply write_strength by (1+λ_h). This directly targets the revision task family — a supported→opposed→supported… chain is parity of negations. Add a 'parity of negations' synthetic task to the battery to measure the unlock, with β ≤ 1 as ablation; note soft routing shrinks β_eff by r_h, so top-1 routing (roadmap) also increases reachable negativity.

Sources:
- Grazzi, Siems, Franke, Zela, Hutter, Pontil. Unlocking State-Tracking in Linear RNNs Through Negative Eigenvalues. ICLR 2025. arXiv:2411.12537 — <https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.12537>
- Merrill, Petty, Sabharwal. The Illusion of State in State-Space Models. ICML 2024. arXiv:2404.08819 — <https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.08819>
- Sarrof, Veitsman, Hahn. The Expressive Capacity of State Space Models: A Formal Language Perspective. arXiv:2405.17394 — <https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.17394>

### DeltaProduct: n_h Householder micro-steps per token buy state tracking and length extrapolation, keeping WY parallelism
*status: convergent*

DeltaProduct (Siems, Carstensen, Zela, Hutter, Pontil, Grazzi; NeurIPS 2025) reads DeltaNet's recurrence as one gradient step per token on the recall loss and instead takes n_h steps, making the state transition a PRODUCT of n_h generalized Householder matrices — interpolating between rank-1-update DeltaNet and dense (near-orthogonal) transitions. This provably enlarges the reachable transition group (compositions of two reflections are rotations), so larger group word problems become solvable at fixed depth, and empirically gives markedly better length extrapolation than DeltaNet — directly relevant to REFA's length-extrapolation roadmap item. Cost is n_h× keys/values per token; the WY chunkwise algorithm is unchanged (a chunk simply contains n_h·C reflections).

**Math.** A_t = Π_{i=1..n_h}(I − β_{t,i} k_{t,i} k_{t,i}^T), β_{t,i} ∈ (0,2) ⇒ eigenvalues of each factor in [−1,1]; products realize rotations by 2θ between reflection planes, densifying the reachable subgroup of O(R). n_h=1: abelian-ish/reflection-limited; n_h≥2: dihedral-type and richer word problems at one layer. Complexity: chunkwise WY over n_h·C reflections, O((n_hC)²R) per chunk for the triangular solve; state size unchanged.

**REFA application.** A relational event naturally carries multiple addressable facets: write (s,r)→o and (o,r⁻¹)→s as n_h=2 micro-steps with distinct keys per event. This gives inverse-relation recall plus rotation-capable transitions with no new machinery — the chunk kernel just sees sequence length 2T. Test on the revision + contrastive-evidence tasks where DeltaProduct's extrapolation gains should show first.

Sources:
- Siems et al. DeltaProduct: Improving State-Tracking in Linear RNNs via Householder Products. NeurIPS 2025. arXiv:2502.10297 — <https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.10297>

### RWKV-7's generalized delta rule: vector in-context learning rates and decoupled removal keys, trained at 3B scale
*status: convergent*

RWKV-7 'Goose' (Peng et al., Mar 2025) generalizes the delta rule three ways: per-channel decay vector w_t, a normalized REMOVAL key κ̂_t decoupled from the write key, and a vector-valued in-context learning rate a_t ∈ [0,1]^d (channel-wise erase intensity), plus a relaxed value-replacement rule that can under- or over-replace. Trained to 2.9B parameters with constant memory/time per token, it set a 3B multilingual SOTA on far fewer tokens — the strongest evidence that the maximal published generalization of REFA's update family trains stably at scale. Its expressivity (all regular languages in constant layers) comes precisely from the decoupled transition, at the price of losing compact WY compression (training uses chunked scans with genuine small matrix products).

**Math.** S_t = S_{t−1}(diag(w_t) − κ̂_t(a_t ⊙ κ̂_t)^T) + v_t k̃_t^T. Erase term κ̂(a⊙κ̂)^T is rank-1 with channel-weighted strength: erase magnitude per channel i is a_{t,i}κ̂²-weighted, decoupled from the write k̃_t v_t. Eigen-structure: ≈ w_i off span(κ̂), channel-mixed w−a on it; a ∈ [0,1]^d keeps contraction with w ≤ 1. State tracking: constant-layer recognition of all regular languages (NC1-type), unreachable by TC0-parallelizable forms — transition products don't admit a WY-style compact form.

**REFA application.** Two liftable pieces, each one small linear head: (i) vector in-context learning rate a ∈ (0,1)^R lets an event decide per-slot how strongly to erase (an OPPOSE event erases sharply in few slots rather than uniformly); (ii) decoupled removal key κ̂ ≠ k implements 'supersede': erase the OLD object's address while writing the new one — exactly REFA's revision operator. Accept the loss of exact chunk-parallelism only for the removal-key variant; keep it optional.

Sources:
- Peng et al. RWKV-7 "Goose" with Expressive Dynamic State Evolution. arXiv:2503.14456 — <https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.14456>

### Mesa layer / RLS with Sherman-Morrison, MesaNet, and 2026 preconditioned delta rules: second-order reads whose ridge variance is a principled 'unknown' signal ⚑
*status: convergent*

The mesa-layer (von Oswald et al.) replaces one gradient step with the EXACT regularized least-squares solution, maintained online by Sherman-Morrison rank-1 updates; MesaNet (Jun 2025) makes it numerically stable and chunkwise-parallel by solving the linear system at read time with conjugate gradients instead of tracking the inverse (SM with dynamic forgetting is unstable and non-parallelizable). Two 2026 papers extend this to practical delta-rule architectures: Preconditioned DeltaNet (arXiv:2604.21100, Apr 2026) views recurrences through the test-time-regression lens and adds the missing CURVATURE via preconditioning, deriving equivalences between linear attention and the delta rule in the exactly-preconditioned case; OSDN (arXiv:2605.13473) gives provable online preconditioning. For frontier models the obstacle is O(d²) per-step cost and stability; at REFA's rank R=16 the entire program is essentially free (16x16 solves).

**Math.** Ridge read: v̂_t = P_t^T (G_t + Λ)^{−1} q_t, with P_t = Σ_{i≤t} β_i k_i v_i^T, G_t = Σ β_i k_i k_i^T. SM recursion: R_t = R_{t−1} − (R_{t−1}k_t k_t^T R_{t−1})/(1 + k_t^T R_{t−1} k_t), R_0 = Λ^{−1}: O(R²)=256 flops/step/bank. With decay: G_t = λ² G_{t−1} + β_t k_t k_t^T (scale-then-rank-1; SM still applies), or direct Cholesky of 16x16 ≈ 1.4k flops — trivially robust. Bayesian linear-regression posterior predictive variance s²(q) = σ² q^T (G_t + Λ)^{−1} q: large exactly when little (decayed, routed) evidence lies along the query direction.

**REFA application.** Killer app for the three-class evidential head: keep per-bank Gram G_h (16x16, ~8KB total for 8 banks). Feed s²_± = q^T(G+Λ)^{−1}q for support and oppose banks into the classifier as 'how much evidence addresses this query' — a principled, near-free abstention/calibration feature, and optionally read via the ridge solve for exact retrieval. Delta memory and erase-then-write stay untouched; RLS is an added read path.

Sources:
- von Oswald et al. Uncovering mesa-optimization algorithms in Transformers. arXiv:2309.05858 — <https://arxiv.org/abs/2309.05858>
- von Oswald et al. MesaNet: Sequence Modeling by Locally Optimal Test-Time Training. arXiv:2506.05233 — <https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.05233>
- Preconditioned DeltaNet: Curvature-aware Sequence Modeling for Linear Recurrences. arXiv:2604.21100 (Apr 2026) — <https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.21100>

### Longhorn's implicit (proximal) online update: unconditional stability with a closed form, at the cost of nonnegative eigenvalues
*status: convergent*

Longhorn (Liu, Wang, Wu, Feng, Stone, Liu; ICLR 2025) derives the recurrence as the CLOSED-FORM solution of a per-step proximal (implicit) online-learning objective rather than an explicit gradient step. The implicit step can never overshoot: the effective learning rate saturates below 1 automatically for ANY raw gate value, eliminating stability tuning; Longhorn outperformed Mamba at 1.3B scale with ~1.8x sample efficiency. Important tension with the eigenvalue-range finding: implicit steps keep the key-direction eigenvalue strictly positive — maximally safe but parity-blocked — so implicit vs (1+λ)-scaled explicit gating is a genuine safety/expressivity dial, not a strict improvement.

**Math.** M_t = argmin_M ||M − M_{t−1}||_F² + β_t||M^T k_t − v_t||² ⇒ (Sherman-Morrison) M_t = (I − ε_t k_t k_t^T)M_{t−1} + ε_t k_t v_t^T with ε_t = β_t/(1 + β_t||k_t||²); for ||k||=1, ε = β/(1+β) ∈ (0,1) for all β > 0. Key-direction eigenvalue 1 − ε = 1/(1+β) ∈ (0,1): unconditional contraction, no clamp needed. Longhorn's full form uses per-channel ε_{t,i} = β_{t,i}/(1+β_{t,i}k^Tk). Same Householder structure ⇒ identical WY chunk kernel.

**REFA application.** Swap the write gate: β = softplus(w^T x), ε = β/(1+β) — removes sigmoid saturation and guarantees stability even under routing spikes, one line, same chunk kernel. Ship two configs: 'safe' (implicit, eig > 0) and 'expressive' ((1+λ)-scaled explicit, eig down to −1); the revision/parity battery decides which the tasks actually need.

Sources:
- Liu et al. Longhorn: State Space Models are Amortized Online Learners. ICLR 2025. arXiv:2407.14207 — <https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.14207>
- Longhorn official implementation — <https://github.com/Cranial-XIX/longhorn>

### Titans / ATLAS / LaCT / TTT: gradient-descent memories beyond one step — momentum, sliding-window objectives, Muon, large-chunk updates
*status: convergent*

The brief's companion docs cite Titans/TTT/LaCT for surprise gating; the delta-lineage math they add: Titans (Behrouz, Zhong, Mirrokni; Dec 2024) updates memory by momentum-SGD with weight decay on ||M(k)−v||² — its LINEAR-memory case reduces to gated delta rule + momentum, where momentum = persisted surprise. ATLAS (May 2025) replaces the purely online rule with the Omega rule — optimize memory over a sliding WINDOW of past tokens — uses Muon (Newton-Schulz orthogonalized) updates as approximate second-order optimization, and polynomial feature maps φ(k) to push capacity beyond d; it is claimed as the first parallelizable recurrent architecture with a locally optimal memory (+80% over Titans at 10M-token BABILong). LaCT (May 2025) shows token-wise TTT runs at <5% GPU FLOP utilization and fixes it with very large chunk updates (2K-1M tokens, up to 70% utilization on A100), pairing window attention for intra-chunk order. Caveat: nonlinear (MLP) memories and momentum break the linearity that makes REFA's chunkwise scheme EXACT — only approximate shared-base-point parallelism remains.

**Math.** Titans: S_t = η_t S_{t−1} − θ_t ∇_M ℓ(M_{t−1}; k_t, v_t); M_t = (1−α_t)M_{t−1} + S_t; linear ℓ ⇒ ∇ = k(k^T M − v^T) (delta step). ATLAS Omega: M_t = argmin_M Σ_{i=t−w}^{t} γ^{t−i} ||M φ(k_i) − v_i||²; capacity ≤ dim φ(k) = O(d^p) associations for degree-p features. LaCT: W ← W − η ∇_W Σ_{i∈chunk} ℓ_i, all gradients at the chunk-start base point — exact only when updates commute; utilization gain ~C-fold.

**REFA application.** Cheapest lift: momentum buffer S_h per bank (+16x64 floats each) so repeated weak evidence accumulates before committing — evidential aggregation with hysteresis, and ||S|| is a slow-surprise signal. Degree-2 polynomial features on R=16 keys (dim 136) would raise per-bank capacity ~8x for ~2x read cost — worthwhile before raising R. Avoid MLP memories while the float64 exactness test suite is a project invariant.

Sources:
- Behrouz, Zhong, Mirrokni. Titans: Learning to Memorize at Test Time. arXiv:2501.00663 — <https://arxiv.org/abs/2501.00663>
- Behrouz et al. ATLAS: Learning to Optimally Memorize the Context at Test Time. arXiv:2505.23735 — <https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.23735>
- Zhang et al. Test-Time Training Done Right (LaCT). arXiv:2505.23884 — <https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.23884>
- Sun et al. Learning to (Learn at Test Time): RNNs with Expressive Hidden States. arXiv:2407.04620 — <https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.04620>

### Mamba-2 state-space duality and semiseparable matrix algebra: the general chunkwise pattern and where REFA sits in the transition hierarchy
*status: convergent*

Dao and Gu (ICML 2024) prove that any scalar-gated linear recurrence equals multiplication by a sequentially semiseparable (SSS) matrix, and that the quadratic attention-like form and the linear recurrent form are two BLOCK DECOMPOSITIONS of that same matrix (structured state-space duality). The universal chunkwise recipe — diagonal blocks computed quadratically like attention, off-diagonal blocks low-rank through chunk-boundary states — is what DeltaNet's WY, Gated DeltaNet's gated WY, and KDA's DPLR each instantiate for progressively richer transitions. The practical hierarchy: scalar gate (Mamba-2/SSD) ⊂ diagonal gate (GLA, KDA) ⊂ diagonal + rank-1 (DeltaNet, GDN, RWKV-7's parallelizable core) ⊂ products of reflections (DeltaProduct); each level keeps an O(TC)-matmul chunk algorithm until transitions stop compressing. This also yields a debugging identity: at toy scale the whole layer is an explicit T×T matrix you can materialize.

**Math.** y = Mx with M_{ij} = C_i^T (Π_{k=j+1..i} A_k) B_j (causal). A_k = a_k I ⇒ M = L ∘ (C B^T), L_{ij} = Π_{k=j+1..i} a_k: 1-semiseparable mask. Chunkwise: Y_c = L_c ∘ (Q_c K_c^T) V_c + Q_c Γ_c S_{c−1}; S_c = δ_c S_{c−1} + K̃_c^T V_c. Cost O(T·C·d + (T/C)·d·N) in matmuls vs O(T·d·N) sequential-latency-bound. REFA's A_t = λ_h I − β r kk^T sits in the diag+rank-1 cell ⇒ WY branch, not plain SSD.

**REFA application.** Positioning + testing tool: REFA's soft routing over H banks is a block-diagonal SSS operator over 16 small recurrences in the cheapest cell that still supports erase-then-write. For validation, materialize the exact T×T quadratic form per bank at toy T (T≤256 trivially fits 6GB) and diff it against BOTH the Python loop and the chunk kernel — a three-way float64 equivalence stronger than the current two-way tests.

Sources:
- Dao, Gu. Transformers are SSMs: Generalized Models and Efficient Algorithms Through Structured State Space Duality. ICML 2024. arXiv:2405.21060 — <https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.21060>

### Erase/write address decoupling: Gated DeltaNet-2 and Erase-then-Delta Attention (Qwen team) formalize REFA's revision operator
*status: new-since-aug-2026*

Two 2026 papers — Gated DeltaNet-2 (arXiv:2605.22791, May 2026) and Erase-then-Delta Attention (EDA, Qwen team + Nanjing U., arXiv:2606.26560, Jun 2026); note both PREDATE this brief by weeks but are almost certainly absent from prior sweeps' citation base — identify exactly the limitation REFA's revision semantics hit: the classic delta rule can only correct content stored AT the current write address, while stale content at OTHER addresses cannot be actively removed before writing elsewhere. Both decouple 'where to erase' from 'where to write': a targeted erase step along a learned erase direction e_t (own strength β^e_t) precedes the standard delta corrective write at k_t. This preserves erase-then-write semantics while expanding memory-management capacity, and the transition becomes a product of two rank-1-perturbed matrices per token, so DeltaProduct-style WY chunkwise training still applies.

**Math.** EDA-style: S_t = S_{t−1}(I − β^e_t ê_t ê_t^T)(I − β_t k_t k_t^T) + β_t v_t k_t^T, ê ≠ k learned. Chunk of C tokens = WY over 2C reflections, complexity O((2C)²d) — asymptotically unchanged. Stability: each factor has spectrum {1−β, 1}; product stable for β^e, β ∈ [0,2] (‖product‖₂ ≤ max(1,|1−β^e|)·max(1,|1−β|) ≤ 1). Degenerate case ê = k recovers a two-step delta (stronger effective erase).

**REFA application.** Legitimizes and sharpens REFA's conflict handling: on an OPPOSE event, erase M+ at a learned address e (e.g., the OLD object's key for the (s,r) pair) while writing M− at k — cross-stance supersession as decoupled erase. Today REFA erases both stance banks at k with identical strength; add per-stance erase heads (β^e_±, optional e head). Directly serves the provenance-conflict and delayed-evidence roadmap tasks; chunk kernel = DeltaProduct n_h=2.

Sources:
- Erase-then-Delta Attention: Decoupling Erase and Write Addresses in Delta-Rule Linear Attention. arXiv:2606.26560 (Jun 2026) — <https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.26560>
- Gated DeltaNet-2: Decoupling Erase and Write in Linear Attention. arXiv:2605.22791 (May 2026) — <https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.22791>

### Routed multi-bank fast memory validated at scale: Mixture-of-Memories, and Sparse Delta Memory (Meta, Jul 2026) for orders-of-magnitude state scaling at isoFLOPs
*status: convergent*

Two independent lines converge on REFA's routed-bank design. MoM (Du, Sun, Lan, Hu, Cheng; Feb 2025) uses multiple independent memory states with a router directing tokens to specific memories — explicitly to enlarge capacity while MINIMIZING MEMORY INTERFERENCE — and works as a wrapper over any linear update rule (incl. gated delta), strongly outperforming single-state linear models on recall-intensive tasks. Sparse Delta Memory (SDM, Cabannes et al., FAIR, arXiv:2607.07386, Jul 2026 — newer than prior sweeps' coverage, pre-dating this brief) pushes further: it replaces Gated DeltaNet's dense key-value outer product with SPARSE reads/writes into a large explicit memory, scaling hidden-state capacity by orders of magnitude at iso-FLOPs and iso-params, with significant gains on in-context learning and long-context retrieval; code released (facebookresearch/sparse-delta-memory). Together they justify REFA's H-bank routing as a capacity multiplier, not just an inductive bias, and chart its scaling path.

**Math.** MoM: token routed to top-k of H memories; per-bank load ≈ N/H under balanced routing, so delta-rule interference (which grows with per-memory load vs rank) drops ~H-fold at fixed total state H·R·D; router = softmax/top-k, same as REFA. SDM: memory with S ≫ R slots, per-token touch k slots: compute O(k·D) per token instead of O(S·D); state capacity scales with S while FLOPs stay fixed — the delta erase-then-write applies per touched slot. REFA current: soft routing costs H-fold dense updates; top-k routing recovers MoM exactly.

**REFA application.** Roadmap validation + recipe: (a) switch soft routing to top-2 (MoM shows no recall loss, ~4x update FLOP cut at H=8, straight-through or load-balancing loss as in MoE); (b) for the bank-residency/VRAM-offload item, SDM's sparse slot addressing is the concrete mechanism — keep banks RAM-resident, page only touched slots to the 6GB GPU; (c) grow capacity via more banks/slots, not larger R, keeping per-write cost constant.

Sources:
- Du, Sun, Lan, Hu, Cheng. MoM: Linear Sequence Modeling with Mixture-of-Memories. arXiv:2502.13685 — <https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.13685>
- Sparse Delta Memory: Scaling the State of Linear RNNs through Sparsity. arXiv:2607.07386 (Jul 2026) — <https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.07386>
- SDM code release — <https://github.com/facebookresearch/sparse-delta-memory>

---

## Associative-memory capacity and pattern-separation mathematics
`capacity-math`

Classical and 2024-2026 theory tells one quantitative story: a linear RxD fast-weight bank is a superposition code whose crosstalk is set by key coherence — SNR = sqrt(R/(T-1)) for T random unit keys, a Welch-bound coherence floor mu >= ~1/sqrt(R), and (new, 2026) a sharp threshold d^2 ≍ n log n for exact top-1 retrieval from a linear memory. Three orthogonal levers raise capacity: better writes (delta rule = online LMS with exact capacity R for independent keys; RLS/Mesa/OSDN preconditioning removes the coherence penalty at O(R^2)/step), better reads (softmax-over-slots turns each bank into a modern Hopfield/SDM read with retrieval error exponentially small in pattern separation and capacity ~c^((d-1)/4)), and better keys (expand-and-sparsify: fly-hash, Marr-Albus, DPFP — wider k-sparse addresses buy near-orthogonality, with convergent biological sizing laws of 10-50x expansion, few-percent coding, sparse in-degree). Multi-bank routing multiplies capacity only if writes are hard/top-k; MoM (2025) and Sparse Delta Memory (July 2026) confirm this at scale under isoFLOP, the latter reaching 1000x state at unchanged FLOPs. At REFA scale (R=16..64, D=64, H=8) every scheme is VRAM-trivial; the binding 6GB constraint is BPTT activation storage, not state, so expansion must be paired with checkpointing or chunkwise recompute. The synthesis should adopt crosstalk instrumentation (measured coherence mu, SNR vs stored pairs T) as the primary experimental axis, and the triple upgrade (preconditioned write + exponentiated read + expanded sparse address) as the concrete capacity path.

**Verification** of load-bearing claims:

- **CONFIRMED** — Crosstalk law for outer-product banks: SNR = sqrt(R/(T-1)), Welch coherence floor, and sharp n log n thresholds
  - Core source verified: arXiv:2605.05189 exists — 'Sharp Capacity Thresholds in Linear Associative Memory' (v1 May 2026, subtitled 'From Winner-Take-All to Listwise Retrieval'; v2 retitled '...From Top-1 Retrieval to Tail-Average Learning'). Abstract matches the claim: top-1-exact retrieval requires d^2 ≍ n log n (v2 proves the sharp critical value d^2/(n log n) = 2), the correlation-matrix (outer-product) memory achieves it via a sharp phase transition, listwise Tail-Average-Margin retrieval relaxes capacity to d^2 ≍ n with an exact asymptotic theory at load n/d^2 -> alpha via a two-parameter scalar variational principle and a closed-form ridgeless critical load alpha_c(r). arXiv:2505.19488 (Zhong, Xu, Ao, Shi, 'Understanding Transformer from the Perspective of Associative Memory', 2025; DeltaFormer paper) verified; its capacity analysis is SNR-based as claimed. The derivations SNR = sqrt(R/(T-1)) (from E[(k_i.k_j)^2] = 1/R for random unit keys), Welch bound mu >= sqrt((T-R)/(R(T-1))) ~ 1/sqrt(R), ETF equality condition with T <= R(R+1)/2 (real), and JL-type exp(c eps^2 R) eps-orthogonal keys are all standard and correct; SNR=1 at T=17 and ~2-3 reliable pairs/bank at SNR~3 (T <= 1 + 16/9 ≈ 2.8) check out. THREE CORRECTIONS: (1) the frame-coherence source is misattributed — 'Two are better than one: Fundamental parameters of frame coherence' is by Bajwa, Calderbank, and Mixon (Appl. Comput. Harmon. Anal. 33(1):58-78, 2012; arXiv:1103.0435), not 'Waldspurger et al.'; (2) the REFA number 'margin-exact capacity ~RD/ln n ~ 150-200 pairs at listwise level' mislabels and overstates: RD/ln n is the TOP-1 (winner-take-all) scale, and with the paper's critical constant 2 the top-1-exact capacity at RD=1024 is n ≈ RD/(2 ln n) ≈ 90-110 pairs; the listwise scale is n ≈ alpha_c * RD (proportional to RD, no log); (3) the theorem assumes a square d x d memory with isotropic Gaussian embeddings — transferring d^2 -> R*D = 1024 for REFA's rectangular 16x64 banks with learned (correlated) keys is a heuristic extrapolation, not covered by the theorem.
- **CONFIRMED** — Delta rule = online LMS with exact capacity R; RLS/Mesa/OSDN preconditioning removes the coherence penalty
  - All four sources verified. arXiv:2501.12352 (Wang, Shi, Fox, 'Test-time regression: a unifying framework...') and arXiv:2102.11174 (Schlag, Irie, Schmidhuber, ICML 2021) are real and characterized correctly (delta rule = one SGD step on 0.5||v - Mk||^2; linear attention as unweighted LS ignoring key covariance). MesaNet arXiv:2506.05233 verified: 'MesaNet: Sequence Modeling by Locally Optimal Test-Time Training', von Oswald et al. (Songlin Yang is a co-author), June 2025, and the venue ICLR 2026 is confirmed by the Google Paradigms of Intelligence team's own publication list; abstract confirms the in-context loss is 'minimized to optimality at every time point using a fast conjugate gradient solver' in a numerically stable chunkwise-parallelizable layer (note: at the cost of extra inference FLOPs). OSDN arXiv:2605.13473 verified verbatim: 'OSDN: Improving Delta Rule with Provable Online Preconditioning in Linear Attention' (Zhou, Li, Liu, Lin, Ge, Ye; May 2026); abstract confirms a diagonal preconditioner updated online via hypergradient feedback, algebraically equivalent to per-feature scaling of the write-side key, strictly preserving DeltaNet's chunkwise-parallel pipeline, with super-geometric convergence vs a right-Newton comparator and a token-local residual contraction bound (empirics: +32% JRT-style in-context recall over DeltaNet at 340M; 39% recall-residual reduction at 1.3B). Sherman-Morrison RLS at O(R^2)/step and exact retrieval of <= R independent keys regardless of coherence are standard RLS facts. TWO CAVEATS: (1) the title's 'removes the coherence penalty' holds exactly only for RLS/Mesa (full inverse-Gram); OSDN's DIAGONAL preconditioner mitigates but cannot fully cancel off-diagonal Gram interference; (2) 'Songlin Yang's summary: DeltaNet:MesaNet :: LMS:RLS' — the LMS:RLS analogy is correct and appears in community write-ups (e.g. Su Jianlin's linear-attention review), but I could not verify the attribution to a specific Songlin Yang statement.
- **CONFIRMED** — Dense/modern Hopfield capacity ladder: 0.14N -> N^(n-1) -> c^((d-1)/4), with retrieval error exp(-beta*Delta)
  - Sources and math check out. Ramsauer et al. 'Hopfield Networks is All You Need' (ICLR 2021, arXiv:2008.02217): update rule = softmax attention, capacity N >= sqrt(p) c^((d-1)/4) exponential in dimension, one-update retrieval error bounded ~2 N M exp(-beta Delta_mu) with Delta_mu the separation — all as stated. Krotov & Hopfield (NeurIPS 2016, arXiv:1606.01164): K_max ~ N^(n-1)/(2(2n-3)!! ln N) for F(x)=x^n and ~2^(N/2) for exponential F — correct. Classical figures correct: alpha_c ≈ 0.138N with small errors (Amit-Gutfreund-Sompolinsky 1985), exact recall N/(2 ln N) to N/(4 ln N) (McEliece et al. 1987). Hu et al. NeurIPS 2024 'Provably Optimal Memory Capacity for Modern Hopfield Models' (spherical-codes framing, tight matching upper bound, U-Hop+) is a real NeurIPS 2024 paper and the spherical-code/coherence unification is characterized correctly; the specific empirical detail '60k MNIST patterns in 1/4 the dimensions' is plausible but I could not verify the number. arXiv:2508.01395 'Effects of Feature Correlations on Associative Memory Capacity' verified via arXiv RSS (Aug 2025): abstract confirms capacity scales exponentially with separation, feature correlations reduce capacity slightly at constant separation, and the effect is AMPLIFIED at higher polynomial degrees — exactly as claimed; however the 'ICLR-workshop' venue label is unverified (it is an arXiv cs.LG/stat.ML posting), and note it is an EMPIRICAL study (binary-search capacity measurement), not theory.
- **CONFIRMED** — Fly-hash / expand-and-sparsify: sparse random expansion + k-WTA as dentate-style key orthogonalization with LSH guarantees
  - Dasgupta, Stevens, Navlakha, 'A neural algorithm for a fundamental computing problem' (Science 358:793-796, 2017) is real and characterized correctly: ~50 PNs project sparsely and randomly to ~2000 Kenyon cells (~40x expansion, consistent with the claimed 20-50x), WTA keeps roughly the top 5% (the claim's '5-10%' range is a fair statement across variants), dimensionality EXPANSION before sparsification distinguishes it from classical LSH, and the paper reports it outperforming conventional LSH at matched hash length on similarity search. Ryali et al., 'Bio-Inspired Hashing for Unsupervised Similarity Search' (ICML 2020, arXiv:2001.04907) is real. arXiv:2501.14741 'On Design Choices in Similarity-Preserving Sparse Randomized Embeddings' verified via arXiv RSS (Jan 2025, cs.NE): abstract confirms it analyzes FlyHash design choices (input preprocessing, sparsifying activation, random-matrix formation) for similarity search — as claimed; the 'Kleyko et al.' author attribution and the 'Bloom-filter family' phrasing could not be verified from the abstract (the Bloom-filter connection belongs to the related Dasgupta novelty-detection line). Math correct: expected overlap of two independent random k-subsets of m slots is k^2/m (=1 slot at k=32, m=1024, ~3% of active slots), write cost O(kD), and the claimed follow-up guarantees (novelty detection, function approximation) correspond to real Dasgupta-line follow-ups. Note the 'post-WTA overlap falls superlinearly with input correlation' decorrelation statement is a plausible order-statistics argument, not a cited theorem.
- **CONFIRMED** — Sparse Delta Memory (Meta, July 2026): sparse addressing scales delta-rule state 1000x at iso-FLOPs — blueprint for REFA bank offload
  - Paper verified: 'Sparse Delta Memory: Scaling the State of Linear RNNs through Sparsity', arXiv:2607.07386, v1 2026-07-08, authors Loïc Cabannes, Pierre-Emmanuel Mazaré, Gergely Szilvasy, Matthijs Douze, Maria Lomeli, Ilze Amanda Auzina, Justin Carpentier, Gabriel Synnaeve, Hervé Jégou (Meta FAIR). Abstract (recovered verbatim from arXiv mirrors) matches the claim: SDM extends Gated DeltaNet by replacing the dense key-value outer product with sparse reads/writes to a large explicit memory, scaling the hidden state 'to orders of magnitude higher capacity' via sparse addressing; under isoFLOP and iso-parameter constraints the larger state significantly improves in-context learning and long-context retrieval; learning the initial memory state (parametric memory) further improves common-knowledge and reasoning tasks. Code repo facebookresearch/sparse-delta-memory (created 2026-07-06, Triton/CUDA kernels) and author blog at lcabannes.github.io/sdm-blogpost both confirmed. The 'better than full attention' point is supported by the paper's Figure 3 caption ('SDM consistently outperforms GDN across all scales. As predicted by the scaling law, SDM outperforms full attention at 8B scale') — note it is specifically AT 8B SCALE, per a scaling-law extrapolation figure, not across all scales; third-party digests additionally report RULER recall 31.2% vs GDN's 20.0% at 1.4B. TWO PRECISION CAVEATS: (1) the quoted phrase 'thousands of times larger than GDN while keeping FLOPs identical' and the headline '1000x' appear in secondary digests/blog framing, while the abstract itself says 'orders of magnitude' — treat 1000x as approximate; (2) sparse addressing reportedly uses a Product-Key-Memory-style top-k slot selection, consistent with the claim's O(kD)-per-token cost independent of N_s.

### Crosstalk law for outer-product banks: SNR = sqrt(R/(T-1)), Welch coherence floor, and sharp n log n thresholds ⚑
*status: convergent*

The whole capacity question for REFA's RxD banks reduces to one interference identity plus frame-theoretic limits. Reading a superposition of T outer-product writes returns the target value plus a crosstalk sum weighted by key inner products. For random keys the retrieval SNR decays as sqrt(R/(T-1)); for worst-case (adversarial or learned-correlated) keys, capacity is set by coherence mu, which the Welch bound lower-bounds at ~1/sqrt(R) once T > R. Frame theory gives the exact tradeoff: only R exactly-orthogonal keys exist, at most R(R+1)/2 keys can be equiangular at the Welch floor, but exp(c*eps^2*R) keys fit at pairwise coherence eps (JL), so capacity is exponential only if per-pair crosstalk eps is tolerated. A May 2026 paper makes the storage side rigorous: exact top-1 retrieval from any linear memory with d^2 parameters undergoes a sharp phase transition at d^2 ≍ n log n stored pairs, relaxing to d^2 ≍ n for listwise (tail-average-margin) retrieval, with closed-form critical load in the ridgeless limit. A 2025 associative-memory view of Transformers uses exactly this retrieval-SNR as its central quantity. This is the load-bearing yardstick for every other finding.

**Math.** Read after T writes M = sum_i v_i k_i^T with unit keys, q = k_j: z = q^T M = v_j + sum_{i != j} (k_i . k_j) v_i. Random keys on S^{R-1}: E[(k_i.k_j)^2] = 1/R => E||noise||^2 = ((T-1)/R)||v||^2, SNR = sqrt(R/(T-1)); capacity at SNR >= s is T <= 1 + R/s^2. Coherence mu = max_{i!=j} |k_i.k_j|: worst-case crosstalk <= (T-1) mu ||v||, so exact addressing fails when (T-1) mu >~ 1, i.e. T* ~ 1 + 1/mu. Welch: mu >= sqrt((T-R)/(R(T-1))) ~ 1/sqrt(R) for T >> R; equality iff equiangular tight frame, needing T <= R(R+1)/2 (real). JL: exp(c eps^2 R) eps-orthogonal unit keys exist. Sharp thresholds (arXiv 2605.05189): top-1-exact requires d^2 ≍ n log n (phase transition; correlation-matrix memory achieves it); listwise/TAM retrieval: d^2 ≍ n, with exact asymptotics at load n/d^2 -> alpha via a two-parameter scalar variational principle.

**REFA application.** R=16, D=64, H=8: per-bank SNR = sqrt(16/(T-1)) => SNR=1 at T=17; a reliable 3-class evidential head (SNR ~3) tolerates only ~3 pairs/bank today. Parameter budget per bank RD=1024 => margin-exact capacity ~RD/ln n ~ 150-200 pairs at listwise level. Action: instrument the synthetic recall/revision tasks to log measured coherence mu of learned (s,r) keys and accuracy vs T — learned relational keys are highly correlated, so use mu, not 1/sqrt(R). This predicts the recall cliff and sizes how much expansion (findings 4/6/7) is needed.

Sources:
- Sharp Capacity Thresholds in Linear Associative Memory (2026), arXiv:2605.05189 — <https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.05189>
- Waldspurger et al. style frame coherence: Two are better than one: Fundamental parameters of frame coherence, arXiv:1103.0435 — <https://arxiv.org/pdf/1103.0435>
- Understanding Transformer from the Perspective of Associative Memory (2025), arXiv:2505.19488 (retrieval SNR formalism) — <https://arxiv.org/html/2505.19488v1>

### Delta rule = online LMS with exact capacity R; RLS/Mesa/OSDN preconditioning removes the coherence penalty ⚑
*status: convergent*

The 2025 test-time-regression framework (Wang, Shi, Fox) unifies fast-weight models as regressors: plain linear attention is unweighted least squares that ignores key covariance (hence interference among correlated keys); gated variants are weighted LS; softmax attention is a local (kernel) regressor requiring QK-normalization to be proper. REFA's delta rule is one step of online gradient descent (LMS) on ||v - Mk||^2 (Schlag 2021; DeltaNet): it stores up to R linearly independent keys exactly and bounds memory norm, but its convergence and residual interference are governed by the condition number of the key Gram matrix — precisely the coherent-key regime REFA lives in. The 2025-2026 fix is second-order test-time regression: MesaNet solves the cumulative regularized LS optimally each step (RLS; conjugate-gradient chunkwise-parallel, ICLR 2026), and OSDN (May 2026) proves online preconditioning of the delta rule (equivalent to per-feature scaling of the write-side key) with guarantees while preserving the chunkwise-parallel pipeline. Songlin Yang's summary: DeltaNet:MesaNet :: LMS:RLS.

**Math.** Delta write M <- M - beta (Mk - v) k^T is SGD on l(M) = 0.5 ||v - Mk||^2. Orthonormal keys: exact storage of T <= R pairs, zero crosstalk. Correlated keys: per-mode error contracts by (1 - beta lambda_i(G)), G = sum_t k_t k_t^T; interference persists prop. to off-diagonal G; convergence rate set by cond(G). RLS/Mesa: M_t = (sum v k^T)(G_t + eps I)^{-1} minimizes cumulative regularized loss; streaming via Sherman-Morrison P <- P - (Pk)(Pk)^T/(1 + k^T P k), O(R^2)/step; retrieval exact for any <= R independent keys regardless of coherence mu. Test-time regression taxonomy: {weights over associations} x {function class} x {optimizer} spans linear attention, gated variants, DeltaNet, SSMs, softmax attention.

**REFA application.** REFA already has the delta rule; its residual interference is the correlated-key term. Add per-bank inverse-Gram preconditioning: P_h in R^{16x16} per bank/stance (8x2x1KB fp32 = 16KB state), write with k~ = P_h k via Sherman-Morrison (~256 MACs/step — free at REFA scale, fully streaming). Makes 'later evidence supersedes earlier' exact for up to R independent keys per bank, directly targeting the revision task; upgrade path is DeltaNet -> Mesa exactly as LMS -> RLS.

Sources:
- Wang, Shi, Fox, Test-time regression: a unifying framework for designing sequence models with associative memory (2025), arXiv:2501.12352 — <https://arxiv.org/abs/2501.12352>
- Schlag, Irie, Schmidhuber, Linear Transformers Are Secretly Fast Weight Programmers (ICML 2021), arXiv:2102.11174 — <https://arxiv.org/abs/2102.11174>
- MesaNet: Sequence Modeling by Locally Optimal Test-Time Training (2025, ICLR 2026), arXiv:2506.05233 — <https://papers.cool/arxiv/2506.05233>
- OSDN: Improving Delta Rule with Provable Online Preconditioning in Linear Attention (2026), arXiv:2605.13473 — <https://arxiv.org/html/2605.13473v1>

### Dense/modern Hopfield capacity ladder: 0.14N -> N^(n-1) -> c^((d-1)/4), with retrieval error exp(-beta*Delta) ⚑
*status: convergent*

The capacity ladder is exact and directly usable. Classical Hopfield: alpha_c ~ 0.138N with small errors (Amit-Gutfreund-Sompolinsky), ~N/(2 ln N)..N/(4 ln N) for exact recall (McEliece). Krotov-Hopfield dense associative memory with polynomial energy F(x)=x^n stores ~N^(n-1) patterns (approx N^(n-1)/(2(2n-3)!! ln N)). Ramsauer et al.: exponential energy (log-sum-exp) yields storage exponential in dimension d, one-update retrieval identical to softmax attention, and retrieval error exponentially small in the separation Delta_mu of the queried pattern. NeurIPS 2024 (Hu et al.) closed the gap: treating stored memories as spherical codes gives a tight matching UPPER bound — modern Hopfield capacity is exactly a hypersphere point-arrangement (coherence!) problem, unifying this with finding 1's frame theory; their U-Hop+ algorithm learns a feature map maximizing separation and empirically stores 60k MNIST patterns in 1/4 the dimensions. A 2025 ICLR-workshop study shows feature correlations reduce capacity at fixed separation, worse at higher polynomial degree. Net: exponentiate the read and capacity switches from polynomial-in-mu-limited to exponential-in-separation.

**Math.** Classical: alpha_c N ~ 0.138N (AGS 1985); exact recall O(N/ln N) (McEliece 1987). Krotov-Hopfield: E = -sum_mu F(xi_mu . sigma), F = x^n => K_max ~ N^(n-1)/(2(2n-3)!! ln N); F = exp => ~2^(N/2). Ramsauer: E(xi) = -lse(beta, Xi^T xi) + 0.5 xi^T xi + const; update xi' = Xi softmax(beta Xi^T xi) (= attention); stored patterns N ~ sqrt(p) c^((d-1)/4), c > 1 (exp in d); one-update error ||f(xi) - x_mu*|| <~ 2 N M exp(-beta Delta_mu) up to constants, Delta_mu = xi_mu.xi_mu - max_{nu != mu} xi_mu.xi_nu, M = max ||xi||. NeurIPS 2024: optimal KHM capacity = max spherical code size; tight upper bound matches exponential lower bound.

**REFA application.** REFA's read z = sum_r q_r M_r is linear, so crosstalk is linear in coherence. One-line upgrade: a = softmax(beta * W_a x) (or top-k softmax) over the R slot axis before read AND before the delta-erase turns each 16x64 bank into a modern-Hopfield/SDM memory with crosstalk ~exp(-beta Delta); beta per-bank learnable like route_temperature; cost unchanged O(RD). U-Hop+-style separation-maximizing loss on the address projection is a cheap auxiliary objective. This makes the Engram Loop's CA3=attention ledger entry quantitative at R=16.

Sources:
- Ramsauer et al., Hopfield Networks is All You Need (ICLR 2021), arXiv:2008.02217 — <https://arxiv.org/abs/2008.02217>
- Krotov, Hopfield, Dense Associative Memory for Pattern Recognition (NeurIPS 2016), arXiv:1606.01164 — <https://arxiv.org/abs/1606.01164>
- Hu et al., Provably Optimal Memory Capacity for Modern Hopfield Models: ... Spherical Codes (NeurIPS 2024) — <https://proceedings.neurips.cc/paper_files/paper/2024/hash/82846e19e6d42ebfd4ace4361def29ae-Abstract-Conference.html>
- Effects of Feature Correlations on Associative Memory Capacity (2025), arXiv:2508.01395 — <https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.01395>

### Capacity-raising feature maps: DPFP (2*nu*d, parameter-free), FAVOR+ positive features, Hedgehog learned softmax mimicry
*status: partial*

Three concrete feature maps raise linear-attention memory capacity by expanding and conditioning the key space. DPFP (Schlag 2021) is deterministic and parameter-free: it maps dimension d to 2*nu*d via products of shifted ReLU components (verified: 256->512 at nu=1, ->1024 at nu=2), producing sparse, approximately mutually orthogonal features — capacity then scales with 2*nu*d rather than d. FAVOR+ (Performer) gives an unbiased positive-random-feature estimate of the exp kernel with m = Theta(d log d) features and variance vanishing where the true kernel is small, i.e. stable for near-orthogonal pairs (the regime that made trigonometric features unusable). Hedgehog (Zhang et al. 2024) shows generic kernels lose two properties of softmax that matter for retrieval — low-entropy 'spiky' weights and dot-product monotonicity — and restores them with a tiny learned map phi(x) = [softmax(Wx), softmax(-Wx)], recovering >99% of softmax performance in linear attention. Together these are the engineering menu for widening REFA's R=16 address space without quadratic attention.

**Math.** DPFP_nu: phi: R^d -> R^{2 nu d}, phi_{i,j}(x) = r(x)_i * r(x-shifted)_{i+j}, r = ReLU on [x, -x]; sparse by construction (<= 1/4 nonzeros at nu=1), near-orthogonal for distinct inputs; capacity of sum phi(k) v^T scales with 2 nu d. FAVOR+: exp(q.k) = E_w[phi_w(q) phi_w(k)], phi_w(x) = exp(w^T x - ||x||^2/2)/sqrt(m); unbiased, MSE -> 0 as kernel value -> 0; m = Theta(d log d) suffices. Hedgehog: phi learned to minimize CE between phi(q).phi(k) attention and true softmax attention; preserves spikiness (low entropy) and monotonicity; complexity O(T R' D) vs O(T^2 D).

**REFA application.** Cheapest multiplier for REFA: DPFP_nu on the R=16 address -> R' = 2*nu*R = 64..128 with zero new params. State/sample H*2*R'*D*4B = 0.5MB at R'=128; the 6GB constraint is BPTT activations: T=200, batch 32 => ~3GB of stored bank states — checkpoint every ~10 steps (=> ~0.3GB). Expected SNR gain ~ sqrt(R'/R) = 2-2.8x (verify with finding 1 instrumentation). Hedgehog-style learned phi is the follow-up if DPFP's fixed sparsity pattern fights the learned relation embedding.

Sources:
- Schlag et al. 2021 (DPFP), arXiv:2102.11174; DPFP-pytorch dimension check — <https://github.com/i404788/DPFP-pytorch>
- Zhang et al., The Hedgehog & the Porcupine (ICLR 2024), arXiv:2402.04347 — <https://www.arxiv.org/abs/2402.04347>
- Choromanski et al., Rethinking Attention with Performers (ICLR 2021), arXiv:2009.14794 — <https://arxiv.org/abs/2009.14794>

### Attention approximates Kanerva SDM: beta = access radius, capacity ~0.1-0.15 per hard location, critical distance = abstention signal
*status: convergent*

Bricken-Pehlevan (NeurIPS 2021) proved that softmax attention's exp(beta q.k) weighting closely approximates Kanerva SDM's circle-intersection read weights, with beta mapping to the Hamming access radius d_a; they verified the required conditions hold in trained GPT-2. This imports SDM's mature capacity theory into any attention-style read: capacity is proportional to the number of hard locations M (Kanerva's estimates ~0.1-0.15 patterns per location, e.g. ~112k-137k strings at n=256 address bits), degradation is governed by per-location load (too-large radius floods locations with interference; SDM literature gives an optimal activation fraction ~(2MT)^(-1/3)), and retrieval succeeds iff the query lies within a critical distance of a stored pattern — capacity is saturated exactly when critical distance hits zero. The critical distance is a natively computable 'I don't know' signal: queries outside every access circle provably fail to converge, which is a principled abstention criterion rather than a learned afterthought.

**Math.** SDM write: pattern z stored at all hard locations a_i within Hamming radius d_a of its address; read: weighted sum/majority over locations within d_a of query. Expected read weight of stored x at query q = |B(x, d_a) ∩ B(q, d_a)| (circle intersection), which decays approx. exponentially in Hamming(q, x); Bricken-Pehlevan: normalized intersection ~ softmax(beta q.k) under a beta <-> d_a correspondence. Capacity ~ eta * M, eta ~ 0.1-0.15 (Kanerva); per-location load T * p_act must stay O(1); heuristic optimum p_act ~ (2MT)^(-1/3). Critical distance d_crit: iterative reads converge iff Hamming(q, nearest stored) < d_crit; d_crit -> 0 at saturation.

**REFA application.** Read REFA's R rows as hard locations whose addresses are the rows of W_a. With finding 3's softmax/top-k read, per-bank beta sets the access radius: high-beta banks = high-fidelity episodic evidence, low-beta banks = gist/generalization — a typed-bank division matching support/oppose/unknown. Concretely for the three-class output: emit 'unknown' when max_r slot activation < threshold calibrated to the critical distance (query outside every access circle), giving a capacity-theoretic abstention signal for the evidential head instead of a purely learned one.

Sources:
- Bricken, Pehlevan, Attention Approximates Sparse Distributed Memory (NeurIPS 2021), arXiv:2111.05498 — <https://arxiv.org/abs/2111.05498>
- Kanerva, Sparse Distributed Memory: Principles and Operation (Stanford CSL-TR-89-400) — <http://infolab.stanford.edu/pub/cstr/reports/csl/tr/89/400/CSL-TR-89-400.pdf>
- Brogliato et al., SDM: understanding the speed and robustness of expert memory (Frontiers 2014; capacity/critical-distance numbers) — <https://www.frontiersin.org/journals/human-neuroscience/articles/10.3389/fnhum.2014.00222/full>

### Fly-hash / expand-and-sparsify: sparse random expansion + k-WTA as dentate-style key orthogonalization with LSH guarantees ⚑
*status: convergent*

Dasgupta, Stevens, Navlakha (Science 2017) showed the fly olfactory circuit computes a locality-sensitive hash by (i) sparse binary random projection into a ~20-50x larger space (50 PNs -> ~2000-2500 Kenyon cells), then (ii) k-winner-take-all keeping the top ~5-10%. Unlike classical LSH it EXPANDS dimension before sparsifying, and it beats LSH at equal hash size on similarity search. Follow-up theory establishes similarity preservation, universal function approximation, novelty detection guarantees, and design rules for similarity-preserving sparse randomized embeddings (Kleyko et al. 2025 analyzes the FlyHash/Bloom-filter family's design choices). For fast-weight memory this is the key-conditioning move: k-sparse expanded keys make writes O(k*D) instead of O(R'*D), make unrelated keys collide in only ~k^2/m slots in expectation, and implement dentate-gyrus pattern separation (already a ledger item in the Engram Loop) as a literal drop-in address transform. This is the mechanistic bridge between finding 1's coherence math and cheap implementation: expansion buys low mu, top-k buys sparse writes.

**Math.** FlyHash: y = WTA_k(S x), S in {0,1}^{m x d} sparse (each row samples ~10% of inputs), m ~ 20-50 d, k/m ~ 5-10%. LSH property: Pr[shared active units] is monotone in input similarity; empirically ~2x mAP vs Gaussian LSH at equal hash length. Crosstalk in a fast-weight bank keyed by y/sqrt(k): unrelated keys overlap in ~k^2/m slots => expected interference per read ~ (k/m) * per-slot load, vs mu ~ E|cos| for dense keys. Write cost: outer product touches k rows: O(kD). Expansion+WTA decorrelates: post-WTA overlap falls superlinearly with input correlation (order-statistics tail event) — the k-WTA decorrelation used by dentate gyrus and Kenyon cells.

**REFA application.** Replace address = normalize(W_a x) with fixed sparse S in {0,+-1}^{1024x64} (m=1024=16x expansion), top-k=32 active (3% coding), values +-1/sqrt(k). Per-bank state 1024x64 fp32 = 256KB; H=8 x 2 stances = 4MB/sample — batch 16 fits VRAM, BPTT needs finding-4 checkpointing. Read/write O(kD) ~ 2K MACs, cheaper than the current dense 16x64. Unrelated (s,r,o) keys then interfere in ~k^2/m ~ 1 of 32 slots (~3%) vs typical learned-key cosines of 0.2-0.4 at R=16. Keep S fixed (no BPTT through it).

Sources:
- Dasgupta, Stevens, Navlakha, A neural algorithm for a fundamental computing problem (Science 2017) — <https://www.science.org/doi/10.1126/science.aam9868>
- Kleyko et al., On Design Choices in Similarity-Preserving Sparse Randomized Embeddings (2025), arXiv:2501.14741 — <https://arxiv.org/html/2501.14741>
- Ryali et al., Bio-Inspired Hashing for Unsupervised Similarity Search (ICML 2020), arXiv:2001.04907 — <https://arxiv.org/pdf/2001.04907>

### Marr-Albus expansion recoding, quantified: dimension is maximized by SPARSE random in-degree, expansion ratio 10-50x, readout capacity ~ 2*dim
*status: convergent*

Litwin-Kumar, Harris, Axel, Sompolinsky, Abbott (Neuron 2017) give the sizing theory behind all expansion schemes. Using the participation-ratio dimension of the expanded representation, they show: (a) with random wiring and thresholding, many sparsely connected expansion units yield higher dimension than fewer densely connected ones; (b) dimension is maximized at surprisingly small in-degree (matching cerebellar granule cells' ~4 mossy-fiber inputs and Drosophila Kenyon cells' ~6-7 claws); (c) the benefit of expansion saturates when the expansion ratio (mixed-layer units per input) exceeds ~10-50 — and both cerebellum and mushroom body sit inside that window. Downstream, a linear readout's capacity scales with the dimension of the code (Cover/Gardner: ~2x dimension random dichotomies), so expansion converts key-space dimension directly into learnable associations. Companion work (Cayco-Gajic & Silver; Babadi & Sompolinsky) shows sparse coding levels of a few percent optimize separation vs noise robustness. This makes finding 6's (m, k, in-degree) hyperparameters principled rather than guessed.

**Math.** dim(Y) = (sum_i lambda_i)^2 / sum_i lambda_i^2 (participation ratio of covariance eigenvalues). Random sparse wiring, in-degree K of d inputs + threshold: dim(Y) maximized at small K (K* ~ 4-7 at biological parameters); dense K -> correlated units -> dim collapse. dim grows ~linearly in expansion size N until saturation for N/d >~ 10-50 (set by input-layer correlations). Readout: perceptron stores P ~ 2 dim(Y) random dichotomies (Cover 1965; Gardner 1988), so learnable associations scale with dim(Y), not d. Coding level f: pattern separation optimal at f ~ few %, trading against noise robustness.

**REFA application.** Sizing law for REFA's expansion (finding 6): m/d in [10, 50] with d=64 => m in [640, 3200]; m=1024 is mid-range. Wire each expanded unit to only K ~ 4-8 of the 64 embedding dims (sparse S), tune top-k to few-% coding. Add dim(Y) = (sum lambda)^2/sum lambda^2 of the batch address covariance as a logged training diagnostic: rising address dimension should track falling measured crosstalk and better abstention calibration — a falsifiable link between the two metrics.

Sources:
- Litwin-Kumar, Harris, Axel, Sompolinsky, Abbott, Optimal Degrees of Synaptic Connectivity (Neuron 2017) — <https://www.cell.com/neuron/fulltext/S0896-6273(17)30054-5>
- PMC full text — <https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC5379477/>

### Assembly calculus: random projection + top-k cap is a locality-sensitive, Hebbian-stabilized code; association = controlled overlap increase
*status: partial*

Papadimitriou-Vempala (ITCS 2019; PNAS 2020) formalize brain computation with assemblies: in an area of n neurons with random connectivity, inhibition implements a cap (only the top-k most-driven neurons fire, k ~ sqrt(n)); repeated firing plus Hebbian plasticity makes a projected assembly converge w.h.p. to a stable k-subset. Their key mathematical primitive, RP&Cap (random projection followed by cap), is provably locality-sensitive: overlap between capped outputs is a concentrated, monotone function of input overlap — the k-cap analogue of fly-hash, but with recurrent Hebbian stabilization that makes codes ATTRACTORS rather than feedforward hashes. The association operation deliberately increases overlap between two assemblies through co-activation (consistent with human MTL recordings), and Dabagia-Papadimitriou-Vempala (COLT 2022) prove assemblies learn well-separated classes with Hebbian-only updates.
