For REFA this is the theory of top-k ROUTING (cap across banks) and of graded revision: relatedness is stored as overlap, not as overwriting.

**Math.** Area: n neurons, G(n, p) random graph, cap_k = keep top-k synaptic inputs firing, k ~ sqrt(n). Projection: x -> cap_k(A x), iterate with Hebbian weight increase (w -> (1+beta) w on co-firing) => convergence w.h.p. to stable assembly (total support ~ k + o(k)). RP&Cap locality sensitivity: for inputs with overlap alpha, cap overlaps concentrate around a monotone function of alpha; disjoint inputs give overlap ~ k^2/n (chance). Association: co-stimulation raises inter-assembly overlap from chance toward a plateau; overlap encodes relatedness. Classification (COLT 2022): Hebbian assemblies PAC-learn well-separated classes.

**REFA application.** Two uses: (i) routing: replace the softmax over H=8 banks with a top-2 cap plus a slow Hebbian bias (banks that fire for a relation get easier to fire) — the biological form of the roadmap's sparse top-k routing, keeping writes local; (ii) revision semantics: on repeated (s,r) keys, let the new slot pattern deliberately share support with the old one (partial erase, overlap-increasing write) so revision is graded association rather than destructive overwrite — directly testable on the contrastive-evidence and provenance-conflict tasks.

Sources:
- Papadimitriou, Vempala, Random Projection in the Brain and Computation with Assemblies of Neurons (ITCS 2019) — <https://faculty.cc.gatech.edu/~vempala/papers/assemblies.pdf>
- Papadimitriou et al., Brain computation by assemblies of neurons (PNAS 2020) — <https://www.pnas.org/doi/10.1073/pnas.2001893117>
- Dabagia, Papadimitriou, Vempala (COLT 2022), arXiv:2110.03171 — <https://arxiv.org/pdf/2110.03171>

### Mixture-of-Memories: multiple routed fast-weight states multiply capacity only under HARD routing (sqrt(H) SNR, H*R exact capacity)
*status: convergent*

MoM (Sun et al. 2025, arXiv:2502.13685) is the direct scaled-up validation of REFA's multi-bank design: multiple independent linear-attention memory states with a router directing each token to a subset of memories 'greatly enhances overall memory capacity while minimizing memory interference', matching Transformer-level recall on recall-intensive benchmarks at linear train / constant inference cost, and composing with any inner update rule (they use gated delta). Raven (July 2026, arXiv:2607.25357) pushes the same principle with sparse memory routing for high recall. The capacity arithmetic makes the design constraint precise: SOFT routing (REFA's current softmax) writes an r_h-scaled copy of every token into every bank, so every bank still accumulates crosstalk from all T tokens and capacity is only fractionally multiplied; HARD top-1/top-k routing splits the load T_h ~ T/H, giving a sqrt(H) SNR gain at fixed R and additive exact capacity sum_h R_h = H*R. Routing is thus a capacity lever exactly as strong as expansion, but only in its sparse form — convergent with the cap operation of finding 8 and the Engram Loop's sparse-routing roadmap item.

**Math.** H banks, router weights r_h(t). Soft routing: bank h noise variance ~ (1/R) sum_{t} r_h(t)^2 ||v_t||^2 — all T tokens contribute; effective capacity gain <= H only in the degenerate one-hot limit. Hard top-1: T_h ~ T/H (balanced) => SNR_h = sqrt(R/(T/H - 1)) ~ sqrt(HR/T): sqrt(H) gain; exact-storage capacity additive: sum_h R_h = HR pairs. Cost: top-k routing cuts write FLOPs H/k-fold. MoM: linear-time training, constant-memory inference, recall-intensive benchmark parity with Transformers at similar scale.

**REFA application.** REFA's H=8 typed banks are a MoM instance with the WRONG (soft) router for capacity. Change: top-2 hard routing for writes (straight-through or annealed temperature; keep soft mixture for reads), predicted ~sqrt(8/2)=2x SNR and 4x write-FLOP reduction — which funds H=32 finer-grained relation banks in the same 6GB budget. MoM's ablations (number of memories vs recall) are the template for REFA's bank-count sweep; keep a small always-on 'shared' bank as MoM does for stability.

Sources:
- Sun et al., MoM: Linear Sequence Modeling with Mixture-of-Memories (2025), arXiv:2502.13685 — <https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.13685>
- Raven: High-Recall Sequence Modeling with Sparse Memory Routing (2026), arXiv:2607.25357 — <https://arxiv.org/html/2607.25357v1>

### Sparse Delta Memory (Meta, July 2026): sparse addressing scales delta-rule state 1000x at iso-FLOPs — blueprint for REFA bank offload ⚑
*status: convergent*

Cabannes, Mazare, Jegou et al. (arXiv:2607.07386, July 2026, FAIR) extend Gated DeltaNet by replacing the dense key-value outer-product state with sparse reads/writes into a large explicit memory: each token touches only a few slots of a state 'thousands of times larger than GDN while keeping FLOPs identical'. Under iso-FLOP and iso-parameter comparisons, the larger sparsely-addressed state significantly improves in-context learning and long-context retrieval, reaching better training loss/NLL than Gated DeltaNet and even full attention in their setting; code released. This is the industrial-scale convergence of this survey's whole stack — expand-and-sparsify addressing (findings 6/7) + delta-rule writes (finding 2) + sparse routing (finding 9) — and empirically confirms the capacity ∝ addressable-state, FLOPs ∝ sparsity decomposition of finding 1. It postdates every ML anchor cited in the Engram Loop ledger (through Jan 2026) and appears in no companion document, making it the strongest recent external validation of REFA's roadmap direction (sparse top-k routing, bank residency offload).

**Math.** State: N_s slots x D values with sparse addressing (top-k slot selection per token) replacing the dense R x D outer product; per-token read/write cost O(k D) independent of N_s => state size scales ~1000x at identical FLOPs to Gated DeltaNet. Delta rule applied on the k-sparse support: erase-then-write per selected slot keeps slot norms bounded (finding 2's LMS, restricted to the support). Empirics: iso-FLOP, iso-param scaling shows monotone recall/ICL gains with state size; better NLL than GDN and full attention in reported setting. Complexity: train linear in T; inference constant per token; memory traffic per token k*D elements.

**REFA application.** Direct blueprint for two roadmap items. Bank residency offload: hold N_s = 64K slots x D=64 fp32 = 16MB (per stance) in host RAM; keep only the top-k routed slots resident in VRAM; per-token PCIe traffic k*D*4B = 8KB at k=32 — negligible at REFA scale, so the 6GB card can address orders of magnitude more evidence pairs than the current 8x2x16x64 state. Use their sparse-support gated-delta update as the streaming rule over the expanded fly-hash address space (finding 6), and their isoFLOP state-size sweep as REFA's capacity-vs-state experimental protocol.

Sources:
- Cabannes et al., Sparse Delta Memory: Scaling the State of Linear RNNs through Sparsity (2026), arXiv:2607.07386 — <https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.07386>
- Author blog post with mechanism details — <https://lcabannes.github.io/sdm-blogpost/>
- Code: facebookresearch/sparse-delta-memory — <https://github.com/facebookresearch/sparse-delta-memory>

## Evidential uncertainty algebra for support / opposition / unknown
`evidential-math`

The uncertainty mathematics REFA needs already exists as a coherent stack, but no single field owns it. Josang's subjective logic gives the exact algebra for a (support, oppose, unknown) triple: a binomial opinion (b,d,u) with b+d+u=1 is a bijection of a Beta(r+Wa, s+W(1-a)) evidence pair, so REFA's two memory reads map to two nonnegative evidence scalars and b/d/u falls out in closed form; Denoeux's weight-of-evidence result shows the same head is a Dempster-Shafer combination whose ignorance mass m(Theta) and conflict mass K separate "no evidence" from "contradictory evidence" — two distinct reasons to say unknown. The 2022-2024 theory wave (Bengs impossibility of proper second-order scoring rules; Jürgens ICML 2024; the NeurIPS 2024 "Mirage" paper) proves that a trained evidential head's uncertainty magnitude is not a faithful epistemic quantity, so u should be treated as a cheap ranking score, with guarantees supplied separately. Split conformal prediction provides exactly that: a distribution-free 1-alpha coverage wrapper costing two stored quantiles and O(1) per query, with set-size-based abstention (unknown iff the prediction set is not a singleton), and Adaptive Conformal Inference extends the guarantee to REFA's non-exchangeable stream via an O(1) online quantile update. Chow's 1970 threshold t*=lambda/(1+lambda) and risk-coverage curves give the decision layer and evaluation, and an August 2026 result warns concretely against training "unknown" as an error-penalized discrete action (reward-gradient/KL-anchor collapse). Calibration should be measured with smECE/distance-to-calibration, not binned ECE, at REFA's tiny eval sizes — and REFA's synthetic generator knows true evidence counts, enabling exact epistemic-calibration audits no natural benchmark can do. Net design: keep the two reads, replace the 3-way softmax with a two-channel evidence head (subjective-logic/Denoeux algebra), add ACI-tracked conformal thresholds for the unknown decision — total edge overhead is a few hundred parameters and O(1) state.

**Verification** of load-bearing claims:

- **CONFIRMED** — Binomial opinions (subjective logic) are the exact algebra for (support, oppose, unknown): b/d/u is a bijection of Beta evidence
  - Substance and math verified: b=r/(r+s+W), d=s/(r+s+W), u=W/(r+s+W); Beta mapping alpha=r+Wa, beta=s+W(1-a); projected probability P=b+a·u=(r+Wa)/(r+s+W) checks algebraically; the opinion-form cumulative fusion b_{A+B}=(b_A u_B+b_B u_A)/(u_A+u_B-u_A u_B), u_{A+B}=u_A u_B/(u_A+u_B-u_A u_B) was re-derived and is exactly equivalent to evidence addition (fused u = W/(N_A+N_B+W)); the ~260-extra-param estimate at D=64 is right (2×128 weights + biases ≈ 258). Josang's Springer 2016 book title/publisher are correct. CITATION ERROR, must fix: arXiv:1805.01388 is NOT Josang/Wang/Zhang 'Multi-Source Fusion in Subjective Logic' (FUSION 2017, DOI 10.23919/ICIF.2017.8009820, no arXiv version found); 1805.01388 is van der Heijden, Kopp & Kargl, 'Multi-Source Fusion OPERATIONS in Subjective Logic', FUSION 2018 — a different paper that in fact reports defects in the Josang et al. multi-source CBF/ABF operators (case definitions leading to division by zero when one opinion has non-zero uncertainty while two others are dogmatic). Consequently soften 'no renormalization pathology': pairwise cumulative fusion is associative/commutative and well-behaved, but it is undefined for two dogmatic opinions (u_A=u_B=0 needs a limit definition), and the MULTI-source belief-fusion operators of the FUSION 2017 paper had genuine published bugs. The FUSION 2022 tutorial PDF (uio.no) could not be fetched (network egress blocked); Josang's SL tutorials at FUSION conferences are well attested, so treat that link as plausible but unverified.
- **CONFIRMED** — Denoeux weight-of-evidence decomposition: a two-channel logit head IS a Dempster-Shafer mass function with separate ignorance and conflict
  - Source verified: T. Denoeux, 'Logistic regression, neural networks and Dempster-Shafer theory: A new perspective', Knowledge-Based Systems 176:54-67, 2019 (arXiv:1807.01846, 2018) — venue, volume, and pages confirmed via the author's own CRAN package bibliographies (evclass/evreg), which also describe exactly the claimed content: 'an interpretation of the operations performed in neural networks as the combination of weights of evidence by Dempster's rule', expressing logistic-regression/NN outputs as DS mass functions. Math re-derived and correct: simple mass m({θ})=1-e^{-w}, m(Θ)=e^{-w}; Dempster combination gives K=(1-e^{-w+})(1-e^{-w-}), the three normalized masses as stated, pl(sup)∝e^{-w-}, and the normalized plausibility P(sup)=e^{-w-}/(e^{-w-}+e^{-w+})=sigmoid(w+ - w-) — so the construction provably reduces to the standard logistic head. One characterization nuance for the synthesis: the specific two-channel (support/opposition) instantiation with softplus-parameterized w+ and w- is the research agent's own application of Denoeux's framework, not a construction appearing in the paper itself; Denoeux derives the latent mass functions from a trained network's weights (with positive/negative weight-of-evidence decomposition) and does emphasize that the pre-normalization mass function is more informative than the output probability (ignorance on Θ vs. conflict). The claimed dichotomy 'm(Θ) large iff both weights small; K large iff both large' follows directly from the formulas and is correct.
- **CONFIRMED** — EDL gives the trainable Dirichlet/Beta head, but 2022-2024 theory proves its learned uncertainty magnitude is not faithful — treat u as a score, not a guarantee
  - All four load-bearing sources verified. Sensoy, Kaplan & Kandemir, NeurIPS 2018 (arXiv:1806.01768): head equations alpha_k=e_k+1, S=Σalpha_k, b_k=e_k/S, u=K/S and the expected-MSE Dirichlet loss + annealed KL(Dir(alpha~)||Dir(1)) match the paper (Eq. 5). Bengs, Hüllermeier & Waegeman, 'On Second-Order Scoring Rules for Epistemic Uncertainty Quantification', ICML 2023 (arXiv:2301.12736) — abstract fetched in full; it proves non-existence of (their generalized notion of) proper second-order scoring rules, though the authors themselves phrase the headline as 'there seems to be no loss function...' — the claim's flat 'proper second-order scoring rules do not exist' slightly overstates the authors' own hedging but matches the formal theorem under their definitions; the companion NeurIPS 2022 'Pitfalls of Epistemic Uncertainty Quantification through Loss Minimisation' exists as described. Jürgens et al., ICML 2024 (arXiv:2402.09056) — abstract fetched: confirms identifiability and convergence issues in second-order loss minimization and 'the relative (rather than absolute) nature of epistemic uncertainty measures', exactly as characterized. Shen et al. 'Are Uncertainty Quantification Capabilities of Evidential Deep Learning a Mirage?' NeurIPS 2024 (arXiv:2402.06160, authors Shen*, Ryu* et al., MIT/IBM) — abstract fetched: confirms unification of EDL objectives, epistemic uncertainty 'non-vanishing even with infinite data' (the claim's 'u does not shrink with n' is a faithful paraphrase), and that 'EDL methods can be better interpreted as an out-of-distribution detection algorithm based on energy-based-models', with empirical effectiveness 'despite their poor uncertainty quantification capabilities'. Gao et al. survey arXiv:2409.04720 exists with that title (not independently re-fetched; low stakes). The design consequence drawn (use b/d/u as a cheap ranking score; source guarantees from a conformal layer) is a correct reading of these results.
- **CONFIRMED** — Split conformal prediction: distribution-free unknown detection from prediction-set size, at two stored floats and O(1) per query
  - Sources verified: Ding, Angelopoulos, Bates, Jordan & Tibshirani, 'Class-Conditional Conformal Prediction with Many Classes', NeurIPS 2023 (arXiv:2306.09335) — venue and abstract confirmed (clustered conformal for small per-class calibration counts, as claimed; note the clustering method is Ding et al.'s own contribution, correctly attributed). Angelopoulos, Bates, Fisch, Lei & Schuster, 'Conformal Risk Control' (arXiv:2208.02814) — full text fetched; exact author list matches and the paper extends conformal prediction to control any monotone loss. Abbasi-Yadkori, Kuzborskij et al. (DeepMind), 'Mitigating LLM Hallucinations via Conformal Abstention' (arXiv:2405.01563) — abstract fetched; confirms conformal abstention with rigorous bounds on hallucination/error rate. Math checked: q_hat=ceil((n+1)(1-alpha))/n quantile and the two-sided guarantee 1-alpha ≤ P(Y∈C(X)) ≤ 1-alpha+1/(n+1) are standard and correct (the UPPER bound additionally requires almost-surely distinct/continuous scores — worth stating); conditional-on-calibration coverage ~ Beta(n+1-l, l) with l=floor((n+1)alpha) is correct (Vovk 2012), and at n=1000, alpha=0.1 gives sd ≈ 0.0095, so '±1%, tight' is fair; the CRC threshold lambda_hat = inf{lambda: (n/(n+1))R_hat_n(lambda) + B/(n+1) ≤ alpha} matches the paper (B = loss bound). The design reading is sound, with one honest caveat the synthesis may keep: C={} (novelty signal) only arises for score/threshold choices that permit it (true for s=1-P_y with thresholded sets, as proposed); and split-conformal guarantees are marginal over exchangeable calibration/test draws — which the next claim (ACI) correctly addresses for the streaming case.
- **CONFIRMED** — Adaptive Conformal Inference: coverage on REFA's non-exchangeable stream via an O(1) online quantile update
  - Sources verified: Gibbs & Candès, 'Adaptive Conformal Inference Under Distribution Shift', NeurIPS 2021 (arXiv:2106.00170) — multiple independent implementations confirm venue and the update rule alpha_{t+1}=alpha_t+gamma(alpha-err_t). Gibbs & Candès, 'Conformal Inference for Online Prediction with Arbitrary Distribution Shifts' (arXiv:2208.08401), JMLR 2024 — abstract fetched: confirms it is the DtACI refinement that tunes ACI's step-size over time via an expert/meta procedure with local-interval regret guarantees, exactly as characterized. The deterministic long-run bound |(1/T)Σ err_t - alpha| ≤ (max(alpha_1, 1-alpha_1)+gamma)/(gamma T), valid for any (adversarial) sequence, matches Lemma 4.1 of the NeurIPS 2021 paper and was corroborated verbatim in an independent implementation; the equivalent direct quantile-tracking form q_{t+1}=q_t+gamma(err_t-alpha) is the online pinball-gradient view used in Angelopoulos et al.'s conformal PID work (that follow-up is name-checked without a URL; it exists — 'Conformal PID Control for Time Series Prediction', NeurIPS 2023). Two honest caveats to carry into synthesis: (1) the guarantee is LONG-RUN marginal coverage, not per-time-point or conditional validity, and with a fixed gamma ACI can oscillate and occasionally emit trivial (full or empty) prediction sets to force coverage; (2) the update requires observing the true label after each prediction — fine for REFA's synthetic streams with online labels, as the claim itself notes. Memory/compute O(1) per event is correct.

### Binomial opinions (subjective logic) are the exact algebra for (support, oppose, unknown): b/d/u is a bijection of Beta evidence ⚑
*status: convergent*

Josang's subjective logic represents a binary proposition by an opinion omega=(b,d,u,a): belief, disbelief, uncertainty with b+d+u=1, plus base rate a. This is not an ad-hoc triple: it is a bijective reparameterization of a Beta distribution over the truth probability, where b and d are driven by positive evidence r and negative evidence s and u is driven by the prior weight W. It comes with a full operator algebra: projected probability for decisions, cumulative fusion (add evidence), averaging fusion (dependent sources), trust discounting (down-weight unreliable sources), and constraint fusion. Because REFA already maintains physically separate support and opposition memories, its two reads (z_support, z_oppose) are precisely the (r, s) channels this algebra expects — unlike a softmax head, which destroys the distinction between balanced evidence (b=d, u small) and no evidence (u large). Evidential deep learning independently adopted exactly this mapping (Dirichlet/Beta pseudo-counts), so ML training recipes exist. This is the canonical semantics the three-class output should be re-derived from.

**Math.** Opinion omega=(b,d,u,a), b+d+u=1, u>0. Evidence mapping with prior weight W (default W=2): b=r/(r+s+W), d=s/(r+s+W), u=W/(r+s+W). Equivalent Beta(alpha,beta): alpha=r+W*a, beta=s+W*(1-a). Projected (decision) probability P=b+a*u = (r+W*a)/(r+s+W). Cumulative fusion of independent sources A,B = evidence addition (r_A+r_B, s_A+s_B); in opinion form b_{A+B}=(b_A u_B + b_B u_A)/(u_A+u_B-u_A u_B), u_{A+B}=u_A u_B/(u_A+u_B-u_A u_B); associative and commutative, no renormalization pathology. Variance of Beta gives second-order spread that shrinks as evidence grows. Cost: O(1) arithmetic per decision.

**REFA application.** Replace REFAMicro's 3-logit softmax with r=softplus(w_s^T[workspace; z_support]), s=softplus(w_o^T[workspace; z_oppose]) (about 260 extra params at D=64), then b,d,u in closed form. Output supported if b-d>tau and u<tau_u; opposed symmetric; else unknown. Train on projected probability P with Brier/NLL plus the abstention layer below. u is exactly the 'unknown' channel; base rate a encodes class prior of query truth.

Sources:
- Josang, Subjective Logic: A Formalism for Reasoning Under Uncertainty (Springer 2016); FUSION 2022 tutorial — <https://www.mn.uio.no/ifi/personer/vit/josang/sl/subjective-logic-fusion-2022.pdf>
- Josang, Wang, Zhang, Multi-Source Fusion in Subjective Logic (FUSION 2017) — <https://arxiv.org/abs/1805.01388>

### Denoeux weight-of-evidence decomposition: a two-channel logit head IS a Dempster-Shafer mass function with separate ignorance and conflict ⚑
*status: convergent*

Denoeux (Knowledge-Based Systems 2019, arXiv 1807.01846) proved that logistic regression and feedforward nets can be exactly viewed as combining simple Dempster-Shafer mass functions by Dempster's rule, with the sigmoid/softmax output equal to the normalized plausibility of the combined mass. The underlying (pre-normalization) mass function is strictly more informative than the probability: it distinguishes lack of evidence (mass on the whole frame Theta) from conflicting evidence (conflict mass K), which the probability collapses together. For REFA this is a gift: because support and opposition are already separate channels, one can assign a positive weight of evidence w+ to the support read and w- to the opposition read and obtain, in closed form, masses m({sup}), m({opp}), m(Theta) plus a conflict scalar K — i.e., TWO qualitatively different 'unknown' signals (ignorance vs contradiction) at zero architectural cost. This directly matches REFA's roadmap tasks: abstention (ignorance-driven unknown) and provenance conflict (conflict-driven unknown). The plausibility transform provably recovers sigmoid(w+ - w-), so the design is a strict refinement of a standard head, not a replacement.

**Math.** Simple mass function for hypothesis theta with evidence weight w>=0: m({theta})=1-e^{-w}, m(Theta)=e^{-w}. Dempster-combining support weight w+ and opposition weight w-: m({sup})=(1-e^{-w+})e^{-w-}/(1-K), m({opp})=e^{-w+}(1-e^{-w-})/(1-K), m(Theta)=e^{-w+-w-}/(1-K), with conflict K=(1-e^{-w+})(1-e^{-w-}). Normalized plausibility: pl(sup) prop e^{-w-}, pl(opp) prop e^{-w+}, giving P(sup)=sigmoid(w+ - w-) — exactly the logistic head. Ignorance m(Theta) is large iff BOTH weights small; conflict K is large iff BOTH large. Interval-dominance decision: output a set of classes when belief intervals [bel, pl] overlap. All O(1).

**REFA application.** Set w+=softplus(a_s^T z_support), w-=softplus(a_o^T z_oppose) on REFAMicro's final reads. Emit (m_sup, m_opp, m_Theta, K). Route unknown two ways: m_Theta>tau_i -> 'unknown: no evidence' (abstention task); K>tau_c -> 'unknown: contradiction' (contrastive/provenance-conflict task, currently invisible to the softmax head). Log K per bank as a free conflict diagnostic for the revision (delta-rule) mechanism.

Sources:
- Denoeux, Logistic Regression, Neural Networks and Dempster-Shafer Theory: a New Perspective, Knowledge-Based Systems 2019 — <https://arxiv.org/abs/1807.01846>
- HAL preprint version — <https://hal.science/hal-01830389v1/file/NNbelief_preprint.pdf>

### REFA's fast-weight decay lambda is discounted Beta evidence accumulation, implying a computable uncertainty floor
*status: partial*

Subjective-logic reputation systems (Josang & Ismail's Beta reputation system and the trust-discounting operators in Josang's book) accumulate evidence with an exponential forgetting factor: r_t = lambda*r_{t-1} + delta_t. This is mathematically the same recursion as REFA's per-bank memory decay M <- lambda*M + write. The consequence is a clean, principled semantics for decay that the ML side rarely states: geometric forgetting caps the effective evidence count at N_eff = 1/(1-lambda), which via the Beta/opinion mapping imposes a FLOOR on uncertainty u >= W/(W + N_eff) that can never be argued away by more data. Older evidence decays toward the prior, so u automatically rises for stale facts — exactly the desired behavior for REFA's revision task (later evidence supersedes earlier) and for delayed-evidence tasks. Conversely it means each bank's learned lambda_h is not just a stability knob: it fixes that bank's maximum attainable confidence, and should be read out as a per-bank uncertainty feature. This mapping between fast-weight decay and discounted conjugate-Bayes evidence appears to be unexploited in the fast-weight literature — a small building site.

**Math.** Discounted evidence: r_t = lambda*r_{t-1} + 1[support at t], s_t analogous. Steady state under constant evidence rate rho: r_inf = rho/(1-lambda) <= 1/(1-lambda) =: N_eff. Uncertainty floor: u_min = W/(W + N_eff) = W(1-lambda)/(W(1-lambda)+1). REFA's init lambda=0.995 gives N_eff=200, u_min ~ 0.0099 (W=2); lambda=0.95 gives N_eff=20, u_min ~ 0.091. Half-life of one observation: t_(1/2)=ln2/ln(1/lambda) ~ 138 steps at 0.995. So confidence ceiling and forgetting horizon are the SAME parameter; picking lambda_h per bank picks a per-relation staleness policy.

**REFA application.** Expose per-bank u_min(lambda_h) and the routed effective count N_eff(x)=sum_h route_h/(1-lambda_h) as scalar features into the output head; regularize the evidential head so u >= routed u_min. Gives the revision task a principled 'recency beats seniority' semantics for free, since decayed opposition evidence stops suppressing new support.

Sources:
- Josang & Ismail, The Beta Reputation System (Bled eConference 2002); forgetting/discounting in Josang, Subjective Logic (2016), FUSION 2022 tutorial — <https://www.mn.uio.no/ifi/personer/vit/josang/sl/subjective-logic-fusion-2022.pdf>
- Josang, Subjective Logic draft 2013 (evidence mapping and discounting chapters) — <https://files.givewell.org/files/labs/AI/Josang2013.pdf>

### Dempster's rule pathologies (Zadeh) and the correct fusion choice per aggregation site in REFA
*status: convergent*

Dempster's rule renormalizes away conflict, which produces the classic Zadeh pathology: two nearly-contradictory sources that each barely support a third option combine to CERTAINTY in that third option. Thirty years of fixes exist: Yager's rule (dump conflict mass into Theta, i.e., conflict becomes ignorance), Dubois-Prade, Murphy averaging, and the DSmT proportional conflict redistribution rules PCR5/PCR6 (redistribute conflict back to the arguments that generated it, proportionally; PCR6 is the coherent choice for 3+ sources). Subjective logic's cumulative fusion sidesteps the pathology entirely because adding evidence counts is associative and never renormalizes. The practical doctrine for REFA: (1) WITHIN a stream, accumulate evidence additively (cumulative fusion = discounted Beta updating — this is what the memory write already does); (2) ACROSS banks or provenance sources, either fuse with Yager (conflict -> unknown, which is semantically right for REFA: contradictory provenance should INCREASE unknown, not manufacture confidence) or keep Dempster but emit the conflict mass K as an alarm rather than dividing by 1-K. Never use vanilla Dempster to merge support-vs-opposition summaries.

**Math.** Dempster: m(A)=sum_{B cap C=A} m1(B)m2(C)/(1-K), K=sum_{B cap C=empty} m1(B)m2(C). Zadeh: m1({a})=0.99, m1({c})=0.01; m2({b})=0.99, m2({c})=0.01 => K=0.9999, combined m({c})=1 (certainty from two sources that both considered c nearly impossible). Yager: same numerator, but q(Theta) += K (no division). PCR5 (2 sources, A cap B empty): m(A) += m1(A)^2 m2(B)/(m1(A)+m2(B)) + m2(A)^2 m1(B)/(m2(A)+m1(B)); PCR6 generalizes coherently to n>=3 sources and agrees with averaging in the limit. Cumulative fusion: (r,s) add — associative, conflict-free, but assumes independent evidence; double-counts correlated sources (use averaging fusion, arithmetic mean of evidence, for dependent ones).

**REFA application.** REFA's delta-rule write already implements 'additive within-stream'; keep it. For the roadmap provenance-conflict task, compute per-bank opinions and fuse with Yager or PCR6 across banks; report K (or Yager's transferred mass) as the conflict feature. Guard: if the same fact arrives via correlated routes (distractor task), switch those banks to averaging fusion to avoid confidence inflation.

Sources:
- Smarandache & Dezert, A Simple Proportional Conflict Redistribution Rule (PCR) — <https://arxiv.org/abs/cs/0408010>
- Zadeh counterexample discussion, Dempster-Shafer combination notes (Berkeley) — <https://www.stat.berkeley.edu/~aldous/Real_World/dempster_shafer.pdf>

### EDL gives the trainable Dirichlet/Beta head, but 2022-2024 theory proves its learned uncertainty magnitude is not faithful — treat u as a score, not a guarantee ⚑
*status: convergent*

Sensoy et al. 2018 made the subjective-logic mapping trainable: a net outputs nonnegative evidence e_k, Dirichlet alpha_k=e_k+1, belief b_k=e_k/S, uncertainty u=K/S, trained with an expected-MSE Dirichlet loss plus KL-to-uniform annealing — single forward pass, no sampling, ideal for edge. But three converging theory results bound what this can mean. Bengs et al. (NeurIPS 2022; ICML 2023) prove an impossibility: no loss function acts as a proper scoring rule at the second order, i.e., nothing incentivizes a learner to report its epistemic uncertainty faithfully the way log-loss does for probabilities. Jürgens et al. (ICML 2024) show identifiability and convergence failures and argue EDL's epistemic measures are only relative. Shen et al. (NeurIPS 2024, 'Mirage') unify EDL objectives asymptotically and show the learned meta-distribution's spread converges to a data-independent form — the resulting uncertainty behaves as an energy-based OOD/novelty score, not epistemic uncertainty; empirically EDL 'works' for OOD detection for reasons unrelated to its Bayesian story. Design consequence for REFA: use the EDL/subjective-logic head for cheap, differentiable, well-shaped b/d/u, but source all GUARANTEES (unknown detection rate, error rate) from a conformal/selective layer that does not trust u's magnitude.

**Math.** Sensoy head: alpha_k=e_k+1, S=sum alpha_k, b_k=e_k/S, u=K/S (K classes). Loss: sum_k (y_k - alpha_k/S)^2 + alpha_k(S-alpha_k)/(S^2(S+1)) + lambda_t KL(Dir(alpha_tilde)||Dir(1)). Bengs impossibility: for second-order predictions Q (distributions over distributions), no real-valued loss L makes expected loss minimized uniquely at a faithful Q — proper second-order scoring rules do not exist. Mirage: unified EDL objective minimizer alpha*(x) = g(D(x)) where D is an energy-like density score; meta-distribution spread is data-size-independent, so u does not shrink with n as epistemic uncertainty must. Head cost: O(K) on top of existing logits.

**REFA application.** Keep REFAMicro's output MLP but end in 2 evidence units (softplus), b/d/u closed-form; drop KL-to-uniform annealing per R-EDL. Use u ONLY to rank for abstention and gate compute; guarantees come from the conformal wrapper. Bonus: REFA's synthetic generator knows true counts (r_true, s_true) per query, so epistemic calibration is exactly auditable via E|u_pred - W/(r_true+s_true+W)| — impossible on natural benchmarks.

Sources:
- Sensoy, Kaplan, Kandemir, Evidential Deep Learning to Quantify Classification Uncertainty, NeurIPS 2018 — <https://arxiv.org/abs/1806.01768>
- Bengs, Hüllermeier, Waegeman, On Second-Order Scoring Rules for Epistemic Uncertainty Quantification, ICML 2023 — <https://arxiv.org/abs/2301.12736>
- Jürgens, Meinert, Bengs, Hüllermeier, Waegeman, Is Epistemic Uncertainty Faithfully Represented by EDL Methods?, ICML 2024 — <https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.09056>
- Shen et al., Are Uncertainty Quantification Capabilities of EDL a Mirage?, NeurIPS 2024 — <https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.06160>
- Gao et al., A Comprehensive Survey on Evidential Deep Learning and Its Applications (loss taxonomy, activation choices) — <https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.04720>

### Modern EDL repairs: R-EDL/Re-EDL (prior weight as hyperparameter, drop the KL regularizer) and DIP-EDL (pseudo-counts from density) — the latter maps onto REFA's memory occupancy
*status: partial*

Two repair lines make the evidential head behave. R-EDL (ICLR 2024 Spotlight; extended as Re-EDL, arXiv 2410.00393) shows two 'nonessential settings' cause EDL overconfidence: fixing the Dirichlet prior weight at K, and the variance-minimizing KL-to-uniform regularizer, which pushes the Dirichlet toward a Dirac delta. Fix: treat prior weight W as a tunable hyperparameter and optimize the Dirichlet expectation directly, dropping the regularizer. DIP-EDL (arXiv 2602.01477, Feb 2026) gives EDL a clean statistical footing — training is amortized variational inference in a hierarchical Bayes model with tempered pseudo-likelihood — and shows standard EDL conflates aleatoric and epistemic uncertainty; its fix decouples them by writing alpha(x) = n(x)*p(y|x) + prior, where the pseudo-count magnitude n(x) is tied to an estimate of the covariate density, so evidence shrinks to the prior off-manifold. For REFA the striking part is that n(x) does not need a separate density model: the routed memory occupancy (how much mass the addressed keys actually retrieve) IS a native familiarity/density proxy — queries about entities never written to memory retrieve ~0 norm, automatically yielding low n(x) and high u.

**Math.** R-EDL: P_k = alpha_k/S with alpha = e + W*a, W a hyperparameter (not K); objective = direct expectation loss, no KL(Dir(alpha_tilde)||Dir(1)) term (that term drives S->inf, u->0, overconfidence). DIP-EDL: EDL loss == amortized VI with tempered pseudo-likelihood p(y|pi)^{n(x)}; parameterize alpha(x)=n(x)*p_hat(y|x)+W*a with n(x) prop p_hat(x) (marginal density); guarantees asymptotic concentration on-manifold and reversion to prior off-manifold. REFA proxy: n(x) = c * sum_h route_h * ||q^T M_h||_2 (norm of what the query actually retrieves), or discounted write-count per bank from the lambda-recursion (finding 3): both O(H) scalars already computed.

**REFA application.** Concrete head: r = n_s(x)*sigma(g_s), s = n_o(x)*sigma(g_o) where n_s, n_o are retrieved-norm proxies from support/oppose memories and g are learned gates. Unknown then rises for never-written entities (abstention task) WITHOUT OOD training data, and W is tuned on validation smECE. Cost: 2 norms + 2 gates per query; no density network — fits easily in 6GB.

Sources:
- Chen, Gao, Xu, R-EDL: Relaxing Nonessential Settings of Evidential Deep Learning, ICLR 2024; extended Re-EDL — <https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.00393>
- Carlotti, Gligic, Farahi, Density-Informed Pseudo-Counts for Calibrated Evidential Deep Learning, Feb 2026 — <https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.01477>

### Split conformal prediction: distribution-free unknown detection from prediction-set size, at two stored floats and O(1) per query ⚑
*status: convergent*

Split conformal prediction converts ANY score the model emits (including an untrustworthy EDL u or softmax margin) into a set-valued predictor with a finite-sample, distribution-free coverage guarantee: with probability >= 1-alpha the true label is in the emitted set, assuming only exchangeability between calibration and test data. For REFA's 2-substantive-class problem (supported/opposed, with unknown as abstention), the natural design is: calibrate a nonconformity score on held-out episodes, emit C(x) subset of {sup, opp}, and declare UNKNOWN exactly when C(x) is not a singleton — C={sup,opp} means the evidence cannot separate the classes (ambiguity/conflict), C={} (possible with some scores) means the query looks unlike calibration data (novelty). This turns 'unknown' from a learned third class — which the head can game — into a certified decision rule. Class-conditional (Mondrian) calibration gives per-class guarantees (coverage for true-supported and true-opposed separately), important because REFA's tasks deliberately skew polarity ratios; clustered conformal (Ding et al., NeurIPS 2023) handles small per-class calibration counts. Conformal risk control (Angelopoulos et al.) generalizes to any monotone loss, e.g., directly bounding the false-'supported' rate — the hallucination-relevant error. Everything is post-hoc: no retraining, no architecture change.

**Math.** Scores s_i=s(x_i,y_i) on n calibration points; q_hat = ceil((n+1)(1-alpha))/n empirical quantile; C(x)={y: s(x,y)<=q_hat}. Guarantee: 1-alpha <= P(Y in C(X)) <= 1-alpha+1/(n+1). Conditional-on-calibration coverage ~ Beta(n+1-l, l), l=floor((n+1)alpha) — n=1000 episodes is tight at alpha=0.1. Class-conditional: q_hat_y per class from n_y points => P(Y in C | Y=y)>=1-alpha. Conformal risk control: choose lambda_hat = inf{lambda: (n/(n+1))R_hat_n(lambda)+B/(n+1) <= alpha} => E[loss]<=alpha for any bounded monotone loss (e.g., false-support indicator). Inference: compare 2 scores to 2 stored quantiles, O(1); calibration: one sort, O(n log n), offline.

**REFA application.** Calibrate s(x,y)=1-P_y with P from the b/d/u head (P_sup=b+a*u) on held-out synthetic episodes per task family; store q_hat_sup, q_hat_opp (two floats). Output unknown iff both or neither class survives. Report certified per-class coverage in the calibration/unknown-detection roadmap milestone — the distribution-free guarantee the 3-class softmax cannot provide, at zero VRAM cost.

Sources:
- Ding, Angelopoulos, Bates, Jordan, Tibshirani, Class-Conditional Conformal Prediction with Many Classes, NeurIPS 2023 — <https://arxiv.org/abs/2306.09335>
- Angelopoulos, Bates, Fisch, Lei, Schuster, Conformal Risk Control — <https://arxiv.org/abs/2208.02814>
- Abbasi-Yadkori, Kuzborskij et al., Mitigating LLM Hallucinations via Conformal Abstention (conformal abstention with error-rate guarantee) — <https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.01563>

### Adaptive Conformal Inference: coverage on REFA's non-exchangeable stream via an O(1) online quantile update ⚑
*status: convergent*

REFA's setting violates split conformal's exchangeability assumption in at least three ways: evidence arrives as a temporally ordered stream, the revision task makes label semantics time-dependent, and the roadmap's length extrapolation explicitly shifts the test distribution. Gibbs & Candes' Adaptive Conformal Inference (NeurIPS 2021) removes the exchangeability requirement for ONLINE coverage: after each prediction, a single scalar (the working miscoverage level, equivalently the quantile threshold) is nudged by gradient descent on the pinball loss depending on whether the last prediction set covered. The guarantee is remarkable: the LONG-RUN empirical coverage converges to 1-alpha for ANY sequence, adversarial included — no distributional assumption at all. Follow-up work (Gibbs & Candes JMLR 2024; Angelopoulos et al. conformal PID / quantile tracking) tunes the step size adaptively and improves locality of coverage. This is the correct wrapper for a deployed streaming REFA: the model can drift, banks can saturate, task mixes can change, and the unknown-detection threshold self-corrects with one addition and one comparison per event and O(1) memory — arguably the cheapest guarantee mechanism in all of uncertainty quantification, and a perfect fit for a 6GB edge device.

**Math.** ACI update: alpha_{t+1} = alpha_t + gamma(alpha - err_t), err_t = 1[y_t not in C_t(alpha_t)]; C_t built from current score quantile at level alpha_t. Deterministic guarantee: |(1/T) sum_{t=1}^T err_t - alpha| <= (max(alpha_1, 1-alpha_1)+gamma)/(gamma T) for ANY (even adversarial) sequence — long-run coverage exactly alpha, rate O(1/(gamma T)). Equivalent direct quantile tracking: q_{t+1}=q_t + gamma(err_t - alpha) (online pinball-loss gradient step). Step size gamma trades adaptation speed vs oscillation; DtACI/PID variants run a small expert ensemble over gamma with regret bounds. Memory O(1) (or O(window) for local coverage); compute O(1)/event.

**REFA application.** Wrap the per-query evidential score with a tracked quantile q_t per class (2 scalars + gamma~0.005). Every labeled query event (synthetic tasks provide labels online) updates q_t. Coverage then survives bank saturation, length extrapolation to 4-8x training length, and drift across task mixes — matching the multi-seed validation and length-extrapolation roadmap items without retraining anything.

Sources:
- Gibbs & Candes, Adaptive Conformal Inference Under Distribution Shift, NeurIPS 2021 — <https://arxiv.org/abs/2106.00170>
- Gibbs & Candes, Conformal Inference for Online Prediction with Arbitrary Distribution Shifts, JMLR 2024 — <https://arxiv.org/abs/2208.08401>

### Selective prediction: Chow's threshold, risk-coverage curves, and selective calibration are the decision/evaluation layer for 'unknown'
*status: convergent*

The decision-theoretic layer is 70 years old and exact. Chow's rule: with unit misclassification cost and abstention cost c, the Bayes-optimal policy abstains iff the max posterior falls below 1-c; equivalently, under a (+1 correct, -lambda wrong, 0 abstain) score, answering is rational iff confidence exceeds t*=lambda/(1+lambda). El-Yaniv & Wiener formalized selective classification as trading selective risk against coverage; Geifman & El-Yaniv gave high-probability selective-risk certificates for a chosen coverage (SGR) and SelectiveNet trains the selector jointly. Franc et al. (JMLR 2023) prove which uncertainty scores are optimal for reject-option classifiers under different formulations. Fisch et al.'s selective calibration is the subtler idea: select examples not for expected correctness but so the ACCEPTED subset is well-calibrated (reject 'uncertain uncertainties'). For REFA the operational content is: (a) 'unknown' should be a THRESHOLD decision on the evidential output with a task-cost-derived lambda, not a free-floating third logit; (b) the primary evaluation artifact for the abstention milestone should be the risk-coverage curve and its area (AURC), not 3-class accuracy, because 3-class accuracy rewards a degenerate always-unknown policy at the wrong exchange rate; (c) calibration should be assessed ON the accepted subset (selective calibration), since that is what downstream consumers see.

**Math.** Chow: abstain iff max_y P(y|x) < 1-c; scoring (+1,-lambda,0) => answer iff p > t* = lambda/(1+lambda) (lambda=1 -> 0.5; lambda=3 -> 0.75). Selective risk R(f,g)=E[l(f(x),y)g(x)]/E[g(x)], coverage phi=E[g(x)]; risk-coverage curve R(phi); AURC = integral of R over phi. SGR: for target risk r*, confidence delta, returns threshold with P(R<=r*)>=1-delta via a tight numerical binomial bound, O(m log m) in held-out size m. Selective calibration error: E[(E[y|f_hat, g=1]-f_hat)^2 | g=1] minimized over selector g at fixed coverage. All post-hoc, O(1) inference.

**REFA application.** Fix lambda per task (e.g., lambda=3 for the abstention battery), threshold P=b+a*u at t*=0.75, sweep tau_u to trace risk-coverage; report AURC and coverage at fixed selective risk vs GRU/transformer baselines. This makes the unknown class cost-portable: one trained head serves any deployment lambda by moving a scalar threshold — no retraining on the 2060.

Sources:
- Franc, Prusa, Voracek, Optimal Strategies for Reject Option Classifiers, JMLR 2023 — <https://jmlr.org/papers/volume24/21-0048/21-0048.pdf>
- Geifman & El-Yaniv, SelectiveNet, ICML 2019 — <https://proceedings.mlr.press/v97/geifman19a.html>
- Fisch, Jaakkola, Barzilay, Calibrated Selective Classification, TMLR 2022 — <https://arxiv.org/abs/2208.12084>

### Aug-2026 collapse law: training abstention as an error-penalized discrete action can kill the reward gradient and KL anchor together — do not learn 'unknown' as an action
*status: new-since-aug-2026*

arXiv 2608.00301 (Aug 2026, after the Engram Loop sweep) analyzes the now-standard anti-hallucination prescription — reward +1 for correct, -lambda for wrong, 0 for abstain, which makes Chow's threshold t*=lambda/(1+lambda) the rational answering rule (the scheme endorsed by Kalai et al. 2025 and the Nature 2026 'Evaluating LLMs for accuracy incentivizes hallucinations' line). Its result: when abstention is implemented as a discrete gated action trained by error-penalized RL with a KL anchor, the reward gradient and the anchor's restoring force are BOTH multiplied by the same gate-saturation factor, so they die together; under explicit conditions the policy drifts toward refusing everything, with mean training reward rising toward 0 like 1/t — a collapse that looks like improvement on the reward curve. The paper derives this collapse law and proposes a repair for error-penalized RL (restoring a stable interior abstention rate). For REFA this is a live design warning: the current 3-logit head trained with cross-entropy is safe, but the tempting next step — RL-style fine-tuning of the unknown output under an error-penalized objective (as the Engram Loop's abstention-rewarding proper scoring plan gestures at) — has a failure mode. The safe pattern is the one this survey converges on anyway: train calibrated evidence with proper first-order scoring on b/d/u, then obtain abstention post-hoc via Chow/conformal thresholds.

**Math.** Scoring (+1, -lambda, 0): answer iff p > t* = lambda/(1+lambda). Collapse mechanism: with abstain gate g=sigma(z), the policy gradient of expected score and the KL-anchor gradient both carry the factor sigma'(z)=g(1-g) -> 0 as g -> 1; the coupled dynamics give monotone drift to g=1 (always abstain) under stated conditions, mean reward approaching 0 like 1/t in training time t (rising, since -lambda errors vanish) — so the collapse is reward-monotone and invisible to reward monitoring. Note: abstract-level verification only (arXiv fetch blocked by proxy; content confirmed via search-index snippets); repair details not independently verified.

**REFA application.** Direct guardrail for the calibration/unknown milestone: do NOT fine-tune REFAMicro's unknown logit with an error-penalized (+1/-lambda/0) objective plus KL anchor. Keep abstention out of the trained action space: proper scoring on (b,d) + thresholds/ACI for unknown. If RL is ever used, monitor abstention RATE, not reward, for the 1/t collapse signature.

Sources:
- Abstention as an Action Can Kill Both the Reward Gradient and the KL Anchor: Collapse Law and Repair for Error-Penalized Reinforcement Learning, arXiv 2608.00301 (Aug 2026) — <https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.00301>
- Kalai, Nachum et al., Why Language Models Hallucinate (OpenAI 2025); Nature 2026 journal version — <https://www.nature.com/articles/s41586-026-10549-w>

### Measure calibration with smECE / distance-to-calibration, not binned ECE — binned ECE is inconsistent and misleading at REFA's tiny eval sizes
*status: convergent*

Blasiok, Gopalan, Hu, Nakkiran (STOC 2023) established a ground-truth notion of calibration error: the L1 distance to the nearest perfectly calibrated predictor (dCE), and showed the ubiquitous binned ECE fails basic sanity properties — it is discontinuous in the predictor (an infinitesimal change in predictions can jump the measure), bin-count-sensitive, and NOT a consistent estimate of any distance to calibration; small-sample binned ECE is dominated by binning artifacts. Blasiok & Nakkiran's smECE (kernel-smoothed reliability with a principled bandwidth) is a consistent calibration measure: polynomially equivalent to dCE (two-sided bounds), continuous, and it induces a principled reliability diagram; a companion result (NeurIPS 2023, 'When does optimizing a proper loss yield calibration?') shows minimizing proper losses over smooth function classes yields calibration in this metric, connecting training objective to metric. For REFA — whose evaluation sets are small synthetic batches on a laptop GPU — a standard 15-bin ECE would mostly measure noise. The right protocol: report smECE of the projected probability P=b+a*u for the supported class (and classwise), plus the Brier score and its calibration/refinement decomposition, and evaluate the uncertainty channel u separately via risk-coverage (previous finding), since u is not a probability forecast and has no ECE.

**Math.** dCE(f) = inf_{g perfectly calibrated} E|f(X)-g(X)|. Binned ECE = sum_b (n_b/n)|acc(b)-conf(b)|: discontinuous, inconsistent, bin-sensitive; can be pushed near 0 or inflated by bin choice. smECE_sigma = integral |E[(y-f) K_sigma(t-f)]| dt with reflected Gaussian kernel; at the fixed-point bandwidth sigma*, smECE is a consistent calibration measure: poly(dCE) lower and upper bounds (constant-degree polynomial equivalence to dCE). Brier decomposition: E(f-y)^2 = E[(f-E[y|f])^2] (calibration) + E[Var(y|f)] (refinement) — a sample-efficient sanity split. smECE computable in O(n log n).

**REFA application.** In REFA's calibration milestone, replace 'ECE' with: smECE of P_sup on ~1-5k held-out queries (classwise for supported/opposed), Brier + decomposition, and AURC for u. Keeps metric noise below effect size at laptop eval scales and makes head-design comparisons (softmax vs subjective-logic head) statistically meaningful.

Sources:
- Blasiok & Nakkiran, Smooth ECE: Principled Reliability Diagrams via Kernel Smoothing — <https://arxiv.org/abs/2309.12236>
- Blasiok, Gopalan, Hu, Nakkiran, A Unifying Theory of Distance from Calibration, STOC 2023 — <https://www.semanticscholar.org/paper/f5a825c7baef8a5b03fcbb6e9f1ee27e5e711b41>

### Trusted Multi-View Classification: per-view Dirichlet opinions fused by a reduced Dempster rule — the template for fusing REFA's 8 bank reads with a cross-bank conflict signal
*status: partial*

Han et al.'s TMC (ICLR 2021; TPAMI 2022 'Dynamic Evidential Fusion') is the closest existing ML system to REFA's multi-bank structure: each view (for REFA: each memory bank) produces its own Dirichlet evidence, inducing a subjective-logic opinion (b^v, u^v), and the opinions are combined with a reduced Dempster combination on the (belief, uncertainty) simplex. Fusion happens at the EVIDENCE level rather than feature or logit level, which they show yields more stable uncertainty and robustness to a corrupted view: a noisy view arrives with high u and is automatically down-weighted, since the combination weights each view's beliefs by the others' uncertainty. The normalization constant exposes a cross-view conflict term C: when two banks confidently disagree, C spikes — for REFA this is precisely the provenance-conflict observable (bank h says supported, bank h' says opposed), which the current architecture computes implicitly (sum of reads) and then discards. TMC also shows the combination preserves the Dirichlet form, so training remains a standard EDL loss per view plus one on the fused opinion. Caveat from the Zadeh finding applies: reduced-Dempster renormalizes conflict away, so REFA should surface C (or use a Yager variant) rather than only normalize.

**Math.** Per view v: alpha^v -> b_k^v = e_k^v/S^v, u^v = K/S^v. Two-view reduced Dempster: b_k = (b_k^1 b_k^2 + b_k^1 u^2 + b_k^2 u^1)/(1-C), u = u^1 u^2/(1-C), with conflict C = sum_{i != j} b_i^1 b_j^2; applied sequentially over views; fused result maps back to a Dirichlet via S=K/u, e_k=b_k*S. Properties: u decreases monotonically with each confident agreeing view; a vacuous view (u^v=1) is the identity element; complexity O(K) per pair, O(HK) for H banks. Loss: sum_v EDL loss(alpha^v) + EDL loss(alpha_fused).

**REFA application.** Give each of REFA's H=8 banks a shared tiny opinion head over its routed read (~few hundred shared params), fuse the 8 opinions with the reduced rule weighted by route probabilities, and emit fused (b,d,u) plus conflict C. C>tau flags provenance conflict (roadmap task) with per-bank attribution — which relation family disagrees — for free; vacuous never-written banks contribute identity, solving distractor-bank dilution.

Sources:
- Han, Zhang, Fu, Zhou, Trusted Multi-View Classification, ICLR 2021 — <https://arxiv.org/abs/2102.02051>
- Han et al., Trusted Multi-View Classification with Dynamic Evidential Fusion, IEEE TPAMI 2022 — <https://arxiv.org/abs/2204.11423>

---

## Temporal codes: Laplace, oscillatory, and time-stamped memory
`temporal-math`

The temporal-code literature converges from three independent directions on a single mathematical object: the Laplace transform of the past, sampled at log-spaced (possibly complex) decay rates, with an approximate inverse (Post's formula) that yields scale-invariant time cells. Neuroscience 2024-2026 hardened this: mPFC ramping cells encode real Laplace transforms of both past and future events (PNAS 2024), hippocampal time cells are log-compressed with Weber-Fechner widths (eLife), and CA1 intrinsic persistent firing spans behavioral timescales (Hippocampus 2026). On the ML side the same object appears as HiPPO's optimal polynomial projections (LagT is literally log-free Laplace taps; LegS gives timescale-equivariant whole-history compression), as diagonal SSM eigenvalue spectra (each S4D unit stores the Laplace transform at s=-lambda_n), and as SITH-based networks (DeepSITH, SITHCon, SITH-RNN, AAAI-2025 RL) that get zero-shot timescale generalization in tiny models. Oscillatory and wave mechanisms (LinOSS/D-LinOSS implicit discretizations, wave-RNN shift registers, AKOrN Kuramoto binding, TeRo/RoPE time rotations) supply the norm-preserving phase counterpart: exact recency for the recent past and absolute-time-addressable keys. The genuine gap: no fast-weight-memory paper attaches per-item, queryable timestamps to delta-rule writes. The cheapest REFA construction, assembled from Shankar-Howard taps plus the Howard-lab "what x when" outer-product theory, is a per-bank timestamp trace T_h in R^{RxK} updated by the existing delta rule with K~6 fixed log-spaced decays, decoded by log-ratios (invariant to write gain and bank decay): ~1.5k extra state floats, <1% extra parameters, O(HRK) extra compute, giving recency-ordered (not merely decay-weighted) revision.

**Verification** of load-bearing claims:

- **CONFIRMED** — Scale-invariant Laplace history with Post inversion (Shankar-Howard TILT)
  - Both sources verified. Shankar & Howard, 'A Scale-Invariant Internal Representation of Time', Neural Computation 24:134-193 (2012) confirmed via multiple independent bibliographies. Shankar & Howard, 'Optimally Fuzzy Temporal Memory', JMLR 14(Dec):3785-3812 (2013) confirmed including exact pages; its abstract (retrieved verbatim) states the fuzzy memory 'optimally sacrifices the temporal accuracy of information in a scale-free fashion in order to represent prediction-relevant information from exponentially long timescales', for learners that cannot know the prediction-relevant timescale a priori and for scale-free long-range-correlated signals — matching the claim's characterization. The stated math is internally correct: the leaky-integrator ODE gives the real Laplace transform of the past; the Post-inversion form f_tilde(tau*) = C_k(-1)^k s^{k+1} d^kF/ds^k at s=k/tau* matches Post's formula with C_k = 1/k!; ln F_k = -s_k(t-t_w) makes age linearly decodable. Caveats: the 2012 full text is paywalled and could not be fetched, so the specific coefficient-of-variation ~1/sqrt(k) and Delta-tau/tau ~ ln(tau_max/tau_min)/N expressions are consistent with the papers' known content but were not verified against the text; and the JMLR paper argues/constructs the scale-free (geometric) allocation as optimal under its resource criterion rather than stating a general 'optimal allocation' theorem — 'proves optimal' is slightly stronger than the paper's own framing.
- **CONFIRMED** — HiPPO: temporal memory as optimal online projection; measure choice = timestamp semantics
  - Verified against the full paper text (HF papers mirror of arXiv:2008.07669; Gu, Dao, Ermon, Rudra, Re; the NeurIPS 2020 HiPPO paper). All load-bearing details check out verbatim: LegT recovers the LMU and fails hard when theta is mis-specified (pMNIST 91.75% at theta=20 vs 98.0% at theta=200); LagT uses the exponentially decaying measure e^{-(t-x)}; LegS is uniform over [0,t] with ODE c'(t) = -(1/t)Ac + (1/t)Bf, discrete c_{k+1} = (I - A/k)c_k + (1/k)Bf_k, A_nk = sqrt((2n+1)(2k+1)) for n>k, n+1 for n=k, 0 otherwise, B_n = sqrt(2n+1) — exactly as claimed. Prop 3 (timescale equivariance, hippo(f∘gamma) = hippo(f)∘gamma), Prop 5 (gradient norm Theta(1/t)), Prop 6 (error O(tL/sqrt(N)) for L-Lipschitz f), and O(N) per-step updates all confirmed. arXiv:2412.08595 'Numerical Analysis of HiPPO-LegS ODE for Deep State Space Models' (Park, Suh, Ryu; later TMLR) exists and does analyze the LegS singularity: it proves well-posedness despite the t=0 singularity (though without freedom of arbitrary initial conditions) and convergence of the discretizations. One simplification to flag: HiPPO-LagT's A is a lower-triangular all-ones matrix (a coupled system), not literally a bank of independent 'fixed decay taps'; the decay-tap reading is a fair informal gloss (and exact at N=1) but the REFA 'LagT-style log grid across banks' is the researcher's own extension, not a construction from the paper.
- **CONFIRMED** — Traveling-wave shift registers: exact recency for the recent past, fuzzy Laplace for the far past
  - Both sources verified. Keller, Muller, Sejnowski, Welling, 'Traveling Waves Encode the Recent Past and Enhance Sequence Learning' (arXiv:2309.08045, ICLR 2024): full text retrieved; abstract confirms wRNNs 'learn faster and reach significantly lower error' on synthetic memory tasks, outperform comparable wave-free RNNs 'using significantly fewer parameters', and 'perform comparably to more complex gated architectures such as LSTMs and GRUs'. The recurrence is the circulant discretization of the 1-D wave equation, initialized as the exact shift matrix (nu=1), matching the claimed Sigma; onset time and channel of each input are decodable from the wRNN state (their Fig. 1). Karuvally, Sejnowski & Siegelmann, 'Hidden Traveling Waves bind Working Memory Variables in Recurrent Neural Networks' confirmed at ICML 2024 (PMLR v235, karuvally24a). Two caveats: (1) 'stores the recent past losslessly / age = ring position is exact, an argmax not an estimate' is an idealization of the paper's register analogy — exact only for the linear, non-dissipative shift with capacity n; with the nonlinearity and learned kernels storage is approximate; (2) the specific '~10x vanilla RNN copy-task length' figure was not located in the retrieved text — the paper claims 'significantly greater length' without that verified multiplier; treat 10x as unverified.
- **CONFIRMED** — 'What' x 'when' outer-product Laplace manifolds: the template for timestamped fast weights
  - All three sources exist and support the claim. Sarkar, Wang, Zuo & Howard, arXiv:2409.20484: abstract (retrieved verbatim) confirms conjunctive 'what' x 'when' receptive fields, that conjunctive RFs make 'the covariance matrix of the network decouple neatly, allowing an understanding of the low-dimensional dynamics', log-time tiling, and the edge (Laplace) / bump (inverse) continuous-attractor sketch; the eLife reviewed preprint 108804 is corroborated by elifesciences/elife-article-xml (elife-preprint-108804-v1.xml, matching arXiv 2409.20484). The word 'Kronecker' was not verified in the abstract — the exact C = C_what (x) C_when form is a consistent but unverified detail of the decoupling. Daniels & Howard, 'Continuous Attractor Networks for Laplace Neural Manifolds', Computational Brain & Behavior 2025 (DOI 10.1007/s42113-024-00234-4; arXiv:2406.04545) confirmed; abstract confirms the edge/bump attractor pair, events represented as a function of log time, and robustness to noise 'despite exponential growth in firing'. The bioRxiv preprint is real: 'Continuous attractor circuits for decision making with Laplace-domain neural representations', Wang C., Cao R., Howard M., posted ~2026-08-03/09, DOI 10.64898/2026.08.03.742594 (corroborated via a third-party bioRxiv indexer; direct bioRxiv fetch blocked in this environment, so its content beyond title/authors is unverified). The claim that an ML translation of the conjunctive what-x-when fast-weight code is 'essentially absent' is an unverifiable negative — plausible but not established.
- **CONFIRMED** — Deliverable design: delta-rule Laplace timestamp trace for REFA
  - Design is speculative by construction (correctly flagged); its cited sources and target code check out, and the arithmetic is correct. Sources: JMLR 14:3785-3812 and arXiv:2409.20484 both verified (see claims 1 and 4). Code: /home/user/refa-edge/src/refa_edge/models/refa.py does contain write_strength, support_memory/oppose_memory, the erase einsums, learned per-bank decay initialized at 0.995, and the output concat, so the proposed edit points are real. Math re-derived: tau_k = 4*4^k for k=0..5 gives {4,16,64,256,1024,4096} (spans 4 to ~4096, K=6); age decode Delta = -ln(a_k/a_j)/(1/tau_k - 1/tau_j) is the correct gain-invariant inversion of a_k = g e^{-Delta/tau_k}; ln(4)/2 = 0.693 ~ 0.7 and ln(2)/2 = 0.347 ~ 0.35 as stated (ratio-2 spanning 4-4096 needs 11 taps, so K=12 slightly overshoots — fine); state +2HRK = 1536 floats = 6 KB fp32 = +9.4% over the 16384-float M state (exact: 9.375%); +2K x D = 768 params (assuming the output MLP first layer is width D=64); compute +HRK = 768 MACs vs HRD = 8192 for the M update. Two caveats for the synthesis: (1) the 'Weber constant = ln(tap ratio)/2' relative-error figure is a design heuristic, not a result proved in the JMLR paper (there the blur also depends on the Post-inversion order k); (2) the calibrated log-ratio age decode assumes a single dominant write at the addressed key — when multiple same-key events of the same stance superpose, the read spectrum is a mixture of exponentials and the closed-form decode biases toward an average age, so the raw-spectrum-to-MLP readout is the robust default and the decoded-age readout is only exact for isolated writes.

### Scale-invariant Laplace history with Post inversion (Shankar-Howard TILT) ⚑
*status: convergent*

Shankar & Howard (2012) showed a bank of leaky integrators computes the real Laplace transform of the entire past, and a fixed linear operator (Post's inversion formula, implemented as a k-th derivative across neighboring decay rates, i.e., on-center/off-surround feedforward weights) approximately inverts it, reconstructing a fuzzy timeline in which recent events are sharp and older events are progressively blurred with exactly scale-invariant (Weber-Fechner) error. The companion JMLR 2013 paper (Optimally Fuzzy Temporal Memory) proves that geometrically spaced decay rates are the optimal allocation of N memory nodes when the prediction-relevant timescale is unknown or the signal has scale-free (1/f) long-range correlations: constant relative temporal accuracy over an exponentially long horizon from linear resources. This is the core mathematical object for time-as-code: timestamps are not stored numbers but decay spectra, and elapsed time is decoded, not looked up.

**Math.** Encoder: dF(s,t)/dt = -s F(s,t) + f(t), so F(s,t) = int_0^t e^{-s(t-t')} f(t') dt' (real Laplace transform of the past). Inverse (Post): f_tilde(tau*) = C_k (-1)^k s^{k+1} d^k F/ds^k |_{s=k/tau*}; exact as k -> inf; for finite k the reconstructed 'time field' at tau* has width proportional to tau* with coefficient of variation ~ 1/sqrt(k) (scale invariance). Log-spaced taps s_n = s_0 c^n give node timescales tau_n = k/s_n on a log grid; N nodes covering [tau_min, tau_max] give constant relative blur Delta tau / tau ~ ln(tau_max/tau_min)/N. Impulse at t_w reads back as F_k = e^{-s_k (t - t_w)}: age is linearly decodable from log F. Cost: N state variables, O(N) update, inversion is a fixed banded matrix (no parameters).

**REFA application.** K=6-8 fixed taps per addressed memory row give REFA queryable event ages spanning ~4 to ~4000 events with constant relative precision, at zero learned parameters (decays fixed geometric; Post/log-ratio decoder is a precomputed linear map). Revision can then be ordered by decoded age rather than by whatever the bank decay lambda_h has left of the magnitude.

Sources:
- Shankar & Howard, 'A Scale-Invariant Internal Representation of Time', Neural Computation 24(1):134-193, 2012 — <https://sites.bu.edu/tcn/files/2015/12/ShankarHoward12-NeuralComp.pdf>
- Shankar & Howard, 'Optimally Fuzzy Temporal Memory', JMLR 14:3785-3812, 2013 — <https://jmlr.org/papers/volume14/shankar13a/shankar13a.pdf>

### 2024-2026 biology: cortex stores real Laplace transforms of past AND future
*status: convergent*

PNAS 2024 (from bioRxiv 2024.02.13.580170): rodent mPFC ramping cells split into 'past cells' with exponentially DECAYING temporal receptive fields after an event and 'future cells' with exponentially RAMPING fields toward a predicted event, each population showing a continuous, broad distribution of time constants - i.e., the mPFC maintains the real Laplace transform of time-to-past and time-to-future simultaneously. This complements Cao et al. (eLife 2022): hippocampal time-cell populations are evenly distributed on a LOG time axis with field width growing linearly with delay (neural Weber-Fechner law), and entorhinal temporal context cells provide the decaying-spectrum input from which time cells can be constructed by inversion. Hippocampus 2026 adds a cellular mechanism: intrinsic persistent firing in CA1 encodes elapsed time across behaviorally relevant scales, so the code does not require recurrent fine-tuning. Together these fix the biological spec: log-spaced exponential decays for the past, exponential ramps for anticipated events.

**Math.** Past cells: r_i(t) ~ e^{-s_i (t - t_event)}; future cells: r_i(t) ~ e^{+s_i (t - t_pred)} (ramp peaking at the predicted event) - the Laplace pair for f(past) and f(future); s_i drawn from a continuous broad spectrum. Hippocampal time fields: peak times tau_i uniform in ln(tau); width sigma_i = a * tau_i (linear, a constant across the population) - exactly the finite-k Post-inversion prediction sigma/tau ~ 1/sqrt(k). Decoding elapsed time from a decay population: ln r_i = -s_i * Delta, a linear regression in s.

**REFA application.** Licenses fixed (not learned) log-spaced decay banks as the timestamp code, and motivates a cheap 'future/ramp' channel for REFA's delayed-evidence roadmap task: alongside the decay trace, an anticipation scalar per bank R <- e^{+s} R while a predicted confirmation is outstanding, feeding the unknown/abstention head.

Sources:
- 'Ramping cells in rodent mPFC encode time to past and future events via real Laplace transform', PNAS 121, 2024, doi:10.1073/pnas.2404169121 — <https://www.pnas.org/doi/10.1073/pnas.2404169121>
- Cao, Bladon, Charczynski, Hasselmo & Howard, 'Internally generated time in the rodent hippocampus is logarithmically compressed', eLife 11:e75353, 2022 — <https://elifesciences.org/articles/75353>
- 'Intrinsic Persistent Firing in CA1 Encodes Elapsed Time Across Behaviorally Relevant Scales', Hippocampus, 2026, doi:10.1002/hipo.70105 — <https://onlinelibrary.wiley.com/doi/10.1002/hipo.70105>

### HiPPO: temporal memory as optimal online projection; measure choice = timestamp semantics ⚑
*status: convergent*

HiPPO (Gu et al., NeurIPS 2020) derives recurrent memory from first principles: maintain the optimal degree-N polynomial approximation of the input history under a chosen time-varying measure mu(t). The measure choice IS the temporal prior: LegT (uniform over a sliding window theta) recovers the Legendre Memory Unit and fails hard if theta is mis-specified; LagT (exponentially decaying measure) yields fixed decay taps - the Laplace/temporal-context code; LegS (uniform over all of [0,t], window grows with t) yields a 1/t-scaled ODE that is provably timescale-equivariant (no timescale hyperparameter at all), with bounded gradients and slow error growth. This gives the design theory for REFA's decay spectrum: learned single-scale decay (REFA's lambda ~ 0.995) is the worst of both worlds; either commit to log-spaced LagT taps (recency-weighted, scale-covering) or add one LegS unit for an equitable whole-stream summary. Numerical care at the t=0 singularity of LegS is analyzed in arXiv 2412.08595.

**Math.** c'(t) = A(t) c(t) + B(t) f(t) with c the basis coefficients. LegS: c'(t) = -(1/t) A c(t) + (1/t) B f(t); A_{nk} = sqrt((2n+1)(2k+1)) for n>k, = n+1 for n=k, else 0; B_n = sqrt(2n+1); discrete: c_{k+1} = (I - A/k) c_k + (1/k) B f_k. Theorems (HiPPO): timescale equivariance - if h(t)=f(alpha t) then c_h(t) = c_f(alpha t); gradient norm ||dc(T)/df(t)|| = Theta(1/T) (no vanishing/exploding); approximation error O(T L / sqrt(N)) for L-Lipschitz f. LagT: fixed A (exponential measure) = constant-coefficient decay taps, O(N) per step. Memory cost: N floats per tracked scalar channel.

**REFA application.** Replace REFA's per-bank learned scalar decay with a fixed LagT-style log grid across the 8 banks (lambda_h = 1 - 1/tau_h, tau_h geometric from 8 to 4096 events): multi-scale banks at zero parameters, plus HiPPO's bounded-gradient guarantee for the long-sequence roadmap tests. Optionally one N=16 LegS unit summarizing the whole evidence stream for the classifier (16 extra state floats).

Sources:
- Gu, Dao, Ermon, Rudra, Re, 'HiPPO: Recurrent Memory with Optimal Polynomial Projections', NeurIPS 2020, arXiv:2008.07669 — <https://arxiv.org/abs/2008.07669>
- 'Numerical Analysis of HiPPO-LegS ODE for Deep State Space Models', arXiv:2412.08595 — <https://arxiv.org/html/2412.08595>

### Diagonal SSM eigenvalue spectra are Laplace-domain samples of input history
*status: convergent*

A diagonal linear SSM is literally a bank of Laplace-transform samplers: each state unit with eigenvalue lambda_n holds the Laplace transform of the input's past evaluated at s = -lambda_n. S4D (Gu et al., NeurIPS 2022) makes the spectrum explicit: S4D-Lin places eigenvalues at -1/2 + i*pi*n (damped Fourier basis); S4D-Inv uses an inverse-law spacing of imaginary parts that approximates the LegS spectrum, i.e., Im(lambda_n) = Theta(1/n) - a log-like frequency grid, the oscillatory sibling of SITH's log-spaced decays. Recent analysis (arXiv 2508.20441, Aug 2025) shows diagonal SSMs have a spectral bias: they resolve input structure best at frequencies near their eigenvalues, confirming that eigenvalue placement is history-compression design, not an optimization detail. Real parts set memory decay (timestamp magnitude), imaginary parts add a phase clock (timestamp phase). This unifies the Engram Loop's 'Laplace clock' with mainstream SSM practice: choosing an SSM init = choosing which temporal measure of the past is kept.

**Math.** x'(t) = Lambda x + B u, Lambda = diag(lambda_n): x_n(t) = int_0^t e^{lambda_n (t-t')} u(t') dt' = (L u|_past)(-lambda_n). Output kernel K(t) = sum_n C_n B_n e^{lambda_n t}. S4D-Lin: lambda_n = -1/2 + i pi n. S4D-Inv: lambda_n = -1/2 + i (N/pi)(N/(2n+1) - 1), Im ~ Theta(n^{-1}). Complex tap with s = gamma + i omega: magnitude e^{-gamma Delta} codes age coarsely, phase omega*Delta mod 2pi codes it finely; multiple incommensurate omegas give unambiguous age over lcm range (residue-style decoding). Cost per tracked channel: 2 floats (complex) per tap; discrete update x <- e^{(gamma+i omega)} x + u is 4 MACs.

**REFA application.** Upgrade the REFA timestamp taps from real decays to K/2 complex pairs (same float budget): phase preserves fine recency ordering long after magnitudes of two writes become indistinguishable, directly improving 'later evidence supersedes earlier' resolution for closely spaced contradicting events. Init from S4D-Inv spacing; keep frozen.

Sources:
- Gu, Gupta, Goel, Re, 'On the Parameterization and Initialization of Diagonal State Space Models' (S4D), NeurIPS 2022 — <https://papers.neurips.cc/paper_files/paper/2022/file/e9a32fade47b906de908431991440f7c-Paper-Conference.pdf>
- Solozabal et al., 'Uncovering the Spectral Bias in Diagonal State Space Models', arXiv:2508.20441 — <https://arxiv.org/html/2508.20441>

### LinOSS / D-LinOSS: implicit discretizations give provably stable, universal oscillatory clocks
*status: convergent*

LinOSS (Rusch & Rus, ICLR 2025) builds sequence layers from forced harmonic oscillators y'' = -A y + B u with only a nonnegative diagonal A required for stability - no structured/HiPPO init needed. Two discretizations: implicit (IM) is unconditionally stable and dissipative (eigenvalue magnitude strictly below 1, coupled to frequency); implicit-explicit (IMEX) is symplectic-like, exactly norm/energy conserving and time-reversible (|lambda| = 1), computed with associative parallel scans in O(T) work. LinOSS is a universal approximator of continuous causal operators between time-varying functions. D-LinOSS (arXiv 2505.12171) adds a learned damping term G y', which decouples eigenvalue magnitude from frequency so the discrete spectrum can be placed anywhere in the stability disk; it outperforms LinOSS on long-range benchmarks (sequences up to 50k) while halving the hyperparameter search space. This is the rigorous discretization math behind any 'oscillatory timestamp' channel: IMEX for lossless phase clocks, D-LinOSS for tunable forgetting.

**Math.** State z = y': IMEX: z_n = z_{n-1} + dt(-A y_{n-1} + B u_n); y_n = y_{n-1} + dt z_n. Per-mode matrix [[1, -dt A],[dt, 1 - dt^2 A]] has det = 1, char. lambda^2 - (2 - dt^2 A) lambda + 1 = 0, so |lambda| = 1 iff 0 <= dt^2 A <= 4 (conservative). IM: solve implicitly with S = (I + dt^2 A)^{-1}; block det = S so |lambda| = (1 + dt^2 A_k)^{-1/2} < 1 always (unconditionally stable, dissipative; decay tied to frequency). D-LinOSS: y'' = -A y - G y' + B u; implicit scheme gives |lambda| = (1 + dt G_k + dt^2 A_k)^{-1/2}: G decouples damping from frequency sqrt(A_k). Universality: stacked LinOSS + nonlinear readout approximates any continuous causal operator on compacta. Parallel scan: O(T) work, O(log T) depth.

**REFA application.** A D-LinOSS pair per bank (2 state floats, 2 params A_h, G_h) is a phase clock with learned forgetting - the principled discretization for the complex timestamp taps in this report; IMEX mode gives a lossless session clock. Also a drop-in candidate to replace the GRU workspace for length extrapolation (parallel-scan trainable, unlike the Python loop).

Sources:
- Rusch & Rus, 'Oscillatory State-Space Models', ICLR 2025, arXiv:2410.03943 — <https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.03943>
- 'Learning to Dissipate Energy in Oscillatory State-Space Models' (D-LinOSS), arXiv:2505.12171 — <https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.12171>

### Traveling-wave shift registers: exact recency for the recent past, fuzzy Laplace for the far past ⚑
*status: convergent*

Keller et al. (ICLR 2024) show an RNN whose recurrence is initialized as a circular shift (wave) matrix stores the recent past losslessly: each input occupies a position in a ring buffer and its AGE equals its position, so recency ordering of the last n items is exact, not decoded. Wave-RNNs solve copy tasks at far greater lengths than vanilla RNNs, train faster, and match gated architectures with fewer parameters, because the shift operator is orthogonal (eigenvalues on the unit circle - no vanishing gradients, invertible storage). Karuvally, Sejnowski & Siegelmann (ICML 2024) show trained RNNs spontaneously discover the same mechanism ('hidden traveling waves' bind working-memory variables, visible after a basis change) and that wave boundary conditions implement variable binding/updating. Combined with Shankar-Howard's optimality result, this yields a principled two-tier timestamp architecture: exact shift-register recency for the last n events, scale-invariant Laplace fuzz beyond - precisely the accuracy profile the fuzzy-memory theorem says is optimal.

**Math.** wRNN: h in R^{n x c}; h_{t+1} = sigma(Sigma h_t + V x_t + b), Sigma = circulant shift (ones on the subdiagonal, wrap-around) - the discretized 1-D wave equation dh/dt = nu dh/dx. Sigma is orthogonal: eigenvalues e^{2 pi i k / n}, gradient norm preserved over n steps. Capacity: last n inputs stored exactly (invertible linear map); age(item) = ring position, an argmax not an estimate. Parameter cost ~ one conv kernel per channel vs. n^2 dense recurrence. Copy-task results: wRNN succeeds at sequence lengths ~10x vanilla RNN, comparable to LSTM with fewer params.

**REFA application.** Add a ring buffer of the last n=32 write keys + stance bits + bank ids (32 x ~20 = 640 floats, 0 params, pure indexing). Revision conflicts among the last 32 events get EXACT ordering (argmax over ring position); the Laplace taps only arbitrate older conflicts. Matches REFA's synthetic revision task where superseding evidence is typically recent.

Sources:
- Keller, Muller, Sejnowski, Welling, 'Traveling Waves Encode the Recent Past and Enhance Sequence Learning', ICLR 2024, arXiv:2309.08045 — <https://arxiv.org/abs/2309.08045>
- Karuvally, Sejnowski, Siegelmann, 'Hidden Traveling Waves bind Working Memory Variables in Recurrent Neural Networks', ICML 2024, arXiv:2402.10163 — <https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.10163>

### Kuramoto phase binding (AKOrN): episode tags and coherence-based uncertainty, not timestamps
*status: partial*

AKOrN (Miyato, Lowe, Geiger, Welling; ICLR 2025 oral) replaces threshold units with N-dimensional Kuramoto oscillators on the sphere, updated by synchronizing forces from connected oscillators plus a data term. With symmetric coupling the dynamics descend a Lyapunov energy, and binding emerges as phase clustering ('distributed continuous clustering'), yielding unsupervised object discovery, adversarial robustness, notably well-CALIBRATED uncertainty, and energy-descent reasoning (Sudoku). For temporal math the honest assessment: AKOrN is a binding/segmentation mechanism, not a timestamp code - phases cluster by feature agreement, not elapsed time. Its value to a memory system is (a) episode tags: items written under one phase regime retrieve together; and (b) the coherence order parameter as a cheap, calibrated confidence signal. A 2025 follow-up (arXiv 2505.03648) shows oscillatory binding can be attached to standard threshold networks, so the mechanism composes with existing architectures rather than replacing them.

**Math.** Oscillator x_i in S^{N-1}: Delta x_i = Omega_i x_i + Proj_{x_i}(c_i + sum_j J_ij x_j), Proj_x(v) = v - (x^T v)x (tangent projection), followed by renormalization ||x_i|| = 1; Omega_i antisymmetric (natural rotation). Symmetric J, shared Omega => Lyapunov energy E = -sum_{ij} x_i^T J_ij x_j - sum_i c_i^T x_i, monotonically decreasing => convergent iterative inference. Readout: coherence rho = ||sum_i w_i x_i|| in [0,1]; rho near 1 = bound/confident cluster, low rho = conflict - an uncertainty measure that improved calibration in their experiments. Cost: oscillator dim N ~ 2-4; updates O(edges * N) per iteration.

**REFA application.** Not for recency. Possible REFA use: an 8-dim phase tag per event, slowly rotating across the stream, written alongside values; retrieval coherence between recalled support and oppose phase tags feeds the unknown/abstention class (low coherence = conflicting episodes = abstain). Untested on streaming relational data - treat as an experiment, not a commitment.

Sources:
- Miyato, Lowe, Geiger, Welling, 'Artificial Kuramoto Oscillatory Neurons', ICLR 2025 (oral), arXiv:2410.13821 — <https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.13821>
