- 'Binding threshold units with artificial oscillatory neurons', arXiv:2505.03648 — <https://arxiv.org/pdf/2505.03648>

### Time-aware rotations (TeRo, RotateQVS): absolute-time-addressable keys via unit-modulus phases
*status: convergent*

Temporal knowledge-graph embeddings solved a problem REFA has not yet posed: querying facts BY time. TeRo (COLING 2020) evolves each entity embedding by an elementwise unit-modulus complex rotation indexed by timestamp, scoring (s, r, o, t) by translation in the rotated space, with dual relation embeddings for interval start/end; RotateQVS (ACL 2022) lifts this to quaternion rotations. The key mathematical point: when the rotation angle is linear in wall-clock time, time-rotation is exactly RoPE with real timestamps instead of token positions - inner products depend only on TIME DIFFERENCES, rotations are norm-preserving (compatible with L2-normalized keys), and rotating a query key by the rotation for time t_q retrieves what was written near t_q. This converts a decay-only memory (recency-weighted) into a time-ADDRESSABLE memory (query any epoch) at zero parameters, and is the cheapest known mechanism for 'what did we believe at time t'. It deepens the Engram Loop's theta-gamma RoPE by grounding it in wall-clock time and in the KG literature's empirical success on temporal link prediction.

**Math.** TeRo: e_t = e o w_t, w_t in C^d, |w_{t,i}| = 1 (elementwise rotation); score f = -||e_{s,t} + r - conj(e_{o,t})||_1; O(d) per fact. Linear-phase special case: w_t = e^{i theta_j t} per 2-D block with log-spaced theta_j = theta_0 gamma^{-j} (RoPE): R(t1)^T R(t2) = R(t2 - t1) (relative-time kernel); R orthogonal => ||R(t)k|| = ||k||. Temporal addressing: memory M holds sum_w R(t_w) k_w v_w^T; query q at target time t_q: (R(t_q) q)^T M = sum_w [q^T R(t_q - t_w)... ] - hmm, correctly: match is maximal when t_q = t_w since R(t_q)^T R(t_w) = R(t_w - t_q) -> I. Frequency band sets temporal receptive width ~ 1/theta_j; multi-band gives coarse-to-fine time addressing.

**REFA application.** One-line change to refa.py: k_t = R(t) k before both the delta-rule erase and write (frozen log-spaced theta_j; norm preserved, erase term still targets the same fact re-asserted later only if queried at matching epoch - so use rotation on a SUBSET of key dims to keep fact identity addressable while adding a time-selective subspace). Enables 'belief at time t' probes for the provenance-conflict roadmap task at 0 params.

Sources:
- Xu, Nayyeri, Alkhoury, Yazdi, Lehmann, 'TeRo: A Time-aware Knowledge Graph Embedding via Temporal Rotation', COLING 2020, arXiv:2010.01029 — <https://aclanthology.org/2020.coling-main.139/>
- 'RotateQVS: Representing Temporal Information as Rotations in Quaternion Vector Space for Temporal Knowledge Graph Completion', ACL 2022 — <https://arxiv.org/abs/2010.01029>

### SITH in deep nets (DeepSITH, SITHCon, SITH-RNN, RL): log-spaced decays give zero-shot timescale generalization in tiny models
*status: convergent*

The Shankar-Howard code has been repeatedly validated as a deep-learning component at exactly REFA's model scale. DeepSITH (NeurIPS 2021): stacked SITH modules (Laplace + inverse per layer, geometrically spaced time constants) with dense layers beat LSTMs on time-series decoding. SITHCon (ICML 2022): convolution along the log-tau axis + max-pool makes the network provably INVARIANT to temporal rescaling - trained at one speed, it generalizes zero-shot to f(at), which TCNs and LSTMs cannot. AAAI 2025 (Kabir, Mochizuki-Freeman, Tiganj): swapping LSTM memory for scale-invariant Laplace memory in deep RL agents yields robust learning across a wide range of temporal scales. SITH-RNN (Sarkar & Howard, arXiv 2601.02618, Jan 2026): scale-invariant recurrent priors produce emergent hierarchies of temporal receptive windows, learn faster with orders-of-magnitude fewer parameters, and generalize zero-shot to out-of-distribution timescales on hierarchical language-like tasks. Collectively: fixed log-spaced decay grids are a strong prior, not a limitation, especially for small models.

**Math.** SITH layer: F updated by tau-indexed leaky integrators dF/dt = -s F + f; inverse f_tilde = L_k^{-1} F sampled at tau*_n geometric (ratio c). Rescaling covariance: input f(at) shifts the log-tau representation by log(a): T[f(a.)](tau*) = T[f](a tau*); conv along log-tau is equivariant to this shift; max-pool over log-tau makes outputs invariant. DeepSITH: layer l receptive field ~ c^{N_l} x layer l-1 (geometric growth of temporal receptive windows). Parameter economy: SITH front-end is parameter-free; learnable weights only in 'what' projections - why SITH-RNN reports orders-of-magnitude fewer params than LSTM baselines at matched accuracy.

**REFA application.** REFA's revision/abstention tasks will be tested for length extrapolation (roadmap): fixing the 8 bank decays to a geometric grid (tau_h = 8 * 2.4^h events) and pooling classifier features across banks (max over the log-scale axis) imports SITHCon's invariance argument directly - the trained classifier transfers to streams 4-10x longer without retraining.

Sources:
- Jacques, Tiganj, Howard, Sederberg, 'DeepSITH: Efficient Learning via Decomposition of What and When Across Time Scales', NeurIPS 2021, arXiv:2104.04646 — <https://arxiv.org/abs/2104.04646>
- Jacques, Tiganj, Sarkar, Howard, Sederberg, 'A deep convolutional neural network that is invariant to time rescaling' (SITHCon), ICML 2022, arXiv:2107.04616 — <https://proceedings.mlr.press/v162/jacques22a/jacques22a.pdf>
- Kabir, Mochizuki-Freeman, Tiganj, 'Deep Reinforcement Learning with Time-Scale Invariant Memory', AAAI 2025, arXiv:2412.15292 — <https://ojs.aaai.org/index.php/AAAI/article/view/32124>
- Sarkar & Howard, 'Hierarchical temporal receptive windows and zero-shot timescale generalization in biologically constrained scale-invariant deep networks', arXiv:2601.02618 — <https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.02618>

### 'What' x 'when' outer-product Laplace manifolds: the template for timestamped fast weights ⚑
*status: partial*

Howard-lab theory 2024-2026 specifies precisely HOW to attach time to content: working memory of a sequence should be a CONJUNCTIVE code, the outer product of a stimulus ('what') vector with a Laplace temporal basis ('when') vector - mixed selectivity with receptive fields jointly over stimulus and elapsed time. Sarkar et al. (arXiv 2409.20484; eLife reviewed preprint 108804) show this factorization makes the population covariance decouple as a Kronecker product, giving analyzable low-dimensional dynamics; Daniels & Howard (Comp. Brain & Behavior 2025; arXiv 2406.04545) build continuous-attractor circuits that hold the Laplace manifold (an 'edge' on a log-s neuron axis) and its inverse (a 'bump'), robust to noise despite exponentially graded firing. A bioRxiv preprint dated 2026-08-03 extends Laplace-domain representations to evidence-accumulation/decision circuits - directly the regime of REFA's three-class evidential output. ML translation of the conjunctive 'what x when' fast-weight code is essentially absent - this is the building site the REFA design below fills.

**Math.** Memory state as sum over events: M(t) = sum_e v_e (x) phi(t - t_e), phi_k(Delta) = e^{-s_k Delta}, s_k log-spaced (outer product of what-vector and when-spectrum). Covariance decouples: C = C_what (x) C_when (Kronecker), so PCA/dynamics factorize. Time evolution is content-independent: M(t + dt) = M(t) Lambda_K, Lambda_K = diag(e^{-s_k dt}) - a diagonal right-multiplication, O(RK) per step. Retrieval of event age: for query q matching k_e, q^T M = phi(t - t_e) up to gain; gain cancels in log-ratios: ln(a_k / a_j) = -(s_k - s_j)(t - t_e). Attractor implementation: bump position on log-s axis moves at constant log-speed = exponential real-time drift; noise-robust despite exponential firing range.

**REFA application.** This is the exact algebraic template for adding timestamps to REFA's fast-weight matrices: same key addressing, but the written 'value' is the temporal basis vector instead of content - a second, thin fast-weight matrix per bank whose readout is the age spectrum of whatever evidence the query addresses.

Sources:
- Sarkar et al., '"What" x "When" working memory representations using Laplace Neural Manifolds', arXiv:2409.20484; eLife reviewed preprint 108804 — <https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.20484>
- Daniels & Howard, 'Continuous Attractor Networks for Laplace Neural Manifolds', Computational Brain & Behavior, 2025; arXiv:2406.04545 — <https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.04545>
- 'Continuous attractor circuits for decision making with Laplace-domain neural representations', bioRxiv 2026.08.03.742594 — <https://www.biorxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.08.03.742594v1.full>

### Deliverable design: delta-rule Laplace timestamp trace for REFA (queryable age, recency-ordered revision) ⚑
*status: speculative*

Cheapest construction meeting the mission spec, synthesized from findings 1, 4, 8, 10; no published system does this for fast-weight memories. Alongside each stance matrix M_h^s (R x D), add a timestamp trace T_h^s (R x K), K = 6 fixed log-spaced decay taps. T is updated by the SAME delta rule and routing as M (shared beta, r_h, k - no new gates), except (a) the written 'value' is the all-ones temporal impulse 1_K, and (b) T decays on the K axis by the fixed tap decays instead of the bank decay. Because the delta-rule erase removes the old row before writing, re-asserted evidence automatically gets a fresh timestamp - revision updates age for free. Reading with query q returns an age spectrum per stance; log-RATIO decoding cancels the unknown write gain beta and any shared decay, yielding a calibrated age estimate; alternatively feed both raw spectra (2K = 12 dims) to the output MLP so the classifier learns 'most recent stance wins' directly, upgrading REFA's decay-implicit recency to explicit, queryable recency. Optional upgrades: complex taps (finding 4) for fine ordering of near-simultaneous contradictions; a 32-slot ring buffer (finding 6) for exact ordering of the recent past.

**Math.** Update per step (K-axis decay Lambda_K = diag(e^{-1/tau_k}), tau_k = 4 * 4^k events, k = 0..5, spanning 4 to ~4096): T^s <- (T^s - beta r_h k k^T T^s + beta r_h 1[s = pol] k 1_K^T) Lambda_K. Read: a^s = q^T sum_h r_h T_h^s in R^K; for a single addressed write at t_w: a_k = g e^{-(t - t_w)/tau_k} with unknown gain g. Age decode (gain-invariant): Delta_hat = -ln(a_k / a_j) / (1/tau_k - 1/tau_j), least-squares over valid tap pairs; relative error ~ Weber constant = ln(tau_{k+1}/tau_k)/2 ~ 0.7 for ratio-4 spacing (use ratio 2, K = 12, for ~0.35). Costs at REFA dims (H=8, R=16, D=64): state +2HRK = 1536 floats (6 KB fp32, +9.4% over the 16384-float M state); params +0 for taps (frozen) and +2K x D = 768 in the widened output MLP (<1% of model); compute +O(HRK) ~ 768 MACs/step (M update is O(HRD) ~ 8192/step) - negligible on the RTX 2060.

**REFA application.** Concrete edit to /home/user/refa-edge/src/refa_edge/models/refa.py: allocate T like support/oppose memory with last dim K; reuse write_strength and the erase einsums with T in place of memory; append a^+ and a^- to the output concat. Ablation: three-class accuracy on the revision and provenance-conflict tasks with raw-spectrum readout vs. decoded-age readout vs. baseline (no T).

Sources:
- Shankar & Howard, 'Optimally Fuzzy Temporal Memory', JMLR 14, 2013 (tap spacing and fuzziness bound) — <https://jmlr.org/papers/volume14/shankar13a/shankar13a.pdf>
- Sarkar et al., '"What" x "When" working memory representations using Laplace Neural Manifolds', arXiv:2409.20484 (outer-product template) — <https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.20484>
- REFA implementation this design targets: /home/user/refa-edge/src/refa_edge/models/refa.py
