## Cognitive-map and binding mathematics for relational memory
`relational-structure`

Three literatures have independently converged on the same answer to how (subject, relation, object) should be bound into memory keys: multiplicative/unitary binding of a content-free structural code, with content stored as the value. Neuroscience gives the blueprint (TEM/TEM-t: keys = recurrent structural code, place cells = structure-content conjunctions; Vector-HaSH: content hung on a prefabricated random scaffold; chickadee barcodes: random episodic keys coexisting with structured ones; 2025-2026 replay work: memories as compositional bindings of reusable primitives). VSA supplies the algebra and the honest capacity math: circular-convolution/phasor binding is exact as a key code but superposition holds only M ~ D/(2 ln K) facts per trace (roughly 5-7 facts at D=64 with a 100-1000 item codebook, ~28 at D=256), and resonator networks factor bound vectors with operational capacity growing quadratically in D. Knowledge-graph embedding rediscovered the same operators from the ML side: RotatE is exactly Fourier-HRR binding, HolE is provably equivalent to ComplEx, and hyperbolic rotation models work at d=32 — so unitary per-relation rotations are the field-tested relation operator at REFA's scale. The load-bearing critique of REFA's current design (src/refa_edge/models/common.py) is that its additive identity embedding leaks the object into the key and gives query events systematically different keys from write events; the fixes — rotation-bound (s,r) keys, bilinear/TPR keys, fixed-random barcode key components, per-bank evidence voting a la Monty, and resonator-based audit decoding — are all concrete, cheap at D=64-256, and compatible with the existing delta-rule matrix banks, which the capacity math says should be kept rather than replaced by superposed holographic traces.

**Verification** of load-bearing claims:

- **CONFIRMED** — Factorize keys (structure) from values (content): TEM, TEM-transformer, and key-value memory in the brain
  - Gershman-Fiete-Irie (arXiv:2501.02950, published in Neuron 2025) abstract verified verbatim: key-value systems 'optimize simultaneously for fidelity in storage and discriminability in retrieval' — the load-bearing characterization is exact. The REFA code characterization was verified directly against the repo: EventEmbedding.identity = LayerNorm(e_s + role_0 + e_r + e_o + role_1) (common.py lines 28-32), and in refa.py the address (q=k=normalize(Linear(identity))) and value (tanh(Linear(identity))) are both projections of that single sum, so the object does leak into the key exactly as claimed. TEM citation (Whittington et al., Cell 183:1249-1263, 2020) and TEM-transformer (arXiv:2112.04035, ICLR 2022) are real with correct venues/years and the stated equations match the published models, though the full texts could not be fetched (network egress blocked); one softening: TEM-t shows a close formal correspondence between TEM and transformers with recurrent positional encodings under simplifying assumptions — 'proves this is literally key-value attention' should read 'shows a formal correspondence to'.
- **PLAUSIBLE** — HRR/FHRR binding algebra with honest capacity numbers at D=64-256
  - All three sources are real with correct citations (Plate, IEEE TNN 6(3):623-641, 1995; Frady-Kleyko-Sommer, Neural Computation 30:1449-1513, 2018, arXiv:1803.00412; Thomas-Dasgupta-Rosing, JAIR 72, 2021), but none could be fetched (egress blocked), so the specific published numbers (e.g. 0.1-0.5 bits/component) rest on the claim-author's reading. The math was independently recomputed and is CORRECT throughout: M ~ D/(2 ln K) gives 6.9→~7 (D=64,K=100), 4.6→~5 (K=1000), 13.9→~14 (D=128), 27.8→~28 (D=256,K=100), 13.9→~14 (D=256,K=10^4), ~111 (D=1024,K=100); REFA state 8x16x64=8192 floats / 128 slots per stance verified against the code (memory_rank=16, d_model=64, num_banks=8); the SNR~sqrt(D/M) vs sqrt(2 ln K) threshold argument is the standard Gaussian extreme-value derivation and is of the same linear-in-M, log-in-K form as both cited theories. The binding/unbinding equations are textbook-correct HRR/FHRR. The qualitative conclusion (superposed trace cannot replace matrix banks at D=64; binding fine as key code) follows from the verified math.
- **PLAUSIBLE** — Resonator networks factor bound representations; operational capacity quadratic in D
  - The two Neural Computation 2020 resonator papers (pages 2311-2331 and 2332-2388) and Langenegger et al., Nature Nanotechnology 18:479-485 (2023, in-memory factorization, 'at least five orders of magnitude larger problems') are real papers with correct venues, but none could be fetched (egress blocked), so the quadratic-in-N operational-capacity scaling and the ~10^6-combination-at-N-in-the-low-thousands anchor are consistent with my knowledge of the papers but not re-verified against the text. arXiv:1906.11684 is the correct preprint for the capacity paper; the arXiv ID 2007.03748 given for Resonator Networks paper 1 could not be verified and may be wrong — cite the Neural Computation version instead. The extrapolation arithmetic is correct given the quadratic assumption (10^6 x (256/1500)^2 ≈ 2.9x10^4; x (64/1500)^2 ≈ 1.8x10^3), but note these N=64-256 numbers are the claim-author's own extrapolation, not published results, and published capacity fits were measured at much larger N.
- **CONFIRMED** — KG embedding rediscovered VSA: RotatE = FHRR binding, HolE = ComplEx; unitary relation rotations win at low D
  - RotatE (ICLR 2019, arXiv:1902.10197) full text verified: relation as element-wise rotation in complex space, d_r(h,t)=||h∘r-t||, and it explicitly models symmetry/antisymmetry, inversion, and composition — as claimed. Chami et al. (ACL 2020) full text verified: low-dimensional setting is exactly d=32 and the improvement is 'up to 6.1% MRR'. Two corrections to the characterization: (1) the 6.1% gain is over Euclidean-based baselines at the SAME 32 dimensions (the abstract says over 'previous Euclidean- and hyperbolic-based efforts'), not over baselines 'that need 10x the dimensions'; the paper's separate observation is that at high (500) dimensions hyperbolic and Euclidean perform similarly; (2) 'd=32 matches d~500 Euclidean' overstates it — low-dim hyperbolic approaches but does not fully match high-dim state of the art. ComplEx (ICML 2016, arXiv:1606.06357) and Hayashi-Shimbo (ACL 2017, P17-2088, equivalence of holographic and complex embeddings) are real and correctly characterized (could not fetch; consistent with knowledge: ComplEx = Fourier-domain HolE under conjugate symmetry). The stated RotatE/ComplEx/HolE equations and the 32-phases-per-relation count at D=64 are correct.
- **PLAUSIBLE** — Prefabricated scaffolds and random barcodes: keys should be (partly) fixed, not learned
  - Chandra, Sharma, Chaudhuri, Fiete, Nature 638:739-751 (2025, Vector-HaSH) and Chettih, Mackevicius, Hale, Aronov, Cell 187 (2024, chickadee cache barcodes) are both real papers with correct venues, authors, and years, and the described mechanisms (prefabricated grid-cell scaffold with fixed random projections avoiding the ~0.14N Hopfield cliff in favor of graceful degradation; sparse episode-unique barcodes uncorrelated across nearby caches and reactivated at retrieval) match those papers as I know them — but the texts could not be fetched (egress blocked), so specific details are not re-verified. The third source (eLife reviewed preprint 103512 on barcode activity in a recurrent network model) could not be located or verified at all and should be treated as unconfirmed; the claim does not load-bear on it. The math checks out: Hopfield capacity cliff ~0.138N is the standard result, and random-key overlap ~1/sqrt(D)=0.125 at D=64 is correct. The Gershman-Fiete-Irie discriminability/fidelity framing invoked here was independently verified verbatim from its abstract.

### Factorize keys (structure) from values (content): TEM, TEM-transformer, and key-value memory in the brain ⚑
*status: convergent*

The Tolman-Eichenbaum Machine shows that relational generalization requires factoring a content-free structural code g (position/role in an abstract relation graph, maintained by path integration over relations) from sensory content x, binding them only in hippocampus-like conjunctions (place cells). TEM-t proves this is literally key-value attention: keys are recurrent structural codes, values are content, and place cells are the conjunctive key-value slots; structure transfers zero-shot to new content because the transition weights are shared. Gershman-Fiete-Irie's Neuron 2025 review generalizes: keys and values should be REPRESENTATIONALLY DISTINCT because keys optimize discriminability and values optimize fidelity. REFA violates this: EventEmbedding.identity = LayerNorm(e_s + role_0 + e_r + e_o + role_1), and both the address (key) and value are linear projections of this single sum, so (a) the object leaks into the key, making query events (masked/placeholder object) address different slots than the write events they must retrieve, and (b) key and value cannot be optimized for their different jobs.

**Math.** TEM: g_t = f(W_{a_t} g_{t-1}) (relation-indexed transition matrices, shared across environments); conjunction p_t = g_t (x) x_t (outer/elementwise product); Hebbian store M <- M + eta p_t p_t^T; recall from structural cue alone via attractor iteration p^{tau+1} = h(M p^tau). TEM-t: p_t = softmax(g_t^T G_{1:t-1}) X_{1:t-1}, i.e. attention with q = current structural code, K = past structural codes, V = past content; equivalent to modern-Hopfield retrieval, with recurrent positional encodings playing the role of g. Zero-shot structural transfer holds because W_a is content-independent; a mixed key k(s,r,o) breaks the invariance k(query) = k(write) whenever o is unobserved at query time.

**REFA application.** Split the addressing path in refa.py: k = W_k(e_s, e_r) only (never e_o); v = W_v e_o. Then the query "s r ?" reproduces the write key exactly. Write a second, role-flipped slot k' = W_k'(e_o, e_r) with value e_s to support inverse queries "? r o". Route banks from e_r alone (pure structure) so banks specialize by relation type. This is a ~10-line change to EventEmbedding/REFAMicro and directly targets the relational-recall and revision tasks.

Sources:
- Whittington et al., The Tolman-Eichenbaum Machine, Cell 183:1249-1263 (2020) — <https://www.cell.com/cell/fulltext/S0092-8674(20)31388-X>
- Whittington, Warren, Behrens, Relating transformers to models and neural representations of the hippocampal formation, ICLR 2022, arXiv:2112.04035 — <https://arxiv.org/abs/2112.04035>
- Gershman, Fiete, Irie, Key-value memory in the brain, Neuron (2025), arXiv:2501.02950 — <https://arxiv.org/abs/2501.02950>

### HRR/FHRR binding algebra with honest capacity numbers at D=64-256 ⚑
*status: convergent*

Holographic Reduced Representations bind two D-vectors into one D-vector by circular convolution; Fourier HRR (unit-modulus complex phasors) makes binding = phase addition with EXACT unbinding by conjugation. The critical distinction for REFA: binding used as a KEY CODE (one fact per storage slot) costs no capacity, but SUPERPOSING bound pairs into one trace does — crosstalk limits a D-dim trace to M ~ D/(2 ln K) reliably retrievable pairs against a K-item cleanup codebook. Concretely: D=64, K=100 -> ~7 facts; D=64, K=1000 -> ~5; D=128, K=100 -> ~14; D=256, K=100 -> ~28; D=256, K=10^4 -> ~14. Frady-Kleyko-Sommer's exact theory shows all VSA variants share these curves and achievable information is ~0.1-0.5 bits/component (theoretical max 1 bit/neuron, unreachable due to crosstalk); Thomas-Dasgupta-Rosing (JAIR 2021) give rigorous finite-D recovery guarantees of the same linear-in-M, log-in-K form. Consequence: a superposed holographic store CANNOT replace REFA's matrix banks at D=64, but HRR/FHRR binding is the right way to compress (s,r) into a single key without concatenation.

**Math.** Bind: z = a (*) b, (a(*)b)_j = sum_k a_k b_{(j-k) mod D}; O(D log D) via FFT: z = F^{-1}(F(a) . F(b)). Unbind: b_hat = a^dagger (*) z, a^dagger_j = a_{-j mod D} (exact iff a unitary). FHRR: a_j = e^{i theta_j}, bind = phase add, unbind = conjugate multiply, always exact. Bundle s = sum_{m=1}^M a_m (*) b_m: unbinding one factor gives signal 1 + noise of variance ~ M/D, SNR ~ sqrt(D/M); cleanup against K codewords reliable iff sqrt(D/M) >~ sqrt(2 ln K), so M* ~ D/(2 ln K). Comparison at equal state: REFA per-stance state is 8 banks x (16x64) = 8192 floats storing 8x16 = 128 exact, ERASABLE slots; one D=8192... a D=1024 HRR trace holds only ~1024/(2 ln 100) ~ 111 noisy, non-erasable facts. Delta-rule matrices dominate at small D for revision tasks.

**REFA application.** Keep the matrix banks; do not move to a superposed trace at D=64. Use binding only in key space: k = e_s (*) e_r (FFT over D=64, ~0 params) or FHRR phases. Raise memory_rank from 16 toward 64: the R=16 address space caps each bank at 16 orthogonal facts and makes any bound key code degenerate; R=D=64 costs 8x2x(64x64) = 65k floats per batch element — trivial on 6GB.

Sources:
- Plate, Holographic Reduced Representations, IEEE Trans. Neural Networks 6(3):623-641 (1995); book: Distributed Representation for Cognitive Structures (2003) — <https://press.uchicago.edu/ucp/books/book/distributed/H/bo3643252.html>
- Frady, Kleyko, Sommer, A theory of sequence indexing and working memory in RNNs, Neural Computation 30:1449-1513 (2018), arXiv:1803.00412 — <https://arxiv.org/abs/1803.00412>
- Thomas, Dasgupta, Rosing, A Theoretical Perspective on Hyperdimensional Computing, JAIR 72:215-249 (2021) — <https://www.jair.org/index.php/jair/article/view/12664>

### Resonator networks factor bound representations; operational capacity quadratic in D ⚑
*status: convergent*

Resonator networks (Frady, Kent, Olshausen, Sommer/Kanerva, Neural Computation 2020, two papers) solve the inverse of binding: given s = x_1 . x_2 . ... . x_F (Hadamard/convolution product), recover which codebook item each factor is, WITHOUT searching the product space. They iterate unbinding-with-current-estimates plus cleanup, 'searching in superposition'. Measured operational capacity (largest search space prod M_f solvable with high accuracy within an iteration budget) scales approximately QUADRATICALLY in vector dimension N for F=2-7 factors, roughly two orders of magnitude above ALS and gradient-based optimization; published demos factor ~10^6-combination problems (three 100-item codebooks) at N in the low thousands. IBM's memristive in-memory factorizer (Nature Nanotech 2023) exploits analog stochasticity + thresholding to solve at least five orders of magnitude larger problems, showing the iteration is robust to noise. Quadratic extrapolation from the 10^6@N~1500 anchor: N=256 -> ~3x10^4 combinations (three ~30-item codebooks); N=64 -> ~2x10^3. For REFA's two-factor decode (entities x relations, e.g. 500x20 = 10^4) N=128-256 is plausibly sufficient, and vocabularies in the current synthetic tasks (tens of entities) are comfortably inside capacity even at D=64.

**Math.** Dynamics per factor f: x_hat_f(t+1) = g( A_f A_f^T ( s (/) prod_{f' != f} x_hat_{f'}(t) ) ), where A_f is the N x M_f codebook, (/) is unbinding (conjugate/correlation), g a nonlinearity (sign or phasor projection); A_f A_f^T x is the cleanup (auto-associative) step. Operational capacity ~ c N^2 (empirical fits, F=2..7). Convergence typically tens-hundreds of iterations; failure mode is limit cycles near capacity. In-memory version: per-iteration matvecs O(1) in crossbar time; >=10^5 x problem-size gain from intrinsic stochastic sampling.

**REFA application.** Two uses: (1) query-time unbinding of bound keys — retrieve trace, then resonate against entity/relation codebooks to name WHICH (s,r) a bank's recalled evidence came from; (2) an audit/interpretability channel: keep an auxiliary trace t_h += route_h (e_s (*) e_r (*) e_o) per bank and factor it offline to enumerate what each bank currently stores — direct support for the provenance-conflict and abstention diagnostics in the roadmap. Cost: iterative matvecs, no training.

Sources:
- Kent, Frady, Sommer, Olshausen, Resonator Networks 2: Factorization Performance and Capacity, Neural Computation 32:2332-2388 (2020), arXiv:1906.11684 — <https://arxiv.org/abs/1906.11684>
- Frady, Kent, Olshausen, Sommer, Resonator Networks 1, Neural Computation 32:2311-2331 (2020), arXiv:2007.03748 — <https://arxiv.org/abs/2007.03748>
- Langenegger et al., In-memory factorization of holographic perceptual representations, Nature Nanotechnology 18:479-485 (2023) — <https://www.nature.com/articles/s41565-023-01357-8>

### KG embedding rediscovered VSA: RotatE = FHRR binding, HolE = ComplEx; unitary relation rotations win at low D ⚑
*status: convergent*

Knowledge-graph embedding independently converged on holographic binding. HolE scores triples by circular correlation r^T (h * t) — literally Plate's HRR similarity; Hayashi & Shimbo (ACL 2017) proved spectral HolE is EQUIVALENT to ComplEx (Re<h, r, conj(t)>). RotatE models each relation as an element-wise rotation in C^{D/2} (t ~ h o r, |r_i| = 1) — exactly FHRR binding with learned per-relation phases, and its real realization is block-diagonal 2x2 Givens rotations, i.e. the RoPE form. Unitary relation operators provably capture symmetry (r o r = 1), antisymmetry, inversion (conjugate), and composition (r3 = r1 o r2) — the relational patterns REFA's tasks need — and are norm-preserving, so keys stay L2-normalized by construction and the recurrent state map has condition number 1. On the small-D question: Chami et al. (ACL 2020) show rotation+reflection operators (in hyperbolic space for hierarchical relations) at d=32 outperform Euclidean baselines that need 10x the dimensions, +6.1% MRR in low-dimensional regimes — direct evidence that D=64 is enough for typed relational addressing if the operator is a rotation, not an additive offset.

**Math.** RotatE: t_i = h_i r_i with r_i = e^{i phi_i}; score d_r(h,t) = -|| h o r - t ||; composition = phase addition phi_3 = phi_1 + phi_2; inverse relation = conjugate r^-1 = r_bar; symmetric iff phi_i in {0, pi}. ComplEx: phi(h,r,t) = Re( sum_k h_k r_k conj(t_k) ). HolE: sigma( r^T (h * t) ), (h * t) = F^{-1}( conj(F(h)) . F(t) ); spectral-domain HolE = ComplEx with conjugate-symmetry constraint (Hayashi-Shimbo). Real form of RotatE binding: k = R_r e_s, R_r = blockdiag( [[cos phi_j, -sin phi_j],[sin phi_j, cos phi_j]] ), j = 1..D/2. Params per relation at D=64: 32 phases. AttH: rotations/reflections + attention in hyperbolic space; d=32 matches d~500 Euclidean on WN18RR/FB15k-237.

**REFA application.** Replace address = normalize(W_a . identity) with k = R_{rel} e_s: per-relation learned 32-phase rotation of the subject key (num_relations x 32 extra params). Inverse queries use R_{rel}^T (free); multi-hop queries compose by adding phase vectors BEFORE the memory read (one read instead of chained reads). Norm preservation lets you drop the normalize call and stabilizes the delta-rule erase term. This is the single cheapest upgrade with field-tested evidence at REFA's exact dimension range.

Sources:
- Sun et al., RotatE, ICLR 2019, arXiv:1902.10197 — <https://arxiv.org/abs/1902.10197>
- Trouillon et al., ComplEx, ICML 2016, arXiv:1606.06357 — <https://arxiv.org/abs/1606.06357>
- Hayashi, Shimbo, On the Equivalence of Holographic and Complex Embeddings, ACL 2017 — <https://aclanthology.org/P17-2088/>
- Chami et al., Low-Dimensional Hyperbolic Knowledge Graph Embeddings, ACL 2020, arXiv:2005.00545 — <https://aclanthology.org/2020.acl-main.617/>

### Tensor-product (bilinear) keys and third-order fast-weight memories: the exact-binding alternative
*status: convergent*

Smolensky's tensor-product representation is the zero-crosstalk limit of binding: role-filler pairs stored as outer products, unbound exactly with dual vectors when roles are linearly independent — HRR is its lossy circulant compression. The modern instantiation is Schlag et al.'s Fast Weight Memory (ICLR 2021): an LSTM writes facts into a THIRD-ORDER fast-weight tensor whose key is the tensor product of two entity/component vectors, i.e. exactly a (subject (x) relation) -> object store, updated with write/erase gates like REFA's delta rule; multiple recursive reads perform transitive/associative inference, with strong results on compositional language reasoning (catbAbI) and POMDPs. Irie & Schmidhuber's 2025 review connects this whole family (delta rule, DeltaProduct/Householder state tracking) to neurobiology. For REFA the message is: a bilinear key k(s,r) = f(e_s (x) e_r) strictly dominates the additive key — (A,r,B) vs (B,r,A) confusions vanish because role order is structural, and subject-relation crosstalk is exactly zero rather than mitigated by LayerNorm.

**Math.** TPR: T = sum_i r_i (x) f_i in R^{d_r x d_f}; unbind f_i = T^T u_i where {u_i} is the dual basis (U = R(R^T R)^{-1}); exact iff roles linearly independent (capacity = rank <= d_r), crosstalk otherwise sum_{j!=i}(u_i . r_j) f_j. FWM: F_t = lambda F_{t-1} + beta_t vec(k1_t (x) k2_t) v_t^T, F in R^{d^2 x d}; read v_hat = F^T vec(k1' (x) k2'), N_r recursive reads for multi-hop. Memory cost for REFA-scale: low-rank heads e' in R^16 give bilinear key vec(e'_s e'_r^T) in R^256; a full per-bank third-order store at d=64 is 64^2 x 64 = 262k floats (~1 MB fp32) x 8 banks x 2 stances = ~17 MB — fits 6GB with room for batching.

**REFA application.** Minimal change: k = W_b vec(e'_s e'_r^T), e' = 16-dim projections, W_b: 256 -> R. This keeps every downstream line of REFAMicro identical (delta-rule erase works unchanged on any key) while removing the additive-key ambiguities. If revision tasks still show interference, promote one or two banks to full FWM third-order tensors — the exact store — and keep the rest low-rank.

Sources:
- Smolensky, Tensor product variable binding, Artificial Intelligence 46:159-216 (1990) — <https://www.sciencedirect.com/science/article/abs/pii/000437029090007M>
- Schlag, Munkhdalai, Schmidhuber, Learning Associative Inference Using Fast Weight Memory, ICLR 2021 — <https://openreview.net/forum?id=TuK6agbdt27>
- Irie et al., Fast weight programming and linear transformers: from machine learning to neurobiology, arXiv:2508.08435 (2025) — <https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.08435>

### Prefabricated scaffolds and random barcodes: keys should be (partly) fixed, not learned ⚑
*status: convergent*

Two 2024-2025 neuroscience results argue REFA's learned address projection is the wrong prior. (1) Vector-HaSH (Chandra, Sharma, Chaudhuri, Fiete, Nature 2025): hippocampal memory = content heteroassociated onto a PREFABRICATED, content-independent scaffold of fixed points built from grid-cell modules and FIXED RANDOM projections; because dynamics stabilize the scaffold rather than the content, it avoids the Hopfield memory cliff (catastrophic collapse at ~0.14N) and instead trades recall detail gracefully against item count, while supporting spatial, episodic and general associative memory in one circuit. (2) Chettih et al. (Cell 2024): chickadee hippocampus stamps each food-cache episode with a sparse, high-dimensional BARCODE uncorrelated across caches even at nearby sites with similar place tuning; the barcode reactivates at retrieval — i.e. the brain uses random episode-unique keys ALONGSIDE structured similarity keys. An eLife 2024/2025 model shows recurrent networks generate such barcodes for efficient memory binding. Gershman-Fiete-Irie frame both as key-value separation: keys are optimized for discriminability (random = maximally separable), values for fidelity.

**Math.** Vector-HaSH: grid state g in prod_i modules (number of joint phases prod_i K_i, exponential in module count at linear neuron cost); place p = kWTA(W_pg g) with W_pg fixed random; return projections trained so (g,p) pairs are fixed points; content s attached by Hebbian heteroassociation W_ps += p s^T, W_sp += s p^T. Scaffold fixed-point count is independent of stored content; recall error grows smoothly with item number (no cliff). Barcodes: pattern-separated codes with corr ~ 0 between caches; retrieval = barcode reactivation. Random-key capacity: N(0, 1/D) keys are near-orthogonal with overlap ~ 1/sqrt(D) ~ 0.125 at D=64, giving ~D usable slots per matrix memory.

**REFA application.** (a) Freeze the address projection (or a large slice of it) as random — learned addressing can collapse key diversity early in training; fixed random keys guarantee pattern separation, matching the delta-rule's need for near-orthogonal keys. (b) Composite keys: k = normalize(alpha k_struct(s,r) + sqrt(1-alpha^2) b_e) with b_e a fixed random barcode per provenance/source; revision (same fact, new evidence) targets the shared structural component while conflicting sources stay linearly separable on the barcode axis — exactly the roadmap's provenance-conflict task.

Sources:
- Chandra, Sharma, Chaudhuri, Fiete, Episodic and associative memory from spatial scaffolds in the hippocampus, Nature 638:739-751 (2025) — <https://www.nature.com/articles/s41586-024-08392-y>
- Chettih, Mackevicius, Hale, Aronov, Barcoding of episodic memories in the hippocampus of a food-caching bird, Cell 187 (2024) — <https://www.cell.com/cell/fulltext/S0092-8674(24)00235-6>
- Barcode activity in a recurrent network model of the hippocampus enables efficient memory binding, eLife reviewed preprint 103512 — <https://elifesciences.org/reviewed-preprints/103512>

### Residue-number phasor binding with modular attractor cleanup (Kymn et al., NeurIPS 2024)
*status: convergent*

Kymn et al. built the explicit bridge between VSA binding and the hippocampal-entorhinal circuit: spatial position is encoded in a residue number system where each residue (x mod m_f, coprime moduli) is a complex phasor vector, and the full position code is their similarity-preserving Hadamard (conjunctive binding) product. Self-consistency between the whole and its residues is enforced by a modular attractor network whose modules correspond to entorhinal grid modules; binding a context vector onto position codes yields hippocampal place-cell-like remapping conjunctions. The payoff is a capacity theorem for structural codes: the number of distinct addressable states is the PRODUCT of the moduli — exponential in the number of modules — at roughly linear dimension cost, with attractor dynamics providing robust cleanup from noisy/partial cues. This is the concrete math for 'structural-code routing': few small modules, multiplicatively composed, denoised per-module.

**Math.** Residue encoding by fractional phasor powers: g_f(x) = z_f^{x mod m_f} where z_f in C^D has unit-modulus components (phases 2 pi k / m_f); position code p(x) = g_1(x) . g_2(x) . ... . g_F(x) (Hadamard). Distinct states = prod_f m_f (coprime; e.g. m = 5,7,11 -> 385 states from 3 modules). Similarity preserved: <p(x), p(x')> falls off with |x - x'|. Cleanup: per-module attractor projects each g_f onto its m_f legal phase states (cost O(m_f D)), then re-binds; context binding c . p(x) gives remapped conjunctive codes. Factorization of a queried code = resonator-style alternating cleanup across modules.

**REFA application.** Use an RNS phase code as the temporal/ordinal part of REFA's key: bind event position as 3 coprime phase modules into k, so 'later evidence supersedes earlier' can be resolved by relative phase comparison rather than only by scalar decay lambda — a recency-addressable memory. Cleanup is cheap (m_f <= 11) and gives length extrapolation: positions beyond the training range still map to legal module states.

Sources:
- Kymn et al., Binding in hippocampal-entorhinal circuits enables compositionality in cognitive maps, NeurIPS 2024, arXiv:2406.18808 — <https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.18808>
- NeurIPS 2024 proceedings page — <https://proceedings.neurips.cc/paper_files/paper/2024/hash/4526cfacdbca6b6e184568dac91bf070-Abstract-Conference.html>

### Successor-representation sketches give streaming multi-hop closure; grid cells as SR eigenvectors
*status: partial*

Stachenfeld et al.'s predictive-map result: place cells encode rows of the successor representation M (expected discounted future state occupancy) and grid cells are its low-frequency EIGENVECTORS (equivalently, eigenvectors of the graph Laplacian for translation-invariant dynamics) — a spectral structural basis learned from one-step transitions. The math REFA can steal is not the spatial story but the closure property: M = (I - gamma T)^{-1} turns locally observed one-hop transitions into globally correct multi-hop reachability, maintained ONLINE by a TD delta rule — the same computational shape as REFA's write rule. REFA currently stores atomic facts and has no mechanism to answer transitive queries (A r B, B r C => A rr C); a per-relation low-rank SR sketch is the streaming-compatible fix, and its eigenvectors offer a principled routing basis (banks as spectral bands of the relation graph rather than arbitrary softmax clusters). The relational (non-spatial, typed-edge) version of this inside a fast-weight memory has no published ML instantiation — a building site.

**Math.** M^pi = sum_{t>=0} gamma^t (T^pi)^t = (I - gamma T^pi)^{-1}; TD update on observing s -> s': M(s,.) <- M(s,.) + alpha [ e_{s'} + gamma M(s',.) - M(s,.) ] (O(K) per event, or O(RD) for a rank-R factored sketch on embeddings). Grid-like codes = top eigenvectors of M; revaluation V = M R is O(K) per reward/fact change. REFA form: per-relation sketch S_r ~ U_r V_r^T in R^{DxD}, rank 4-8, updated S_r <- S_r + alpha k_s (e_o + gamma S_r^T e_o - S_r^T k_s)^T; 2-hop support score = e_s^T S_r S_r e_c (or mixed relations S_{r1} S_{r2}).

**REFA application.** Add one rank-8 SR sketch per relation (num_relations x 8 x 64 x 2 ~ 8k floats per relation) updated by the TD rule each history event; expose e_s^T S_r e_c as an extra feature into the fusion/output MLP. This gives the model multi-hop evidence without chained reads, stays streaming and O(RD) per step, and directly extends the task battery toward composition — the capability the June-2026 holographic-KG study shows superposed stores fail at.

Sources:
- Stachenfeld, Botvinick, Gershman, The hippocampus as a predictive map, Nature Neuroscience 20:1643-1653 (2017) — <https://www.nature.com/articles/nn.4650>

### Thousand Brains / Monty: per-column evidence accumulation with lateral voting and an explicit no-match state
*status: partial*

The open-source Monty system (Thousand Brains Project) is the most complete engineering template for REFA's multi-bank evidential readout. Each 'learning module' (cortical column analog) stores features-at-poses in its own object-centric reference frame; recognition is per-column EVIDENCE accumulation over (object, pose) hypotheses updated by bounded increments as sensorimotor features arrive; columns communicate through a Cortical Messaging Protocol (pose + feature) and reach consensus by lateral VOTING — summing pose-transformed evidence over hypotheses — which sharply reduces steps-to-recognition; and the decision rule has an explicit 'no match' terminal state when no hypothesis clears threshold, i.e. abstention is architectural, not an afterthought. The papers (arXiv:2412.18354, arXiv:2507.04494) document rapid, robust few-shot learning and inference; the framework is running code (tbp.monty on GitHub) but carries little formal capacity theory — the mapping to a trained, calibrated three-class head is open.

**Math.** Per module i, hypothesis space H = {(object o, pose p)}: evidence e_i(o,p) += clip(match(feature_t | o,p), 0, w_max), with pose consistency enforced by the module's reference frame. Voting: e_i(o) += sum_{j in lateral(i)} vote_j(o) (pose-aligned, hypothesis-space union/intersection). Decide o* when max_o e - second_max > theta_margin; declare 'no match' when max_o e < theta_min; else keep sampling (active policy selects next observation). Convergence typically in few steps with tens of columns; voting is O(#neighbors x #hypotheses) per step.

**REFA application.** Restructure the output head as bank-local evidence + vote: each bank h emits a 3-vector l_h = f(q^T M_h^+, q^T M_h^-, ||route||) of support/oppose/unknown log-evidence; final logits = sum_h r_h l_h (router weights = votes). Unknown becomes a first-class hypothesis with its own threshold rather than a residual class, matching Monty's no-match state; per-bank l_h gives free interpretability (which bank drove the verdict) for the calibration/abstention roadmap items.

Sources:
- Clay, Leadholm, Ahmad, The Thousand Brains Project: A New Paradigm for Sensorimotor Intelligence, arXiv:2412.18354 (2024) — <https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.18354>
- Thousand-Brains Systems: Sensorimotor Intelligence for Rapid, Robust Learning and Inference, arXiv:2507.04494 (2025) — <https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.04494>
- tbp.monty open-source implementation — <https://github.com/thousandbrainsproject/tbp.monty>

### 2025-2026 replay findings: memories are compositional bindings, and replay builds them
*status: convergent*

Three recent results reframe replay as compositional computation rather than rote consolidation. Bakermans, Warren, Whittington, Behrens (Nat Neurosci 2025): if state spaces are composed from reusable structural primitives, hippocampal responses are compositional memories BINDING those primitives; agents then behave optimally in new environments with zero new learning, and replay both constructs and consolidates the bindings — verified by showing replay from newly discovered landmarks induces and strengthens remote firing fields. Schwartenbeck et al. (Cell 2023): generative replay in the hippocampal-prefrontal circuit assembles compositional solutions to novel problems (human MEG). New in 2026 (Nat Neurosci, vol 2026): human hippocampal ripples coordinate planning sequences and COMPOSITIONAL representations in neocortex — ripples trigger prefrontal representational shifts that embed relational configurations during online planning. Convergent claim: the binding operation (finding 1/4) is exercised offline/at-decision-time to RECOMBINE stored primitives, which is precisely the training signal a small streaming model can generate for itself for free.

**Math.** Formally lightweight: memory m = Bind(b_1..b_k) over primitive codes (walls, landmarks, rewards, relations); replay samples trajectories/bindings from the composed generative model; zero-shot policy/value follows from composition without weight updates (pi* inferred from composed map). Prediction tested: replay adjacent to new landmark b -> new remote field at Bind(b, g_remote); strengthening with repetition. No published capacity bound; the quantitative content is the verified induction of remote fields and ripple-locked neocortical representational shifts.

**REFA application.** Add a between-episode replay phase (free on 6GB, no new data): sample stored (k, v) pairs from banks, decode them (resonator or nearest-neighbor), recombine subjects/relations/objects into type-consistent pseudo-event sequences, and train router/classifier on them. Targets exactly REFA's weakest planned capabilities — compositional generalization and length extrapolation — and mirrors the Engram Loop's sleep phase but with a RELATIONAL-RECOMBINATION objective rather than plain distillation.

Sources:
- Bakermans, Warren, Whittington, Behrens, Constructing future behavior in the hippocampal formation through composition and replay, Nature Neuroscience 28:1061-1072 (2025) — <https://www.nature.com/articles/s41593-025-01908-3>
- Schwartenbeck et al., Generative replay underlies compositional inference in the hippocampal-prefrontal circuit, Cell 186 (2023) — <https://www.cell.com/cell/fulltext/S0092-8674(23)01025-5>
- Human hippocampal ripples coordinate planning sequences and compositional representations in neocortex, Nature Neuroscience (2026) — <https://www.nature.com/articles/s41593-026-02291-3>

### Mechanistic failure study (Jun 2026): holographic multi-hop failure is single-hop retrieval capacity, not binding algebra
*status: partial*

A June 2026 mechanistic study stress-tested HRR/FHRR memories on zero-shot compositional (multi-hop) KG reasoning and localized WHERE they fail. Result: the bind-unbind algebra and cleanup are NOT the bottleneck. A hop-1 probe shows the memory recovers the correct intermediate entity with high fidelity (MRR 0.896 +/- 0.002 for HRR), yet composition still fails even with a verified-correct intermediate; posing the ground-truth second-hop fact as a standalone atomic query — bypassing composition entirely — recovers it at only 0.26-0.48x average atomic accuracy, uniformly across relation fan-out. So facts that compositional chains pass through are intrinsically harder for the superposed memory to retrieve: a capacity/interference effect already present at a single hop. Secondary finding: FHRR's softmax cleanup is not phase-equivariant, compounding errors only when hop-1 already erred. For REFA this is both a warning (consistent with finding 2's M ~ D/(2 ln K): superposed stores at small D fail on exactly the load-bearing facts) and a ready-made diagnostic protocol.

**Math.** Chain reliability is bounded by the weakest atomic link, not by algebra depth: P(chain correct) ~ prod_h P(atomic retrieval of hop-h fact), and the study shows P(atomic | chain-interior fact) << P(atomic | average fact) (0.26-0.48x), because chain-interior facts sit in high-interference regions of the superposition. Combined with SNR theory: per-fact retrieval SNR ~ sqrt(D / M_eff) where M_eff is local load — heterogeneous across facts, so mean accuracy hides the tail that kills composition. Reported: hop-1 MRR 0.896 +/- 0.002 (HRR); FHRR softmax cleanup not phase-equivariant (secondary effect).

**REFA application.** Adopt the probe protocol in REFA's eval battery: for every multi-hop test item, separately measure atomic retrieval of each chain-interior fact; report tail (worst-link) accuracy, not just mean. Architecturally: keep high-fanout facts (subjects/objects appearing in many chains) in non-superposed matrix slots and let the write gate beta scale with estimated fact load; if bound-key traces are added (finding 3 audit trace), treat them as diagnostic, not the primary store.

Sources:
- Holographic Memory for Zero-Shot Compositional Reasoning in Knowledge Graphs: A Mechanistic Study of Where and Why It Fails, arXiv:2606.24948 (2026) — <https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.24948>

---

## Neuromodulatory gating and three-factor learning
`gating-neuromod`

The neuromodulation literature converges on one grammar: memory and learning are controlled by multiplicative gates (on writes, reads, and recurrent state) plus a factorized plasticity rule — a local, synapse-specific eligibility trace multiplied by a global, delayed scalar modulator. That grammar maps almost one-to-one onto REFA's existing pieces (write gate beta, decay lambda, softmax router, GRU workspace) but REFA currently collapses all of it into instantaneous, feedforward, always-positive gates. Four upgrades have strong warrant: (1) a learning-rate gate — hold delta-rule writes in a decaying eligibility buffer and commit them only when a global modulator (query-time error/surprise) arrives (Fremaux-Gerstner three-factor rule, e-prop), which directly solves the roadmap's delayed-evidence task; (2) a signed, workspace-conditioned write gate (Backpropamine shows SGD trains exactly this at REFA scale); (3) an uncertainty-typed read/reset gate implementing Yu-Dayan's ACh (expected uncertainty down-weights recalled evidence) vs NE (unexpected uncertainty triggers bank decay/reset); and (4) a discrete RL-trained workspace gate in the PBWM striatal go/no-go style protecting the GRU hypothesis state from distractors. Two further transfers are distinctive rather than load-bearing: distributional RPE (Dabney 2020; Lowet 2025's D1/D2 opponency is a direct biological warrant for REFA's M+/M- stance split) suggests per-bank asymmetric write rates yielding an expectile code over evidence for calibrated abstention; and 2025 thalamocortical work (Halassa's multiplicative-coupling model, Hummos' Thalamus) supports a small inferred context latent multiplicatively modulating the router. Astrocyte results (Kozachkov-Slotine-Krotov) add a slow second timescale of multiplicative gain with proven capacity benefits, and e-prop/RTRL removes the BPTT memory bottleneck for streaming training on 6GB.

**Verification** of load-bearing claims:

- **CONFIRMED** — Three-factor rule: eligibility trace x global modulator is the canonical gated-plasticity form
  - Core claim verified. Fremaux & Gerstner 2016 (Front. Neural Circuits 9:85, PMID 26834568) is the standing formalization of three-factor rules exactly as described (synapse-local eligibility trace, global modulator commits the update), and Gerstner et al. 2018 (Front. Neural Circuits 12:53) does review direct experimental eligibility-trace support (Yagishita et al. 2014: dopamine effective only ~0.3-2s after glutamate uncaging on striatal spines, so '~1s window' is fair; He et al. 2015 cortical traces converted by NE/5-HT). CORRECTIONS: (1) the third source, arXiv:2504.05341, exists and is verified as 'Three-Factor Learning in Spiking Neural Networks: An Overview of Methods and Trends from a Machine Learning Perspective' (Mazurek, Caputa, Argasinski, Wielgosz, Apr 2025) — but I could not verify publication in Patterns; cite it as an arXiv preprint, not 'a 2025 Patterns review'. (2) Its abstract covers SNN/neuromorphic methods and 'research gaps' generally; the specific assertion that it identifies fast-weight/test-time-training write steps as an unexploited application is NOT evidenced by the abstract and reads as the research agent's own (reasonable) inference — attribute that argument to the synthesis, not the paper. (3) Math: the generic eligibility/commit ODEs and one-scalar-per-synapse cost are correct, and 2*8*16*64 = 16,384 floats is correct arithmetic. The R-max correspondence is stated loosely: strict unbiased gradient ascent (Xie-Seung/R-max) requires the Hebbian factor to use the centered postsynaptic term (post minus its expectation), not plain pre*post; plain R-STDP needs mean-subtracted reward (RPE) to avoid bias, which is what M = delta supplies. The O(d/tau_e) bias scaling is a plausible heuristic, not a result stated in these sources.
- **CONFIRMED** — Distributional RPE via asymmetric learning rates; D1/D2 opponency biologically mirrors REFA's support/oppose split
  - Both sources verified. Dabney et al. 2020, Nature 577:671-675, is correctly characterized: diverse asymmetric scaling of positive vs negative RPEs across dopamine neurons, convergence to distinct expectiles, and decoding of the full reward distribution from the population. Lowet et al. 2025 'An opponent striatal circuit for distributional reinforcement learning' verified as Nature 639:717-726 (independent citation records; the authors' own code repo alowet/distributionalRL links the exact cited DOI s41586-024-08488-5). The Dryad data description confirms the claimed design: classical conditioning over probability distributions of water amounts, anterior striatum Neuropixels, D1- vs D2-MSN two-photon imaging, and 6-OHDA lesioning of dopamine axons to lateral NAc shell before training — supporting the D1-optimistic/D2-pessimistic opponent coding and the dopamine-ablation manipulation as described; the precise phrasing 'destroys the distributional code while sparing mean value' is consistent with the paper but I could not re-read the abstract to check the 'sparing mean' clause verbatim. Math checked: the expectile fixed point tau_i = alpha_i^+/(alpha_i^+ + alpha_i^-) is exactly Dabney et al.'s asymmetry statistic; sign-thresholded f giving quantiles is correct; P-N/P+N opponent readout matches Lowet's framing. The 'zero extra memory' claim for per-bank asymmetric rates is correct (only 2H scalars of hyperparameters/learned scalars).
- **CONFIRMED** — Yu-Dayan: ACh = expected uncertainty (read-trust gate), NE = unexpected uncertainty (reset/interrupt gate)
  - Direct abstract fetches were blocked by the sandbox network, but these are canonical, unambiguous papers and the characterization matches them. Yu & Dayan 2005, Neuron 46(4):681-692, is correctly cited and correctly summarized: ACh reports expected uncertainty (estimated invalidity of known cues within a context) and proportionally suppresses reliance on top-down/prior predictions; NE reports unexpected uncertainty (evidence of context change) and triggers model revision; the stated inference math (posterior mixes prior with a flat distribution weighted by ACh level, NE raised when observation likelihood under the current model falls below a threshold calibrated to expected uncertainty) matches their approximate-inference algorithm. Dayan & Yu 2006 'neural interrupt' (Network 17:335-350) exists as described. Aston-Jones & Cohen 2005, Annu Rev Neurosci 28:403-450 (PMID 16022602), is the adaptive gain theory paper and is correctly summarized (phasic LC-NE multiplicatively modulates response gain at task-relevant moments). One caveat for the synthesis: the Kalman-gain learning-rate formula eta_t = sigma_t^2/(sigma_t^2+sigma_o^2) is the standard uncertainty-driven learning-rate result from the adjacent literature (e.g. Dayan-Kakade-Montague; Behrens 2007), not an equation appearing in Yu & Dayan 2005 itself. The claim that no standard ML module implements the two-channel split is an assessment, not a sourced fact.
- **CONFIRMED** — PBWM: RL-trained discrete striatal go/no-go gating of PFC stripes — the workspace gate
  - O'Reilly & Frank 2006 'Making working memory work', Neural Computation 18(2):283-328, is correctly cited and the core characterization is accurate: independently gated PFC stripes, striatal Go(D1)/NoGo(D2) competition disinhibiting thalamus to open a stripe's update gate, robust maintenance otherwise, and gate policies learned by dopamine-based RL (the PVLV critic) rather than hand-designed; the paper does explicitly position itself against LSTM-style gradient-learned gating. Three CORRECTIONS: (1) the stated math writes the modulator as a TD error delta = r + V_hat_{t+1} - V_hat_t and labels it PVLV — PVLV is explicitly O'Reilly & Frank's NON-TD alternative critic (separate primary-value and learned-value systems); keep 'DA-modulated RL via PVLV' but do not present the TD-delta equation as PVLV's. (2) g_i in {0,1} is an idealization: PBWM gating emerges from neural dynamics and is only approximately discrete. (3) The maintenance-vs-output-gating distinction as two separate learned gates is developed mainly in later PBWM work (Hazy, Frank & O'Reilly 2006/2007 and successors), so attributing it 'crucially' to the 2006 paper overstates that paper slightly. The 2^S-configurations-with-O(S)-controllers argument is a correct combinatorial statement made in the PBWM literature. The second source is a textbook chapter (secondary; URL unverifiable from this sandbox) — harmless but not load-bearing.
- **CONFIRMED** — Backpropamine: signed, network-generated, retroactive plasticity modulation is trainable by SGD at small scale
  - Verified directly: the uber-research/backpropamine repo README confirms the paper 'Backpropamine: training self-modifying neural networks with differentiable neuromodulated plasticity' (Miconi et al., ICLR 2019, OpenReview r1lrAiA5Ym, Uber AI Labs), and third-party indices confirm arXiv:2002.10585 is the correct arXiv ID (posted 2020 despite the ICLR 2019 venue — the claim's citation is fine). The repo confirms the claimed task suite: grid-maze exploration RL, a cue-reward associative learning task ('sr': identify an unpredictable target stimulus from reward feedback alone), and language modeling via neuromodulated-plastic AWD-LSTM. The equations match the paper: simple variant H(t+1)=Clip(H(t)+M(t)x_i y_j); retroactive variant with exponential eligibility trace E(t+1)=(1-eta)E(t)+eta*x_i y_j (the claim's 1/tau parameterization is equivalent) committed by a signed network-generated M(t); everything trained end-to-end by BPTT/A2C. Two minor CORRECTIONS: (1) 'gains at exactly REFA's scale (tiny RNNs)' is true for the RL experiments (~100-200-unit RNNs) but the language-modeling experiment used AWD-LSTM (~20M+ params), so drop 'exactly' for the LM result, whose reported gain was modest though statistically significant; (2) 'first differentiable three-factor rule in ANNs' is the paper's own positioning (extending Miconi 2018 differentiable plasticity with neuromodulation) and is fair, but is a priority claim, not an independently verified fact.

### Three-factor rule: eligibility trace x global modulator is the canonical gated-plasticity form ⚑
*status: convergent*

Fremaux-Gerstner (2016) formalized neoHebbian three-factor learning: a synapse-local eligibility trace set by pre/post coincidence decays over seconds, and weight change is committed only when a third factor (dopamine reward, novelty, surprise) arrives — turning Hebbian correlation into credit assignment over behavioral delays. Gerstner et al. (2018) review direct experimental confirmation of synaptic eligibility traces (Yagishita striatal spines ~1s dopamine window; He et al. cortical traces). A 2025 Patterns review (arXiv:2504.05341) surveys ML-side implementations and finds the pattern is now standard in SNN/neuromorphic learning (R-STDP, e-prop, MSTDPET) but barely exploited in mainstream fast-weight/test-time-training architectures — where the write step is exactly a plasticity rule that could be eligibility-buffered. This is the single most direct answer to 'how does the brain gate learning': plasticity is proposed everywhere, committed selectively and retroactively.

**Math.** Eligibility: de_ij/dt = -e_ij/tau_e + f(pre_i, post_j) (f = Hebbian or STDP window). Commit: dw_ij/dt = eta * M(t) * e_ij(t), with M(t) the global modulator (e.g., RPE delta_t = r_t + gamma V_{t+1} - V_t). Theory: with M = delta and f = pre*post, the expected update equals the policy gradient of expected reward (R-max/R-STDP correspondence), so the rule is unbiased stochastic gradient ascent on reward. tau_e sets the credit-assignment horizon; delays d with d << tau_e incur bias O(d/tau_e). Memory cost: one extra scalar per synapse.

**REFA application.** Learning-rate gate (top recommendation): REFA's delta-rule write commits instantly. Instead accumulate the proposed update E_h <- rho*E_h + r_h(k v^T - k k^T M_h) as an eligibility buffer (one extra RxD matrix per bank per stance: 2*8*16*64 = 16k floats, trivial on 6GB) and commit M_h <- lambda M_h + beta * m_t * E_h only when a modulator m_t fires (query-time error, surprise, or end-of-episode reward). Directly implements the roadmap's delayed-evidence task and lets one global scalar retroactively gate all banks.

Sources:
- Fremaux & Gerstner (2016) Neuromodulated STDP and theory of three-factor learning rules, Front. Neural Circuits 9:85 — <https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/26834568/>
- Gerstner et al. (2018) Eligibility traces and plasticity on behavioral time scales, Front. Neural Circuits 12:53 — <https://www.researchgate.net/publication/322537506_Eligibility_Traces_and_Plasticity_on_Behavioral_Time_Scales_Experimental_Support_of_NeoHebbian_Three-Factor_Learning_Rules>
- Three-factor learning in SNNs: overview from an ML perspective, Patterns (2025), arXiv:2504.05341 — <https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.05341>

### Distributional RPE via asymmetric learning rates; D1/D2 opponency biologically mirrors REFA's support/oppose split ⚑
*status: convergent*

Dabney et al. (Nature 2020) showed VTA dopamine neurons carry a distributional value code: individual neurons have diverse asymmetric scaling of positive vs negative RPEs, so each converges to a different expectile of the reward distribution; the full reward distribution can be decoded from the population. Lowet et al. (Nature 2025) extended this to striatum: D1 MSNs preferentially encode the upper tail and D2 MSNs the lower tail of the reward distribution, and chronic dopamine-input ablation destroys the distributional code while sparing mean value — an opponent two-population implementation of distributional RL. This is a gating result about learning rates: the brain diversifies plasticity gain by sign of the error, and it maintains two opposing evidence stores whose asymmetric updates jointly represent a distribution rather than a point estimate.

**Math.** Update: z_i <- z_i + alpha_i^+ * delta_i if delta_i > 0, else z_i + alpha_i^- * delta_i, with delta_i = r + gamma*sample(Z') - z_i. Fixed point: z_i converges to the tau_i-expectile of the return distribution, tau_i = alpha_i^+ / (alpha_i^+ + alpha_i^-). With f(delta)=sign(delta), quantiles instead. A population spanning tau in (0,1) encodes the full distribution; spread across expectiles = epistemic+aleatoric width usable for risk/abstention. Opponent form (Lowet): P population (alpha^+ dominant, D1) and N population (alpha^- dominant, D2); decision statistic combines P - N (mean-like) and P + N (uncertainty-like).

**REFA application.** REFA already has the opponent pair (M+, M-); Lowet 2025 is its direct biological warrant. Upgrade: give each of the H=8 banks a distinct asymmetry pair (beta_h^+, beta_h^-) spanning tau_h in (0.1..0.9) so banks encode expectiles of evidence strength rather than 8 redundant copies. The classifier then reads an evidence distribution: output 'unknown' when the expectile spread straddles zero (upper expectile supports, lower opposes). Gives a trained, distribution-based abstention signal at zero extra memory.

Sources:
- Dabney et al. (2020) A distributional code for value in dopamine-based reinforcement learning, Nature 577:671-675 — <https://www.nature.com/articles/s41586-019-1924-6>
- Lowet et al. (2025) An opponent striatal circuit for distributional reinforcement learning, Nature 639:717-726 — <https://www.nature.com/articles/s41586-024-08488-5>

### Yu-Dayan: ACh = expected uncertainty (read-trust gate), NE = unexpected uncertainty (reset/interrupt gate) ⚑
*status: partial*

Yu & Dayan (Neuron 2005) gave the standing mathematical account of two uncertainty-typed control signals, distinct from the ACh encode/retrieve dial already in the Engram ledger. Acetylcholine reports EXPECTED uncertainty — the known unreliability of predictive relations within the current context — and should suppress reliance on top-down/stored predictions in proportion to it (gating reads). Norepinephrine reports UNEXPECTED uncertainty — evidence that the context itself changed — and should trigger model reset and a transient learning-rate surge (Dayan-Yu 2006 'neural interrupt'). Aston-Jones & Cohen (2005) complement this with adaptive gain theory: phasic LC-NE globally multiplies neural response gain, sharpening winner-take-all decisions at task-relevant moments. Together they define a two-channel gate: continuous down-weighting of memory reads by tracked unreliability, plus discrete reset when mismatch exceeds what tracked unreliability predicts. No standard ML module implements this split; fragments exist as adaptive learning rates and change-point detection.

**Math.** Expected uncertainty a_t ~ 1 - gamma_hat_t (estimated cue validity); inference mixes prior vs likelihood as p(target) ∝ (1-a_t)*prior + a_t*flat, i.e., read weight on stored prediction ∝ (1-a_t). Unexpected uncertainty n_t ∝ P(context switch | o_1:t), approximated by thresholding the model-likelihood: raise NE when P(o_t | current model) < threshold given a_t; on NE event, reset posterior to uniform. Learning rate follows total uncertainty, Kalman-gain form eta_t = sigma_t^2/(sigma_t^2 + sigma_o^2) — high after resets, decaying with stability. Gain modulation (LC): f(x) = 1/(1+e^{-g*x}), phasic NE transiently raises g.

**REFA application.** Read gate + reset gate: per bank, compute mismatch m_h,t = ||k^T M_h - v||^2 at write time; EMA gives expected uncertainty eps_h (ACh analog). Scale read contribution by 1/(1+eps_h) — distrust chronically unreliable banks. When m_h,t >> eps_h (unexpected), fire an NE event: transiently boost that bank's decay (lambda_h down, partial erase) and raise write gain beta — fast revision when a source flips. Costs H scalars of state; targets the revision, conflict, and distractor tasks.

Sources:
- Yu & Dayan (2005) Uncertainty, neuromodulation, and attention, Neuron 46:681-692 — <https://www.sciencedirect.com/science/article/pii/S0896627305003624>
- Aston-Jones & Cohen (2005) An integrative theory of locus coeruleus-norepinephrine function: adaptive gain, Annu Rev Neurosci 28:403-450 — <https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/16022602/>

### PBWM: RL-trained discrete striatal go/no-go gating of PFC stripes — the workspace gate ⚑
*status: convergent*

O'Reilly & Frank (Neural Computation 2006) built the canonical model of basal-ganglia gating of working memory: PFC is divided into independently updatable 'stripes'; striatal Go (D1) and NoGo (D2) populations compete, and a Go win disinhibits thalamus, opening that stripe's gate so it loads new content; otherwise the stripe robustly maintains. Crucially, gate policies are LEARNED by dopamine-modulated RL (PVLV critic), solving 'when is this input worth storing?' without a homunculus; maintenance gating and output gating are separate decisions. Modern gated RNNs (LSTM/GRU) are the acknowledged ML descendant, but they differ in three ways PBWM argues matter: PBWM gates are (near-)discrete, per-stripe (content is chunked), and trained by a reward signal decoupled from the content pathway rather than by the same end-to-end gradient. This is the direct blueprint for gating REFA's GRU workspace updates.

**Math.** Stripe update: h_i^{t+1} = g_i^t * u_i^t + (1 - g_i^t) * h_i^t with g_i in {0,1}; g_i = [Go_i - NoGo_i > theta] via BG->thalamus disinhibition. Go/NoGo weights trained by DA RPE: dw ∝ delta * x * y with delta = r + V_hat_{t+1} - V_hat_t (PVLV); D1 (Go) potentiates on delta>0, D2 (NoGo) on delta<0. Independent gates over S stripes give 2^S update configurations with O(S) controllers — combinatorial WM control at linear cost. Output gating is a second gate on read-out, separating 'maintained' from 'currently in use'.

**REFA application.** Workspace gate: REFA's GRUCell updates on every event, so distractors erode the hypothesis state. Add g_t = discrete(sigma(w_g.[x_t, recalled+, recalled-]/T)) (Gumbel/straight-through) so workspace = g_t*GRU(fused, ws) + (1-g_t)*ws; optionally split the 64-d workspace into 4 stripes with separate gates. Train g via task loss plus an RL term rewarding abstention accuracy. Also add an output gate deciding whether workspace or raw recall drives the classifier at query time.

Sources:
- O'Reilly & Frank (2006) Making working memory work: a computational model of learning in the prefrontal cortex and basal ganglia, Neural Computation 18:283-328 — <https://cseweb.ucsd.edu//~gary/PAPER-SUGGESTIONS/OReillyFrank06_pbwm-neural-comp-2006.pdf>
- PBWM overview, Computational Cognitive Neuroscience (O'Reilly & Munakata) — <https://med.libretexts.org/Bookshelves/Pharmacology_and_Neuroscience/Computational_Cognitive_Neuroscience_3e_(O'Reilly_and_Munakata)/10:_Executive_Function/10.03:_The_PBWM_Computational_Model>

### Backpropamine: signed, network-generated, retroactive plasticity modulation is trainable by SGD at small scale ⚑
*status: convergent*

Miconi et al. (ICLR 2019) showed that a recurrent network can GENERATE its own neuromodulatory signal M(t) that gates Hebbian fast-weight updates, with everything trained end-to-end by ordinary gradient descent — the first differentiable three-factor rule in ANNs. Two variants: 'simple' (M(t) multiplies the instantaneous Hebbian product before it enters the fast weight) and 'retroactive' (M(t) multiplies a decaying eligibility trace of past products, so a later modulator commits earlier candidate updates — dopamine-style retroactive gating). M(t) is signed, so the network can actively reverse recent learning. Gains were shown on RL (maze, cue-reward association) and language modeling at exactly REFA's scale (tiny RNNs). This is the closest existing ML precedent for upgrading REFA's write gate: beta is currently feedforward (a function of the current event only) and non-negative; Backpropamine demonstrates that a state-conditioned, signed, retroactive gate is optimizable without any new training machinery.

**Math.** Plastic weights: y_j = sigma(sum_i (w_ij + alpha_ij * H_ij(t)) x_i). Simple: H_ij(t+1) = Clip(H_ij(t) + M(t) * x_i y_j). Retroactive: E_ij(t+1) = (1 - 1/tau) E_ij(t) + (1/tau) x_i y_j; H_ij(t+1) = Clip(H_ij(t) + M(t) * E_ij(t)), with M(t) = tanh(w_M . h_t) a signed scalar (or per-neuron vector) computed by the network itself. All of {w, alpha, w_M, tau} trained by BPTT/A2C. Clip keeps fast weights bounded (stability); signed M performs both potentiation and directed erasure.

**REFA application.** Write gate upgrade: replace beta_t = sigmoid(w.x_t) with a signed, workspace-conditioned modulator m_t = tanh(w_m.[workspace_t, x_t, recalled]) that multiplies both the immediate write and the eligibility commit (finding 1). The workspace — which has integrated the evidence stream — can then veto or reverse writes that contradict accumulated context, which pure event-level gating cannot. Proven trainable at 10k-1M param scale; zero new infrastructure.

Sources:
- Miconi, Rawal, Clune, Stanley (2019) Backpropamine: training self-modifying neural networks with differentiable neuromodulated plasticity, ICLR 2019, arXiv:2002.10585 — <https://arxiv.org/abs/2002.10585>
- Uber Research reference implementation — <https://github.com/uber-research/backpropamine>

### e-prop / forward eligibility: O(1)-memory online credit assignment replaces BPTT for the fast-weight path
*status: convergent*

Bellec et al. (Nature Communications 2020) proved that the BPTT gradient for recurrent networks factorizes into a forward-propagated, synapse-local eligibility trace times an instantaneous learning signal, and that dropping the (biologically impossible) through-network temporal terms still yields near-BPTT performance (TIMIT, Atari) — including a reward-based variant where the learning signal is a global RPE, i.e., a literal three-factor rule derived from gradient theory. The practical content for REFA: memory for training becomes independent of sequence length, enabling online/streaming learning. Moreover, for REFA specifically the approximation can be made EXACT on the memory pathway: because the fast-weight recursion M_t = lambda M_{t-1} + (write terms) is linear in M, the sensitivity dM_t/dtheta obeys the same cheap linear recursion (an RTRL that does not explode), which is the same structure as the repo's exact streamed linear rewrite. This is the mathematics of the 'learning-rate gate' at training time rather than inference time.

**Math.** Exact factorization: dE/dW_ji = sum_t L_j^t * e_ji^t with e_ji^t = psi_j^t * epsilon_ji^t; forward recursion epsilon_ji^t = (d h_j^t/d h_j^{t-1}) epsilon_ji^{t-1} + d h_j^t/d W_ji. Memory O(#params), vs BPTT O(#neurons * T). For a linear fast-weight state M_t = lambda M_{t-1} + beta_t Delta_t(theta): dM_t/dtheta = lambda dM_{t-1}/dtheta + d(beta_t Delta_t)/dtheta — exact forward gradient at the cost of one shadow tensor per parameter group. Reward-based e-prop: L_j^t -> global delta_t, recovering the three-factor rule as an approximation to policy gradient.

**REFA application.** Training-side learning-rate gate: REFA now backprops through a Python loop, storing B x H x R x D memory tensors per step — the length bottleneck on 6GB. Keep BPTT for the GRU workspace over a short window but train the write/decay/router parameters with the exact forward accumulator (linear-in-M RTRL). Memory becomes O(1) in T, unlocking the roadmap's length-extrapolation experiments (T in the thousands) on the RTX 2060.

Sources:
- Bellec et al. (2020) A solution to the learning dilemma for recurrent networks of spiking neurons, Nat Commun 11:3625 — <https://www.nature.com/articles/s41467-020-17236-y>

### Burst multiplexing: one channel for content, a second (burst) channel for signed error, in a single pass
*status: partial*

Payeur, Guerguiev, Zenke, Richards, Naud (Nature Neuroscience 2021) showed that if plasticity is gated by high-frequency bursts, and bursting is controlled by apical dendrites receiving top-down feedback, then a hierarchical circuit performs online, local, single-phase credit assignment: the EVENT rate of a neuron carries the feedforward signal while its BURST PROBABILITY carries the top-down error, multiplexed on the same wire and demultiplexed by short-term plasticity (facilitating synapses read bursts, depressing ones read events). Greedy et al. (NeurIPS 2022, BurstCCN) hardened this into an ML architecture that approximates backprop with a single phase using bursting + STP + dendrite-targeting interneurons. Guerguiev-Lillicrap-Richards (eLife 2017) is the ancestor: segregated apical compartments let feedback set local targets (plateau potentials) without corrupting the feedforward pass. The architectural lesson is orthogonal to the three-factor scalar: error feedback can be a per-unit SIGNED channel physically distinct from the content channel, delivered while inference continues.

**Math.** BDSP rule: dw_ij/dt = eta * [B_j(t) - P_bar_j(t) E_j(t)] * e_tilde_i(t), where E_j = event (spike/burst onset) train, B_j = burst train, P_bar_j = EMA of burst probability, e_tilde_i = presynaptic trace. Steady state: Delta w ∝ (p_j - p_bar_j) * pre, and p_j = f(apical top-down input) encodes error sign, so the rule tracks the top-down gradient sign. Multiplexing: information rate splits as event rate r (feedforward) and burst fraction p (feedback) — two signals per wire; STP demultiplexes.

**REFA application.** Dual-channel write: keep per-event content writes (event channel), and at query/feedback time broadcast the classification error as a signed 'burst' signal that multiplies the eligibility buffer of finding 1 — retroactively correcting the polarity/magnitude of recent writes in one pass, no separate backward phase through the memory. Concretely: on query, m_t = (y_true - y_hat) projected to a per-bank scalar; commit M_h += beta * m_t,h * E_h.

Sources:
- Payeur et al. (2021) Burst-dependent synaptic plasticity can coordinate learning in hierarchical circuits, Nat Neurosci 24:1010-1019 — <https://www.nature.com/articles/s41593-021-00857-x>
- Greedy et al. (2022) Single-phase deep learning in cortico-cortical networks (BurstCCN), NeurIPS 2022, arXiv:2206.11769 — <https://arxiv.org/abs/2206.11769>
- Guerguiev, Lillicrap, Richards (2017) Towards deep learning with segregated dendrites, eLife 6:e22901 — <https://elifesciences.org/articles/22901>

### Dendritic gated networks: fixed random gating + per-neuron convex online learning (cerebellar warrant)
*status: convergent*

Sezener et al. (bioRxiv 2021, DeepMind + neuroscientists) proposed Dendritic Gated Networks, the biological version of Veness et al.'s Gated Linear Networks: each neuron holds multiple weight vectors (dendritic branches); a FIXED random context function (biologically, interneurons silencing specific branches; in ML, random hyperplanes) selects which branch is active for the current input; only the active branch's linear weights learn, by a local convex (delta/log-loss) rule — no backprop at all. Consequences proved/shown: each neuron faces a convex online-learning problem (fast, with online guarantees), the network is highly resistant to catastrophic forgetting (different inputs use disjoint weight sets), and it is far more data-efficient than backprop nets on one-pass streams. The predicted branch-specific interneuron gating was observed in cerebellar Purkinje cells in vivo. This is a READ/ROUTE gate with the opposite philosophy to REFA's learned softmax router: gating carries no learned parameters, learning is confined to content weights.

**Math.** GLN neuron: p_out = sigma(w_{c(x)}^T logit(p_in)), context c(x) = [sign(v_1.x), ..., sign(v_K.x)] in {0,1}^K selecting one of 2^K weight vectors, v_k fixed random. Update: w_{c(x)} <- Proj(w_{c(x)} - eta (p_out - y) logit(p_in)) — convex log-loss per neuron, so online-convex-optimization regret bounds (O(sqrt(T))) apply per unit; no gradient flows between units. Forgetting resistance: two inputs interfere only if they share context cells, P ~ product of halfspace collision probabilities, decaying exponentially in K.

**REFA application.** Two uses: (a) streaming classifier head — replace the MLP output with a small DGN gated on workspace state for one-pass, forgetting-resistant, convex calibration of the 3-class evidential output (well-calibrated probabilities are what abstention needs); (b) a zero-parameter router baseline — route events to banks by K=3 fixed random hyperplanes over the relation embedding (2^3 = 8 banks), isolating whether REFA's learned router actually earns its parameters.

Sources:
- Sezener et al. (2021) A rapid and efficient learning rule for biological neural circuits (Dendritic Gated Networks), bioRxiv 2021.03.10.434756 — <https://www.biorxiv.org/content/10.1101/2021.03.10.434756v2>
- Veness et al., Gated Linear Networks (AAAI 2021), arXiv:1910.01526 — <https://arxiv.org/abs/1910.01526>
- DeepMind reference notebook — <https://github.com/google-deepmind/deepmind-research/blob/master/gated_linear_networks/colabs/dendritic_gated_network.ipynb>

### Thalamocortical routing 2023-2025: multiplicative context coupling and inference-time latent gating
*status: partial*

Recent modeling grounded in Halassa's mediodorsal-thalamus experiments converges on the thalamus as a low-dimensional multiplicative controller of cortical computation rather than a relay. Zhang et al. (bioRxiv July 2025; authors include Halassa) show that multiplicative RNN-FNN couplings — a thalamic loop imposing Hebbian-weight amplification onto cortical synaptic-neuronal coupling — enable context-dependent gating, rapid task switching, and faster learning, reproducing PFC-MD data in context-dependent decision making plus cortico-thalamo-cortical and EC-hippocampus variants. Hummos (ICLR 2023, 'Thalamus') distilled the algorithmic core: alternate gradient descent between network weights (slow) and a thalamus-like latent context vector updated at INFERENCE time; latent updates parse experience into discrete contexts, mitigate forgetting, and build disentangled task abstractions. Complementary 2023-2025 theory maps cortico-thalamic loops onto attention/precision-weighted gating. The shared math: context enters multiplicatively (gain on weights or logits), is low-dimensional, and can be inferred online instead of learned.

**Math.** Multiplicative coupling: effective weight W_eff = W ∘ (1 + U m_t) with m_t a low-dim thalamic state (rank-limited gain modulation); vs additive input, multiplicative m rescales the RNN Jacobian, switching dynamical regimes without weight change. Hummos: z_{t+1} = z_t - eta_z grad_z L(x_t; W, z) at inference; W updated only when z-inference plateaus — a two-timescale EM-like alternation. Capacity: with C contexts, additive nets need O(C) capacity in W; multiplicative low-rank gain reuses one W with a d_z-dim selector, d_z << dim(W).

**REFA application.** Router context gate: add a small latent z (dim ~8) updated online (Hummos-style few gradient steps on recent prediction error, matching the repo's TTT lineage) that multiplicatively modulates router logits: route = softmax((W_r x) ∘ (1 + U z)/tau). Handles provenance/regime switches (speaker A becomes unreliable) by re-aiming reads/writes across banks in a few steps without rewriting bank contents — a targeted fix for the provenance-conflict roadmap task.

Sources:
- Zhang, Altrabulsi, Xu, Wimmer, Halassa, Chen (2025) Multiplicative couplings facilitate rapid learning and information gating in recurrent neural networks, bioRxiv 2025.07.11.663676 — <https://www.biorxiv.org/content/10.1101/2025.07.11.663676v1>
- Hummos (2023) Thalamus: a brain-inspired algorithm for biologically-plausible continual learning and disentangled representations, ICLR 2023, arXiv:2205.11713 — <https://arxiv.org/abs/2205.11713>

### Astrocytes as slow third-factor units: attention normalization and supralinear memory capacity
*status: convergent*

Two rigorous constructions make astrocytes computational rather than metaphorical. Kozachkov & Slotine et al. (PNAS 2023) built an explicit neuron-astrocyte network whose tripartite synapses (astrocyte process wrapping a synapse) implement transformer self-attention: the slow astrocyte calcium state accumulates key-query products over time and multiplicatively modulates hidden-to-output weights, performing the softmax normalization — a recurrent, biologically-timed implementation of attention. Kozachkov, Slotine & Krotov (PNAS May 2025) then showed neuron-astrocyte networks implement Dense Associative Memory: storing memories partly in the lateral network of astrocytic processes yields supralinear capacity scaling laws (quartic-energy DenseAM K_max ~ N^3 with N^2 processes, i.e., memories-per-unit grows with network size), outperforming known biological DenseAM implementations and far exceeding Hopfield's K ~ 0.14N. The common operational content: a SECOND, SLOWER state variable multiplicatively coupled to synaptic transmission both gates communication and adds storage capacity.

**Math.** 2023: astrocyte state s(t) integrates ds/dt = (-s + sum_j phi(q.k_j))/tau_astro (tau_astro >> tau_neuron); output y = (W ∘ g(s)) h approximates attention out = sum_j softmax(q.K)_j v_j with high probability. 2025 (NAAM): energy E(v, s) over neurons v and astrocyte-process states s with tripartite coupling; minima store K patterns with K_max ∝ N^3 for quartic energy realized by N^2 processes — memories per compute unit ~ N (supralinear), vs Hopfield K_max ≈ 0.14 N. Capacity boost comes specifically from process-to-process (lateral astrocytic) connections.

**REFA application.** Second timescale of gain: add per-bank slow gain a_h with da_h/dt = (-a_h + phi(recent write traffic))/tau_slow, tau_slow ~ 50-100 events, multiplying reads z = sum_h r_h a_h q^T M_h. Frequently-written banks become high-conductance (consolidation dial, Benna-Fusi-like but multiplicative and O(H) cost). The 2025 capacity result also motivates a cheap experiment: low-rank lateral coupling between banks (A in R^{HxH} on read) to test capacity gains beyond independent banks.

Sources:
- Kozachkov, Kastanenka, Krotov (2023) Building transformers from neurons and astrocytes, PNAS 120:e2219150120 — <https://www.pnas.org/doi/10.1073/pnas.2219150120>
- Kozachkov, Slotine, Krotov (2025) Neuron-astrocyte associative memory, PNAS 122:e2417788122 (arXiv:2311.08135) — <https://www.pnas.org/doi/10.1073/pnas.2417788122>
