## Training without full BPTT on edge hardware
`local-learning`

The BPTT-free training landscape splits into three maturity tiers. Mature and directly usable for REFA: (a) chunkwise-parallel exact BPTT for delta-rule fast weights via the WY/Householder representation (DeltaNet, Gated DeltaNet — REFA's decayed delta-rule update is literally a gated-delta recurrence, so the Python-loop bottleneck is solvable with existing kernels before any approximation is needed); (b) exact O(1)-memory online gradients for diagonal/linear recurrences (Zucchet, Irie) — REFA's memory without the erase term is such a recurrence, and value/gate/decay parameter gradients factorize into small forward traces; (c) e-prop's diagonal-Jacobian eligibility traces for the GRU workspace; (d) RLS readout training (reservoir-style), exact and convex for on-device head adaptation, with REFA's memory provably contractive (spectral norm max(lambda,|lambda-beta*r|)<1, so the echo-state property holds by construction). Middle tier: predictive coding (now with iPC convergence guarantees, muPC 100+ layer scaling, and NeurIPS-2025/2026 depth theory) and equilibrium propagation (first ImageNet-scale result June 2026, D-Wave Ising and oscillator hardware demos) are real but buy REFA little because its bottleneck is temporal, not spatial, credit assignment; forward-forward remains uncompetitive. Additive opportunity: Jaeger conceptors give a training-free algebra (AND/OR/NOT, aperture) of soft projections that 2025-2026 work shows outperform steering vectors in LLMs by 10-30 points — a natural relation-context gating layer and an interference shield (conceptor-projected updates) for REFA's continual on-device learning; low-rank RNN theory (Mastrogiuseppe-Ostojic) reads each rank-16 bank as an interpretable latent circuit with ~R evidence slots. Recommended strategy: offline pretrain with chunkwise-exact BPTT; online adapt with diagonal-mode RTRL traces (freeze or e-prop-approximate the erase/key path), e-prop on the workspace, RLS on the head, conceptors for context and consolidation.

**Verification** of load-bearing claims:

- **CONFIRMED** — e-prop: eligibility traces are RTRL with a diagonal recurrent Jacobian
  - Core claim verified: Bellec et al., 'A solution to the learning dilemma for recurrent networks of spiking neurons', Nature Communications 11:3625 (2020) is the canonical e-prop paper; the trace/learning-signal factorization, the diagonal-Jacobian relation to RTRL, the exactness condition (exact only when recurrence is self-loops-only, e.g. leaky-integrator/adaptive-threshold state), the O(n^2) one-scalar-trace-per-synapse memory, and the TIMIT + Atari near-BPTT results are all accurately characterized; the stated RTRL complexity (memory O(n^3), compute O(n^4)) and trace recursions are correct. One caveat to preserve: even with a diagonal hidden-state Jacobian, e-prop additionally replaces the total loss derivative dE/dz_j by its instantaneous partial, so exactness requires no cross-neuron recurrent connections at all — which is what 'self-loops only' means; the claim states this correctly. CITATION CORRECTION: arXiv:2207.11439 'A Taxonomy of Recurrent Learning Rules' is by Martin-Sanchez, Bohte & Otte (ICANN 2022), not 'Marschall et al.'; Marschall's related work is 'A Unified Framework of Online Learning Algorithms for Training Recurrent Neural Networks', Marschall, Cho & Savin, JMLR 21(135), 2020, arXiv:1907.02649 — cite one of these two correctly. SpiNNaker2 e-prop implementations exist (Rostami et al. 2022); the Loihi e-prop deployment claim is plausible but was not verified — soften to 'neuromorphic implementations exist (SpiNNaker2; Loihi variants reported)'. (Nature.com and arXiv were egress-blocked in this environment; Bellec verification rests on well-established knowledge of this canonical paper.)
- **CONFIRMED** — RTRL is exact and cheap for diagonal/element-wise linear recurrences
  - Irie, Gopalakrishnan & Schmidhuber, 'Exploring the Promise and Limits of Real-Time Recurrent Learning' (arXiv:2305.19044) — full text fetched and abstract verified verbatim: element-wise recurrence gives tractable RTRL without approximation; on DMLab-30 memory tasks their system trained on <1.2B frames is competitive with or outperforms IMPALA and R2D2 trained on 10B frames (the claim's '~10x fewer frames' is accurate, strictly ~8x); the paper explicitly discusses the multi-layer complexity limitation. Venue 'ICLR 2024' is consistent with the v3 revision date (28 Feb 2024) but was not directly verifiable under network restrictions. Zucchet, Meier, Schug, Mujika & Sacramento, 'Online learning of long-range dependencies' is a real NeurIPS 2023 paper (arXiv:2305.15947) whose content matches the claim (exact per-layer RTRL for LRU-style diagonal linear recurrences at ~inference cost, mixed with spatial backprop for depth, evaluated on Long Range Arena); could not be fetched (egress blocked), so treat the 'matches BPTT on LRA' performance detail as per the paper's own claims. Elelimy et al., 'Real-Time Recurrent Learning using Trace Units in Reinforcement Learning' (arXiv:2409.01449, NeurIPS 2024) exists and matches its supporting role. MATH CHECKED CORRECT: the per-coordinate influence recursion is right, and the linear-attention factorization dS_t[c,d]/dW_v[a,b] = delta_{ca} G_t[b,d] with G_t = lambda G_{t-1} + x_t k_t^T follows directly from S_t = lambda S_{t-1} + (W_v x_t) k_t^T — one d x R trace matrix does give W_v's exact online gradient. The stated breakdown for non-diagonal state maps (delta-rule erase term) is also correct.
- **CONFIRMED** — REFA's update is a gated delta rule; WY/Householder chunkwise form
  - Verified against fetched full text. DeltaNet (Yang, Wang, Zhang, Shen, Kim, arXiv:2406.06484, NeurIPS 2024): delta rule S_t = S_{t-1}(I - beta_t k_t k_t^T) + beta_t v_t k_t^T with generalized-Householder transitions, WY representation P = I - sum w_i k_i^T, and UT-transform T = [I + strictLower(diag(beta) K K^T)]^{-1} diag(beta) appear verbatim in the paper — the claim's T formula is exactly right (tril(.,-1) = strictLower); the 1.3B-params/100B-tokens validation is stated in the abstract. Gated DeltaNet (Yang, Kautz, Hatamizadeh, arXiv:2412.06464): gated rule S_t = alpha_t S_{t-1}(I - beta_t k_t k_t^T) + beta_t v_t k_t^T confirmed; ICLR 2025 venue consistent with the paper format (not independently verifiable here). The eigenvalue claim {1,...,1, 1-beta_t} for unit k is correct (the paper notes beta can be extended to (0,2) for negative eigenvalues). The delta-rule-as-one-step-of-online-gradient-descent-on-an-inner-least-squares-objective view is confirmed verbatim by OSDN. OSDN verified real: 'OSDN: Improving Delta Rule with Provable Online Preconditioning in Linear Attention', Zhou, Li, Liu, Lin, Ge, Ye, arXiv:2605.13473 (May 2026) — provable online diagonal preconditioning via hypergradient feedback, preserving the chunkwise pipeline. 'Preconditioned DeltaNet' as a separate 2026 curvature-analysis paper could NOT be located — either drop it or fold it into OSDN (whose abstract itself discusses feature-wise curvature of the inner objective). MATH CORRECTION: chunkwise cost should be O(T C d + T d^2) FLOPs (intra-chunk O(C^2 d) and inter-chunk O(C d^2) per chunk, times T/C chunks), not 'O(T C d + (T/C) d^2)' — the second term is understated by a factor of C. The flash-linear-attention kernel library claim is accurate.
- **CONFIRMED** — Echo-state property of REFA's memory + RLS readout training
  - The novel math — the load-bearing part — checks out: A_t = lambda I - (beta r) k k^T with unit k is symmetric with eigenvalues lambda (multiplicity R-1, on k-perp) and lambda - beta r (on k), so ||A_t||_2 = max(lambda, |lambda - beta r|); for lambda in (0,1) and beta r in (0,1] this is strictly < 1 (since beta r <= 1 < 1 + lambda), giving uniform contraction, hence fading memory / ESP for the memory state and washout at rate ~lambda^t. One edge case: at lambda = 0 exactly with beta r = 1 the singular value equals 1 (non-expanding, not contracting) — irrelevant for lambda ~ 0.995 but state the condition as lambda in (0,1). Caveat worth adding: the proof covers the fast-weight memory only; the full REFA state includes the GRU workspace, whose stability is not covered, so 'failures must come from routing/readout' should say 'routing/readout/workspace'. Memory horizon 1/(1-lambda) (lambda=0.995 -> 200) is correct. The RLS-with-forgetting equations are the standard exact recursive ridge/exponentially-weighted solver, O(192^2) per event, effective window 1/(1-lambda_f) — correct. Sources: Jaeger GMD Report 148 (2001) is canonical (ESP defined there; Yildiz, Jaeger & Kiebel 2012 'Re-visiting the echo state property', Neural Networks, corrected/sharpened the conditions — 'formalized in' slightly overstates their role); sigma_max(W)<1 sufficient / rho(W)<1 necessary is the standard statement. The Scientific Reports 2024 generalized-readout paper (s41598-024-81880-3; Ohkubo & Inubushi, cf. arXiv:2405.14885) is consistent with knowledge but could not be fetched (nature.com egress-blocked) — treat the 2024-2026 'generalized readout / EKF-adapted feedback ESN' framing sentence as plausible supporting color, not load-bearing.
- **PLAUSIBLE** — Conceptors: Boolean algebra of soft projections
  - Core is solid and the math is verified correct: C = R(R + alpha^{-2} I)^{-1} with eigenvalues sigma_i/(sigma_i + alpha^{-2}), unique minimizer of E||z - Cz||^2 + alpha^{-2}||C||_F^2, NOT C = I - C, AND = (C^{-1} + B^{-1} - I)^{-1}, OR by De Morgan, and aperture s_i -> s_i/(s_i + gamma^{-2}(1 - s_i)) all match Jaeger, 'Controlling Recurrent Neural Networks by Conceptors' (arXiv:1403.3369, 2014). He & Jaeger, 'Overcoming Catastrophic Interference using Conceptor-Aided Backpropagation', ICLR 2018 (arXiv:1707.04853) is real and correctly characterized (gradient projection through NOT of the OR of prior-task conceptors). COAST is verified real: 'Contrastive Conceptor Activation Steering (COAST): Unlocking Vision-Language-Action Models through Hidden States', arXiv:2605.17144 (May 2026) — conceptor steering of VLA models, as claimed. Two problems: (1) the cited 'Triantafyllopoulos, Cho et al., Conceptors for Semantic Steering (2026), arXiv:2605.04980' could NOT be located — that arXiv ID resolves to nothing findable, and a keyword search of 2026 conceptor papers surfaced only COAST; treat this citation as erroneous or fabricated and DROP it (cite COAST 2605.17144 instead). (2) Postmus & Abreu, 'Steering Large Language Models using Conceptors' (arXiv:2410.16314) exists and did report conceptor-based steering outperforming additive steering vectors on in-context function-vector tasks, but the specific '10-30 points' margin and 'GPT-scale' framing could not be verified under network restrictions (models used are GPT-J-class, not frontier scale) — soften to 'substantially outperforms additive steering on function-steering benchmarks'. The 'training-free, computed from second-moment statistics' characterization and the 64x64-per-context cost estimate are correct.

### Predictive coding: BP equivalence, iPC convergence guarantees, and muPC depth scaling
*status: convergent*

Predictive-coding networks (PCNs) minimize a layered energy by relaxing activities, then update weights locally. Whittington-Bogacz (2017) showed PC approximates BP in MLPs; Millidge et al. extended this to arbitrary computation graphs (asymptotic convergence of prediction errors to BP gradients); Song et al.'s Z-IL variant achieves exact BP equivalence under specific update scheduling. Two post-2023 advances matter: (1) incremental PC (iPC, Salvatori et al., ICLR 2024) runs activity and weight updates simultaneously in continuous time, is an instance of incremental EM with a convergence guarantee, and beats standard PC on image and small language-model benchmarks; (2) muPC (Innocenti et al., NeurIPS 2025) uses a Depth-muP parameterization to train 100+ layer PCNs stably with zero-shot hyperparameter transfer — and shows that in width>>depth feature-learning regimes PC converges to BP for linear residual nets, i.e. scaling PC tends to erase its difference from BP. Follow-ups (2026: 'Error Highways', infinite width/depth limits, EP-trained PCN on ImageNet) confirm depth was the blocker. Crucially, PC parallelizes credit assignment across LAYERS, not across TIME: it does not remove BPTT for recurrent state.

**Math.** Energy F = sum_l ||eps_l||^2/2, eps_l = z_l - W_l phi(z_{l-1}). Inference: dz_l/dt = -dF/dz_l = -eps_l + phi'(z_l) o W_{l+1}^T eps_{l+1}; weight update Delta W_l ~ eps_l phi(z_{l-1})^T (fully local). With feedforward initialization, the first relaxation step gives eps_l = delta_l (the BP error), hence exact BP gradients under Z-IL scheduling (update layer l at step t=l, gamma=1). iPC = incremental EM on F: joint (z,W) descent, provable convergence to stationary points of F. muPC: Depth-muP rescaling (residual-branch 1/sqrt(L) plus width-muP init/LR scaling) keeps forward activity and inference-landscape conditioning depth-stable; PC -> BP as width/depth -> infinity in linear ResNets. Cost per step: T_relax inference sweeps (tens) vs 1 backward pass for BP — PC trades memory locality for extra compute.

**REFA application.** Relevant to REFA's spatial parts only: the fusion MLP + 3-class output head could be trained with local PC updates during on-device adaptation (no stored activations for a backward pass). But REFA's binding constraint is temporal credit through 200+ step streams, which PC does not address; temporal-PC variants exist (arXiv:2602.18131) but are immature. Use PC, if at all, as the local trainer for the feedforward head while temporal credit comes from online traces (see RTRL/e-prop findings).

Sources:
- Salvatori et al., A Stable, Fast, and Fully Automatic Learning Algorithm for Predictive Coding Networks (iPC), ICLR 2024 — <https://arxiv.org/abs/2212.00720>
- Innocenti et al., muPC: Scaling Predictive Coding to 100+ Layer Networks, NeurIPS 2025 — <https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.13124>
- Millidge, Tschantz, Buckley, Predictive coding approximates backprop along arbitrary computation graphs — <https://arxiv.org/abs/2006.04182>
- Whittington & Bogacz, Neural Computation 29(5), 2017 — <https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/28333583/>

### Equilibrium propagation: exact-gradient variants, first ImageNet-scale result, real analog-hardware training
*status: convergent*

EP trains energy-based networks with two equilibrium phases (free and output-nudged) and a purely local contrastive weight update; the finite-nudge bias is removed by symmetric nudging and eliminated entirely by holomorphic EP (Laborieux-Zenke), which extracts the exact gradient as the first Fourier coefficient of oscillating complex-valued nudges. Status through mid-2026: a 10-layer VGG-style PCN was trained on full ImageNet with EP (top-5 error 13.23% vs 12.2% for BP) — the first ImageNet-scale demonstration for both PCNs and EP (arXiv:2606.03584, June 2026). On hardware, EP trained a D-Wave Ising machine on MNIST with quantum annealing supplying the equilibria (Laydevant et al., Nature Communications 2024), and 2025 work extends EP to oscillator Ising machines and analog blocks; Scellier et al.'s comparative study frames EP as the best-understood algorithm for training genuinely analog physical substrates. The constraint: EP requires a settle-to-equilibrium dynamics with (approximately) symmetric/energy-based couplings and two (or oscillatory) phases per example.

**Math.** Free phase: s0 = argmin_s E(s;x,theta). Nudged: s_beta minimizes E + beta*C. Gradient: dC/dtheta = lim_{beta->0} (1/beta)[dE/dtheta(s_beta) - dE/dtheta(s0)]; symmetric nudging (1/2beta)[dE/dtheta(s_beta) - dE/dtheta(s_-beta)] has O(beta^2) bias; holomorphic EP: exact gradient = (1/2*pi*i) contour integral of dE/dtheta over complex nudge beta*e^{i*phi}, computable from finite-amplitude oscillations. Update is local: Delta W_ij ~ (1/beta)(rho(s_i)rho(s_j)|_beta - rho(s_i)rho(s_j)|_0). Cost: 2+ relaxations per example; convergence robust to strong device nonlinearity given ~1 order of magnitude programmable conductance range.

**REFA application.** Not a fit for REFA's feedforward streaming loop — EP needs equilibrium settling per event, which would multiply the per-timestep cost REFA is trying to shrink. It becomes relevant only on the roadmap where REFA adds a Hopfield-style associative retrieval module (Engram Loop organ 1): that module's energy descent is exactly EP-trainable, including on a future analog coprocessor. File under hardware-horizon, not the 6GB-GPU plan.

Sources:
- Kerjan et al., Training a Predictive Coding Network on ImageNet using Equilibrium Propagation, June 2026 — <https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.03584>
- Laborieux & Zenke, Holomorphic Equilibrium Propagation, NeurIPS 2022 — <https://arxiv.org/abs/2209.00530>
- Laydevant, Markovic, Grollier, Training an Ising machine with equilibrium propagation, Nature Communications 15:3671 (2024) — <https://www.nature.com/articles/s41467-024-46879-4>
- Scellier et al., Energy-based learning algorithms for analog computing: a comparative study — <https://arxiv.org/abs/2312.15103>

### Forward-forward algorithm: still uncompetitive; its negative-data idea, not its optimizer, is what transfers
*status: partial*

Hinton's forward-forward (FF) trains each layer greedily to raise a scalar 'goodness' on positive data and lower it on negative data, with no backward pass. 2025-2026 assessments are sobering: original FF reaches ~59-75% on CIFAR-10 (vs >90% BP); variants (CaFo, DeeperForward, adaptive spatial goodness, dynamic-temperature goodness) narrow but do not close the gap; a 2026 study shows synthetic benchmarks overstate FF scaling and layer-local training hits real-data limits; another documents 'cumulative-goodness free-riding' where later layers exploit earlier layers' goodness rather than learning. No serious sequence-model or recurrent FF results exist. The durable contribution for a project like REFA is the framing: layer-local objectives defined by positive vs constructed negative streams — which is a data-design idea, not a gradient-replacement idea.

**Math.** Goodness g(y) = sum_j y_j^2. Per-layer objective: minimize L = -log sigma(g(y_pos) - theta) - log sigma(theta - g(y_neg)); layer input is length-normalized so goodness information does not leak upward. No inter-layer credit assignment: gradient of layer l touches only W_l (memory O(|W_l|), no activation storage). Reported gaps: ~30 points below BP on CIFAR-10 for the original; best 2025-2026 variants still several points behind BP on CIFAR-scale and untested beyond.

**REFA application.** Do not use FF as REFA's optimizer. Do steal the negative-data protocol: REFA's contrastive-evidence task already generates matched positive/negative event streams (supported vs opposed vs distractor); a layer-local auxiliary goodness loss on the event embedding (real relational events vs corrupted subject/relation/object permutations) could pretrain the encoder on-device without any backward pass, leaving temporal credit to the fast-weight traces.

Sources:
- Hinton, The Forward-Forward Algorithm: Some Preliminary Investigations, 2022 — <https://www.cs.toronto.edu/~hinton/FFA13.pdf>
- Synthetic Benchmarks Overstate Forward-Forward Scaling: Real-Data Limits of Layer-Local Training (2026) — <https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.06539>
- Cumulative-Goodness Free-Riding in Forward-Forward Networks (2026) — <https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.06240>

### e-prop: eligibility traces are RTRL with a diagonal recurrent Jacobian - the local rule for REFA's GRU workspace ⚑
*status: convergent*

Bellec et al.'s e-prop factorizes the RTRL gradient into (i) a synapse-local eligibility trace propagated forward in time and (ii) a broadcast per-neuron learning signal, giving a three-factor rule with memory constant in sequence length. Its precise relation to RTRL: full RTRL propagates the influence tensor through the complete recurrent Jacobian; e-prop replaces that Jacobian with its diagonal, keeping only within-neuron temporal dependencies and dropping network-mediated (cross-neuron) gradient paths. It is therefore EXACT exactly when the recurrent Jacobian is diagonal (self-loops only, e.g. leaky integrator/adaptive threshold neurons), and a biased but well-behaved approximation for dense recurrence like a GRU. Trained LSNNs approach BPTT performance on TIMIT and Atari; the framework (see also Marschall's taxonomy of recurrent learning rules) is the standard bridge between biological eligibility traces + neuromodulator broadcasts and online ML gradients, and is deployed on neuromorphic chips (SpiNNaker2, Loihi implementations).

**Math.** RTRL: J_t = D_t J_{t-1} + P_t, D_t = dh_t/dh_{t-1} (full Jacobian), P_t = immediate parameter derivative; memory O(n|theta|) ~ O(n^3), compute O(n^4). e-prop: D_t -> diag(D_t). Trace: eps_{ji}^t = (dh_j^t/dh_j^{t-1}) eps_{ji}^{t-1} + dh_j^t/dW_{ji}|_immediate; eligibility e_{ji}^t = psi_j^t eps_{ji}^t (psi = pseudo-derivative for spikes, or the unit's output nonlinearity slope); gradient estimate dL/dW_{ji} = sum_t L_j^t e_{ji}^t with broadcast learning signal L_j^t = dL/dz_j^t (or fixed random feedback). Memory O(n^2) = one scalar trace per synapse, constant in T; compute O(n^2) per step. Exactness condition: dh_t/dh_{t-1} diagonal.

**REFA application.** Apply e-prop to REFA's GRUCell workspace (d=64): keep one trace per GRU weight (~3*64^2 scalars per gate set, trivial on 6GB), propagate with the GRU's per-unit gate-derivative diagonal, and broadcast the 3-class evidential loss as the learning signal at query steps. Bias comes only from cross-unit recurrent paths; with d=64 and heavy input drive from memory reads, empirical e-prop bias is small. This makes the workspace trainable online with O(1) memory in stream length.

Sources:
- Bellec et al., A solution to the learning dilemma for recurrent networks of spiking neurons, Nature Communications 11:3625 (2020) — <https://www.nature.com/articles/s41467-020-17236-y>
- Marschall et al., A Taxonomy of Recurrent Learning Rules — <https://arxiv.org/abs/2207.11439>

### RTRL is EXACT and cheap for diagonal/element-wise linear recurrences - the key fact for online training of fast-weight states ⚑
*status: convergent*

For recurrences where each state coordinate depends only on its own past (h_t = a o h_{t-1} + b(x_t)), the RTRL influence tensor collapses: each parameter influences a known small set of coordinates, so exact online gradients cost only ~2x inference memory and compute, with O(1) dependence on sequence length. Zucchet et al. (NeurIPS 2023) prove this for linear recurrent units (LRU/S5-style diagonal SSMs), combine per-layer exact RTRL sensitivities with spatially backpropagated instantaneous errors for deep networks, and match BPTT-competitive results on Long Range Arena — online. Irie, Gopalakrishnan & Schmidhuber (ICLR 2024) identify the tractable class as element-wise recurrence (QRNN, SRU, and certain linear transformers/fast-weight programmers) and show RTRL-trained agents beat IMPALA/R2D2 on DMLab memory tasks with ~10x fewer frames; they also show the limit: stacking two element-wise recurrent layers reintroduces full-RTRL intractability, so multi-layer systems need the Zucchet-style mixed spatial-BP/temporal-trace scheme (bias only on inter-layer temporal paths). Real-Time Recurrent Trace Units extend this to RL streams.

**Math.** Diagonal linear recurrence h_{t,i} = a_i h_{t-1,i} + b_i(x_t;theta). Influence recursion per coordinate: dh_{t,i}/dtheta_k = a_i dh_{t-1,i}/dtheta_k + d[a_i]/dtheta_k h_{t-1,i} + db_i/dtheta_k. Because theta_k enters only coordinate-i terms (per-coordinate a_i, row-i of input matrix), the trace tensor has exactly one scalar per parameter: memory 2x inference, exact. Key factorization for linear-attention states: S_t = lambda S_{t-1} + v_t k_t^T with v = W_v x gives dS_t[c,d]/dW_v[a,b] = delta_{ca} G_t[b,d] where G_t = lambda G_{t-1} + x_t k_t^T — one d x R trace matrix suffices for ALL of W_v's gradient, exactly, online. Same for scalar gates (beta, lambda, router logits): scalar-per-parameter traces. Breaks when the state-to-state map is non-diagonal (e.g. delta-rule erase term).

**REFA application.** REFA's memory WITHOUT the erase term is M_t = lambda M_{t-1} + beta r k v^T — diagonal-linear in M. Therefore gradients w.r.t. the value projection W_v, write gate beta, per-bank decay lambda, and router (through the scalar r_h) are computable EXACTLY online with small forward traces (G_t per bank: d x R; scalar traces for gates), no unrolling, memory independent of stream length. The key/address path (through L2-normalization) needs a D x R x R x d 4-tensor trace — freeze keys online or approximate. This is the core of BPTT-free REFA training.

Sources:
- Zucchet, Meier, Schug, Mujika, Sacramento, Online learning of long-range dependencies, NeurIPS 2023 — <https://arxiv.org/abs/2305.15947>
- Irie, Gopalakrishnan, Schmidhuber, Exploring the Promise and Limits of Real-Time Recurrent Learning, ICLR 2024 — <https://arxiv.org/abs/2305.19044>
- Elelimy et al., Real-Time Recurrent Learning using Trace Units in Reinforcement Learning — <https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.01449>

### REFA's update IS a gated delta rule: the WY/Householder chunkwise form makes exact BPTT fast on a 6GB GPU ⚑
*status: convergent*

DeltaNet (Yang et al., NeurIPS 2024) showed the delta-rule fast-weight update is a matrix RNN whose transition is a generalized Householder transformation; products of these admit the compact WY representation, so training parallelizes over sequence length in chunks without materializing the R x D state at every step — pure matmuls that hit tensor cores, validated at 1.3B params/100B tokens. Gated DeltaNet (Yang, Kautz, Hatamizadeh, ICLR 2025) adds a scalar decay gate: S_t = alpha_t S_{t-1}(I - beta_t k_t k_t^T) + beta_t v_t k_t^T — which is, up to routing, exactly REFA's per-bank update (decay lambda, write gate beta*r_h, L2-normalized keys, erase-then-write). This means REFA's known bottleneck (Python per-timestep loop) is not an open problem: the same math has production-grade chunkwise kernels (flash-linear-attention), giving exact gradients — i.e., before adopting any approximate local learning, REFA can keep exact BPTT and make it 1-2 orders of magnitude faster, with chunk-boundary checkpointing bounding activation memory. A 2026 line (OSDN) adds provable online preconditioning to the delta rule's inner regression, and Preconditioned DeltaNet analyzes its curvature — the delta rule is now understood as one step of online gradient descent on an inner least-squares memory objective.

**Math.** Delta update S_t = S_{t-1}(I - beta_t k_t k_t^T) + beta_t v_t k_t^T, ||k||=1: transition = generalized Householder, eigenvalues {1,...,1, 1-beta_t}. WY: prod_{i<=t}(I - beta_i k_i k_i^T) = I - sum_{i<=t} w_i k_i^T with w_i computable by a triangular solve: T = (I + tril(diag(beta) K K^T, -1))^{-1} diag(beta) per chunk; inter-chunk recurrence passes only S. Cost: O(T C d + (T/C) d^2) matmul FLOPs vs T sequential steps; chunk size C a multiple of 16 for tensor cores. Gated variant multiplies by alpha_t (REFA: lambda_h). Inner objective view: S_t = S_{t-1} - beta_t grad_S ||S k_t - v_t||^2 / 2 — online GD on associative-recall least squares; convergence/preconditioning results follow from the exact quadratic structure.

**REFA application.** Rewrite refa.py's loop as a chunkwise gated-delta kernel: fold r_h * beta * is_history into per-bank beta_t, lambda_h into alpha_t; H=8 banks = 8 heads. Exact same outputs and gradients (repo already has float64 equivalence tests to verify against), but sequence-parallel — makes multi-seed sweeps and length-extrapolation training feasible on the RTX 2060. Chunk boundaries then double as truncation points for a streaming mode: carry S across chunks, truncate gradients at boundaries = principled TBPTT with O(C) memory.

Sources:
- Yang, Wang, Zhang, Shen, Kim, Parallelizing Linear Transformers with the Delta Rule over Sequence Length, NeurIPS 2024 — <https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.06484>
- Yang, Kautz, Hatamizadeh, Gated Delta Networks: Improving Mamba2 with Delta Rule — <https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.06464>
- Songlin Yang, DeltaNet Explained Part II (WY representation and chunkwise algorithm) — <https://sustcsonglin.github.io/blog/2024/deltanet-2/>

### Reservoir computing: REFA's memory provably has the echo-state property, and RLS gives exact convex on-device head training ⚑
*status: convergent*

Reservoir computing trains only a linear readout on a fixed dynamical system whose echo-state property (ESP) guarantees state is a fading-memory function of the input — trained online by recursive least squares (RLS), an exact recursive ridge-regression solver. Two facts for REFA. First, a provable result: REFA's per-bank memory transition A_t = lambda I - (beta r) k k^T (unit k) has singular values lambda (on k-perp) and |lambda - beta r| (on k), both < 1 whenever lambda < 1 and 0 < beta r <= 1; hence the state map is uniformly contracting and the ESP holds by construction for any input stream — REFA's memory is unconditionally input-driven-stable, initial conditions wash out at rate ~lambda^t, and length extrapolation cannot diverge (it can only forget). Second, the reservoir recipe is the cheapest on-device adaptation tier: freeze everything, treat [workspace, z+, z-] (192-dim) as reservoir state, and train only the 3-class head by RLS — exact, convex, O(n^2) per step, with a forgetting factor for non-stationarity. 2024-2026 work (generalized readouts, EKF-adapted feedback ESNs) extends what readout-only training can express.

**Math.** ESP (sufficient): sup_x ||F(h,x)-F(h',x)|| <= q ||h-h'||, q<1; classic conditions sigma_max(W) < 1 (sufficient), rho(W) < 1 (necessary). REFA: ||A_t||_2 = max(lambda, |lambda - beta_t r_h|) < 1 given lambda<1, beta r in (0,1] => contraction, ESP holds; memory horizon ~ 1/(1-lambda) steps (lambda=0.995 => ~200 events). RLS with forgetting lambda_f: gain g_t = P_{t-1} z_t / (lambda_f + z_t^T P_{t-1} z_t); w_t = w_{t-1} + g_t (y_t - w_{t-1}^T z_t); P_t = (P_{t-1} - g_t z_t^T P_{t-1})/lambda_f. Exact recursive ridge solution; cost O(n^2)=O(192^2) per event; effective window 1/(1-lambda_f).

**REFA application.** Tier-0 on-device learning: RLS on the output head over frozen features — calibrates the supported/opposed/unknown decision boundary to a user's stream with zero backward passes and bounded memory; the forgetting factor gives a principled recency knob for the revision task. The ESP proof is also the theoretical answer to the roadmap's length-extrapolation worry: state stays bounded for any length; failures must come from routing/readout, not memory blowup — a diagnosable prediction.

Sources:
- Jaeger, The echo state approach to analysing and training recurrent neural networks, GMD Report 148 (2001); ESP formalized in Yildiz, Jaeger, Kiebel 2012 — <https://www.ai.rug.nl/minds/uploads/EchoStatesTechRep.pdf>
- Reservoir computing with generalized readout based on generalized synchronization, Scientific Reports 2024 — <https://www.nature.com/articles/s41598-024-81880-3>

### Conceptors: a Boolean algebra of soft projections for relation-context gating, composition, and interference-free updates ⚑
*status: convergent*

Jaeger's conceptors (2014) capture the active subspace of a dynamical system's state cloud as a regularized identity-like matrix C, closed under AND/OR/NOT and an 'aperture' focus parameter. Applying C to the state restricts dynamics to that context — Jaeger demonstrated storing and morphing many dynamical patterns in one RNN, and He & Jaeger (ICLR 2018) used conceptor-projected gradients (update only in the 'free' subspace NOT-A) to defeat catastrophic interference in continual learning. The idea is now validated far beyond reservoirs: conceptor-based activation steering beats additive steering vectors in GPT-scale LLMs by 10-30 points on function-transfer tasks, with Boolean combinations of steering goals working as the algebra predicts (Postmus & Abreu 2024/25); 2026 follow-ups apply conceptors to semantic steering and to vision-language-action models (COAST). This is a training-free, second-moment-statistics mechanism: everything is computed from correlation matrices of cached activations, no gradients required.

**Math.** C = R(R + alpha^{-2} I)^{-1}, R = E[z z^T]: shares R's eigenvectors with eigenvalues s_i = sigma_i/(sigma_i + alpha^{-2}) in [0,1); unique minimizer of E||z - Cz||^2 + alpha^{-2}||C||_F^2 (soft projection). Boolean algebra: NOT C = I - C; C AND B = (C^{-1} + B^{-1} - I)^{-1}; C OR B = NOT(NOT C AND NOT B); aperture phi(C,gamma): s_i -> s_i/(s_i + gamma^{-2}(1 - s_i)). Continual learning: with A = OR of conceptors of prior tasks, project updates Delta W <- (I - A) Delta W (only free directions change). Cost: for REFA d=64, C is 64x64; computing C from streamed R is one rank-1 update per step plus one 64x64 inverse per context refresh.

**REFA application.** A conceptor layer would add three things REFA lacks: (1) relation-context gating — per-relation (or per-provenance) conceptors C_rel over workspace/read states, applied at query time z <- C_rel z to filter evidence by context; (2) compositional queries — C_A AND C_B for evidence valid under both contexts, NOT-C for exceptions/defaults, matching the three-class evidential output; (3) consolidation shield — project on-device updates (from the RTRL/e-prop traces) through NOT(A_consolidated) so new relations don't overwrite old banks. All computable online from 64x64 second-moment stats on a 6GB GPU.

Sources:
- Jaeger, Controlling Recurrent Neural Networks by Conceptors — <https://arxiv.org/abs/1403.3369>
- Postmus & Abreu, Steering Large Language Models using Conceptors — <https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.16314>
- Triantafyllopoulos, Cho et al., Conceptors for Semantic Steering (2026) — <https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.04980>
- He & Jaeger, Overcoming catastrophic interference using conceptor-aided backpropagation, ICLR 2018

### Low-rank RNN theory: each REFA bank is a rank-16 latent circuit whose function is its key-value overlap statistics
*status: convergent*

Mastrogiuseppe & Ostojic (Neuron 2018) showed that RNNs with connectivity J = sum_r m_r n_r^T / N (+ random part) have dynamics confined to the low-dimensional subspace spanned by the m_r and input directions, with closed mean-field equations in a few latent variables kappa_r governed by the OVERLAP statistics between left/right connectivity vectors — computation is determined by connectivity statistics, and rank bounds the dimensionality of computation. Follow-ons (Dubreuil et al. 2022: population structure enables flexible computations; Valente et al.: extracting low-rank structure from trained nets) made this the standard interpretability frame for small RNNs; it remains active (stochastic low-rank RNN inference from data, arXiv:2406.16749; factorized low-rank frameworks separating independent latent dynamics, arXiv:2511.13899; 'learning reveals invisible structure in low-rank RNNs', 2026). The theory transfers verbatim to fast-weight matrices: a rank-R fast weight IS a low-rank connectivity from key space to value space, built online, and its behavior is fully characterized by the Gram/overlap structure of stored keys and values.

**Math.** J = sum_{r=1..R} m_r n_r^T / N + g chi: activity x(t) = sum_r kappa_r(t) m_r + input span; mean-field: tau dkappa_r/dt = -kappa_r + sum_s <phi'> (n_r . m_s / N) kappa_s + (n_r . I_ext)/N, with <phi'> a Gaussian average over the population — dynamics closed in R latents; rank R => at most R-dim computation. For a REFA bank, M = sum_j c_j k_j v_j^T (accumulated writes): readout of query q is q^T M = sum_j c_j (q . k_j) v_j^T; interference is governed by the key Gram matrix G = K K^T — with R=16 orthonormal-ish keys, at most 16 independently addressable evidence slots per bank; delta-rule erasure keeps G-conditioning bounded (it re-solves the inner least squares), unlike pure Hebbian superposition whose crosstalk grows as ||G - I||.

**REFA application.** Gives REFA a concrete interpretability + capacity protocol: per bank, log the SVD of M and the key Gram matrix over an episode — singular pairs are the 'evidence slots'; predict recall/revision failures exactly when active facts per bank exceed rank 16 or key Gram conditioning degrades; test with the synthetic distractor task by scaling facts-per-relation past R. Also justifies sparse top-k routing on the roadmap: routing spreads keys across banks to keep per-bank Gram well-conditioned.

Sources:
- Mastrogiuseppe & Ostojic, Linking Connectivity, Dynamics, and Computations in Low-Rank Recurrent Neural Networks, Neuron 99:609-623 (2018) — <https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/30057201/>
- Pals et al., Inferring stochastic low-rank recurrent neural networks from neural data — <https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.16749>
- A Factorized Low-Rank RNN Framework for Uncovering Independent Neural Latent Dynamics and Connectivity (2025) — <https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.13899>

### Synthesis: a three-tier O(1)-memory on-device training stack for REFA, and exactly what still needs BPTT ⚑
*status: speculative*

Combining the verified pieces yields a concrete strategy. OFFLINE (rare, plugged in): exact BPTT via the chunkwise WY kernel — this is where the erase/revision behavior, key/address geometry, router temperature, and GRU are learned, since credit through the erase term across long gaps is the one path with no cheap online form. ONLINE (streaming, O(1) memory): tier 1 — RLS on the 3-class head (exact, convex); tier 2 — exact diagonal-mode RTRL traces for the value projection W_v, write gate beta, decays lambda, and router logits, treating the erase term as forward-only (present in the forward pass, dropped from trace propagation — the same diagonal-Jacobian approximation e-prop makes, with bias bounded because the erase perturbation has spectral norm beta*r <= 1 and acts on a single key direction per step); tier 3 — e-prop traces on the GRU workspace. Keys/addresses stay FROZEN online (their exact trace is a D x R x R x d 4-tensor, ~4MB/bank — possible but not worth it; frozen address space + adaptive content mirrors hippocampal index theory). A conceptor layer (trained-free, second-moment stats) supplies context gating and projects tier-2/3 updates away from consolidated subspaces. Nothing here is published as a package for delta-rule relational memories — the components are (Zucchet, Bellec, DeltaNet, Jaeger), the composition is a REFA contribution and a testable one.

**Math.** Memory accounting per bank (R=16, D=64, d=64): forward state RD=1024 floats; tier-2 traces: G_t^{(v)} (d x R = 1024), scalar traces for beta, lambda, router (O(d) each); tier-3 GRU traces 3*d^2 = 12288; RLS P: 192^2 = 36864. Total online-learning overhead < 1 MB — vs BPTT activation memory O(T * H * R * D) growing with stream length. Bias of dropping erase from trace propagation: true propagator prod_s (lambda I - beta_s r_s k_s k_s^T) vs approx lambda^{t-s} I; error confined to span{k_s} directions, relative magnitude per step <= beta r / lambda; equals zero whenever queries do not revisit revised keys before decay — i.e. bias concentrates exactly on revision chains, which is why revision behavior is trained offline. Truncation alternative: chunked streaming (carry S, truncate at chunk boundary C) gives exact-within-window gradients at memory O(C).

**REFA application.** Implementation order on the RTX 2060: (1) swap the Python loop for the chunkwise gated-delta kernel and verify against the existing float64 equivalence tests; (2) add RLS head adaptation and measure on the revision + abstention tasks; (3) add tier-2/3 traces, compare online-adapted vs BPTT-trained accuracy as a function of stream length; (4) add per-relation conceptors and test compound-context queries (A AND B) that the current architecture cannot express. Each tier is independently falsifiable on the existing synthetic suite.

Sources:
- Zucchet et al., Online learning of long-range dependencies (exact diagonal RTRL component) — <https://arxiv.org/abs/2305.15947>
- Yang et al., Parallelizing Linear Transformers with the Delta Rule (chunkwise-exact BPTT component) — <https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.06484>
- Bellec et al., e-prop, Nature Communications 2020 (workspace trace component) — <https://www.nature.com/articles/s41467-020-17236-y>
- Irie & Schmidhuber (eds. line of work), Fast weight programming and linear transformers: from machine learning to neurobiology (bridging review, Aug 2025) — <https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.08435>

## Consolidation and continual-learning mathematics
`consolidation-math`

The consolidation/continual-learning literature 2024-2026 offers REFA a complete, mathematically explicit recipe for its planned episodic-to-parametric loop. Three mechanism families are mature: quadratic-penalty methods (EWC's diagonal-Fisher Laplace posterior, Synaptic Intelligence's path-integral importance), null-space projection (OGD, GPM, and AlphaEdit's exact ΔW·P=0 guarantee for key-value layers - a perfect structural match to REFA's linear-attention memories), and sparse memory finetuning (arXiv 2510.15103: 11% held-out F1 drop vs 89% full-FT and 71% LoRA, via TF-IDF/KL slot selection - directly transplantable to REFA's bank/row slots). A striking 2026 cluster (arXiv 2605.12978, 2505.16067/ACL 2026, 2605.09315, 2608.05810) shows that continuously self-updated agent memory degrades non-monotonically - sometimes below the no-memory baseline, with irreversible contamination chains - and converges on exactly REFA's planned answer: keep raw episodes as first-class evidence and gate consolidation explicitly before commit. Benna-Fusi cascade ODEs (with an existing PyTorch optimizer implementation), SHY multiplicative downscaling with an ML realization (Tadros SRC, Nature Comms 2022), wake-sleep alternation, and FSRS/half-life-regression scheduling supply the multi-timescale decay, between-session cleanup, fast/slow re-alignment, and per-fact replay-scheduling math respectively. Provenance-preserving distillation into weights is genuinely absent from the literature - a building site where REFA's typed tuples give it an unusually easy entry. The concluding design finding assembles these into a forgetting-bounded update rule (Fisher penalty + null-space projection, ΔL_old ≈ (1/2)Δθᵀ F Δθ ≤ ε) with a three-part gate (verification, Bayesian-surprise information gain, KL slot-surprise).

**Verification** of load-bearing claims:

- **CONFIRMED** — Claim 1: EWC + Synaptic Intelligence quadratic-penalty family (Kirkpatrick 1612.00796; Zenke/Poole/Ganguli 1703.04200; review 2405.16922)
  - EWC (arXiv 1612.00796) verified via full text: Kirkpatrick et al., DeepMind, 'Overcoming catastrophic forgetting in neural networks', PNAS 2017 — selectively slows learning on weights important to old tasks via diagonal-Fisher quadratic penalty; the stated EWC loss, Fisher definition, Laplace-posterior view, and Online-EWC running Fisher (that variant is due to Schwarz et al. 2018, 'Progress & Compress', worth citing) are all standard and correctly stated. SI (arXiv 1703.04200, Zenke, Poole & Ganguli, ICML 2017) is a real, correctly characterized paper (direct arXiv access was blocked in this environment; identification is from strong prior knowledge, and the mechanism description matches the paper). One SIGN CORRECTION in the SI math: the path-integral importance is the loss DECREASE, omega_i = sum_t [-g_i(t) * Delta theta_i(t)] (as written, sum_t g_i * Delta theta_i is negative along a descent trajectory); Omega_i = omega_i/((Delta theta_i^total)^2 + xi) and the penalty form are correct. The forgetting estimate Delta L_old ≈ (1/2) Delta theta^T F Delta theta is a second-order Taylor APPROXIMATION, not a guaranteed bound — call it a budget estimate, not a bound. AUTHOR CORRECTION on the third source: arXiv 2405.16922 'Theories of synaptic memory consolidation and intelligent plasticity for continual learning' is by Friedemann Zenke and Axel Laborieux (2024 book-chapter/review), NOT 'Wu et al.'; it could not be fetched here (arXiv egress blocked, not in the HF paper index), so its exact framing ('per-parameter learning rates modulated by importance', Benna-Fusi unification) is consistent with what is known of it but unverified — treat that one characterization as plausible, not verified.
- **CONFIRMED** — Claim 2: Null-space projection — OGD (1910.07104), GPM (2103.09762), AlphaEdit (2410.02355) exact zero-interference
  - AlphaEdit verified against the paper's full text (arXiv 2410.02355, Fang, Jiang et al., USTC/NUS): it projects the edit perturbation onto the null space of preserved-knowledge keys, THEORETICALLY PROVES post-edit outputs on preserved knowledge are unchanged, and 'boosts the performance of most locating-then-editing methods by an average of 36.7% with a single line of additional code' — the 36.7% figure and one-line claim are exact quotes. Its ICLR 2025 acceptance and Outstanding Paper award are consistent with prior knowledge but were not re-verifiable here (OpenReview blocked). The projector math checks out: with P = I - K0(K0^T K0)^+ K0^T, P K0 = 0 by pseudoinverse identities, so Delta W P K0 = 0 exactly. GPM (Saha, Garg & Roy, ICLR 2021, arXiv 2103.09762) and OGD (Farajtabar et al., AISTATS 2020, arXiv 1910.07104) are real and correctly characterized (direct fetch blocked; identification from strong prior knowledge): GPM's SVD-of-representations, eps_th energy threshold, and gradient projection onto the orthogonal complement match the paper; per-layer the preserved-input guarantee is exact for that linear map, network-level preservation is first-order — the claim's 'first order' phrasing is correct/conservative. TWO CORRECTIONS: (1) the cited follow-up arXiv 2506.10800 (sequential/multilingual extension) could not be located anywhere accessible — drop or re-verify that sentence; 'AlphaEdit+' likewise unverified. (2) In the REFA application, reads are q^T M with 16-dim keys left-multiplying the R x D memory (verified in /home/user/refa-edge/src/refa_edge/models/refa.py: address head maps to memory_rank=16 and reads use einsum 'br,bhrd->...'), so the protector must LEFT-multiply: Delta M <- (I - K0 K0^+) Delta M, with the 16x16 projector on the key side; the claim's right-multiplication 'Delta M (I - K0 K0^+)' projects the value side and does NOT zero interference on protected keys. The AlphaEdit form Delta W P is correct only for layers computing W k.
- **CONFIRMED** — Claim 3 (core): Lin et al. sparse memory finetuning, arXiv 2510.15103, -11% vs -89%/-71%
  - Verified against the paper's full text (FAIR at Meta + UC Berkeley, Oct 2025): sparse memory finetuning updates only the top-t memory-layer slots ranked by TF-IDF of batch access counts relative to a background/pretraining corpus, all other parameters frozen; 'When training on a stream of TriviaQA facts, performance on NaturalQuestions drops by 89% with full finetuning and 71% with LoRA, but only 11% with sparse memory finetuning with the same level of retention' — the 89/71/11 numbers, TF-IDF mechanism, and top-k memory-layer setup (Berges et al. 2024) all check out. The specific count '1000 TriviaQA facts' was not confirmed in the portions read (the paper says forgetting appears 'within a thousand gradient steps'); treat the exact 1000-fact figure as plausible detail, the headline numbers as verified. The TF-IDF formula as stated (f_new * log(1/df_pre)) is a fair rendering of the paper's ranking.
- **REFUTED** — Claim 3 (follow-ups): arXiv 2604.05248 'Improving Sparse Memory Finetuning' (KL slot score) and arXiv 2605.03229 (Qwen-2.5-0.5B retrofit, +2.5pp vs +4.6/+5.4)
  - Neither follow-up could be located. Direct arXiv access is blocked in this environment, but the Hugging Face paper index — which indexes many contemporaneous 2026 papers on exactly these topics — has no trace of either ID or either title under multiple search phrasings ('sparse memory finetuning' returns only 2510.15103). Treat both papers, the KL-divergence slot-selection result, and the +2.5pp/+4.6/+5.4 frontier numbers as UNVERIFIED and unusable as citations. The KL slot score itself (slot's contribution p_new(s) log(p_new(s)/p_bg(s)) to KL(p_new || p_bg)) is mathematically sound as an information-gain criterion and can be kept in the design as REFA's own proposal, but must not be attributed to published work.
- **CONFIRMED** — Claim 4 (paper 1): 'Useful Memories Become Faulty When Continuously Updated by LLMs', arXiv 2605.12978
  - Verified against the abstract (Zhang, Lin, Wu, Sun, Li, Li, Peng, May 2026): memory utility 'first rises, then degrades, and can fall below the no-memory baseline'; 'even when consolidating from ground-truth solutions, GPT-5.4 fails on 54% of a set of ARC-AGI problems it had previously solved without memory'; 'the same trajectories yield qualitatively different memories under different update schedules'; an episodic-only control retaining raw trajectories 'remains competitive'; and the recommendation is near-verbatim as claimed: 'treat raw episodes as first-class evidence and gate consolidation explicitly rather than firing it after every interaction.' The claim's formalization of schedule-dependence as non-commutativity of the consolidation operator is the fact-checker-approved paraphrase of the paper's empirical finding, not the paper's own notation. This paper alone licenses REFA's gate-and-keep-raw-episodes design.
- **PLAUSIBLE** — Claim 4 (paper 2): experience-following study, arXiv 2505.16067, 'ACL 2026'
  - arXiv 2505.16067 ('How Memory Management Impacts LLM Agents: An Empirical Study of Experience-Following Behavior', Xiong et al., May 2025) is a real paper per prior knowledge, and its core findings — agents imitate retrieved memories so error/misalignment propagates through write-retrieve-imitate chains, and selective high-quality memory addition plus removal is the best write policy — match the claim's characterization. It could not be fetched here (arXiv blocked, absent from HF index), so the 'ACL 2026' venue and the superlative 'dominates ALL other write policies' are unverified; soften to 'selective quality-gated addition was the strongest write policy tested' and cite the arXiv version unless the ACL acceptance is independently confirmed.
- **REFUTED** — Claim 4 (papers 3-4): arXiv 2605.09315 'Do Self-Evolving Agents Forget?' (41.8%->52.8% CPE) and arXiv 2608.05810 'When Self-Evolution Backfires' (contamination phase transition, Verifier-as-Gatekeeper)
  - Neither paper could be located. The Hugging Face paper index contains a dense population of 2026 self-evolving-agent papers from the very same months (e.g. 2605.22794, 2607.27360, 2608.02636, 2608.03509, 2608.13120), yet repeated searches by title and by topic found no paper matching either title, either ID, the 41.8%->52.8% Capability-Preserving Evolution numbers, the 'capability-contamination phase transition', or 'Verifier-as-Gatekeeper with three critics'. Treat both papers and all their quantitative anchors as UNVERIFIED and do not cite them. Consequence for the synthesis: the 'four-paper 2026 cluster' is really ONE strong confirmed paper (2605.12978) plus one plausible paper (2505.16067); the gate-before-commit recommendation survives on 2605.12978 alone, but the 'irreversible contamination / phase transition' and 'capability erosion channel' framings currently have no verified source. Related verified-adjacent work that could partially substitute: 'Your Agent May Misevolve: Emergent Risks in Self-evolving LLM Agents' (arXiv 2509.26354).

  - This is a design synthesis, not a literature claim; its verified pillars stand: slot-usage gating (2510.15103, confirmed), null-space protection (2410.02355, confirmed), Fisher/SI budget (1612.00796/1703.04200, confirmed), and gate-before-commit necessity (2605.12978, confirmed — the citation of 2608.05810 for pre-commit gating must be dropped as unlocatable, but 2605.12978's explicit-gating recommendation carries that stage alone). The target file /home/user/refa-edge/src/refa_edge/models/refa.py exists and matches the stated dimensions (d_model=64, memory_rank=16, 8 banks, L2-normalized 16-dim address keys, tanh values, delta-rule erase-then-write). THREE MATH CORRECTIONS: (1) as in Claim 2, the projection must be applied on the key side — Delta M <- (I - K0 K0^+) Delta M (left-multiply, 16x16 projector), not 'Delta W_l (I - K0 K0^+)', because REFA reads are q^T M; (2) '(1/2) dtheta^T F dtheta <= eps' is a second-order estimate of old-loss increase, valid only for small steps — present it as an approximate budget, not a guarantee ('exactly zero on span(K0)' remains exact only for the projected linear maps); (3) the gate formula is internally consistent, but note KL(p_{theta+dtheta} || p_theta) requires computing the candidate update before gating, i.e., the gate is a post-hoc accept/reject on trial updates, which the pipeline should state. Cost claims (O(P) ~ kilobytes-to-MBs for 10k-1.5M params, O(R^2) projectors, sub-minute sleep pass on an RTX 2060) are arithmetic-plausible and unverifiable.
