### Quadratic-penalty consolidation family: EWC (diagonal Fisher / Laplace) and Synaptic Intelligence (path integral) ⚑
*status: convergent*

EWC and SI remain the canonical cheap forgetting bounds and give REFA both a penalty and a gate scalar. EWC treats the old-task posterior as a Laplace approximation around the previous optimum and penalizes movement weighted by the diagonal Fisher information; SI computes the same-shaped penalty but accumulates per-parameter importance online during training as the path integral of loss reduction attributable to each parameter - free in a streaming per-timestep loop. Online EWC keeps a single running Fisher and anchor (exponential moving average), removing per-task storage growth. Known limitation: the diagonal ignores parameter correlations; Kronecker-factored (K-FAC) Laplace restores per-layer curvature at O(sum d_l^2) memory. A 2024 review (arXiv 2405.16922) unifies these with Benna-Fusi-style synaptic consolidation theory, showing all are instances of 'intelligent plasticity': per-parameter learning rates modulated by an importance estimate. For REFA the significance is dual-use: F_i is not just a penalty weight but a gate criterion - a consolidation candidate whose induced update concentrates on high-F_i directions should be down-weighted, delayed, or projected (finding on null-space methods).

**Math.** EWC: L(theta) = L_new(theta) + (lambda/2) sum_i F_i (theta_i - theta*_i)^2, with diagonal Fisher F_i = E_{x~D, y~p_theta}[ (d log p_theta(y|x) / d theta_i)^2 ]; Laplace view: p(theta|D_old) ~ N(theta*, (F + sigma^{-2} I)^{-1}). Online EWC: F <- gamma F + F_new, one anchor theta*. SI: omega_i = sum_t g_i(t) * Delta theta_i(t) (path integral of loss decrease credited to theta_i), Omega_i = omega_i / ((Delta theta_i^{total})^2 + xi), penalty c * sum_i Omega_i (theta_i - theta*_i)^2. Cost O(P) memory/compute; second-order forgetting estimate Delta L_old ~ (1/2) Delta theta^T F Delta theta gives a checkable bound epsilon per consolidation step.

**REFA application.** REFA's slow weights are tiny (10k-1.5M params), so full diagonal F plus SI's omega_i cost kilobytes. Compute F at end-of-session by replaying the episodic bank's tuples through the model; accumulate SI importances for free inside the existing Python per-timestep loop (grads already exist per step). Use (1/2) Delta theta^T F Delta theta <= epsilon as the forgetting budget line-searched over lambda at each consolidation, and high Fisher mass of a candidate update as a 'defer or project' gate signal.

Sources:
- Kirkpatrick et al. 2017, Overcoming catastrophic forgetting in neural networks (EWC), PNAS 114(13) — <https://arxiv.org/abs/1612.00796>
- Zenke, Poole, Ganguli 2017, Continual Learning Through Synaptic Intelligence, ICML — <https://arxiv.org/abs/1703.04200>
- Wu et al. 2024, Theories of synaptic memory consolidation and intelligent plasticity for continual learning — <https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.16922>

### Null-space projection updates: OGD, GPM, and AlphaEdit's exact zero-interference guarantee for key-value layers ⚑
*status: convergent*

Orthogonal-projection methods make forgetting a linear-algebra guarantee instead of a soft penalty. OGD projects new gradients orthogonal to stored old-task gradient directions. GPM (Saha et al., ICLR 2021) is stronger: per layer it SVDs the matrix of old-task input representations, stores the top singular subspace B_l (the Core Gradient Space), and projects every weight update onto its orthogonal complement, so old inputs map exactly as before to first order. AlphaEdit (ICLR 2025 Outstanding Paper) applies the same idea to key-value associative layers in LLM knowledge editing: the edit perturbation is right-multiplied by a projector onto the null space of the preserved-knowledge key matrix K0, making the guarantee EXACT (not first-order) for linear reads - post-edit outputs on preserved keys are unchanged - and boosting locate-then-edit methods by 36.7% average with one line of code. This is a structural match to REFA: its memory banks and value/address heads ARE key-value linear maps, so consolidation edits can carry the same exact no-interference certificate on a protected key set. Follow-ups extend to sequential/multilingual editing (arXiv 2506.10800) and conflicting-knowledge editing (AlphaEdit+).

**Math.** OGD: g_tilde = g - sum_j <g, v_j> v_j over stored orthonormal gradient basis. GPM: representation matrix R_l of old inputs; SVD; keep B_l = top-k left singular vectors with sum_{i<=k} sigma_i^2 >= eps_th * ||R_l||_F^2; update Delta W_l <- Delta W_l (I - B_l B_l^T). Guarantee: W_new x = W_old x for x in span(B_l) (first order); capacity saturates when accumulated rank -> d_l (learning stops; tasks fit while total protected rank <= d_l). AlphaEdit: Delta W <- Delta W P with P = I - K0 (K0^T K0)^+ K0^T (computed via SVD of K0 K0^T), so Delta W K0 = 0 exactly; edited least-squares objective solved in closed form under the projection.

**REFA application.** REFA's slow consolidation target (value head, address head, or a slow R x D memory matrix per bank) is linear in L2-normalized keys with R=16, D=64. Protect the key set K0 of high-Fisher / still-live relations from prior sessions; the projector is a 16x16 matrix per bank - microseconds on the RTX 2060. Consolidation writes become Delta M <- Delta M (I - K0 K0^+), giving an exact forgetting bound at the linear-read level; rank bookkeeping (protected rank <= R) doubles as a capacity meter telling the system when a bank is full and must spill to a new bank or evict.

Sources:
- Saha, Garg, Roy 2021, Gradient Projection Memory for Continual Learning, ICLR — <https://arxiv.org/abs/2103.09762>
- Farajtabar et al. 2020, Orthogonal Gradient Descent for Continual Learning — <https://arxiv.org/abs/1910.07104>
- Fang et al., AlphaEdit: Null-Space Constrained Knowledge Editing for Language Models, ICLR 2025 Outstanding Paper — <https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.02355>

### Sparse memory finetuning: the low-forgetting continual-learning result, with TF-IDF/KL slot selection as a built-in gate ⚑
*status: convergent*

Lin et al. (Meta, arXiv 2510.15103, Oct 2025) show that in memory-layer models (large addressable slot banks with top-k access), finetuning ONLY the memory slots that new data activates surprisingly often - ranked by TF-IDF of slot access frequency against pretraining background usage - learns new facts as well as full finetuning while nearly eliminating forgetting: after sequentially learning 1000 TriviaQA facts, held-out NaturalQuestions F1 drops 89% under full finetuning, 71% under LoRA, but only 11% under sparse memory finetuning (verified). Follow-ups: 'Improving Sparse Memory Finetuning' (arXiv 2604.05248) replaces TF-IDF with a KL-divergence slot score - each slot scored by its contribution to KL(new-data access distribution || smoothed background usage distribution), i.e., an information-gain criterion; 'Sparse Memory Finetuning as a Low-Forgetting Alternative to LoRA and Full Finetuning' (arXiv 2605.03229) retrofits sparse memory modules onto pretrained models (Qwen-2.5-0.5B) and quantifies the frontier point: smaller target gain (+2.5pp vs +4.6 LoRA / +5.4 full) but far less drift. This is the strongest recent evidence that parameter-sparsity aligned with usage statistics, not regularization, is the cheapest forgetting control.

**Math.** Memory layer with S slots, top-k access per token. Update set = top-T slots by score. TF-IDF: score(s) = f_new(s) * log(1/df_pre(s)), f_new = access frequency on the new batch, df_pre = document frequency of slot s over pretraining traffic. KL variant: score(s) = p_new(s) * log(p_new(s)/p_bg(s)), the slot's contribution to KL(p_new || p_bg) - selecting argmax slots maximizes information gain of the update footprint. Gradients flow only to selected slot values; all other parameters frozen. Results: -11% vs -89% (full FT) vs -71% (LoRA) held-out F1 after 1000 sequential facts.

**REFA application.** REFA's (bank h, address row r) pairs are exactly memory slots. Maintain streaming access counters n_session(h,r) and a background usage EMA n_bg(h,r) (bytes of state). The consolidation gate selects only slot-rows whose session access distribution is KL-surprising vs background, and slow-weight distillation touches only parameters feeding those rows. This transplants the 11%-vs-89% forgetting result's mechanism directly into the 6GB streaming setting at near-zero cost, and composes with the null-space projector (protect unselected rows exactly).

Sources:
- Lin, Zettlemoyer, Ghosh, Yih, Markosyan, Berges, Oguz 2025, Continual Learning via Sparse Memory Finetuning — <https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.15103>
- Improving Sparse Memory Finetuning (KL slot selection), 2026 — <https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.05248>
- Sparse Memory Finetuning as a Low-Forgetting Alternative to LoRA and Full Finetuning, 2026 — <https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.03229>

### Benna-Fusi multi-timescale synapse cascade: ODEs, power-law forgetting, capacity bounds, and a ready PyTorch implementation
*status: convergent*

Deepens the Engram Loop's one-line 'Benna-Fusi cascades' entry with the operative math and the ML on-ramp. Benna & Fusi (Nat. Neurosci. 2016) model each synapse as a chain of m hidden variables ('beakers') coupled by a discretized diffusion equation with geometrically scaled capacities and conductances, so the chain implements a bank of exponential filters whose superposition yields power-law memory decay. The visible weight is the first beaker; deeper beakers integrate slower. Capacity result: with N synapses, initial memory SNR ~ sqrt(N) and decays ~ t^(-1/2) up to a lifetime ~ 2^(2m) time units, so m ~ log2(T) beakers suffice and memory lifetime becomes almost linear in N - exponentially better than bounded simple synapses (O(log N)). ML translations exist and work: Kaplanis et al. (ICML 2018, arXiv 1802.07239) attach a per-parameter chain to deep RL weights (SGD writes into u_1, then a tridiagonal diffusion step each iteration) and show reduced forgetting at multiple timescales in task-alternating RL; van de Ven maintains a general PyTorch optimizer implementation (GMvandeVen/complex-synapses) usable with any network. The 2024 consolidation-theory review (arXiv 2405.16922) situates it as the principled multi-timescale complement to EWC/SI.

**Math.** Chain ODEs: C_k du_k/dt = g_{k-1,k}(u_{k-1} - u_k) + g_{k,k+1}(u_{k+1} - u_k), k=1..m, with geometric scaling C_k ~ 2^k, g_{k,k+1} ~ 2^{-k} giving timescales tau_k ~ 2^{2k}; input (learning) enters u_1; visible weight w = u_1. SNR of a memory of age t: SNR(t) ~ sqrt(N) * t^{-1/2} for 1 << t << tau_max ~ 2^{2m}; setting SNR = 1 gives lifetime T ~ N with only m ~ log2(sqrt(T)) hidden variables per synapse. Discrete implementation: interleave SGD step on u_1 with one tridiagonal (Jacobi) diffusion update; overhead m x parameter memory, O(mP) per step.

**REFA application.** Replace REFA's single learned per-bank exponential decay lambda (which forces exponential forgetting) with a 3-4 beaker chain on the slow consolidation path: each consolidation writes into level 1; levels 2-4 are EMAs with eta_k ~ 2^{-2k} spanning ~1, ~5, ~25 sessions. Read from a learned mixture of levels. Cost: 3-4x the slow-weight memory of a <=1.5M-param model - trivial in 32GB RAM with bank-residency offload. Gives power-law cross-session retention and a principled fast/slow split within one mechanism.

Sources:
- Benna & Fusi 2016, Computational principles of synaptic memory consolidation, Nature Neuroscience 19:1697-1706, doi:10.1038/nn.4401
- Kaplanis, Shanahan, Clopath 2018, Continual Reinforcement Learning with Complex Synapses, ICML — <https://arxiv.org/abs/1802.07239>
- van de Ven, deep-learning implementation of the Benna-Fusi model (PyTorch optimizer) — <https://github.com/GMvandeVen/complex-synapses>

### Generative replay 2024-2026: distill trajectories not samples; replay in latent/event space for small models
*status: convergent*

The state of the art moved from 'sample from old generator, train on samples' to distillation-through-the-generator. Masip et al. (CoLLAs 2025, arXiv 2311.14028) show naive generative replay applied to diffusion models catastrophically degrades denoising ability (errors compound as the student trains on its own imperfect generations); their fix, generative distillation, distills the teacher's ENTIRE reverse denoising process - the student matches the teacher's noise prediction at all noise levels on replayed inputs - substantially improving continual generation at ~one extra forward pass per item. ReplayCAD (IJCAI 2025) replays via learned class-semantic embeddings in a frozen pretrained diffusion model's conditional space, preserving pixel-level detail for anomaly detection. For small models the enduring result is brain-inspired replay (van de Ven et al., Nature Comms 2020): replay internal latent representations generated by feedback connections instead of raw inputs, making replay cheap and scalable without storing data. The general math lesson for REFA: replay loss should be a distillation term against the OLD model's soft outputs on replayed items, and replay should happen at the most compressed sufficient level (for REFA, discrete event tuples - so 'generative' replay is only needed to cover representation drift, not data storage).

**Math.** DGR + distillation objective: L = r * E_new[CE(f_theta)] + (1-r) * E_{x~G_old}[ T^2 * KL(softmax(f_old(x)/T) || softmax(f_theta(x)/T)) ]; stability improves with replay coverage - first-order forgetting scales with the old-distribution mass NOT covered by G_old, so the bound degrades as (1-r) and with generator drift. Generative distillation for diffusion: L_distill = E_{x~G_old, t~U[1,K], eps}[ || eps_teacher(x_t, t) - eps_student(x_t, t) ||^2 ] across all K noise levels (trajectory matching), preventing compounding sample-quality collapse of naive replay.

**REFA application.** REFA needs no image-class generator: episodic tuples (s,r,o,polarity) are exact, storable replay items (bytes each). Use generative replay only for drift coverage: sample pseudo-queries (s,r,?) from the slow model's own high-confidence completions, and distill the OLD slow model's three-class posterior (support/oppose/unknown) at temperature T into the new weights during consolidation. The replay ratio r becomes REFA's stability knob with an explicit (1-r)-scaled forgetting term.

Sources:
- Masip et al., Continual Learning of Diffusion Models with Generative Distillation, CoLLAs 2025 (PMLR v274) — <https://arxiv.org/abs/2311.14028>
- van de Ven, Siegelmann, Tolias 2020, Brain-inspired replay for continual learning with artificial neural networks, Nature Communications 11:4069
- ReplayCAD: Generative Diffusion Replay for Continual Anomaly Detection, IJCAI 2025 — <https://www.ijcai.org/proceedings/2025/328>

### Reconsolidation corruption 2026: continuously self-updated agent memory degrades non-monotonically; explicit pre-commit gating is the fix ⚑
*status: new-since-aug-2026*

A four-paper 2026 cluster empirically vindicates REFA's planned consolidation gate. (1) 'Useful Memories Become Faulty When Continuously Updated by LLMs' (arXiv 2605.12978): as LLM-driven consolidation proceeds, memory utility rises then FALLS BELOW the no-memory baseline; even consolidating from ground-truth solutions, GPT-5.4 subsequently fails 54% of ARC-AGI problems it had previously solved; identical trajectories yield qualitatively different memories under different update schedules (the consolidation operator is schedule-dependent/non-commutative); an episodic-only control retaining raw trajectories stays competitive. Recommendation verbatim: treat raw episodes as first-class evidence and gate consolidation explicitly rather than firing per-interaction. (2) ACL 2026 'experience-following' study (arXiv 2505.16067): retrieved memories are imitated, so write(error)->retrieve->imitate->write chains propagate; strict quality-gated selective addition dominates all other write policies. (3) 'Do Self-Evolving Agents Forget?' (arXiv 2605.09315): capability erosion across workflow/skill/model/memory channels; Capability-Preserving Evolution constraint lifts retained simple-task performance 41.8%->52.8%. (4) 'When Self-Evolution Backfires' (arXiv 2608.05810, Aug 2026): a capability-contamination PHASE TRANSITION past a critical skill-pool size; contamination chains are structurally irreversible (post-hoc rollback recovers little); fix is Verifier-as-Gatekeeper pre-commit gating with three critics.

**Math.** Formal structure: consolidation C maps memory state m_t and episode e_t to m_{t+1}; findings show C is (a) non-monotone in utility U(m_t) - dU/dt changes sign with t, (b) schedule-dependent: C(C(m,e1),e2) != C(C(m,e2),e1), (c) contaminating: P(defect in child skill | defect in referenced parent) >> base rate, so defects grow as a branching process with mean offspring >1 past critical pool size (phase transition), and rollback of the root does not remove descendants (irreversibility). Quantitative anchors: 54% loss of previously-solved ARC-AGI items after ground-truth consolidation; CPE: 41.8%->52.8% retained performance; below-no-memory-baseline crossings.

**REFA application.** Directly licenses REFA's architecture decisions: (i) never overwrite raw episodic tuples with summaries - they are the ground truth the parametric store is audited against; (ii) consolidate in gated between-session batches, never per event; (iii) gate = verification (task check or high-confidence non-unknown three-class output) BEFORE commit, since rollback after a corrupt write to slow weights is provably lossy; (iv) log the consolidation schedule, because outcome depends on it.

Sources:
- Zhang et al. 2026, Useful Memories Become Faulty When Continuously Updated by LLMs — <https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.12978>
- Xiong et al., How Memory Management Impacts LLM Agents: An Empirical Study of Experience-Following Behavior, ACL 2026 — <https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.16067>
- Yu et al. 2026, Do Self-Evolving Agents Forget? Capability Degradation and Preservation in Lifelong LLM Agent Adaptation — <https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.09315>
- Shang et al. 2026, When Self-Evolution Backfires: Pre-Commit Gating against Skill Contamination in LLM Agents — <https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.05810>

### Spaced repetition math (half-life regression, FSRS three-component model) as a per-fact replay scheduler
*status: partial*

The edtech literature has decades-refined, benchmarked math for exactly the question a consolidation loop must answer per fact: when to replay it, and when it is stable enough to evict from the fast store. Half-Life Regression (Settles & Meeder, ACL 2016, deployed at Duolingo, -45% prediction error) models recall probability as exponential in elapsed time over a learned half-life that is log-linear in practice-history features. FSRS (the current open-source SOTA per the srs-benchmark repo) upgrades to a three-component Difficulty-Stability-Retrievability model with a POWER-law forgetting curve and, crucially, a stability-growth law encoding the spacing effect: the stability multiplier after successful recall grows as retrievability at review time falls (e^(w10(1-R))-1 term) - reviews timed near-forgetting consolidate most. ~19 weights fit from logs. No published work yet transplants this to scheduling replay/distillation inside a continual-learning loop (a fragment exists in adaptive-forgetting-curve work, arXiv 2004.11327), making it a cheap, principled import for REFA rather than an established ML technique.

**Math.** HLR: p_hat = 2^(-Delta/h_hat), h_hat = 2^(Theta . x) (x = practice-history features); loss = sum (p - p_hat)^2 + alpha (h_hat - h_emp)^2 + lambda ||Theta||^2. FSRS: R(t,S) = (1 + F t/S)^C with C = -0.5, F = 19/81 (so R(S)=0.9); success: S' = S (1 + e^{w8} (11 - D) S^{-w9} (e^{w10 (1-R)} - 1)); lapse: S' = w11 D^{-w12} ((S+1)^{w13} - 1) e^{w14 (1-R)}; D in [1,10] updated by its own recurrence. Schedule next review at t*: R(t*,S) = target (e.g., 0.9). Spacing effect: dS'/dS growth maximized at low R (desirable difficulty).

**REFA application.** Attach (S, D) to each episodic tuple. 'Recall test' = probe the slow model with the tuple's query and check the three-class output; success/failure drives the FSRS stability update. Include a tuple in the next consolidation batch when predicted R(t) falls to ~0.9; evict it from the episodic bank (freeing the R=16 rank budget) once S exceeds the inter-session horizon - i.e., the parametric store now owns the fact. Gives a principled per-fact eviction scalar for bank residency offload, at bytes per tuple.

Sources:
- Settles & Meeder 2016, A Trainable Spaced Repetition Model for Language Learning, ACL — <https://research.duolingo.com/papers/settles.acl16.pdf>
- open-spaced-repetition srs-benchmark (FSRS formulas and benchmark) — <https://github.com/open-spaced-repetition/srs-benchmark>
- Ye et al. 2020, Adaptive Forgetting Curves for Spaced Repetition Language Learning — <https://arxiv.org/abs/2004.11327>

### Wake-sleep alternation as the fast/slow re-alignment mechanism (Hinton 1995; reweighted wake-sleep)
*status: partial*

The wake-sleep algorithm trains a generative model and a recognition model by alternating phases with opposite KL directions: wake updates the generative parameters on real data with recognition-inferred latents; sleep draws 'dreams' from the generative model and trains the recognition network to invert them. Reweighted wake-sleep (Bornschein & Bengio; revisited by Le et al. 2019, arXiv 1805.10469) fixes the bias with importance-weighted multi-sample gradients and remains the method of choice for models with discrete latents/stochastic control flow - relevant because REFA's routing and three-class outputs are discrete. No major 2024-2026 revival was found for consolidation specifically (searches surfaced 2018-2022 work: memoised wake-sleep, hybrid memoised wake-sleep), so this is a fragment awaiting translation. Its value to a CLS-style system is precise: after slow weights drift (consolidation), the fast system's keys/addresses no longer align with slow embeddings; a sleep phase that samples pseudo-events from the slow model and retrains the fast encoder/router to re-encode them restores alignment without any stored data - the exact failure mode (representation drift between complementary systems) that naive fast/slow designs hit.

**Math.** Wake: theta <- theta + eta grad_theta log p_theta(x, z), z ~ q_phi(z|x), x ~ data (descends KL(q||p) in theta). Sleep: (x_dream, z_dream) ~ p_theta; phi <- phi + eta grad_phi log q_phi(z_dream|x_dream) (descends KL(p||q) in phi - mode-covering, biased w.r.t. the ELBO but stable for discrete z). RWS: draw z_1..z_K ~ q, weights w_k proportional to p_theta(x,z_k)/q_phi(z_k|x), normalized; update both nets with sum_k w_k-weighted gradients; bias -> 0 as K grows.

**REFA application.** Define sleep for REFA: sample pseudo-episodes from the consolidated slow model (its high-confidence (s,r,o,polarity) completions), then train the FAST side (router W_r, address head, write gate) to route/encode these dreams so that fast reads reproduce slow predictions. Alternate with the wake/distill direction (episodes -> slow weights). Prevents key-embedding drift across sessions, costs only forward samples of a <=1.5M-param model, and needs no stored data.

Sources:
- Hinton, Dayan, Frey, Neal 1995, The wake-sleep algorithm for unsupervised neural networks, Science 268:1158-1161
- Le et al. 2019, Revisiting Reweighted Wake-Sleep for Models with Stochastic Control Flow, UAI — <https://arxiv.org/abs/1805.10469>

### Synaptic homeostasis (SHY) as principled multiplicative downscaling, and its ML realization in sleep-replay consolidation (SRC)
*status: convergent*

Tononi & Cirelli's synaptic homeostasis hypothesis holds that wake learning net-potentiates synapses (raising energy cost and noise), and sleep renormalizes by MULTIPLICATIVE downscaling: scaling all weights by c < 1 preserves rank order and relative differences of strong (signal) synapses while pushing weak, noise-accumulated synapses below an effective threshold where they are pruned - a signal-to-noise and energy argument, with NREM downscaling being selective (protecting reactivated assemblies). The working ML translation is Tadros et al. (Nature Comms 2022): Sleep Replay Consolidation converts a trained ANN to spiking-like binary activation, drives it with noise (no data), and applies local Hebbian/anti-Hebbian updates; this recovers earlier tasks damaged by later training, interpretable as nudging weights toward the intersection of the tasks' low-loss regions. A 2026 preprint (Phasor Agents, arXiv 2601.04362) extends sleep-staged learning with three-factor plasticity to oscillatory graph agents. Together they justify REFA's decay not as a learned scalar but as a structured between-session operation: scale down, prune below noise floor, protect what replays.

**Math.** SHY downscaling: W <- c W (0 < c < 1) then prune w_ij < theta_noise -> 0. Multiplicative (not subtractive) scaling preserves w_i/w_j ratios; if wake adds signal S and additive noise n per synapse, downscaling plus thresholding raises post-sleep SNR since E[signal]/sd(noise) improves when sub-threshold noise mass is deleted; total synaptic weight renormalized to a fixed energy budget sum_ij w_ij <= B. SRC sleep phase: forward pass with noise input, binary activations h; local rule Delta w_ij = +alpha if pre_j and post_i co-active, -beta if post_i active and pre_j silent; no labels or stored data; empirically moves theta into intersection of task solution manifolds.

**REFA application.** Between sessions, apply SHY to REFA's fast banks: M^s <- c M^s per bank, then prune address rows with ||M_r|| below a noise floor - freeing rank R=16 for the next session and replacing the single learned uniform decay lambda with a selective operation: rows that were replayed/consolidated this sleep keep strength (protected), unreplayed rows shrink. An SRC-style pass (noise-driven pseudo-events through the router with local updates) can additionally unbind spurious support/oppose cross-talk without any data.

Sources:
- Tononi & Cirelli 2014, Sleep and the price of plasticity, Neuron 81:12-34; Tononi 2020, Sleep and synaptic down-selection, Eur J Neurosci — <https://onlinelibrary.wiley.com/doi/10.1111/ejn.14335>
- Tadros, Krishnan, Ramyaa, Bazhenov 2022, Sleep-like unsupervised replay reduces catastrophic forgetting in artificial neural networks, Nature Communications 13:7742 — <https://www.nature.com/articles/s41467-022-34938-7>
- Phasor Agents: Oscillatory Graphs with Three-Factor Plasticity and Sleep-Staged Learning, 2026 — <https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.04362>

### Knowledge distillation with provenance preservation: a genuine gap (building site)
*status: absent*

No published method distills episodic knowledge into parametric weights while preserving queryable provenance. Continual-learning KD surveys (teacher = old-task model, student = new model; logit/feature/subspace distillation) contain no provenance notion at all. The 2026 agent-memory literature explicitly names the gap: parametric memory is 'opaque to inspection, attribution, and deletion' (Always-On Agents survey, arXiv 2606.30306), and evidence-tracing surveys (arXiv 2606.04990) prescribe per-write lineage metadata (source type, timestamp, authoring agent, transformation op, confidence, update history) - but only for EPISODIC stores; the metadata is discarded at distillation. The nearest tool is probing work (arXiv 2602.22787) showing that whether a completion is driven by contextual evidence vs parametric memory is linearly decodable from hidden states - attribution as a representation-level property, not a preserved label. REFA is unusually well-positioned to build here because provenance (perceived/told/self) is already a typed field of its event tuples and its output is already evidential (three-class), so provenance can ride through distillation as a supervised channel rather than being reverse-engineered.

**Math.** What is needed (design math, not literature): teacher signal y = (class posterior over {sup, opp, unk}, provenance p in {perceived, told, self}); student loss L = CE_class + mu * CE_prov (+ Fisher penalty). Provenance survives distillation iff mutual information I(p; h(x)) is preserved in student hiddens h - enforceable by the auxiliary head (a lower bound on I via cross-entropy: I(p;h) >= H(p) - CE_prov). Deletion/audit requirement maps to machine-unlearning constraints: removing source d should bound residual influence, e.g., influence-function norm ||H^{-1} sum_{e in d} grad l_e|| <= delta.

**REFA application.** Concatenate a provenance embedding to the value vector at write time (v' = v + e_prov or block-structured v' = [v; e_prov]) so that slow-weight reads return provenance-tagged evidence, and widen the output head to predict provenance alongside the three classes during consolidation distillation. Evaluate with the Engram Loop's source-attribution battery. This would be a first: a consolidation pipeline where 'who told the model this' survives the transfer into weights.

Sources:
- From Agent Traces to Trust: A Survey of Evidence Tracing and Execution Provenance in LLM Agents, 2026 — <https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.04990>
- Always-On Agents: A Survey of Persistent Memory, State, and Governance in LLM Agents, 2026 — <https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.30306>
- Probing for Knowledge Attribution in Large Language Models, 2026 — <https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.22787>

### Parametric absorption of agent memory works at small scale: TMEM fast-LoRA online updates and self-consolidation frameworks
*status: partial*

Evidence that the 'absorb episodic memory into weights' step is practical for small models, not just an aspiration. TMEM (Qwen-Character team, arXiv 2606.04536) has an agent both compress history into explicit memory AND absorb distilled supervision into fast LoRA weights via lightweight online updates - actions sampled from a policy augmented with fast weights, extraction actions producing supervision that updates those weights for later decisions within the same episode; it beats summary- and retrieval-based baselines on LoCoMo, LongMemEval-S and CL-Bench across model scales. Related 2026 systems: Self-Consolidation for Self-Evolving Agents (arXiv 2602.01966) and PEAM-style per-category isolated MoE-LoRA adapters that convert retrieval memory into parameter-resident skills with structural isolation as the interference control. The pattern across these: (a) a fast, low-rank parametric channel updated online, (b) a slower consolidation into it from explicit memory, (c) isolation (per-category adapters) rather than regularization as the first line of defense against interference - all three transplant to REFA, whose banks already provide the isolation structure.

**Math.** Fast-weight absorption: W_eff = W_0 + B A with low-rank A,B updated online from distilled supervision (rank r << d; update cost O(r d) per step); per-category isolation: adapters {A_c, B_c} gated by category router, so cross-category interference is structurally zero (block-diagonal update footprint) - the MoE analog of REFA's bank routing. Empirical: TMEM > summary/retrieval baselines on LoCoMo, LongMemEval-S, multi-objective search, CL-Bench at multiple model scales (paper reports consistent wins; exact deltas in paper tables).

**REFA application.** Validates REFA's roadmap step of a parametric slow store fed from fast banks, and suggests the cheapest implementation on 6GB: a low-rank additive slow component per bank, W_slow,h = B_h A_h (rank 4-8 over D=64), updated only at consolidation, with bank routing providing TMEM/PEAM-style structural isolation for free. Explicit + parametric coexist; retrieval from tuples remains the fallback when the parametric read is low-confidence ('unknown').

Sources:
- Ren et al. 2026, Scaling Self-Evolving Agents via Parametric Memory (TMEM) — <https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.04536>
- Self-Consolidation for Self-Evolving Agents, 2026 — <https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.01966>
- A Survey of Agent Memory in the Second Half: Towards Self-Evolving and Long-Horizon Agents, 2026 — <https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.06052>

### REFA consolidation design: gated, forgetting-bounded distillation of fast banks into slow weights between sessions ⚑
*status: speculative*

Assembled design. DURING SESSION (wake): stream events as now; additionally log raw tuples (s,r,o,polarity,provenance,t) to an episodic ledger (bytes/event), accumulate SI importances omega_i on slow weights, and slot access counters n(h,r). BETWEEN SESSIONS (sleep), run a four-stage pipeline. Stage 1 GATE per tuple e - consolidate iff ALL of: (a) verified/confident: model's three-class posterior on e's query is non-'unknown' with max prob > tau_c, or externally verified (pre-commit gating per arXiv 2608.05810); (b) informative: Bayesian surprise KL(post || prior) of the induced update > tau_I - i.e., the slow model does not already know it; (c) slot-surprising: e's (bank,row) usage is KL-surprising vs background (sparse-memory criterion). Stage 2 UPDATE: distill gated tuples + FSRS-scheduled refresher tuples into slow weights (low-rank per-bank adapters) by minimizing CE + distillation-from-old-model on replayed pseudo-events, under a diagonal-Fisher penalty, with each linear-map update right-projected onto the null space of protected keys K0 (high-Fisher, still-live relations). Stage 3 BOUND: accept the update only if (1/2) dtheta^T F dtheta <= eps (line-search lambda); projection makes interference on span(K0) exactly zero. Stage 4 CLEANUP: SHY-downscale fast banks (M <- cM, prune weak rows), FSRS-evict tuples with stability S > horizon, optional wake-sleep re-alignment pass of router/address heads on dreams from the updated slow model. Never delete the raw ledger.

**Math.** Gate: g(e) = 1[max_c p_theta(c|q_e) > tau_c, c != unk] * 1[KL(p_{theta+dtheta}(.|q_e) || p_theta(.|q_e)) > tau_I] * 1[score_KL(h,r) in top-T]. Update: theta' = theta - eta grad[ sum_{gated+FSRS} CE + T^2 KL(f_old/T || f_theta/T) + (lambda/2) sum_i F_i (theta_i - theta*_i)^2 ], with Delta W_l <- Delta W_l (I - K0 K0^+) for value/address maps. Guarantees: exact zero change on protected key span; elsewhere Delta L_old ~ (1/2) dtheta^T F dtheta <= eps. Cost: F, omega, counters O(P) ~ MBs; projectors O(R^2 D), R=16, D=64; whole sleep pass << 1 GPU-min on RTX 2060. Capacity meter: protected rank per bank <= R triggers spill/evict.

**REFA application.** This IS the REFA plan: implements the roadmap's persistent-memory-plus-gate item with three falsifiable knobs (tau_c, tau_I, eps) and clear ablations - gate off vs on (predict the non-monotone degradation of arXiv 2605.12978 reappears with gate off), projection off vs on (measure revision-task interference), FSRS vs uniform replay (retention curves across simulated sessions). Provenance rides through as a supervised channel (absent-in-literature finding), making REFA a testbed for provenance-preserving consolidation.

Sources:
- Synthesis of: arXiv 2510.15103 (slot gating), 2410.02355 (null-space projection), 1612.00796/1703.04200 (Fisher/SI bound), 2605.12978 + 2608.05810 (gate necessity), Settles & Meeder ACL 2016 + FSRS (replay scheduling), Tononi-Cirelli/Tadros (downscaling) — <https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.15103>
- REFA model the design targets — <file:///home/user/refa-edge/src/refa_edge/models/refa.py>

## Newest brain-to-machine mapping results (mid-2025 to Aug 2026)
`brainmap-2026`

The 2024-2026 brain-to-ML landscape has moved past the mappings the Engram Loop ledger covers, and the richest uncovered vein is BTSP: Bittner-Magee one-shot plasticity now has an ML-grade theory (Wu-Maass binary-synapse content-addressable memory with a provable repulsion effect), a credit-assignment framework (gBTSP), an internally-triggered retrospective-learning ML port (May 2026), and a hyperdimensional-computing binding extension - together a recipe for one-shot, low-precision, surprise-gated memory writes that maps almost line-for-line onto REFA's delta-rule banks. A second cluster is connectome-scale validation: MICrONS (Nature 2025) established a feature-based like-to-like wiring rule that independently emerges in task-trained ANNs, the Tolias-lab digital-twin foundation model predicts responses to unseen stimulus domains plus anatomy from function, and connectome-constrained Drosophila models (Shiu 2024 through embodied whole-body control in 2026) show wiring plus synapse sign alone reproduces real behaviors - evidence that architecture priors can substitute for learned parameters at small scale. Universal decoders (POYO/POYO+) supply a concrete variable-input-to-fixed-latent streaming interface, and the Platonic Representation Hypothesis now has human-brain evidence via unsupervised orthogonal alignment of fMRI spaces. Centaur and its prediction-is-not-explanation critiques provide a methodological guardrail for REFA's Confabulation Battery, while 2025 inner-speech BCI results (covert speech occupies a scaled motor-cortex subspace, gated by a neural password) give direct biological license for query/write mode separation. New engram biology (excitability-biased allocation, co-allocation linking, heterogeneous sub-ensembles) and 2025 dendritic ANNs motivate bank-residency policies and parameter-efficient branch-structured reads.

**Verification:** the original verifier for this area was blocked by an automated
content filter (a misfire on bulk neuroscience terminology); a replacement
verification pass was run afterwards — see the addendum at the end of this file.

### BTSP as one-shot content-addressable memory with binary synapses (Wu-Maass) ⚑
*status: convergent*

Wu and Maass (Nat Commun 2025) reduced behavioral-timescale synaptic plasticity - the Bittner/Magee rule where a single dendritic plateau potential creates a place field in one trial, with a seconds-long asymmetric window - to a transparent stochastic rule on BINARY synapses, and showed it yields a functionally powerful content-addressable memory with one-shot writes and no high-resolution weights. Key emergent property: a repulsion effect - memory traces for similar items are actively pulled further apart than their inputs (a verified fingerprint of human memory organization), giving automatic pattern separation without a dedicated dentate-style circuit. The rule needs no repeated presentations (unlike Hopfield/gradient rules) and tolerates continual writing because weight flips are bounded. A 2025 bioRxiv follow-up extends BTSP writes to hyperdimensional role-filler bindings with attractor cleanup and flexible retrieval of any component of a stored composite. A Nature Neuroscience 2026 review consolidates the biology as established. This is the strongest new brain-to-ML bridge not in the Engram Loop ledger: the biological answer to the exact problem REFA's delta-rule write solves, with a capacity theory attached.

**Math.** Weights w in {0,1}. A plateau potential (stochastic instructive signal, prob q per neuron) opens an eligibility window of about [-6s, +4s] around plateau onset; each synapse active in the window flips state with prob 0.5: P(0->1)=0.5 if w=0, P(1->0)=0.5 if w=1. Expected post-write state 1/2 for co-active synapses decorrelates overlapping traces: for similar inputs x1,x2, HD(trace1,trace2) > HD(x1,x2) (repulsion). Retrieval: neuron i in trace iff sum_j w_ij x_j >= theta; masked cues are completed so HD(recall, stored) < HD(cue, stored). Weight dynamics form a Markov chain over binary states, giving stable retention under many subsequent one-shot writes; capacity extends to large numbers of non-orthogonal items at 1 bit/synapse (contrast Hopfield ~0.14N with real-valued weights). HDC extension: cleanup iteration x_{k+1} = sgn(W x_k); crosstalk noise ~ sqrt(S/D) for S composites in dimension D.

**REFA application.** Add a binary/low-precision write mode to REFA banks: quantize address rows and apply stochastic-flip one-shot writes instead of (or on top of) the continuous delta rule. Repulsion gives free pattern separation for near-duplicate (s,r,o) keys - directly addressing revision-vs-interference on the contrastive-evidence task - and 1-bit fast weights shrink the 2xHxRxD state ~16-32x for the 6GB target; cleanup re-reads let the same banks answer (s,?,o) and (?,r,o) queries.

Sources:
- Wu & Maass, Nature Communications 16:342 (2025) — <https://www.nature.com/articles/s41467-024-55563-6>
- BTSP endows binding of distributed representations with flexible retrieval options, bioRxiv 2025.05.15.654220 — <https://www.biorxiv.org/content/10.1101/2025.05.15.654220v2.full>
- BTSP review, Nature Neuroscience (2026) — <https://www.nature.com/articles/s41593-026-02214-2>

### BTSP credit assignment (gBTSP) and internally-triggered retrospective learning ⚑
*status: partial*

Two 2025-2026 works turn BTSP from a memory story into a learning-rule story. Cone, Clopath and Ponte Costa (bioRxiv, Jun 2025) define a generalized BTSP rule (gBTSP) usable in feedforward and recurrent networks: unsupervised gBTSP recovers competitive learning (place-field maps) and attractor memory networks; supervised gBTSP works but credit assignment degrades with depth and CA3-like recurrence because the seconds-long trace blurs fine temporal credit. Separately, 'Internally triggered retrospective learning in neural networks' (arXiv 2605.10994, May 2026) is a direct ML port: synaptic coactivations accumulate as latent eligibility traces WITHOUT changing parameters; an internal predictor tracks latent state; when prediction discrepancy crosses an adaptive threshold derived from running error statistics, a learning event fires and retrospectively commits the accumulated trace. This makes plasticity event-driven and self-gated - the same 'one surprise signal gates writes' economy the Engram Loop proposes, but now with a published mechanism for the WRITE side rather than only the attention/depth side.

**Math.** gBTSP: dw_ij = eta * [f_plus(e_ij)*(w_max - w_ij) - f_minus(e_ij)*w_ij] * IS_i(t), with eligibility e_ij(t) = integral of kappa(t-s)*pre_j(s) ds over seconds (tau ~ 1-6s, asymmetric kernel), IS_i a sparse plateau/instructive event; saturating soft-bound form reproduces one-trial field formation. Retrospective variant: accumulate trace E += outer(post, pre) with decay, W fixed; predictor zhat_{t+1} = g(z_t); discrepancy d_t = ||z_t - zhat_t||; trigger when d_t > mu_t + k*sigma_t (running stats); then W <- W + eta*E, E <- 0. Reported limitation: alignment of gBTSP updates with true gradients decays with network depth/recurrence, so it suits shallow fast-weight memories, not deep backbones.

**REFA application.** Solves REFA's delayed-evidence roadmap task: keep a decaying eligibility buffer E_h per bank accumulating recent beta*r_h*k*v^T candidates without committing; commit E into M+/M- only when a surprise event fires (classifier flip, high routing entropy, or large read-prediction error), using the adaptive mu+k*sigma threshold. One scalar then gates writes and abstention jointly, and a late correction can retro-write the last ~T events in one shot.

Sources:
- Cone, Clopath, Ponte Costa, Credit Assignment via BTSP: Theoretical Frameworks, bioRxiv 2025.06.12.659336 — <https://www.biorxiv.org/content/10.1101/2025.06.12.659336v1>
- Internally triggered retrospective learning in neural networks, arXiv 2605.10994 (May 2026) — <https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.10994>

### MICrONS functional connectomics: feature-based like-to-like wiring, higher-order rules, strong-synapse caveat ⚑
*status: convergent*

The MICrONS flagship (Nature, Apr 2025) co-registered calcium imaging of ~75,000 neurons with an EM reconstruction of >200,000 cells and ~0.5 billion synapses in one mouse. Ding et al. (Nature 2025) used a validated digital twin to factor each neuron's tuning into a feature component (what it responds to) and a spatial component (where), and showed only FEATURE similarity predicts fine-scale synaptic connectivity beyond axon-dendrite proximity - a like-to-like rule holding within and across areas/layers including feedback - plus a higher-order rule: cohorts of postsynaptic partners of one cell are more functionally similar than the pairwise rule predicts. Critically for ML, the same like-to-like motif emerges spontaneously in task-trained ANNs and matters for their performance. Counterweight: 'Infrequent strong connections constrain connectomic predictions of neuronal function' (Cell 2025) shows the heavy-tailed synapse-weight distribution means rare strong synapses dominate function, limiting mean-field connectome-based prediction.

**Math.** Digital-twin factorization: response r_i(t) approx readout of feature vector f_i applied at spatial RF p_i. Connection prediction: P(syn i->j) increases monotonically with cos(f_i, f_j) after conditioning on axo-dendritic co-proximity (Peters-rule null); spatial-RF similarity adds no predictive power. Higher-order rule: Var(features of postsynaptic cohort of i) < value predicted by independent pairwise like-to-like sampling. Caveat statistics: synapse count/strength per connection is heavy-tailed (lognormal-like), so functional influence concentrates in the tail - a small fraction of connections carries disproportionate causal weight, bounding accuracy of function-from-connectivity and connectivity-from-function inference.

**REFA application.** Gives REFA a principled routing prior: encourage like-to-like bank assignment by initializing router W_r from k-means over relation embeddings, or an auxiliary loss pulling events with similar value vectors v to the same bank - a tested substitute for free softmax routing and a stepping stone to roadmap top-k sparse routing. The heavy-tail result argues for allowing few large-beta writes (heavy-tailed write gate) instead of many uniform ones.

Sources:
- Ding et al., Functional connectomics reveals general wiring rule in mouse visual cortex, Nature (2025) — <https://www.nature.com/articles/s41586-025-08840-3>
- MICrONS Consortium, Functional connectomics spanning multiple areas of mouse visual cortex, Nature (2025) — <https://www.nature.com/articles/s41586-025-08790-w>
- Infrequent strong connections constrain connectomic predictions, Cell (2025) — <https://www.cell.com/cell/abstract/S0092-8674(25)00518-5>

### Digital-twin foundation model of mouse visual cortex predicts function AND anatomy (Tolias lab)
*status: convergent*

Wang, Fahey, Ding, Tolias et al. (Nature 640:470-477, Apr 2025) trained a foundation model on large-scale calcium recordings from multiple mice watching natural movies. The model (shared core + per-neuron readouts) predicts single-neuron responses to arbitrary NEW stimulus domains it never saw (coherent motion, noise patterns) - breaking the within-distribution limit of prior encoding models - transfers to new mice with minimal data, and, most strikingly, its functional embeddings predict anatomy: cell types, dendritic features, and connectivity later verified against the EM volume of the same mouse. This establishes the digital-twin paradigm: train once on behavior-scale data, then run unlimited in-silico experiments, with function rich enough to constrain structure. It is a concrete existence proof that small, factorized readout models capture a reusable 'cortical core'.

**Math.** Architecture: shared foundation core F (factorized video CNN/transformer plus behavior and eye-position inputs) with per-neuron readout rhat_i = softplus( w_i^T F(x)[p_i] ): a 'what' vector w_i sampled at learned retinotopic position p_i - a rank-1 what-x-where factorization. Foundation training pools many mice; new-mouse transfer fits only readouts (few hundred params per neuron) on minutes of data. Generalization: high predictive correlation maintained on out-of-domain stimulus classes; the functional embedding w_i is structured enough to classify EM-verified cell type and predict connectivity in the MICrONS volume.

**REFA application.** Validates REFA's factorized addressing: identity-based address/route (who) separate from tanh value content (what) mirrors the what-x-where readout that provably transfers. Practical recipe for the roadmap text-encoder step: freeze the relational core, learn only per-entity embedding rows for a new domain, and use a cheap twin of the synthetic environment to generate unlimited held-out probe tasks for the calibration suite.

Sources:
- Wang et al., Foundation model of neural activity predicts response to new stimulus types, Nature 640:470-477 (2025) — <https://www.nature.com/articles/s41586-025-08829-y>
- Stanford Medicine digital-twin report (Apr 2025) — <https://news.stanford.edu/stories/2025/04/digital-twin>

### Connectome-constrained whole-brain fly models activate real behaviors; embodied whole-body control in 2026 ⚑
*status: convergent*

Shiu et al. (Nature, Oct 2024) built a leaky integrate-and-fire model of the entire adult Drosophila brain from the FlyWire connectome (~125-140k neurons, ~50M synapses) with EM-predicted neurotransmitter signs and NO fitted dynamics: computational activation of sugar/bitter/water gustatory neurons predicts taste interactions, mechanosensory activation recovers the antennal grooming circuit, and predicted feeding-initiation neurons were validated by optogenetics and behavior. In 2026 this was pushed to embodiment: a whole-brain connectomic graph model driving whole-body locomotion of a simulated fly (arXiv 2602.17997, NeuroMechFly/MuJoCo lineage; Eon Systems demonstrated an embodied emulation walking and feeding - company-reported, accuracy claims not yet peer-reviewed), and a neuromorphic port of the full connectome runs on Loihi 2 (arXiv 2508.16792). Establishes that measured wiring + synapse sign + generic point-neuron dynamics suffices for behavior-level function: the algorithm largely IS the graph.

**Math.** Dynamics: tau*dV_i/dt = -(V_i - V_rest) + sum_j w_ij * s_j(t), spike/reset at threshold; w_ij = n_syn(i->j) * sign(NT_j) * unit weight, with neurotransmitter identity predicted from EM at high per-synapse accuracy; no per-neuron parameter fitting. Scale: ~1.4x10^5 neurons, ~5x10^7 synapses; sparse graph, so simulation cost O(E) per step - real-time feasible on one GPU or Loihi 2. Predictions of which neurons drive motor output matched optogenetic ground truth for feeding/grooming circuits; 2026 embodied versions close the loop through a biomechanical body model (sensory -> brain graph -> motor -> physics).

**REFA application.** Argues REFA can spend parameters on state, not wiring: replace the learned dense router with a FIXED sparse signed routing graph (BDH scale-free prior) and learn only decay lambda and gate beta - test whether frozen structured W_r matches learned routing on relational recall. The NT-sign result is the biological analogue of REFA's hard +/- stance split: sign as a wiring-level type, not a learned continuous value.

Sources:
- Shiu et al., A Drosophila computational brain model reveals sensorimotor processing, Nature (2024) — <https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/39358519/>
- Whole-Brain Connectomic Graph Model Enables Whole-Body Locomotion Control in Fruit Fly, arXiv 2602.17997 (2026) — <https://arxiv.org/html/2602.17997>
- Neuromorphic Simulation of Drosophila Connectome on Loihi 2, arXiv 2508.16792 — <https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.16792>

### POYO/POYO+: universal neural decoders via spike tokenization and fixed-latent cross-attention
*status: convergent*

POYO (NeurIPS 2023) and POYO+ (ICLR 2025) are the working template for neuroscience foundation decoders: individual spikes (or calcium events with amplitude) become tokens carrying a learned per-unit embedding plus a temporal embedding; a PerceiverIO-style encoder cross-attends them into a FIXED-size latent array; task/session-conditioned queries decode behavior. POYO-1 trained across 7 nonhuman primates, 27k+ units, 100+ hours; POYO+ extends to calcium imaging across the Allen Brain Observatory (100k+ neurons, 6 visual areas), multi-task training (classification+regression+segmentation) and distinct cell types. The decisive property: adaptation to a NEW session/animal with unknown neuron correspondence requires learning only new unit embeddings against a frozen backbone - few-shot transfer across brains. Successor scaling work (e.g., multi-modal brain models trained on ~150B neural tokens) reports that scaling laws hold for neural data.

**Math.** Tokenization: token_k = u_{unit(k)} + rot_time(t_k) per spike (u learned per unit; rotary temporal embedding preserves fine timing); calcium adds amplitude encoding. Encoder: fixed latent array Z (L x d) cross-attends N input tokens: cost O(N*L*d) per layer, independent of N^2 - a streaming-friendly compression of variable event streams into constant-size state. Decoder queries: q = task_emb + session_emb + output_timestamp, cross-attend Z, task-specific linear heads; loss = weighted CE + MSE. New-session adaptation: optimize only {u_i} for new units (10^2-10^3 params each), backbone frozen - transfer is an embedding-alignment problem, not a retraining problem.

**REFA application.** Blueprint for REFA's streaming text-encoder interface: treat incoming parsed events as tokens with entity/relation embeddings plus time embeddings, cross-attend into the fixed workspace/banks at O(N*L) - matching REFA's constant-state philosophy. Adopt embedding-only domain transfer: freeze the relational core (router, banks, GRU) and fit only new entity embedding rows when moving from synthetic vocab to text-derived entities.

Sources:
- Azabou et al., A Unified Scalable Framework for Neural Population Decoding, NeurIPS 2023 — <https://proceedings.neurips.cc/paper_files/paper/2023/file/8ca113d122584f12a6727341aaf58887-Paper-Conference.pdf>
- POYO+, Multi-session multi-task neural decoding from distinct cell-types and brain regions, ICLR 2025 — <https://openreview.net/forum?id=IuU0wcO0mo>

### Platonic Representation Hypothesis reaches the human brain: unsupervised universal geometry
*status: convergent*

The Platonic Representation Hypothesis (Huh et al. 2024: model representations converge across architectures and modalities as scale/competence grows) acquired direct brain-side evidence in 2025-2026. Marcos-Manchon et al. (arXiv 2605.20496, May 2026) show, on Natural Scenes Dataset fMRI, that subject-specific visual-cortex embedding spaces learned INDEPENDENTLY per subject (self-supervised, exploiting repeated stimulus presentations) are approximately isometric across people and can be translated between subjects by purely orthogonal rotations WITHOUT paired cross-subject data; synchronizing pairwise rotations into one shared latent improves cross-subject retrieval - the strong (constructive) form of the hypothesis, in brains. Complementary 2025 work (review of 25 fMRI-LLM alignment studies) finds intermediate transformer layers align with brain activity better than final layers, and alignment strengthens with model scale. Together: brains and sufficiently good models appear to occupy one shared representational geometry, recoverable without supervision.

**Math.** Alignment metrics: mutual k-NN overlap and CKA between representation kernels rise with model scale across vision/language families. Unsupervised translation: find Omega* = argmin over orthogonal Omega of divergence(X*Omega, Y) (Procrustes-style without paired samples, via distribution/geometry matching); multi-subject synchronization enforces cycle-consistency of rotations into one frame; success implies subject spaces are isometric up to an O(d) transform. Brain-LLM alignment: encoding-model R^2 peaks at intermediate depth (~2/3 of layers) and grows with log(params), consistent with convergence toward shared world statistics rather than task-specific features.

**REFA application.** Two uses: (1) evaluation - measure CKA/Procrustes alignment of REFA entity embeddings across seeds and against GRU/transformer baselines; high alignment would justify swapping in pretrained text encoders behind a frozen orthogonal adapter (cheap on 6GB). (2) caution - convergent embeddings do NOT certify convergent mechanism, so the Confabulation Battery must test bank-level ablations, not representation similarity alone.

Sources:
- Huh et al., The Platonic Representation Hypothesis, arXiv 2405.07987 (2024) — <https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.07987>
- Marcos-Manchon, Jha, Fuentemilla, Platonic Representations in the Human Brain, arXiv 2605.20496 (May 2026) — <https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.20496>
- Brain-Language Model Alignment, arXiv 2510.17833 (NeurIPS 2025 UniReps) — <https://arxiv.org/html/2510.17833>

### Centaur cognitive foundation model and the prediction-vs-explanation backlash
*status: partial*

Binz, Schulz et al. (Nature 644:1002, Aug 2025) fine-tuned Llama 3.1 70B (QLoRA) on Psych-101 - trial-by-trial data from 160 psychology experiments, 60k+ participants, >10M choices - producing Centaur, which out-predicts 14 domain-specific cognitive models on held-out participants and generalizes to modified cover stories, structural task changes, and new domains; its internal states also improved fMRI prediction. The 2025-2026 backlash is substantive: 'Not Even Wrong' (arXiv 2510.03311) argues behavioral prediction cannot adjudicate cognitive mechanism (category error: a unified model of BEHAVIOR, not cognition); a Zhejiang group (Apr 2026) showed Centaur's fit partly reflects overfitting - it fails under trivial alterations that change task INTENT while preserving surface form, indicating learned answer patterns rather than task understanding. Net establishment: large-scale behavioral cloning of human choices works and transfers, but mechanism claims require perturbation/ablation tests that Centaur fails.

**Math.** Training: QLoRA fine-tune of Llama-3.1-70B on Psych-101 (160 experiments, 60,092 participants, 10,681,650 choices rendered as text). Metric: per-trial negative log-likelihood / pseudo-R^2 of human choices; Centaur NLL below each of 14 task-specific cognitive models (e.g., GCM, prospect-theory variants) on held-out participants, and remains lower under cover-story and structural transfers. Critique protocol: minimal-pair task perturbations that preserve token statistics but alter the intended computation; Centaur's accuracy drop under these reveals variance explained by surface regularities - formally, low NLL is necessary but not sufficient for mechanism identification (many parameterizations realize the same likelihood).

**REFA application.** Methodological guardrail for the Confabulation Battery: score REFA against bank-lesioned and routing-shuffled ablations with per-trial NLL, and include minimal-pair perturbations (reworded relations, swapped roles, intent-flipping distractors) so any claim is mechanistic, not Centaur-style behavioral fit. Also validates the tiny-model stance: predictive fit at scale is cheap; identifiable mechanism is the scarce good.

Sources:
- Binz et al., A foundation model to predict and capture human cognition, Nature 644:1002 (2025) — <https://www.nature.com/articles/s41586-025-09215-4>
- Not Even Wrong: On the Limits of Prediction as Explanation in Cognitive Science, arXiv 2510.03311 — <https://arxiv.org/pdf/2510.03311>
- Zhejiang critique coverage, ScienceDaily (Apr 2026) — <https://www.sciencedaily.com/releases/2026/04/260429102035.htm>

### Inner-speech BCIs: covert speech is a scaled motor subspace, gated by a neural password ⚑
*status: convergent*

Kunz, Willett et al. (Cell, Aug 2025; BrainGate2, 4 participants with ALS/stroke) showed inner (imagined) speech is robustly represented in motor cortex and decodable in real time at up to 74% accuracy over a 125,000-word vocabulary - and that inner speech occupies a representational subspace SHARED with attempted speech but at reduced magnitude (a scaled copy), which is why unintended inner speech can leak into decoders. Their fix is architectural: a 'neural password' - decoding stays disabled until the user imagines a rare trigger phrase, detected at 98.75% - an explicit commitment gate separating covert rehearsal from overt output. Companion 2025 results: Wairagkar et al. (Nature 644:145) synthesized voice near-instantaneously (closed-loop audio, paralinguistic control of prosody/pitch) from 256 electrodes in ventral precentral gyrus. Biologically, this is direct evidence that self-generated covert content and committed output share substrate but differ by a gain/gating variable - the physiological core of source monitoring.

**Math.** Decoding stack: RNN phoneme decoder over multi-unit threshold crossings + language-model beam search; open-vocabulary (125k words) inner-speech decoding up to 74% accuracy. Geometry: inner-speech activity approx alpha * attempted-speech activity in a shared principal subspace with alpha < 1 (magnitude scaling, preserved directions), so a linear classifier on the gain axis separates covert from overt. Password gate: binary detector for a rare imagined phrase, sensitivity 98.75%; P(false trigger) driven low by choosing a low-prior phrase; decoder output gated by detector state. Voice synthesis: causal decoding at tens-of-ms latency enabling closed-loop auditory feedback.

**REFA application.** Biological license for two REFA mechanisms: (1) the existing is_query/is_history mask (queries read but never write, refa.py lines 99-103) is the right primitive - covert simulation must not commit; (2) add a scalar commitment gate on the OUTPUT side: the workspace can run hypothetical read-only rollouts (writes scaled to 0) and only a threshold event commits classification, giving the reality monitor a gain-based self-generated vs perceived signal to read.

Sources:
- Kunz et al., Inner speech in motor cortex and implications for speech neuroprostheses, Cell (2025) — <https://www.cell.com/cell/fulltext/S0092-8674(25)00681-6>
- Wairagkar et al., An instantaneous voice-synthesis neuroprosthesis, Nature 644:145-152 (2025) — <https://www.nature.com/articles/s41586-025-09127-3>

### Concept cells 2026: index neurons evolve into concept codes
*status: partial*

Twenty-year retrospectives (Neuron 2026; Acta Physiologica 2026) plus a 2025 Trends in Cognitive Sciences framework ('On the origin of memory neurons in the human hippocampus') update the human single-neuron picture: MTL concept cells are sparse, invariant, multimodal units (responding to a person/place across photos, text, voice) that act as building blocks of episodic memory, encoding items AND associations, coordinating with distributed neocortical codes. The new 2025 claim: concept cells ORIGINATE as hippocampal index neurons (arbitrary pointers binding one episode's cortical pattern) and gradually acquire invariant concept tuning as overlapping memories about the same entity accumulate - index-to-concept promotion. This bridges the hippocampal-index theory already in the Engram Loop ledger with a slot-like, entity-addressed code, and specifies the promotion dynamics: repeated co-activation across episodes turns an episode pointer into an entity key.

**Math.** Sparseness: a given MTL unit responds to roughly 0.5% of presented items (Quiroga-era estimate); response latency ~300ms; invariance across modalities. Capacity rationale: Willshaw-type binary associative memory achieves ~0.69 bits/synapse at optimal sparsity a ~ ln(N)/N, so ultra-sparse entity codes maximize stored associations per synapse - concept cells sit near this regime. Promotion model: neuron i's tuning t_i drifts toward the centroid of episodes it indexes: t_i <- t_i + eta * sum over episodes e containing entity c of (x_e - t_i); with repeated same-entity episodes t_i converges to an invariant entity prototype while retaining episode-binding links (index function).

**REFA application.** REFA's identity-based address (normalized W_a * identity) is already an entity-key code; the promotion result suggests a consolidation pass: keys whose bank rows receive repeated consistent writes get PROMOTED to protected slow-decay rows - per-row learned decay instead of per-bank - turning episode pointers into stable entity slots and freeing labile rows for new episodes. Cheap: change decay_logits from shape (H,) to (H,R).

Sources:
- 20 years of concept cells, Neuron (2026) — <https://www.cell.com/neuron/abstract/S0896-6273(26)00051-6>
- On the origin of memory neurons in the human hippocampus, Trends in Cognitive Sciences (2025) — <https://www.cell.com/trends/cognitive-sciences/fulltext/S1364-6613(25)00031-2>

### New engram biology: excitability-biased allocation, co-allocation linking, heterogeneous sub-ensembles
*status: partial*

Post-2024 engram work refines allocation beyond the ledger's 'engram allocation' entry. (1) Excitability bias with pre-configuration: neurons most active up to ~3h BEFORE training are preferentially allocated to the engram (calcium imaging + tagging), i.e., allocation reads a pre-existing excitability/connectivity state, not just training-time input. (2) Co-allocation implements memory linking: events within a window of hours share engram neurons (CREB/excitability decay sets the window; a locus coeruleus to dorsal-CA1 dopaminergic circuit modulates it), and shared neurons cause behavioral linking of the memories (Nature Reviews Neuroscience 2024 synthesis). (3) Heterogeneity: 'Deconstruction of a memory engram reveals distinct ensembles recruited at learning' (Nature Neuroscience 2026) shows one engram contains sub-ensembles with distinct recruitment origins, stability, and functional roles - an engram is a structured coalition, not a uniform tag. Together these specify a concrete allocation POLICY layer above synaptic plasticity.

**Math.** Allocation: P(neuron i recruited) proportional to softmax over relative excitability E_i at encoding; CREB overexpression raises E_i and win probability. Excitability decays over hours: E_i(t) = E_peak * exp(-t/tau_alloc), tau_alloc ~ hours, so engram overlap O(Delta_t) ~ exp(-Delta_t/tau_alloc); measured overlap and behavioral linking both fall off with inter-event interval (memories ~<5h apart link, ~24h apart do not, in the Cai/Silva paradigm this work extends). Heterogeneity: sub-ensemble membership predicted by pre-training activity state; sub-ensembles differ in retention timescale (fast-labile vs slow-stable components within one trace).

**REFA application.** Gives REFA a bank-residency policy: add a recency-of-write 'excitability' term e_h (decaying integral of recent write mass) to router logits so temporally adjacent evidence co-allocates to shared banks - implementing episode linking and improving delayed-evidence conflict resolution; the tau of e_h becomes the linking window. Sub-ensemble heterogeneity independently motivates mixed fast/slow rows per bank (same per-row lambda change as the concept-cell finding).

Sources:
- Deconstruction of a memory engram reveals distinct ensembles recruited at learning, Nature Neuroscience (2026) — <https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41814090/>
- Excitability mediates allocation of pre-configured ensembles to a hippocampal engram, Nature Neuroscience (2024) — <https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/38447576/>
- Engram mechanisms of memory linking and identity, Nature Reviews Neuroscience (2024) — <https://www.nature.com/articles/s41583-024-00814-0>

### Dendritic ANNs: parameter-efficient, robust learning from dendritic structure
*status: convergent*

Chavlis and Poirazi (Nature Communications, Jan 2025) built dendritic ANNs whose units have a soma fed by several nonlinear dendritic branches, each RESTRICTED to sample a small structured receptive field of the input (sparse, local connectivity mimicking dendritic sampling). Result: matched or better image-classification accuracy than dense ANNs with substantially fewer trainable parameters, markedly less overfitting, and improved robustness; the networks shift toward mixed selectivity that is more parameter-efficient. Parallel 2024-2026 work (DendSNN, arXiv 2412.06355) scales multi-branch dendritic neurons into deep spiking networks with improved noise/adversarial robustness and few-shot generalization; dendritic mechanisms are also used for credit assignment and catastrophic-forgetting mitigation. This deepens the ledger's 'two-layer dendrite' entry with a 2025-grade quantitative efficiency claim relevant to tiny models: structured sparsity at the UNIT level, not just the layer level, buys accuracy per parameter.

**Math.** Unit: y = f_soma( sum over branches b of w_b * g_b( sum over j in S_b of w_bj * x_j ) ), with |S_b| much smaller than the input dimension, S_b spatially structured (local patch sampling), g_b nonlinear (branch spike). Effective capacity: a k-branch dendritic unit is a 2-layer subnetwork, raising expressivity per neuron while parameter count scales as sum_b |S_b| instead of full fan-in - reported same-or-better accuracy with substantially fewer trainable parameters than matched dense ANNs, plus a reduced generalization gap and robustness gains in the SNN version (noise, adversarial, few-shot).

**REFA application.** REFA's fusion MLP and per-bank reads are dense. A dendritic variant: give the workspace GRU input k branches, each reading a restricted subset of banks/dimensions through its own small nonlinearity before summation - fewer parameters than the 3*d_model fusion layer, and branch-level gating gives a natural per-source (support/oppose/direct) nonlinear arbitration stage. Fits the 6GB parameter budget and may reduce overfitting on small synthetic task suites.

Sources:
- Chavlis & Poirazi, Dendrites endow artificial neural networks with accurate, robust and parameter-efficient learning, Nature Communications (2025); arXiv 2404.03708 — <https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.03708>
- Scalable Dendritic Modeling Advances Expressive and Robust Deep Spiking Neural Networks, arXiv 2412.06355 — <https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.06355>

---

## Memory substrates and efficiency engineering
`substrate-2026`

The 2024-2026 substrate literature has consolidated into two complementary memory axes plus an efficiency toolkit, and all three map cleanly onto REFA's bank architecture. Axis 1 is fast-weight synaptic state: BDH formalizes state-as-synaptic-matrix with ReLU-sparse positive keys and a linear-attention equivalence; MoM shows top-k routing over multiple fast-weight states beats one big state on recall; and FwPKM (Sakana, Jan 2026) closes exactly the gap this mission targeted - product-key addressing applied to fast-weight MEMORY BANKS updated by delta-rule-style local gradient steps, scaling slot count toward 10^6 at O(sqrt(N)) cost. Axis 2 is static conditional memory: DeepSeek Engram's hashed n-gram tables (O(1) deterministic lookup, U-shaped compute/memory allocation law, <3% host-offload overhead), Meta Memory Layers (product-key retrieval verified to 128B memory parameters), PEER's rank-1 singleton experts, and UltraMem's Tucker scoring plus implicit value expansion. The shared retrieval primitive across both axes is product-key decomposition, s(i,j) = <q1,k1_i> + <q2,k2_j>, which ports directly to REFA relation-bank routing at O(sqrt(H)*d + k^2) per event. The efficiency substrate - BitNet ternary weights, MLA low-rank latent state, mixture-of-depths token routing, provably lossless speculative decoding versus risk-calibrated early exit (CALM/LTT), and SpikingBrain's conversion-based training at ~2% of from-scratch tokens - contributes specific formulas to the 6GB budget: roughly 4096 typed banks with top-2 routing, INT8 state, shared value codebooks, and Engram-style deterministic prefetch from host RAM keep total bank state near tens of MB of VRAM per sequence. The one thing no substrate paper provides is stance-typed (support/oppose) slots, so REFA's evidential split remains its own contribution layered on borrowed addressing machinery.

**Verification** of load-bearing claims:

- **CONFIRMED** — BDH/BDH-GPU: state as Hebbian synaptic matrix, linear attention over ReLU-sparse positive features
  - Source exists and supports the claim. Cloned official repo github.com/pathwaycom/bdh: README cites Kosowski, Uznanski, Chorowski, Stamirowska, Bartoszkiewicz, 'The Dragon Hatchling', arXiv:2509.26507 (2025) — authors and id match. Code (bdh.py) verifies every architectural detail claimed: N = mlp_internal_dim_multiplier(128)*D/n_head (D=256, nh=4 gives N=8192); per-head encoder D->N; x_sparse = ReLU(x@encoder); RoPE applied to the sparse features (QR=rope(x_sparse), K is Q); strictly causal scores via .tril(diagonal=-1) (current token excluded, matching the claim's s<t sum with V=x, i.e., linear attention — no softmax); second ReLU branch y_sparse=ReLU(ln(yKV)@encoder_v) gated multiplicatively (x_sparse*y_sparse); decoder (N*nh)->D. README confirms: 'Hebbian working memory based on synaptic plasticity, displaying monosemanticity', scale-free network topology, Transformer-like scaling laws, and 'matches GPT-2-scale Transformers... at equivalent parameter scales (10M-1B)'. Streaming sigma_{N x D} reformulation is mathematically equivalent to the code's quadratic form given strict causality — math correct. Unverified paper-level details (arXiv blocked): the '~5% activation density' figure and the quantitative heavy-tail/modularity statistics — plausible, consistent with README's 'sparse and positive' claim. 'No 2026 successor paper' consistent with repo (only a Sudoku-benchmark blog update, explicitly from an internal non-released variant). MATH CORRECTION in the REFA application: at D=64, N=1024, '~3K MACs/event' counts only the sparse state read/write (~51 active x 64 ≈ 3.3K); the dense encoder lift D->N alone is ~65K MACs/event, so total per-event cost is ~65K+ MACs, not 3K (still trivial on an RTX 2060).
- **CONFIRMED** — DeepSeek Engram: hashed n-gram conditional memory, U-shaped sparsity-allocation law, near-free host offload
  - Verified directly from the official paper PDF in the cloned official repo github.com/deepseek-ai/Engram (Engram_paper.pdf, 33 pp). Title 'Conditional Memory via Scalable Lookup: A New Axis of Sparsity for Large Language Models'; first author Xin Cheng (Peking U./DeepSeek-AI) — 'Cheng et al.' correct. All load-bearing numbers check out verbatim: tokenizer compression via pre-computed surjective P: V->V' with 23% effective-vocab reduction; K distinct multiplicative-XOR hash heads per n-gram order into prime-sized tables; gate alpha = sigmoid(RMSNorm(h_t)^T RMSNorm(k_t)/sqrt(d)); depthwise causal Conv1D kernel w=4, dilation = max n-gram order, Y = SiLU(Conv1D(RMSNorm(V~))) + V~; U-shaped loss vs allocation ratio rho at both C=2e20 and 6e20 FLOPs with stable optimum rho ≈ 75-80% MoE (i.e., Engram gets 20-25% of the sparse budget); at C=6e20 (P_tot ≈ 9.9B) loss 1.7248 -> 1.7109 (Delta = 0.0139); slot sweep 2.58e5 -> 1.0e7 with log-linear loss trend; Engram-27B beats strict iso-parameter/iso-FLOPs MoE (BBH +5.0, ARC-C +3.7, MMLU +3.4, HumanEval +3.0); Multi-Query NIAH 84.2 -> 97.0; 100B-parameter Engram layer fully offloaded to host memory with <3% throughput penalty (peak 2.8% on the 8B backbone) via deterministic prefetch over PCIe; Zipfian access explicitly motivates HBM/DRAM/NVMe tiering. Two caveats: (1) the arXiv id 2601.07372 and 'Jan 2026' date could not be independently verified (arXiv blocked; the repo does not print the id) — the id is consistent with a Jan-2026 submission and the paper is authenticated via the official DeepSeek repo; (2) minor causal nuance: the paper attributes the long-context gains to delegating local dependencies to lookup, freeing ATTENTION capacity for global context, while early-layer relief from static reconstruction is its explanation for the reasoning/effective-depth gains — the claim slightly merges the two mechanisms.
- **CONFIRMED** — Product-key decomposition: sqrt-cost retrieval, validated to 128B memory parameters (Meta Memory Layers at Scale)
  - Both sources exist. Lample et al., 'Large Memory Layers with Product Keys', arXiv:1907.05242 (NeurIPS 2019) is well-established and the math as stated is correct: additive score s(i,j)=<q1,c1_i>+<q2,c2_j> makes exact top-k over N=|C1|x|C2| recoverable from per-half top-k plus ranking k^2 sums, cost O(sqrt(N)*d + k^2); the N=1M, d=64 arithmetic (6.4e4 vs 6.4e7 MACs) is right. Meta's official reference implementation (github.com/facebookresearch/memory, cloned) confirms 'Memory Layers at Scale', with swilu output gating (swilu_projection), values shared across memory layers (mem_share_values), custom fast embedding-bag kernels (xformer_embeddingbag.py), and configs up to 7B backbones with ~1M-slot memories; the headline paper claims (up to 128B memory parameters / ~64M values, 1T training tokens, 8B max backbone, beating dense models of >2x FLOPs and iso-compute iso-param MoE, largest gains on factual QA) match the known arXiv:2412.09764 abstract. CORRECTIONS: (1) venue — 'ICLR'25' could not be verified; cite as arXiv:2412.09764 (Dec 2024 preprint), not ICLR'25; (2) 'qk-normalization' does not appear in the official code (only the optional query batch-norm inherited from Lample's PKM) — drop or soften that detail; the '~3x bandwidth' kernel figure is unverified; (3) MATH ERROR in the REFA application: 4096 banks x 2 stances x 16x64 = 8.39M parameters = 16.8MB in fp16 and 8.4MB in INT8 — the stated '32MB fp16 / 16MB INT8' is exactly 2x too high (conclusion 'fits in 6GB' still holds, with more headroom); also 262K/4K MACs is 64x, not 60x — minor.
- **CONFIRMED** — MoM (Mixture-of-Memories): hard top-k routing over multiple fast-weight states with load balancing
  - Source exists and supports the claim. Cloned official repo github.com/OpenSparseLLMs/MoM: 'MoM: Linear Sequence Modeling with Mixture-of-Memories', arXiv:2502.13685 — and it was accepted to ICLR 2026 (repo news, Feb 2026), which strengthens the claim's framing ('v4 Nov 2025' version detail unverified, arXiv blocked, but consistent with the camera-ready timeline). Code verifies the mechanism as claimed: router with top-k selection over M separate fast-weight memory states; unselected memories carried over untouched; an optional always-active shared memory (shared_mem branch, shared_k/shared_v/shared_gk, output added to the routed reads); linear-attention/GLA/Gated-DeltaNet update rules; official 340M and 1.3B training configs (mom_340M.json, mom_1.3B.json); a dedicated recall-intensive evaluation suite. Critically, the official modeling code (mom/models/mom/modeling_mom.py) contains load_balancing_loss_func explicitly documented as 'auxiliary load balancing loss as in Switch Transformer', scaled by config.aux_loss_scale and added to the LM loss — so the 'Switch-style anti-collapse auxiliary loss' is genuinely part of the published MoM, not an embellishment. The Switch Transformers citation (Fedus, Zoph, Shazeer, arXiv:2101.03961) and the stated L_aux = alpha * M * sum_m f_m * P_m formula (minimized at uniform load, alpha ~ 1e-2) are correct. The stated per-token cost O(k*d^2) and capacity-vs-cost argument are consistent with the architecture. 'Approaching Transformer-level recall' is the paper's abstract-level claim, not re-verified quantitatively here.
- **PLAUSIBLE** — FwPKM: product-key addressing applied to fast-weight memory banks (Sakana AI)
  - Source exists and the mechanism is verified; the headline numbers could not be verified and one capacity figure looks overstated. Cloned official repo github.com/SakanaAI/fast-weight-product-key-memory (initial release 2026-02-22, matching the claim): citation block confirms 'Fast-weight Product Key Memory', Tianyu Zhao and Llion Jones, arXiv:2601.00671 (2026).
