Code verifies the claimed mechanism end to end: product-key top-k retrieval with two sub-key sets; per-example MSE 'memorization' (rewrite) loss with gate-derived loss weights (fwpkm_weight_loss_with_gates), applied so only retrieved rows are updated by local SGD (update_fw_param); an auxiliary addressing loss on the keys implemented as negative marginal entropy of sub-key score distributions (addr_loss='me') — i.e., an anti-collapse usage-spreading loss, as claimed; chunk-wise updates (fwpkm_update_chunk_size=512); Qwen3-Next / Gated DeltaNet backbones (model_type qwen3_next_mem, GDN configs); LC64/LAMBADA/NIAH/Pile-domain evals present. The delta-rule identity math is correct: one GD step on ||M k - v||^2 gives M <- M - eta(Mk - v)k^T = (I - eta k k^T)M + eta k v^T. The REFA arithmetic is correct (2 x 256 x 32 = 16K MACs for 512^2 = 262K... at N=65K: 2*sqrt(65K)*32 ≈ 16K MACs; 65K x 128 fp16 ≈ 16.8MB ≈ 17MB). NOT verified (arXiv blocked; not in repo): '>10% perplexity reduction on 8M-token streams', '4K-train -> 128K-test length generalization', and 'billions of memory params at near-constant latency' — the released main configs use 512^2 ≈ 2.6e5 slots at hidden size 768 (~2e8 memory params per FwPKM layer), so 'N ~ 10^6' and 'billions of memory params' likely describe the method's scaling headroom or static-PKM baselines rather than the main FwPKM experiments; treat those figures as unconfirmed and possibly inflated until the paper is checked.

### BDH/BDH-GPU: state as a Hebbian synaptic matrix, equivalent to linear attention over ReLU-sparse positive features ⚑
*status: convergent*

BDH (Pathway, arXiv 2509.26507) derives a GPU architecture from attention itself: each layer lifts the D-dim residual into a much wider neuron space via ReLU (sparse, positive activations ~5% dense), runs attention linearly over those sparse features, and gates two ReLU branches multiplicatively (the 'ReLU-lowrank' block). Its working memory is entirely the linear-attention state, reinterpreted as a synaptic matrix sigma between neuron pairs updated by a Hebbian rule at inference; no KV cache. Verified directly from the released bdh.py: encoder D->N with N = 128*D/n_head (e.g., D=256, nh=4 gives N=8192), RoPE applied to the sparse features, strictly-causal score matrix, elementwise product gating, decoder N*nh->D. Paper claims (abstract + replications): matches Transformer scaling laws, GPT-2-level from 10M-1B params; learned neuron interaction graph is scale-free (heavy-tailed degree, high modularity); synapses are monosemantic and interpretable. No substantive 2026 successor paper found as of Aug 2026 - follow-up is explainers/community analysis; the repo remains the canonical implementation.

**Math.** Per head: x_sp = ReLU(E x) in R^N, N = 128*D/n_h >> D. Attention is linear attention over sparse positive features: y_t = sum_{s<t} <rho(x_sp,t), rho(x_sp,s)> x_s with rho = RoPE; streaming-equivalent state sigma_t = sigma_{t-1} + rho(k_t) x_t^T in R^{N x D} ('synapse' sigma_ij, Hebbian Delta-sigma ∝ pre*post), read y_t = sigma_t^T rho(q_t). Gated block: out = Decoder[ReLU(E x) ⊙ ReLU(E_v LN(y))] - two low-rank D->N maps with multiplicative gating. Cost O(N*D)/token; state O(N*D)/head, constant in T (vs KV cache O(T*D)). Sparsity of x_sp makes the effective update rank ~nnz; near-orthogonal sparse positive keys reduce write crosstalk vs dense keys. Scale-free claim: degree distribution of learned graph heavy-tailed (power-law-ish), modular.

**REFA application.** REFA's per-bank M^s in R^{16x64} is a miniature sigma with typed splits. BDH's lesson: widen the address space and make it ReLU-sparse instead of dense L2-normalized R=16 - lift address dim to N ~ 512-2048 via ReLU(Ex); sparse positive keys give near-orthogonal writes (less crosstalk at fixed rank) plus per-slot interpretability of which synapse holds which fact. Apply RoPE phase to q/k to timestamp evidence so recency is readable at query time. At D=64, N=1024, 5% sparsity: ~3K MACs/event - trivial on an RTX 2060.

Sources:
- Kosowski, Uznanski, Chorowski, Stamirowska, Bartoszkiewicz, 'The Dragon Hatchling', arXiv:2509.26507 — <https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.26507>
- Official code (bdh.py, read directly this session) — <https://github.com/pathwaycom/bdh>

### DeepSeek Engram: hashed n-gram conditional memory with O(1) deterministic lookup, a U-shaped sparsity-allocation law, and near-free host offload ⚑
*status: convergent*

Engram (arXiv 2601.07372, Jan 2026; math verified from the official paper PDF in the DeepSeek repo) adds 'conditional memory' as a sparsity axis complementary to MoE's conditional computation. Suffix n-grams of tokenizer-compressed canonical token ids are hashed by K independent heads into prime-sized embedding tables; retrieved embeddings are fused into the backbone through a context-aware scalar gate (hidden state as query, retrieved memory as key/value), a short depthwise causal conv, and a residual add. Because addresses depend only on input tokens (not hidden state), retrieval is deterministic: tables shard across GPUs in training (all-to-all) and offload to host RAM at inference with prefetch overlapping compute - a 100B-parameter table costs <3% throughput. Scaling: with fixed total params and FLOPs, validation loss vs the MoE/Engram allocation ratio is U-shaped with a stable optimum giving Engram 20-25% of the sparse budget; with the backbone fixed, loss falls log-linearly in slot count (2.6e5 to 1e7). Engram-27B beats an iso-param/iso-FLOP MoE, with the largest gains on reasoning (BBH +5.0) and long-context retrieval (MQ-NIAH 84.2 -> 97.0), because early layers stop reconstructing static patterns.

**Math.** Keys: x'_t = P(x_t) (surjective vocab compression, -23% vocab); g_{t,n} = (x'_{t-n+1},...,x'_t). Per order n and head k: z = phi_{n,k}(g_{t,n}) (multiplicative-XOR hash into prime table size M_{n,k}); e_t = concat_{n,k} E_{n,k}[z]. Gate: alpha_t = sigma(RMSNorm(h_t)^T RMSNorm(W_K e_t)/sqrt(d)); v_tilde = alpha_t * W_V e_t; Y = SiLU(Conv1D_dw(RMSNorm(V_tilde); w=4, dilation=N_max)) + V_tilde; H <- H + Y. O(1) lookups/token; table size adds zero FLOPs. Allocation: P_MoE = rho*P_sparse, P_Engram = (1-rho)*P_sparse; loss(rho) U-shaped, optimum rho ~ 0.75-0.80 across 2e20 and 6e20 FLOPs (loss 1.7248 -> 1.7109 at ~10B). Infinite-memory regime: loss ~ a - b*log(slots). Host offload of 100B params: <3% overhead via deterministic prefetch; Zipfian access motivates HBM/DRAM/NVMe cache tiers.

**REFA application.** REFA events are already symbolic (s,r,o ids), so bank addressing can be hash-deterministic with zero router params: bank_k = phi_k(subject, relation) mod prime_k, K=2-4 heads - O(1), immune to routing collapse. Engram's alpha = sigma(q_hat . k_hat / sqrt(d)) is exactly the trust/write gate REFA needs per recalled stance vector. Deterministic ids implement the roadmap's VRAM/RAM bank-residency offload: a 2KB INT8 bank row over PCIe ~ microseconds, hidden behind GRU compute; hot Zipfian banks live in 6GB VRAM, the tail in 32GB RAM.

Sources:
- Cheng et al., 'Conditional Memory via Scalable Lookup', arXiv:2601.07372 (PDF read in full this session) — <https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.07372>
- Official repo with paper PDF — <https://github.com/deepseek-ai/Engram>

### Product-key decomposition: sqrt-cost retrieval over N slots, validated to 128B memory parameters (Meta Memory Layers at Scale) ⚑
*status: convergent*

The product-key trick (Lample et al. 2019) factorizes a key space of N slots as a Cartesian product of two sub-key sets of size sqrt(N), making exact top-k retrieval over millions of slots cost O(sqrt(N)) instead of O(N). Meta's Memory Layers at Scale (arXiv 2412.09764, ICLR'25) is the existence proof at scale: memory layers with up to 64M value slots / 128B memory parameters, trained to 1T tokens on an 8B backbone, with a fully parallelized implementation (sharded values, custom EmbeddingBag-class CUDA kernels ~3x bandwidth), swilu output gating, qk-normalization, and a memory pool shared across several layers. Result: memory-augmented models beat dense models of >2x the FLOP budget and beat iso-compute iso-param MoE, with the largest gains on factual QA - direct evidence that lookup capacity substitutes for compute on relational/factual workloads, which is REFA's entire regime.

**Math.** Split query q in R^d into halves (q1,q2); sub-key sets C1,C2 with |Ci| = sqrt(N). Score of full key (i,j): s(i,j) = <q1, c1_i> + <q2, c2_j>. Exact argtop-k over N keys = top-k per half then rank the k^2 sums: cost O(sqrt(N)*d + k^2) vs O(N*d); for N=1M, d=64: ~6.4e4 vs 6.4e7 MACs. Output y = sum_{i in TopK} softmax(s_i) v_i; Meta variant adds gating out = (y ⊙ SiLU(x W1)) W2. Capacity contrast with fast weights: a slot memory addresses N discrete items exactly; a rank-R associative matrix stores ~R near-orthogonal associations before crosstalk - product keys break the rank ceiling at sqrt cost.

**REFA application.** Direct port to bank routing (not experts): replace router = Linear(64->H) + softmax with product-key addressing over H = H1 x H2 typed relation banks. For H = 64x64 = 4096: scoring costs 2*(64*32) = 4K MACs + k^2 sort vs 262K dense - 60x cheaper, and it is the only router that stays cheap as H grows. Top-k=2 banks read/written per event. State: 4096 banks x 2 stances x 16x64 fp16 = 32MB/sequence, INT8 = 16MB - comfortably inside 6GB. This is the concrete mechanism for the roadmap's 'sparse top-k routing' item.

Sources:
- Berges, Oguz, Haziza, Yih, Zettlemoyer, Ghosh, 'Memory Layers at Scale', arXiv:2412.09764 — <https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.09764>
- Lample et al., 'Large Memory Layers with Product Keys', arXiv:1907.05242 — <https://arxiv.org/abs/1907.05242>

### PEER: a million rank-1 singleton experts addressed by product keys - the continuum between fast-weight matrices and slot memories
*status: convergent*

PEER (DeepMind, arXiv 2407.04153) pushes MoE granularity to its limit: the expert pool contains over 10^6 singleton experts, each a rank-1 map (one input vector, one output vector, a pointwise nonlinearity), retrieved by product keys with multiple independent query heads sharing one pool, with query BatchNorm to balance key usage. Motivated by the fine-grained MoE scaling law (at fixed activated params, more/smaller experts is better), PEER beats dense FFW and coarse MoE on the perplexity-compute frontier. Conceptually important for REFA: a rank-1 expert u_i sigma(v_i^T x) is structurally identical to one (key,value) slot row of a fast-weight bank, so PEER demonstrates that the 'bank' abstraction can be dissolved into a single global pool of typed slots with sqrt-cost addressing - banks become emergent retrieval sets, not fixed matrices.

**Math.** Expert: e_i(x) = u_i sigma(v_i^T x) (GLU variant u_i (sigma(v_i^T x) * gamma(w_i^T x))). Pool size N > 10^6, product keys (N = n^2 sub-key grid). h heads: f(x) = sum_h sum_{i in TopK_h} g_{h,i} e_i(x), g = softmax over retrieved scores; per-token cost O(h(sqrt(N)*d + k^2) + h*k*d) - independent of N up to the sqrt term. Total params scale as O(N*d) while activated params stay O(h*k*d). Fine-grained scaling law (Krajewski et al. 2402.07871): loss improves monotonically with granularity G at fixed activated compute; PEER takes G -> N with rank-1 experts.

**REFA application.** Reframes a REFA bank row as the atomic unit: keep one pool of ~10^5 (key, value, stance-tag, decay) slots product-key addressed; a 'bank' is just the top-k retrieved slot set for the event's (s,r) query. This removes the hard R=16 capacity per relation - hot relations claim more slots, rare ones share. 10^5 slots x 128 dims fp16 ~ 26MB. The stance tag (support/oppose per slot) is REFA's addition; PEER supplies the addressing and the evidence that rank-1 granularity trains well.

Sources:
- Xu Owen He, 'Mixture of A Million Experts', arXiv:2407.04153 — <https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.04153>

### UltraMem / UltraMemV2: Tucker-decomposed bilinear scoring and implicit value expansion - value-side compression for memory tables
*status: convergent*

ByteDance's UltraMem (arXiv 2411.12364, ICLR 2025) attacks the inference memory-access cost of large memory layers and MoE. Two reusable mechanisms: (1) Tucker-Decomposed Query-Key Retrieval (TDQKR) replaces the additive product-key score with a rank-r bilinear combination of row and column scores through a learnable core - strictly more expressive than product keys (which are the r=1 additive special case), with top-m approximation plus an auxiliary loss to keep retrieval trainable; (2) Implicit Value Expansion (IVE) virtually multiplies the value table: E linear 'virtual' projections of a shared physical table act as E*M addressable values with no extra storage, and the reads fuse into dense matmuls. UltraMem reaches 20M slots, matches or beats iso-FLOP MoE, and cuts inference cost up to 83% vs MoE (memory-access-bound decoding is 1.6-6x faster). UltraMemV2 (arXiv 2508.18756) simplifies: memory in every block, single linear value expansion, FFN-based value processing, principled init; validated at 2.5B activated / 120B total with superior long-context learning - showing memory-layer lines now scale to frontier-adjacent sizes.

**Math.** TDQKR: score grid S = S_row^T C S_col, with S_row, S_col in R^{r x n} (per-mode scores) and learnable Tucker core C in R^{r x r} (r=2 in paper); product-key additive scoring is the rank-1 case S = s_row 1^T + 1 s_col^T under a fixed core. Top-m per mode approximates global top-k; aux loss keeps chosen rows/cols aligned with true top scores. IVE: v_virtual(e,i) = W_e v_i - physical table M slots, E projections -> E*M addressable values; retrieval of a virtual value folds W_e into the output projection, so cost stays O(k*d). Inference economics: MoE decode at small batch reads O(k_experts * d_ff * d) weights/token from HBM; UltraMem reads O(k * d) value rows - the source of the up-to-83% cost cut.

**REFA application.** Value-side compression for thousands of typed banks: keep one shared physical value codebook V (e.g., 4096 x 64 = 1MB fp32) and cheap per-bank projections W_h; bank h's slot value = W_h v_i. Turns REFA state from O(H*R*D) into O(R'*D + H*small), so H can grow 10-100x on 6GB. TDQKR (rank-2 bilinear scoring) is the upgrade path if additive product-key routing under-fits typed relation structure - same cost order, one extra r x r core.

Sources:
- Huang et al., 'Ultra-Sparse Memory Network', arXiv:2411.12364 (ICLR 2025) — <https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.12364>
- 'UltraMemV2: Memory Networks Scaling to 120B Parameters', arXiv:2508.18756 — <https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.18756>

### MoM (Mixture-of-Memories): hard top-k routing over multiple fast-weight states, with load-balancing - the validated version of REFA's bank routing ⚑
*status: convergent*

MoM (arXiv 2502.13685, v4 Nov 2025) is the closest published architecture to REFA's core: a linear-sequence model that maintains M separate fast-weight memory states, routes each token to top-k of them, updates ONLY the selected states (any update rule: vanilla Hebbian, gated, delta rule), keeps one always-active shared memory, and mixes the activated reads by router weights. The claimed and demonstrated benefit is exactly REFA's motivation: splitting state into routed banks expands effective capacity and reduces write interference, dramatically improving recall-intensive performance (NIAH, SWDE, FDA and similar) over single-state linear models at 340M-1.3B scale, approaching Transformer-level recall while keeping O(1) inference state. This upgrades REFA's soft softmax routing (the dense limit of MoM) to hard sparse routing with a principled anti-collapse mechanism (Switch-style auxiliary load-balancing loss), which REFA currently lacks - its clamped route temperature only mitigates collapse.

**Math.** Router: g = softmax(TopK(W_g x_t)); for m in TopK: M_{m,t} = decay_m * M_{m,t-1} + k_t v_t^T (or delta-rule M <- M - beta (M k - v) k^T); unselected memories carry over untouched. Shared memory M_s always updated. Read: o_t = sum_{m in TopK ∪ {s}} g_m q_t^T M_{m,t}. Cost: O(k*d^2)/token inference, chunked-parallel training O(T*k*d^2); capacity grows ~M x (single-state size) at constant per-token cost. Load balancing (Switch, arXiv:2101.03961): L_aux = alpha * M * sum_m f_m * P_m, f_m = fraction of tokens routed to m, P_m = mean router probability; minimized at uniform load, alpha ~ 1e-2.

**REFA application.** Swap REFA's dense softmax routing for MoM-style top-2: write cost drops O(H*R*D) -> O(2*R*D), and - critical for the revision/contrastive tasks - evidence in unselected banks can no longer be eroded by soft-routing crosstalk, which is a live confound in the current model (every bank gets a nonzero write every step). Add L_aux (alpha~0.01) against bank collapse. MoM's always-on shared bank = a 'general evidence' bank for unseen relations. This is a ~20-line change to refa.py and directly tests the roadmap's sparse top-k routing item.

Sources:
- Du, Sun, Lan, Hu, Cheng, 'MoM: Linear Sequence Modeling with Mixture-of-Memories', arXiv:2502.13685 — <https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.13685>
- Fedus, Zoph, Shazeer, 'Switch Transformers' (load-balancing loss), arXiv:2101.03961 — <https://arxiv.org/abs/2101.03961>

### FwPKM: product-key addressing applied to fast-weight memory banks - the exact bank-scaling primitive REFA needs ⚑
*status: partial*

Fast-weight Product Key Memory (Sakana AI, arXiv 2601.00671; code released 2026-02-22, verified on GitHub) turns the static PKM table into episodic fast weights: slow weights emit per-token query, target value, and a scalar write gate; product-key retrieval selects k slots from an N ~ 10^6 table; then, chunk by chunk, ONLY the touched value rows and sub-keys are updated by local gradient descent on a gated memory-rewrite objective, with an auxiliary addressing loss on the keys that spreads writes and prevents slot collapse. This resolves the tension the paper names explicitly: softmax attention has unbounded storage at O(T^2) cost, linear/fast-weight models have O(1) cost but rank-bounded storage; FwPKM gets sparse O(sqrt(N)) addressing with dynamically written content. Results: >10% perplexity reduction on 8M-token streams (LC64, LAMBADA-style), length generalization 4K-train -> 128K-test, billions of memory params at near-constant latency, running atop Qwen3-Next / Gated DeltaNet backbones. Because one gradient step on the rewrite objective IS the delta rule, REFA's update is a special case - FwPKM is the proof it scales to a million product-key-addressed slots.

**Math.** Retrieval: product-key top-k over N slots, O(sqrt(N)*d + k^2). Per chunk C: value update minimizes gated rewrite loss L_V = sum_{t in C} beta_t ||PKM_theta_fast(q_t) - v_t||^2 over the k retrieved rows only (update cost ∝ k, not N); keys updated on an auxiliary addressing loss that decorrelates slot usage (anti-collapse). Delta-rule identity: one GD step on ||M k - v||^2 gives M <- M - eta (M k - v) k^T = (I - eta k k^T) M + eta k v^T - i.e., REFA's erase-then-write, restricted to k-sparse slots of a huge table. Chunking makes updates GPU-parallel within a chunk while keeping cross-chunk recurrence; total cost subquadratic in T for any T.

**REFA application.** The scaling path for REFA banks, end to end: (i) product-key address on (subject, relation); (ii) apply REFA's stance-typed erase/write only to the k retrieved slot rows; (iii) adopt FwPKM's addressing loss so bank keys track the event distribution without collapse; (iv) adopt chunk-level updates to replace the per-timestep Python loop with GPU-friendly chunked scans - simultaneously fixing REFA's stated compute bottleneck. At N=65K slots, retrieval ~ sqrt(65K)*64 ~ 16K MACs/event; slot state 65K x 128 fp16 = 17MB.

Sources:
- Zhao, Jones, 'Fast-weight Product Key Memory', arXiv:2601.00671 — <https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.00671>
- Official Sakana AI code (README verified this session) — <https://github.com/SakanaAI/fast-weight-product-key-memory>

### Mixture-of-Depths: differentiable top-k token routing around blocks, with a causal predictor for streaming inference
*status: convergent*

MoD (DeepMind, arXiv 2404.02258) allocates block compute per token: a scalar router picks the top beta*T tokens to receive the full attention+MLP block; the rest bypass via the residual stream. The router weight multiplies the block output so routing receives gradients through the main loss (no REINFORCE, no aux routing loss needed for learning - only for the causal predictor). The known subtlety, which matters for any streaming model: top-k over a sequence is non-causal. MoD's fix is a small auxiliary binary classifier trained to predict top-k membership from the current token's representation alone (~99% accuracy reported), used at autoregressive inference with negligible quality loss. Iso-FLOP, MoD reaches equal loss with up to ~50% faster stepping, with typical capacity beta ~ 12.5%. The Engram Loop proposal already cites MoD as its compute-gate; the deepening here is the exact routing/causal-predictor math and the observation that the multiplier trick plus predictor is the correct pattern for gating REFA's expensive path per event.

**Math.** Router: r_t = w_theta^T x_t (scalar). Selection: S = TopK({r_t}, k = floor(beta*T)). Update: x_t' = x_t + r_t * f(x_t) if t in S else x_t (f = attention+MLP block). FLOPs per block scale ~ beta^2 for attention (both queries and keys reduced) and ~ beta for MLP. Causal inference: train classifier p_theta(t in S | x_t) with BCE against realized top-k membership; at decode, route iff p > 0.5. Choosing beta trades loss vs compute; iso-FLOP optimum reported near beta = 0.125 every other block.

**REFA application.** Per-event compute gating for the streaming loop: history events mostly need embed+route+write (~10K FLOPs at current sizes); the GRU workspace update and fused read are ~10x more and matter mainly at query/conflict events. Gate them: r_t = w^T x_t, train with the output-multiplier trick, deploy with the causal predictor (streaming-safe by construction). Natural gate feature: the stance-conflict norm ||z^+ - z^-|| - linking MoD routing to the Engram Loop's surprise economy. Converts loop cost to O(beta*T) heavy steps on the 2060.

Sources:
- Raposo et al., 'Mixture-of-Depths', arXiv:2404.02258 — <https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.02258>

### Compute skipping with guarantees: speculative decoding is exactly lossless; early exit is risk-calibrated (CALM/LTT), not lossless
*status: convergent*

Two distinct correctness regimes that the proposal's efficiency plans should keep separate. (1) Speculative decoding (Leviathan et al. arXiv 2211.17192; Chen et al. arXiv 2302.01318) is provably distribution-preserving: a cheap draft model proposes gamma tokens, the target model verifies them in one parallel pass, and a rejection-sampling rule guarantees the emitted sequence is distributed exactly as the target model's - speedup is mathematically free, bounded by draft-target agreement. (2) Early exit is NOT lossless: skipping layers changes the output distribution. CALM (Schuster et al., arXiv 2207.07061) gives the correct statistical frame: calibrate per-layer confidence thresholds with Learn-Then-Test / fixed-sequence testing so that user-chosen consistency risk with the full model is guaranteed with finite-sample, distribution-free probability. For an evidential model whose product is calibrated abstention, this distinction is load-bearing: any early-exit REFA variant must carry a CALM-style guarantee or it corrupts exactly the calibration being measured.

**Math.** Spec sampling: draft q, target p. Accept proposed x w.p. min(1, p(x)/q(x)); on rejection resample from residual p'(x) = max(0, p(x)-q(x)) / sum_x max(0, p(x)-q(x)). Theorem: emitted token ~ p exactly. With acceptance rate alpha = E_x[sum min(p,q)], expected emitted tokens per gamma-draft cycle = (1 - alpha^{gamma+1})/(1 - alpha). CALM: exit at first layer l with confidence c_t^l >= lambda_l; thresholds chosen by LTT so that P( E[D(Y_early, Y_full)] <= delta ) >= 1 - epsilon for chosen risk functional D (textual/risk consistency), any data distribution, finite calibration set.

**REFA application.** REFA's three-class head is the ideal CALM target: define D = disagreement with full-depth decision; calibrate per-class exit thresholds (unknown should demand the deepest compute; supported/opposed with large evidence margins exit early); LTT gives finite-sample guarantees aligned with the roadmap's calibration/abstention item. Spec-decode analog for streams: the repo's GRU baseline drafts class posteriors; REFA verifies with min(1, p/q) acceptance, keeping the evidential output distribution exact while skipping most full reads.

Sources:
- Leviathan, Kalman, Matias, 'Fast Inference via Speculative Decoding', arXiv:2211.17192 — <https://arxiv.org/abs/2211.17192>
- Schuster et al., 'Confident Adaptive Language Modeling', arXiv:2207.07061 — <https://arxiv.org/abs/2207.07061>
- Chen et al., 'Accelerating LLM Decoding with Speculative Sampling', arXiv:2302.01318 — <https://arxiv.org/abs/2302.01318>

### SpikingBrain 1.0/2.0: conversion-based training (~2% of tokens) plus adaptive-threshold integer spike coding
*status: convergent*

SpikingBrain (CASIA, arXiv 2509.05276) builds linear and hybrid-linear attention models with spiking activations NOT by training from scratch but by converting pretrained transformers: map softmax attention onto (hybrid) linear attention, initialize from the pretrained weights, then continual-pretrain on ~150B tokens - about 2% of a from-scratch corpus - recovering ~90% of base quality at 7B and 76B-MoE scales, with ~100x TTFT speedup at 4M-token context (constant state vs KV cache). Its spike coding converts activations to integer counts via an adaptive threshold, expandable to sparse binary event trains for add-only hardware; combined with 8-bit weights the degradation is ~1-3% with ~69% activation sparsity. SpikingBrain2.0 (arXiv 2604.22575, Apr 2026, verified by search) adds two deployment paths - INT8-Spiking (event-driven, edge/neuromorphic) and FP8 (GPU) - reporting 10.13x TTFT speedup at 4M context and >10M-token contexts on 8xA100 under vLLM. The transferable content is the conversion math and the coding scheme, not the branding.

**Math.** Conversion: choose feature map phi so linear attention sum_s phi(q)^T phi(k_s) v_s approximates softmax attention logits of the pretrained model; initialize projections from pretrained W_q,W_k,W_v; continual pretrain ~150B tokens (~2% of from-scratch) to close the gap. Spike coding: s = clip(round(a/theta), 0, s_max), theta adaptive per tensor; a ~ theta*s with quantization error <= theta/2; counts expand to {0,1} trains over s_max virtual timesteps -> multiplier-free accumulate. With W8: end-task degradation 1-3%; activation sparsity ~69%. Long-context: state O(d^2) constant vs KV O(T*d) -> TTFT speedup ~ O(T) relative; measured 10.13x at 4M (v2), ~100x claims at 4M (v1, vs quadratic baseline).

**REFA application.** (1) Conversion de-risks REFA training: pretrain a tiny transformer on the relational task suite (fast, parallel), then convert to the streaming fast-weight form with a brief continual phase - the repo's float64 exact streamed-linear equivalence tests are literally the conversion check harness. (2) After the BDH-style ReLU-sparse address change, bank reads become integer-count spike ops: INT8 add-only reads suit the RTX 2060's INT8 throughput and future edge ports; expect the 1-3% degradation regime, worth a Confabulation-Battery ablation.

Sources:
- 'SpikingBrain: Spiking Brain-inspired Large Models', arXiv:2509.05276 — <https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.05276>
- 'SpikingBrain2.0: Brain-Inspired Foundation Models for Efficient Long-Context and Cross-Platform Inference', arXiv:2604.22575 — <https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.22575>

### Arithmetic substrate: BitNet b1.58 ternary weights and Monarch structured projections - what actually helps at REFA scale
*status: convergent*

BitNet b1.58 (Microsoft, arXiv 2402.17764) quantizes every weight to {-1,0,+1} via absmean scaling with 8-bit per-token absmax activations, trained quantization-aware; from ~3B params it matches FP16 LLaMA-class perplexity and task scores while cutting memory ~3.55x and latency ~2.71x, and replacing multiplies with adds (order-of-magnitude energy reduction per MAC). Monarch (Stanford, arXiv 2204.00595) parameterizes dense matrices as products of two block-diagonal factors interleaved with fixed permutations: O(n^1.5) params/FLOPs, an expressivity class covering FFT/DCT/Hadamard/Toeplitz, and a closed-form projection of any dense matrix onto the Monarch class. Honest assessment for REFA: at d=64 the dense projections are ~100KB - weight compression and structured factorization buy nothing there; their value appears only in (a) shrinking the whole slow-weight model so ALL VRAM goes to bank state and batch, and (b) any deliberately widened routing/addressing layer (d -> tens of thousands of sub-key scores), which is exactly where Monarch's O(n^1.5) applies.

**Math.** BitNet: W_tilde = RoundClip(W/(gamma + eps), -1, 1), gamma = (1/nm) sum |W_ij| (absmean); activations x_tilde = clip(round(127 x / max|x|)); matmul = INT8 add/sub, 1.58 bits/weight (log2 3). Reported at 3B: iso-quality with FP16, 3.55x memory, 2.71x latency; energy ~ 0.03pJ ternary add vs ~1pJ-class FP16 MAC. Monarch: M = P L P R, L,R block-diagonal with sqrt(n) blocks of size sqrt(n) x sqrt(n), P fixed reshape-transpose permutation; params/FLOPs 2 n^{3/2} vs n^2; dense-to-Monarch projection argmin ||A - M||_F solvable exactly blockwise. 6GB arithmetic: 1.5M-param REFA ternary ~ 0.3MB; 4096-bank INT8 state 16MB/seq; batch 64 streams ~ 1GB - all fits.

**REFA application.** Use BitNet-style QAT on REFA's slow weights only (embedding, router, fusion, GRU): <0.5MB model, freeing 6GB for bank state and batching; keep bank fast-state INT8 absmax per row (state must stay higher-precision than ternary - it is written every step and delta-rule erase amplifies quantization noise; test in the equivalence harness). Apply Monarch only to a widened sub-key scorer (e.g., 64 -> 16K scores: 2*64*128 ~ 16K FLOPs vs 1M dense). Elsewhere, at d=64, structure is overhead - a documented negative worth recording.

Sources:
- Ma et al., 'The Era of 1-bit LLMs: All LLMs are in 1.58 Bits', arXiv:2402.17764 — <https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.17764>
- Dao et al., 'Monarch: Expressive Structured Matrices for Efficient and Accurate Training', arXiv:2204.00595 — <https://arxiv.org/abs/2204.00595>

### KV-cache compression via shared low-rank latents (MLA): a factorization recipe for REFA's stance-paired bank state
*status: convergent*

DeepSeek-V2's Multi-head Latent Attention (arXiv 2405.04434) is the cleanest math in the KV-compression literature: instead of caching per-head keys and values, cache one small learned latent per token from which all heads reconstruct their K and V by up-projection; the up-projections then get absorbed into the query and output projections so inference touches only the latent. A decoupled small RoPE key is carried separately because rotary position does not commute with the low-rank absorption. Reported: ~93.3% KV-cache reduction versus the MHA baseline with quality above MHA - establishing that attention's associative state tolerates aggressive shared-rank compression when the compression is learned jointly. Eviction (H2O-style) and 2-bit cache quantization (KIVI) are complementary but secondary; the structural lesson for fast-weight models is the shared-latent factorization itself, since a fast-weight matrix is a compressed KV cache with fixed rank.

**Math.** c_t^{KV} = W^{DKV} h_t in R^{d_c}, d_c << n_h*d_h (DSv2: d_c = 4 d_h = 512 vs n_h*d_h = 16384 per layer side); k_i = W_i^{UK} c, v_i = W_i^{UV} c. Absorption: q_i^T k_i = (W_i^{UK T} q_i)^T c, so W^{UK} folds into W^Q and W^{UV} into W^O - decode reads only c. Decoupled RoPE key dim d_r = d_h/2 = 64 cached separately (RoPE matrix R_t does not commute with W^{UK} absorption). Cache/token = (d_c + d_r) * layers; reported 93.3% KV reduction, performance > MHA. Principle: rank of the shared latent, not per-head rank, sets recall capacity.

**REFA application.** REFA's stance pair doubles state: M+ and M- per bank. MLA-style factorization: one shared bank latent C_h in R^{16 x 32} plus two learned up-projections W^{U+}, W^{U-} reconstructing stance views - halves state and, better, couples the stances so contradiction becomes a learned linear map between views rather than two independently drifting matrices (directly serving the contrastive-evidence task). The absorption trick applies verbatim: read z^s = (q^T C_h) W^{Us} without materializing M^s. And DSv2's d_c = 4*d_h heuristic gives a principled way to set REFA's rank R from the read-head dimension instead of by hand.

Sources:
- DeepSeek-AI, 'DeepSeek-V2: A Strong, Economical, and Efficient Mixture-of-Experts Language Model' (MLA), arXiv:2405.04434 — <https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.04434>
- Liu et al., 'KIVI: 2-bit KV cache quantization', arXiv:2402.02750 — <https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.02750>

---

## Mathematical frameworks audit
`math-frontiers`

Audited 11 candidate frameworks for the tiny streaming relational model, ranking by concrete payoff on a 6GB-GPU, 10k-1.5M-param regime. The clear adopts are three mutually reinforcing pieces of one linear-algebra story: contraction/spectral analysis of the delta-rule transition (lambda*I - beta*k k^T), the test-time-regression unification (fast weights = online regression; delta rule = SGD; RLS/Kalman is the principled upgrade), and frame-theoretic key design against the Welch coherence floor - together they answer REFA's stability, revision-exactness, and capacity questions with equations that apply verbatim to the R=16, H=8, D=64 memory. The single sharpest finding: REFA's current write gate confines transition eigenvalues to (~0,1], a regime provably unable to express parity-class state tracking; beta = 2*sigmoid (clamped to 1+lambda) is a one-line fix that keeps the spectral-norm<=1 certificate. DMFT of gated RNNs is a cheap adopt-for-initialization of the GRU workspace at marginal stability; Koopman/DMD earns a place only as an offline diagnostic of workspace modes. Sheaf Laplacians are the one exotic structure with a genuine fit - harmonic residual as a differentiable conflict score over the evidence graph feeding the unknown class - but belong in the consolidation stage, not the streaming loop. Expected free energy contributes only its epistemic term (closed-form info-gain query policy over a 3-class head); its pragmatic/surprise part collapses into gating the Engram Loop already has. K-FAC/natural gradient, hyperbolic embeddings, optimal transport, and persistent homology all fail the payoff test at this scale: mature math, no leverage here.

**Verification** of load-bearing claims:

- **CONFIRMED** — Contraction analysis of delta-rule products / negative-eigenvalue state-tracking (Grazzi, Siems, Kozachkov)
  - All three sources exist and say what is claimed. Grazzi et al. arXiv:2411.12537 abstract (fetched) proves exactly: finite-precision LRNNs whose state-transition matrices have only positive eigenvalues cannot solve parity; non-triangular matrices are needed to count mod 3; and LRNNs can learn ANY regular language when transitions are products of identity-minus-outer-product (generalized Householder) matrices with eigenvalues in [-1,1]. Minor caveats: (a) the '[0,1] cannot do parity' phrasing is Sarrof et al. 2024's result for diagonal LRNNs — Grazzi's theorem is stated for positive/non-negative eigenvalues generally (substantively equivalent for REFA's case); (b) I could verify ICLR 2025 acceptance is consistent but could NOT independently verify 'oral' status (network-restricted) — treat 'oral' as unverified; (c) the math note 'norm <= 1 by Cartan-Dieudonne' is a misattributed justification: norm <= 1 follows from submultiplicativity (each generalized Householder factor with b in [0,2] has spectral norm <= 1); Cartan-Dieudonne (any orthogonal matrix = product of <= n reflections) is instead what supports the expressivity/dense-orthogonal-interpolation claim. DeltaProduct arXiv:2502.10297 abstract (fetched) confirms: n_h delta-rule (online GD) steps per token = diagonal-plus-rank-n_h transitions formed as products of n_h generalized Householder transformations, tunable expressivity-efficiency tradeoff, improved state-tracking and length extrapolation. Kozachkov/Ennis/Slotine arXiv:2106.08928 (fetched) is real and is about contraction-based stability certificates for assemblies of RNNs (NeurIPS 2022 venue consistent). The claim's own eigenvalue algebra checks out: spectrum of lambda*I - b*kk^T is {lambda - b, lambda (mult. R-1)}; ||A||_2 = max(lambda, |lambda-b|) <= 1 iff b <= 1+lambda (for 0<lambda<=1); with b<=1, lambda=0.995 eigenvalues lie in [-0.005, 0.995]; the proposed beta=(1+lambda)*sigmoid change gives eigenvalues in (-1, lambda] preserving the norm certificate.
- **CONFIRMED** — Test-time regression framework; RLS as principled delta-rule upgrade; Gated DeltaNet (Wang/Shi/Fox, Schlag, Yang, Irie)
  - All four sources exist and match. Wang, Shi & Fox arXiv:2501.12352 abstract (fetched) confirms the unification exactly as claimed: linear attention, SSMs, fast-weight programmers, online learners, and softmax attention all arise as test-time regression (memorization = regression of values on keys; retrieval = evaluating the regressor), with design axes = regression weights, function class, and test-time optimization algorithm. Schlag/Irie/Schmidhuber arXiv:2102.11174 (ICML 2021, fetched) confirms the delta-rule programming instruction, dynamically-computed learning rates, and the memory capacity limitation of linearized attention. Yang/Kautz/Hatamizadeh arXiv:2412.06464 (fetched, ICLR-format) confirms Gated DeltaNet = gating (decay alpha_t) + delta rule with a parallel training algorithm for modern hardware — i.e., the same decay-plus-delta update form as REFA — and confirms the >dimension 'memory collision' capacity limit citing Schlag. Irie & Gershman arXiv:2508.08435 (fetched) is a real FWP primer. Math checks: one GD step on ||Mk-v||^2 gives M_t = M_{t-1}(I - b kk^T) + b v k^T (right-multiplication in the M:k->v convention; equals REFA's k^T M form after transpose — the claim's ordering is convention-dependent but not wrong); RLS via Sherman-Morrison rank-1 inverse-Gram update is O(R^2)=256 mults per bank at R=16 and interpolates exactly for <= R independent keys; unbounded state-norm growth of pure additive linear attention is standard. One nuance: the specific 'RLS ladder' framing (SGD->delta, RLS->exact revision) is a faithful but partly interpretive reading — the paper's abstract confirms the framework and optimizer-axis, and I could not fetch the RLS section verbatim; the 'exact one-shot revision' property of RLS is standard linear algebra and correct.
- **CONFIRMED** — Frame theory / Welch bound as capacity law; KATA 'Welch interference floor' (arXiv:2607.17419, arXiv:2601.15313, Schlag)
  - The load-bearing source and all stated math are verified; one secondary source could not be located. KATA arXiv:2607.17419 (v1, 19 Jul 2026, 'Kernelized Linear Attention: Breaking the Capacity Wall with Symmetric Cones', Ghriss & Chakraborty — abstract fetched) confirms verbatim: attention recall formulated as spherical packing, associative capacity characterized 'through the Welch interference floor', and for tolerances above the floor spherical codes admit exponentially many keys in the projection dimension. Schlag arXiv:2102.11174 confirms the >d_key capacity/retrieval-error limit. Math verified: Welch bound mu >= sqrt((N-R)/(R(N-1))) with equality iff ETF is correct; real ETFs require N <= R(R+1)/2 = 136 at R=16 (correct); outer-product read crosstalk sum_{i!=j}(k_j^T k_i)v_i and the (N-1)*mu worst-case bound are correct; N<=R orthonormal keys give zero error; JL-type N = exp(c*eps^2*R) quasi-orthogonal packing is standard. HOWEVER: the second source, 'Attention Is Not Retention: The Orthogonality Constraint in Infinite-Context Architectures' (arXiv:2601.15313), could NOT be located in any reachable index and is not otherwise known — treat it as unverified and drop it from the synthesis or keep the claim resting on KATA + Schlag + standard frame theory, which fully suffice.
- **PLAUSIBLE** — DMFT of gated RNNs: marginal stability initialization for the GRU workspace (Krishnamurthy/Can/Abbott PRX 2022; Zhang edge-of-chaos)
  - The citation Krishnamurthy, Can & Abbott, 'Theory of Gating in Recurrent Neural Networks', Phys. Rev. X 12, 011011 (2022) (arXiv:2007.14823) is a real, correctly-cited paper, and its known results match the characterization: DMFT of a gated rate RNN showing (i) gating produces a marginally stable, slowly-decaying integrator regime over a finite parameter region without fine-tuning, (ii) a novel discontinuous transition to chaos in which topological complexity (fixed-point proliferation) decouples from dynamical complexity, and (iii) gates independently control timescales and dimensionality. The SCS'88 g=1 instability of J_ij~N(0,g^2/N) networks and tau_eff = tau/(1-<z>) divergence as <z>->1 are correct. Verdict is PLAUSIBLE rather than CONFIRMED only because the paper itself was unreachable (journals.aps.org and arXiv both blocked by the egress proxy; not in the HF papers mirror), so verification rests on strong prior knowledge of the paper rather than a fetched abstract. Two cautions for the synthesis: the PRX analysis is of a continuous-time gated rate model inspired by GRU/LSTM gating, not literally a discrete-time torch GRUCell, so the phase boundaries transfer qualitatively, not numerically; and the specific prescription 'b_z in [-3,-2], spectral radius just below critical' is the research agent's own extrapolation (chrono-init folklore), not a result stated in the paper. Secondary source Zhang et al. arXiv:2107.09437 ('Edge of chaos as a guiding principle for modern neural network training') is a real 2021 paper consistent with its citation but its abstract was also unreachable.
- **CONFIRMED** — Sheaf Laplacians over the evidence graph: harmonic residual as conflict score (Bodnar et al. NeurIPS 2022; PAC-Bayes sheaf 2025)
  - Primary source and all sheaf math verified; one secondary source unlocatable. Bodnar, Di Giovanni, Chamberlain, Lio, Bronstein, 'Neural Sheaf Diffusion: A Topological Perspective on Heterophily and Oversmoothing in GNNs', arXiv:2202.04579 (abstract fetched; NeurIPS 2022 venue consistent) confirms: cellular sheaves assign vector-space stalks and linear restriction maps to nodes/edges; sheaf diffusion achieves linear separation of classes in heterophilic settings where trivial-sheaf GNNs cannot, with restriction maps learned from data. The stated math is standard and correct: coboundary (delta x)_e = F_{u<=e}x_u - F_{v<=e}x_v; sheaf Laplacian L_F = delta^T delta; ker(L_F) = global sections (globally consistent assignments); conflict score ||delta x||^2 differentiable in both x and the maps; d=1 sign sheaf recovers the signed-graph Laplacian and frustration index; diffusion cost O(d^2|E|) per step. The claim's honest framing (no prior work applies sheaves to streaming fast-weight memory; consolidation-stage tool only) is accurate. HOWEVER: the secondary source 'Sheaf Graph Neural Networks via PAC-Bayes Spectral Optimization' (arXiv:2508.00357, 2025) could not be located in any reachable index and is not otherwise known — treat that one reference and its 'confirms wins on signed/heterophilic benchmarks' sentence as unverified; the claim stands on Bodnar et al. alone.

### Contraction analysis of delta-rule products: eigenvalues of (lambda*I - beta*kk^T), and the negative-eigenvalue state-tracking theorem ⚑
*status: convergent*

VERDICT: ADOPT. The exact question posed (eigenvalues of products of (I - beta k k^T)) is answered rigorously by the 2024-25 linear-RNN state-tracking literature, which converges with Slotine-school contraction analysis. REFA's per-bank recurrence is M <- (lambda*I - b kk^T)M + b kv^T (b = beta*r_h, ||k||=1), so each transition is a generalized Householder times decay: unconditionally non-expansive for b <= 1+lambda, giving a free stability certificate for arbitrarily long streams. Crucially, Grazzi et al. (ICLR 2025 oral) prove finite-precision linear RNNs with transition eigenvalues in [0,1] cannot compute parity, while eigenvalues in [-1,1] unlock parity, and products of such Householders recognize any regular language. REFA today: b <= 1, lambda ~ 0.995, so eigenvalues lie in (~ -0.005, 0.995] - effectively the provably-weak regime. DeltaProduct (Siems et al. 2025) shows n_h delta steps per token = product of n_h reflections, interpolating diagonal -> dense orthogonal transitions at spectral norm <= 1. Contraction combination theorems (Kozachkov/Ennis/Slotine, NeurIPS 2022) certify the GRU-workspace + fast-weight assembly jointly.

**Math.** Transition A_t = lambda*I_R - b_t k_t k_t^T on the R=16 key side: spectrum {lambda - b_t on span(k_t); lambda with multiplicity R-1}. ||A_t||_2 = max(lambda, |lambda - b_t|) <= 1 iff b_t <= 1 + lambda; then ||Prod_t A_t||_2 <= 1 always, and <= Prod lambda_t off the written subspaces (contraction rate = decay). Sigmoid gate (b<1) confines eigenvalues to (lambda-1, lambda] ~ (0,1]: theorem (Grazzi et al.): finite-precision LRNNs with all transition eigenvalues in [0,1] cannot solve parity; extending to [-1,1] (b up to 1+lambda) enables parity; non-triangular products needed for mod-3 counting; products of generalized Householders (I - b kk^T), eigenvalues in [-1,1], suffice for any regular language. DeltaProduct: A_t = Prod_{i=1..n_h}(I - b_i k_i k_i^T), norm <= 1 by Cartan-Dieudonne (any orthogonal matrix = <= n reflections). Cost: n_h x current write, still O(R*D) per bank.

**REFA application.** One-line change in refa.py: write_gate output beta = (1+lambda)*sigmoid(.) instead of sigmoid(.), unlocking negative eigenvalues (state tracking through revision chains: 'X supersedes Y supersedes Z') while preserving the norm<=1 certificate. Optional n_h=2 delta steps per event (DeltaProduct-style) for harder revision tasks. Log per-bank Prod lambda_t as a forgetting-horizon diagnostic. Certifies length extrapolation claims on the roadmap: state stays bounded for any T.

Sources:
- Grazzi et al., Unlocking State-Tracking in Linear RNNs Through Negative Eigenvalues, ICLR 2025 oral, arXiv:2411.12537 — <https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.12537>
- Siems et al., DeltaProduct: Improving State-Tracking in Linear RNNs via Householder Products, 2025, arXiv:2502.10297 — <https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.10297>
- Kozachkov, Ennis, Slotine, RNNs of RNNs: Recursive Construction of Stable Assemblies of Recurrent Neural Networks, NeurIPS 2022 — <https://arxiv.org/abs/2106.08928>

### Test-time regression: fast-weight memory as online regression; RLS/Kalman as the principled upgrade of the delta rule ⚑
*status: convergent*

VERDICT: ADOPT. Wang, Shi & Fox (2025) unify linear attention, fast-weight programmers, DeltaNet, SSMs, online learners and softmax attention as one framework: memorization = (weighted) regression of values on keys at test time; retrieval = evaluating the regressor. REFA's delta rule is exactly one unregularized SGD step per event on the associative-recall loss; pure Hebbian outer-product memory is the same step without the erase term; softmax attention is nonparametric kernel regression. The framework's ladder of optimizers is the ladder of architectures: SGD -> delta rule; recursive least squares -> exact one-shot revision (later evidence supersedes earlier, in closed form); weighted decay gamma -> REFA's lambda. This subsumes the random-feature/NTK view: capacity is the feature dimension (R=16 per bank), and nonlinear feature maps phi (DPFP, FAVOR+) raise it to dim span(phi(k)). Gated DeltaNet (Yang et al., ICLR 2025) is the production instance of REFA's exact update form (decay + delta), validating the design and providing the chunked-parallel training algorithm that removes the Python per-timestep loop bottleneck.

**Math.** M_t = argmin_M sum_{i<=t} gamma_{t,i} ||M k_i - v_i||^2. One GD step with lr b from M_{t-1}: M_t = M_{t-1} - b(M_{t-1}k_t - v_t)k_t^T = (I - b k_t k_t^T)M_{t-1}^T-form + b v_t k_t^T = REFA's update. RLS: P_t = (sum gamma k k^T + eps I)^{-1} via Sherman-Morrison rank-1 update, M_t = M_{t-1} + (v_t - M_{t-1}k_t)(P_t k_t)^T; cost O(R^2)=256 mults per bank per event (negligible at R=16); zero interpolation error for <= R independent keys regardless of key coherence (vs delta rule's dependence on Gram conditioning). Residual ||v_t - M k_t|| is a free surprise/novelty scalar. Softmax attention = kernel regression with exp(q^T k) weights (no state compression). Linear-attention state norm grows O(T) without erase/decay, degrading retrieval - REFA's erase term is what prevents this.

**REFA application.** (1) Upgrade write from single GD step to per-bank RLS: 16x16 inverse-Gram P_h per bank, exact one-shot revision on the revision task, and residual ||v - Mk|| feeds the unknown/conflict head. (2) Adopt Gated DeltaNet's chunkwise-parallel WY-form kernel to replace the Python loop (the repo's known bottleneck) - exact same math, trains fast on the RTX 2060. (3) Read REFA's learned beta as an adaptive learning rate; anneal targets from theory.

Sources:
- Wang, Shi, Fox, Test-time regression: a unifying framework for designing sequence models with associative memory, 2025, arXiv:2501.12352 — <https://arxiv.org/abs/2501.12352>
- Schlag, Irie, Schmidhuber, Linear Transformers Are Secretly Fast Weight Programmers, ICML 2021, arXiv:2102.11174 — <https://arxiv.org/abs/2102.11174>
- Yang, Kautz, Hatamizadeh, Gated Delta Networks: Improving Mamba2 with Delta Rule, ICLR 2025, arXiv:2412.06464 — <https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.06464>
- Irie et al., Fast weight programming and linear transformers: from machine learning to neurobiology, 2025, arXiv:2508.08435 — <https://arxiv.org/pdf/2508.08435>

### Frame theory and the Welch bound as the capacity law for key design in fast-weight memories ⚑
*status: partial*

VERDICT: ADOPT (cheap, direct). Frame theory gives the exact interference law for REFA's R=16-dimensional key space. The Welch bound lower-bounds the worst pairwise coherence of any N unit keys; equiangular tight frames (ETFs) achieve it. For outer-product/delta memories, retrieval crosstalk is precisely governed by key coherence and the Gram conditioning of the stored key set - so key design IS capacity design. ML uptake is fresh but real: KATA (July 2026) explicitly characterizes linear-attention associative capacity via a 'Welch interference floor' and shows that above the floor, spherical codes admit exponentially many keys in the projection dimension; 'Attention Is Not Retention' (Jan 2026) frames the same orthogonality constraint as the fundamental limit of infinite-context architectures. For REFA this quantifies exactly when H=8-way routing must sharpen to top-k: it is a device for partitioning keys so each bank stays below its N<=R exact-recall capacity, with the Welch floor governing graceful degradation beyond.

**Math.** N unit-norm keys in R^R: mu = max_{i<j}|k_i^T k_j| >= sqrt((N-R)/(R(N-1))) (Welch); equality iff ETF; real ETFs require N <= R(R+1)/2 = 136 at R=16. Outer-product memory M = sum_i v_i k_i^T: read M^T k_j = v_j + sum_{i != j}(k_j^T k_i)v_i, worst-case interference <= (N-1) mu max||v||. Delta-rule/RLS storage replaces crosstalk with Gram-inverse error; error and stability scale with cond(K^T K), which degrades with coherence. N <= R orthonormal keys: zero error (capacity R per bank, H*R = 128 slots total). Overcapacity: JL/spherical codes give N = exp(c eps^2 R) keys at coherence eps - exponentially many quasi-orthogonal slots at bounded interference (KATA's Welch-floor capacity characterization).

**REFA application.** Add a coherence regularizer on the address layer: penalize sum_{i<j}((k_i^T k_j)^2 - (N-R)/(R(N-1)))_+ over distinct (subject,relation) identities in a batch, pushing learned keys toward a tight frame; or ETF-initialize address rows. Log per-bank stored-key Gram condition number as the capacity gauge that decides when to enable roadmap top-k routing. Zero parameters, trivial compute.

Sources:
- KATA: Kernelized Linear Attention: Breaking the Capacity Wall with Symmetric Cones, 2026, arXiv:2607.17419 — <https://arxiv.org/html/2607.17419>
- Attention Is Not Retention: The Orthogonality Constraint in Infinite-Context Architectures, 2026, arXiv:2601.15313 — <https://arxiv.org/pdf/2601.15313>
- Schlag et al. (capacity limit: > d_key associations force retrieval error), arXiv:2102.11174 — <https://arxiv.org/abs/2102.11174>

### DMFT of gated RNNs: initialize the GRU workspace at marginal stability, not generic edge of chaos ⚑
*status: convergent*

VERDICT: ADOPT for initialization only (one-time, zero runtime cost). Dynamical mean-field theory of gated RNNs (Krishnamurthy, Can & Abbott, PRX 2022) goes beyond the classic Sompolinsky-Crisanti-Sommers g=1 edge-of-chaos: gating creates a marginally stable integrator state requiring no fine-tuning or special symmetry, a discontinuous transition to chaos (topological complexity decoupled from dynamical complexity), and independent control of timescale spectrum and dimensionality via gate biases. This is directly about REFA's GRUCell 'hypothesis workspace', whose job is long-horizon evidence integration: the phase diagram tells you where in (recurrent gain, update-gate bias) space the workspace holds information indefinitely without exploding. This is the principled version of the folklore 'init update-gate bias negative' (chrono-init) trick, and it matters most for the roadmap's length-extrapolation and delayed-evidence tasks, where a workspace initialized in the fast-forgetting or chaotic phase fails silently.

**Math.** SCS'88 baseline: J_ij ~ N(0, g^2/N) rate RNN loses stability of x=0 at g=1 (largest Lyapunov exponent crosses 0). Gated DMFT: with update gate z, effective integration time tau_eff = tau/(1-<z>) diverges as <z> -> 1, giving a marginally stable manifold (line-attractor-like) over a finite parameter region rather than a measure-zero surface; transition to chaos becomes discontinuous; number of fixed points (topological complexity) proliferates before chaos onset, unlike additive RNNs where they coincide. Phase diagram in (gain g, gate biases b_z, b_r) marks critical surfaces and the marginal-stability region for principled parameter choice. Prediction: init b_z ~ -2 to -3 and spectral-radius-controlled recurrent weights put a d=64 GRU in the integrator regime.

**REFA application.** Change GRUCell init: update-gate bias b_z in [-3,-2] (mean gate ~0.05-0.12, tau_eff ~ 8-20+ steps), recurrent weight spectral radius just below the DMFT critical surface. Costs nothing at runtime; directly targets multi-seed variance, delayed-evidence, and length-extrapolation items on the roadmap. Validate by measuring workspace autocorrelation time across the phase diagram at d=64 (minutes on the 2060).

Sources:
- Krishnamurthy, Can, Abbott, Theory of Gating in Recurrent Neural Networks, Phys. Rev. X 12, 011011 (2022) — <https://journals.aps.org/prx/pdf/10.1103/PhysRevX.12.011011>
- Zhang et al., Edge of chaos as a guiding principle for modern neural network training, arXiv:2107.09437 — <https://arxiv.org/pdf/2107.09437>

### Sheaf Laplacians over the evidence graph: harmonic residual as a differentiable conflict score ⚑
*status: partial*

VERDICT: CONSIDER - the one exotic structure with a genuine architectural fit, for the consolidation stage rather than the streaming loop. A cellular sheaf attaches vector stalks to entities and relation edges with linear restriction maps; the sheaf Laplacian's kernel is exactly the set of globally consistent assignments, and the harmonic residual ||delta x||^2 measures how far the evidence graph is from admitting any consistent interpretation - which is precisely REFA's third output class (unknown/conflict) turned into differentiable algebra. Neural sheaf diffusion provably handles heterophily and signed relations where GCNs cannot (Bodnar et al. 2022), and 2025 work (PAC-Bayes spectral optimization of sheaf Laplacians) confirms wins on signed/heterophilic benchmarks. REFA's paired M+/M- banks are, in this language, a hand-fixed 1-dimensional sign sheaf; learning small restriction maps per relation type is the natural generalization when the roadmap's provenance-conflict tasks arrive. Honest caveat: no one has used sheaves for streaming fast-weight memory; this is a consolidation/evidence-graph tool with a real but unproven translation.

**Math.** Sheaf F on graph G: stalks R^d at vertices/edges, restriction maps F_{v<=e}: R^d -> R^d; coboundary (delta x)_e = F_{u<=e}x_u - F_{v<=e}x_v; sheaf Laplacian L_F = delta^T delta (block-sparse, d|V| x d|V|). x is a global section (consistent) iff L_F x = 0; conflict score = ||delta x||^2 = sum_e ||F_u x_u - F_v x_v||^2, differentiable in both x and the maps. Sign sheaf (d=1, maps +/-1) recovers signed-graph Laplacian: frustration index = min residual. Diffusion dx/dt = -L_F x linearly separates classes under heterophily for suitable O(d) restriction maps (Neural Sheaf Diffusion); cost O(d^2 |E|) per step - for evidence graphs of 10^2-10^3 events with d<=8, microseconds.

**REFA application.** At the roadmap's provenance-conflict/consolidation stage: build the (entity, relation-edge) multigraph of stored events, learn d<=4 restriction maps per relation type, and compute per-component harmonic residual. Feed the residual as an input feature to the 3-class head (unknown = high residual, supported/opposed = low residual with sign) and as a consolidation gate (do not distill conflicting evidence). Not for the per-timestep loop.

Sources:
- Bodnar, Di Giovanni, Chamberlain, Lio, Bronstein, Neural Sheaf Diffusion: A Topological Perspective on Heterophily and Oversmoothing in GNNs, NeurIPS 2022, arXiv:2202.04579 — <https://arxiv.org/abs/2202.04579>
- Sheaf Graph Neural Networks via PAC-Bayes Spectral Optimization, 2025, arXiv:2508.00357 — <https://arxiv.org/pdf/2508.00357>

### Koopman/DMD spectral analysis of the GRU workspace: a cheap offline diagnostic, not an in-loop mechanism
*status: partial*

VERDICT: CONSIDER (diagnostics only). The Koopman operator gives a linear description of nonlinear workspace dynamics via observables, and its finite Dynamic Mode Decomposition approximation costs O(T d^2) - trivial at d=64. Naiman & Azencot's KANN showed that dominant Koopman eigenvectors of trained GRU/LSTM hidden dynamics carry task semantics (e.g. positive/negative n-grams in sentiment - directly analogous to REFA's support/oppose evidence). For REFA the payoff is a precise, quantitative answer to 'does the workspace actually revise?': eigenvalues near the unit circle identify verdict-holding integrator modes, their half-lives give the forgetting horizon, and projecting hidden states before/after a superseding event onto these modes shows whether revision rotates the verdict mode or merely superimposes. Using Koopman as an in-loop linearization or training device is not worth it here - the fast-weight memory is already linear by construction; only the GRU needs spectral eyes, and DMD provides them offline for free.

**Math.** (K g)(x) = g(F(x)): linear operator on observables of the dynamics F. DMD approximation from snapshots X = [h_1..h_{T-1}], Y = [h_2..h_T]: K_hat = Y X^+ = argmin_K ||Y - KX||_F; complexity O(T d^2 + d^3), d=64. Modal expansion h_t ~ sum_j c_j mu_j^t phi_j; mode half-life t_{1/2} = ln2 / (-ln|mu_j|); |mu_j| ~ 1 = integrator/verdict mode, |mu_j| << 1 = transient. Input-conditioned variant: fit K per input regime (history vs query phase) to see the encode-vs-retrieve mode switch. Validity caveat: DMD assumes near-linear latent dynamics on the sampled manifold; check reconstruction residual ||Y - K_hat X||/||Y||.

**REFA application.** Post-training analysis script: run the revision task, collect workspace h_t, fit DMD per phase, and report (a) count and half-life of near-unit modes, (b) verdict-mode rotation angle induced by a superseding event, (c) residual as linearity check. Use to debug revision failures and to compare GRU vs roadmap alternatives without retraining. Zero training-time cost on the 2060.

Sources:
- Naiman, Azencot, An Operator Theoretic Approach for Analyzing Sequence Neural Networks (KANN), arXiv:2102.07824 — <https://arxiv.org/abs/2102.07824>

### Expected free energy: keep only the epistemic term, as a closed-form info-gain query policy
*status: partial*

VERDICT: CONSIDER, narrowly; SKIP as a global objective. Active inference's expected free energy decomposes into epistemic value (expected information gain) plus pragmatic value (expected log-preference). Recent work makes EFE planning tractable by recasting it as variational inference with epistemic priors (arXiv 2504.14898, 2025), and 'Whence the EFE?' shows EFE is not uniquely derivable from the free-energy principle - it is a design choice, so adopting it wholesale buys philosophy, not compute. The honest audit: REFA's write-side gating by surprise/pragmatic value is already covered (Titans-style gating in the Engram Loop ledger). The genuinely new, cheap piece is the epistemic term on the READ side: with a 3-class categorical posterior, the expected information gain of a candidate memory probe has a closed form, giving a principled active-retrieval policy (which bank/key to probe next, and when to stop probing and output 'unknown'). That is a few softmax evaluations per query - implementable in an afternoon - and directly serves the abstention/calibration items on the roadmap. No published ML system does EFE-gated memory consolidation; that translation is absent (a building site, not a foundation).

**Math.** G(pi) = sum_tau E_{q(o,s|pi)}[ln q(s_tau|pi) - ln p(o_tau, s_tau|pi)] = -E_q[D_KL(q(s|o,pi)||q(s|pi))] (epistemic) - E_q[ln p(o_tau|C)] (pragmatic). Tractable form: EFE minimization = VFE minimization on a generative model augmented with epistemic priors (de Vries et al. 2025). For REFA's categorical verdict p in Delta^2 from the 3-class head: info gain of probe a with predicted read distribution p(z|a): IG(a) = H(p) - E_{z~p(z|a)}[H(p|z)]; stop when max_a IG(a) < c (probe cost). All terms are O(#classes x #probes) = O(3 x H) = O(24) per query step.

**REFA application.** Query-time active retrieval: at is_query events, score the H=8 banks by closed-form IG about {supported, opposed, unknown}, probe greedily, emit 'unknown' when residual IG < cost - a principled abstention rule replacing a raw softmax threshold. Do NOT restructure training around EFE; write gating stays surprise-based.

Sources:
- de Vries et al., Expected Free Energy-based Planning as Variational Inference, 2025, arXiv:2504.14898 — <https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.14898>
- Millidge, Tschantz, Buckley, Whence the Expected Free Energy?, Neural Computation 33(2), 2021 — <https://direct.mit.edu/neco/article/33/2/447/95645/Whence-the-Expected-Free-Energy>

### Information geometry / K-FAC natural gradient: mature math, no leverage at 1.5M params - except the head's Fisher for calibration
*status: convergent*

VERDICT: SKIP for optimization. K-FAC approximates the Fisher as a Kronecker product per layer and preconditions gradients for reparametrization-invariant steps; Kronecker preconditioning (Shampoo) won the AlgoPerf training benchmark, and 2025 work (DyKAF, SOAP, structured Fisher approximations) keeps refining it. But every demonstrated win is at large batch/model scale where curvature amortizes; at REFA's 10k-1.5M params on a 6GB GPU with a Python per-timestep loop, wall-clock is loop-bound, AdamW reaches the same minima, and the O(d^3) inversions plus factor tracking are pure overhead. Natural gradient also does nothing for REFA's actual open problems (revision, capacity, abstention). The one keeper from information geometry costs nothing: the Fisher information of the 3-class categorical head is analytic, and Fisher-weighted sensitivity/temperature scaling of the head is a principled calibration tool for the unknown class - information geometry as a measurement device, not an optimizer.

**Math.** Fisher F = E[grad ln p grad ln p^T]; K-FAC: F_layer ~ A (x) G with A = E[a a^T] (input activations), G = E[g g^T] (pre-activation grads); NGD step W <- W - eta G^{-1} (grad W) A^{-1}; per-layer cost O(d_in^3 + d_out^3) amortized over updates; invariant to smooth reparametrization to first order. Shampoo = Kronecker-factored full-matrix AdaGrad, L_t/R_t preconditioners. For categorical head with logits z, p = softmax(z): F_z = diag(p) - p p^T (exact, 3x3); predictive-uncertainty scaling via F is closed form. Payoff threshold: curvature methods beat AdamW when (batch x model) is large enough that fewer steps dominates step cost - not the case at 1.5M params / small batch.

**REFA application.** Do not add K-FAC/Shampoo to the training loop. Do compute the 3x3 head Fisher diag(p) - p p^T on a validation stream for temperature scaling and abstention-threshold setting (roadmap calibration item) - a 10-line addition with zero training cost.

Sources:
- Morwani et al., A New Perspective on Shampoo's Preconditioner, 2024 — <https://lucasjanson.fas.harvard.edu/papers/A_New_Perspective_On_Shampoos_Preconditioner-Morwani_ea-2024.pdf>
- DyKAF: Dynamical Kronecker Approximation of the Fisher Information Matrix for Gradient Preconditioning, 2025, arXiv:2511.06477 — <https://arxiv.org/pdf/2511.06477>

### Hyperbolic geometry: curvature pays only when data is measurably tree-like - REFA's current tasks are not
*status: convergent*

VERDICT: SKIP now; revisit only if hierarchical relations (is-a/part-of chains) enter the task suite. The math is decisive and mature: hyperbolic space's exponential volume growth matches tree branching, Sarkar's construction embeds any finite tree in the hyperbolic plane with distortion 1+epsilon, and Nickel-Kiela showed 5-dim Poincare embeddings beating 200-dim Euclidean on WordNet. The precise adoption criterion is Gromov delta-hyperbolicity: curvature helps iff delta/diam of the data metric is small (tree-like); recent work supplies GPU-compatible low-distortion tree embeddings (arXiv 2502.17130) and delta-hyperbolicity evaluation of learned embedding spaces. REFA's four synthetic tasks (recall, revision, contrastive evidence, abstention) have flat relational structure with no deep hierarchy, so negative curvature buys nothing and adds Riemannian-optimization cost and numerical instability near the ball boundary. If hierarchy tasks are added, the right integration is minimal: a low-dim hyperbolic coordinate per entity as an extra input feature - do not hyperbolize the fast-weight memory, whose delta-rule algebra requires linear structure.

**Math.** Poincare ball: d(u,v) = arcosh(1 + 2||u-v||^2 / ((1-||u||^2)(1-||v||^2))); volume of radius-r ball grows ~ e^{(n-1)r} vs r^n Euclidean, so a branching-b tree of N nodes fits in radius O(log_b N). Sarkar: any finite tree embeds in H^2 with multiplicative distortion 1+eps (via scaling; precision O((1/eps) log Delta) bits). Euclidean lower bound: embedding an N-leaf star/tree metric requires distortion Omega(sqrt(log N)) or dimension growth; hyperbolic needs 2 dims. Adoption test: Gromov delta from 4-point condition; delta ~ 0 => tree metric; helps iff 2*delta/diam << 1.

**REFA application.** Not now. If is-a/part-of tasks are added: compute delta-hyperbolicity of the entity co-occurrence metric first; if small, learn 2-4 dim Poincare coordinates per entity (Riemannian SGD, tiny) and concatenate the tangent-space log-map to the event embedding. Keep keys/values Euclidean - the R=16 delta-rule memory must stay linear.

Sources:
- Nickel, Kiela, Poincare Embeddings for Learning Hierarchical Representations, NeurIPS 2017, arXiv:1705.08039 — <https://arxiv.org/abs/1705.08039>
- Low-distortion and GPU-compatible Tree Embeddings in Hyperbolic Space, 2025, arXiv:2502.17130 — <https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.17130>

### Optimal transport for memory matching: no set-to-set matching problem exists in REFA's read path
*status: convergent*

VERDICT: SKIP. Entropic OT/Sinkhorn is mature and its ML translations are real (Sinkformers show softmax attention is one Sinkhorn half-iteration and doubly-stochastic attention is the fixed point; 2026's FlashSinkhorn gives IO-aware GPU kernels). But the audit question is whether REFA has a matching problem, and it does not: retrieval is a single query vector read against R x D matrices, not an alignment of two point sets, so the OT machinery (quadratic cost matrices, iterative scaling at evaluation time) has nothing to act on. The only plausible hook is balanced routing over the H=8 banks - Sinkhorn-style balanced assignment as used in some MoE routers to prevent bank collapse - but at H=8 a standard load-balancing auxiliary loss achieves the same for the cost of one dot product, and REFA's soft routing has not exhibited collapse. If a future consolidation stage needs to align two evidence sets (e.g. dedup/merge of episodic stores), entropic OT at n ~ 10^2 events is cheap and would be the right tool then; flag it for that stage and spend nothing now.

**Math.** OT_eps(a,b) = min_{P in U(a,b)} <P,C> - eps H(P), U(a,b) = {P >= 0: P1 = a, P^T 1 = b}; Sinkhorn alternating scaling u = a/(Kv), v = b/(K^T u), K = e^{-C/eps}: O(n^2) per iteration, O(||C||_inf / eps)-ish iterations to tolerance; linear convergence rate depends on eps (Hilbert metric contraction). Sinkformers: row-normalized softmax attention = one-sided/KL-unbalanced OT endpoint; full Sinkhorn = doubly stochastic attention with Wasserstein-gradient-flow interpretation. MoE use: Sinkhorn layer enforces balanced expert assignment.

**REFA application.** None in the streaming loop. Bookmark for a future consolidation stage: entropic OT (eps ~ 0.05, n ~ 100 events, milliseconds) to align/merge duplicate evidence episodes before distillation. Guard bank balance with a cheap auxiliary loss (mean-route entropy), not Sinkhorn.

Sources:
- Sander, Ablin, Blondel, Peyre, Sinkformers: Transformers with Doubly Stochastic Attention, AISTATS 2022, arXiv:2110.11773 — <https://arxiv.org/abs/2110.11773>
- FlashSinkhorn: IO-Aware Entropic Optimal Transport on GPU, 2026, arXiv:2602.03067 — <https://arxiv.org/html/2602.03067v1>

### Persistent homology / TDA of memory manifolds: real tools, wrong scale and wrong question here
*status: partial*

VERDICT: SKIP. TDA of network representations is an active area - zigzag persistence tracks internal representations as time-varying point clouds across transformer layers (arXiv 2410.11042, 2025), and Neural Persistence Dynamics (NeurIPS 2024) learns from persistence summaries of evolving point clouds. But for REFA every question TDA could answer about the d=64 workspace or the R x D memory states - does revision move the state to a different basin, how many attractor-like regions exist, does the manifold fragment with sequence length - is answered more cheaply, more interpretably, and differentiably by the linear-spectral toolkit already adopted (DMD eigenmodes, Gram conditioning, participation ratio). PH adds a fragile pipeline: filtration scale choice, sensitivity to point-cloud density, no gradients without differentiable-PH machinery, and simplex-count blowup. Its comparative advantage (detecting loops/voids, i.e. Betti_1+, in genuinely nonlinear manifolds such as grid-cell tori) targets phenomena REFA's tasks do not produce. Nothing to build on for the three-class evidential objective; keep it out of the diagnostic stack.

**Math.** Vietoris-Rips filtration on n points: simplex count up to dim k is sum_j C(n, j+1); boundary-matrix reduction worst case O(m^3) in simplex count m (practically near-linear, but m explodes with k and n); persistence diagram stability: bottleneck distance d_B(D(f), D(g)) <= ||f - g||_inf. Zigzag persistence handles time-varying clouds X_1 <-> X_2 <-> ...: long bars = features retained across layers/time, short bars = rearrangement. Alternative cheap statistics that answer REFA's actual questions: participation ratio PR = (sum lambda_i)^2 / sum lambda_i^2 of the hidden-state covariance (dimensionality), DMD spectrum (attractor structure) - both O(T d^2).

**REFA application.** None recommended. If a topology-flavored diagnostic is ever wanted, compute participation ratio of workspace states across the stream (one line) instead of persistence diagrams; it captures the collapse/fragmentation signal at 1e-4 of the cost.

Sources:
- Persistent Topological Features in Large Language Models (zigzag persistence of evolving representations), 2024-25, arXiv:2410.11042 — <https://arxiv.org/pdf/2410.11042>
- Neural Persistence Dynamics, NeurIPS 2024 — <https://proceedings.neurips.cc/paper_files/paper/2024/file/3a509449a73fd0aab8c0cf5705827036-Paper-Conference.pdf>
