## Scout: brain mechanisms absent from the existing ledgers
`neuro-scout`

Scouted brain mechanisms absent from the Engram Loop 26-mapping ledger and Waves-Over-Weights P1-P10, selected for direct porting potential to REFA's typed M+/M- fast-weight banks. Caveat: this sandbox's web access was fully exhausted/blocked (WebSearch budget 200/200 spent by the sweep; proxy 403s on arXiv, PNAS, eLife, Nature, PLoS, Semantic Scholar - verified by direct curl tests), so sources are cited from pre-2026 model knowledge with stable identifiers, claims are kept conservative, and nothing is marked new-since-aug-2026 because post-cutoff literature could not be checked; the synthesis should spot-check the flagged low-confidence identifiers. Five load-bearing finds: (1) the Drosophila mushroom body is a literal support/opposition evidence memory - sparse random expansion (already ported as FlyHash/BioHash) feeding paired approach/avoid MBON channels where dopamine writes by depressing the opposing channel and compartments span a spectrum of decay constants; the valence half is unported and isomorphic to REFA's M+/M- with per-bank lambda. (2) Inhibitory engrams (Vogels iSTDP; Barron 2017) show the brain's opposition memory is a learned cancellation channel with an E-I balance setpoint, making 'unknown' the balanced default - unported and a principled basis for REFA's abstention class. (3) ACT-R's declarative equations are a 40-year-old closed-form evidential retrieval calculus (log power-law base-level activation, fan effect, threshold plus logistic noise equals calibrated retrieval failure) mapping directly onto bank decay spectra and the unknown head. (4) Gershman's latent-cause CRP math converts REFA's always-revise delta rule into a principled revise-vs-allocate-new-trace gate using the prediction error the code already computes. (5) Retrieval-induced forgetting with Norman's oscillating-inhibition algorithm supplies an unported query-time write rule that selectively suppresses intruding competitors. Supporting finds: cerebellar/perceptron capacity math bounding REFA at roughly H*R clean associations, splitter-cell/CSCG context-split addressing, global-workspace top-k bottlenecks, overfitted-brain dream-corruption augmentation, and uncertainty-gated VTE-style deliberation cycles.

**Verification:** the original verifier for this area was blocked by an automated
content filter (a misfire on bulk neuroscience terminology); a replacement
verification pass was run afterwards — see the addendum at the end of this file.

### Drosophila mushroom body valence circuits: a real support/opposition memory, only half-ported ⚑
*status: partial*

The adult fly mushroom body implements paired-channel evidential memory. ~50 antennal glomeruli project randomly (each Kenyon cell samples ~6-7) to ~2000 KCs; APL feedback inhibition keeps ~5% active (sparse expansion coding). KCs converge onto ~34 MBONs (21 types) tiling ~15 compartments; each MBON is approach- or avoid-biased and behavior is a weighted vote between the two channels. Learning: dopamine in a compartment coincident with KC activity depresses the active KC-to-MBON synapses of that compartment; punishment DANs innervate approach-driving compartments, so aversive learning is written by weakening the approach channel (and vice versa) - a write to one stance is a depression of the opposing readout. Compartments have distinct acquisition/forgetting time constants (gamma1 fast, alpha1 slow): a bank of parallel traces with per-compartment decay. ML ports exist only for the expansion/hashing stage (FlyHash LSH Science 2017; fly Bloom-filter novelty PNAS 2018; BioHash ICML 2020; FlyVec ICLR 2021). The two-channel valence plasticity plus compartmental decay spectrum - the part isomorphic to REFA's M+/M- with per-bank lambda - has no mainstream ML translation.

**Math.** FlyHash: h(x)=top-k(Wx), W sparse binary (~6 of 50 inputs per row), 40x expansion (50 to 2000), k/N~5%; expected overlap of two random codes k^2/N ~ 5 cells, so interference is low; outperforms SimHash at fixed hash length on ANN retrieval. Valence readout: V = sum_c sigma_c (w_c . h), sigma_c in {+1,-1}. Compartment plasticity: dw_c/dt = -eta * d_c(t) * h(t) + (w0 - w_c)/tau_c, with d_c = dopamine in compartment c and tau_c spanning minutes (gamma1) to days (alpha1) - a spectrum of exponentials approximating power-law forgetting. Sparse-code capacity: C(2000,100) ~ 10^140 codewords; associative readout capacity scales with synapses per MBON divided by k times load.

**REFA application.** (1) Replace dense address with sparse binary random expansion + top-k over R (fly hash) - decorrelates keys and lifts effective capacity of each R x D bank at near-zero cost. (2) Adopt the fly write rule: an OPPOSE event also depresses what the support channel currently retrieves for k (one extra erase term -beta r_h k (k^T M^{anti-s})), directly serving the contrastive-evidence task. (3) Initialize decay logits log-spaced to span timescales like MB compartments (fast gamma1-like to slow alpha1-like banks).

Sources:
- Dasgupta, Stevens, Navlakha, 'A neural algorithm for a fundamental computing problem', Science 358:793 (2017), DOI 10.1126/science.aam9868 — <https://www.science.org/doi/10.1126/science.aam9868>
- Aso et al., 'Mushroom body output neurons encode valence and guide memory-based action selection in Drosophila', eLife 3:e04580 (2014) — <https://elifesciences.org/articles/04580>
- Aso & Rubin, 'Dopaminergic neurons write and update memories with cell-type-specific rules', eLife 5:e16135 (2016) — <https://elifesciences.org/articles/16135>
- Hige et al., 'Heterosynaptic plasticity underlies aversive olfactory learning in Drosophila', Neuron 88:985 (2015)
- Modi, Shuai, Turner, 'The Drosophila mushroom body: from architecture to algorithm', Annu Rev Neurosci 43:465 (2020)

### Inhibitory engrams: opposition memory as a learned cancellation channel with an E-I balance setpoint ⚑
*status: absent*

Barron, Vogels, Dolan & Behrens (PNAS 2017) synthesize evidence that excitatory engrams acquire co-tuned inhibitory replicas: the default state of a stored memory is silence via precise excitation-inhibition balance, and expression occurs through transient targeted disinhibition. The mechanism is inhibitory synaptic plasticity (Vogels et al., Science 2011): inhibitory synapses potentiate on pre-post coincidence and relax toward a target rate, driving detailed per-stimulus E-I balance that hides Hebbian assemblies without erasing them; suppression of unwanted memories is active inhibitory learning, not weight deletion. This is the brain's actual opposition memory - negation stored as a learned cancellation channel with a balance setpoint rather than as a separate negative-evidence bin. No mainstream ML memory system implements this (gating and adversarial setups are structurally different): essentially unported.

**Math.** iSTDP rule (Vogels 2011): Delta w_inh = eta * x_pre * (y_post - rho0); the fixed point sets inhibitory current to cancel excitatory drive so the postsynaptic rate goes to rho0 (acts as a local integral controller on rate error, provably stabilizing runaway Hebbian excitation). Detailed balance: for each stored pattern mu, I_inh^mu ~ I_exc^mu, so net response ~ 0 until a disinhibition gate d>0 releases it: y^mu = f(I_exc^mu - (1-d) I_inh^mu). Consequence: the silent state carries zero readout but full stored structure; forgetting = balancing, remembering = controlled imbalance.

**REFA application.** Reinterpret M- as a cancellation memory with a setpoint: besides explicit OPPOSE writes, add a slow balancing write Delta M^- = eta_b r_h k (z_net)^T where z_net = z^+ - z^-, driving retrieved net evidence toward 0 unless refreshed. Then z_net gives the three-class output naturally: supported (aligned, large), opposed (anti-aligned), unknown (balanced, |z_net| < eps) - abstention becomes the default state. A learned disinhibition gate from the workspace can transiently unmask old evidence for provenance/conflict checks.

Sources:
- Barron, Vogels, Dolan, Behrens, 'Inhibitory engrams in perception and memory', PNAS 114:6666 (2017), DOI 10.1073/pnas.1701812114 — <https://www.pnas.org/doi/10.1073/pnas.1701812114>
- Vogels, Sprekeler, Zenke, Clopath, Gerstner, 'Inhibitory plasticity balances excitation and inhibition in sensory pathways and memory networks', Science 334:1569 (2011), DOI 10.1126/science.1211095

### ACT-R declarative memory: a 40-year-old closed-form evidential retrieval calculus ⚑
*status: partial*

ACT-R's declarative module (Anderson et al. 2004; rational basis in Anderson & Schooler 1991) is a quantitatively fitted retrieval theory: chunk activation = base-level (log of power-law-decayed use history) + spreading activation from context (with the fan effect: associative strength falls with the log of how many facts share a concept) + partial-matching penalty + logistic noise. Retrieval succeeds only above a threshold - giving a principled 'retrieval failure' (unknown) - and latency is exponential in activation. Base-level learning is a rational adaptation: the odds a memory is needed decay as a power law of time since use, matching environmental statistics. Blending (Lebiere) returns an activation-weighted consensus over matching chunks - soft evidential aggregation. ML status: partial - ACT-R persists as a symbolic architecture; LLM hybrids appeared 2024+ (LLM-ACTR / 'Cognitive LLMs'); agent memory stacks use ad-hoc recency-frequency scores (generative agents); nothing connects these equations to fast-weight/streaming memories.

**Math.** A_i = B_i + sum_j W_j S_ji - P sum_k (1 - sim_k) + eps(s); base-level B_i = ln sum_{j=1..n} t_j^{-d}, d ~ 0.5; fan effect S_ji = S - ln(fan_j); recall probability P_i = 1/(1 + exp(-(A_i - tau)/s)); latency T = F e^{-A_i}; blending V* = argmin_V sum_i p_i (1 - sim(V, V_i))^2 with p_i = softmax(A_i / t). Key structural fact: each use decays as t^{-d}, and t^{-d} ~ sum_h a_h e^{-lambda_h t} for log-spaced lambda_h - a small spectrum of exponentials reproduces power-law retrieval odds (the Laplace/Benna-Fusi trick applied at the retrieval level).

**REFA application.** (1) Emulate ACT-R power-law forgetting by writing each event into ~3 banks with log-spaced lambda_h and a learned readout mixture - testable against single-lambda. (2) Make the unknown head ACT-R's threshold: P(unknown) = 1 - sigma((A - tau)/s) with A a learned function of z^+, z^- - calibrated abstention with two parameters. (3) Fan-effect benchmark: interference should grow as ln(fan) per entity; add fan-controlled splits to the synthetic suite as a falsifiable prediction.

Sources:
- Anderson, Bothell, Byrne, Douglass, Lebiere, Qin, 'An integrated theory of the mind', Psychological Review 111:1036 (2004), DOI 10.1037/0033-295X.111.4.1036
- Anderson & Schooler, 'Reflections of the environment in memory', Psychological Science 2:396 (1991)
- ACT-R home (equations and software) — <http://act-r.psy.cmu.edu/>
- Wu et al., 'Cognitive LLMs: integrating cognitive architectures and large language models' (LLM-ACTR), 2024 (arXiv; ID unverified in this sandbox)

### Latent-cause inference and event segmentation: the revise-vs-new-trace gate REFA hard-codes away ⚑
*status: partial*

Gershman's latent-cause framework (Gershman, Blei & Niv 2010; Gershman, Monfils, Norman & Niv, eLife 2017) formalizes exactly the decision REFA's delta rule currently skips: does new evidence revise an existing trace or open a new one? Observations are clustered into latent causes under a Chinese-restaurant-process prior; small prediction error under the current cause leads to updating the existing memory (reconsolidation/modification window), large PE leads to inferring a new cause and allocating a fresh trace - extinction that preserves the old memory, explaining renewal and spontaneous recovery. Structured Event Memory (Franklin, Norman, Ranganath, Zacks & Gershman, Psych Rev 2020) scales this: events are latent causes with learned RNN dynamics under a sticky-CRP; an event boundary is a posterior shift, and boundaries trigger episodic writes. ML status: partial - SEM runs on video/narrative; DP-mixture change-point ideas appear in task-free continual learning; no memory-augmented sequence model uses posterior-over-causes as its write/revise gate.

**Math.** CRP prior: P(c_t = k | c_{1:t-1}) = N_k/(t-1+alpha) for existing k, alpha/(t-1+alpha) for a new cause; sticky-SEM adds P(c_t = c_{t-1}) proportional to N_k + lambda_sticky. Posterior: P(c_t | o_{1:t}) proportional to prior * N(o_t; f_c(o_{t-1}), Sigma). Revise-vs-allocate rule: revise iff -||o_t - f_c||^2/(2 sigma^2) + ln N_c > ln alpha + const; event boundary = change in argmax posterior. Streaming complexity O(K) per step for K active causes - no replay buffer required.

**REFA application.** Gate the delta write with a streaming CRP test using quantities the code already computes: PE e = ||v - sum_h r_h k^T M_h^s|| (predicted_support/oppose in refa.py). If -e^2/(2 sigma^2) + ln N_h > ln alpha, keep delta-rule revision in the routed bank; else route to the emptiest bank (new cause), leaving the old trace intact - renewal-style context recovery with one comparator plus per-bank counts N_h. PE spikes double as event boundaries for committing/resetting the GRU workspace.

Sources:
- Gershman, Monfils, Norman, Niv, 'The computational nature of memory modification', eLife 6:e23763 (2017) — <https://elifesciences.org/articles/23763>
- Franklin, Norman, Ranganath, Zacks, Gershman, 'Structured Event Memory: a neuro-symbolic model of event cognition', Psychological Review 127:327 (2020), DOI 10.1037/rev0000177
- Gershman, Blei, Niv, 'Context, learning, and extinction', Psychological Review 117:197 (2010), DOI 10.1037/a0017808

### Retrieval-induced forgetting via oscillating inhibition: an unported query-time write rule ⚑
*status: absent*

Retrieval-induced forgetting (Anderson, Bjork & Bjork 1994): retrieving a memory suppresses related competitors, cue-independently - retrieval is itself a write. The mechanistic account is Norman, Newman & Detre's oscillating-inhibition learning algorithm (Psych Rev 2007): a theta-frequency oscillation scans inhibition strength during each retrieval; when inhibition dips below baseline, competitors 'pop up' and their active synapses are weakened; when inhibition rises above baseline, the target barely survives and its weakest supporting synapses are strengthened. This yields targeted, competition-dependent forgetting and strengthening without labels, selective to competitors that actually intrude on the retrieval. ML status: absent - machine unlearning is offline and label-driven; contrastive learning requires negatives to be supplied, not discovered by an inhibition sweep. A genuine building site.

**Math.** Inhibition oscillates I(t) = I_0 + A sin(omega t). Units active only when I < I_0 are competitors: Delta w = -eta_c * x * y on their active synapses; target units still active at I > I_0 receive Delta w = +eta_t * x * y on weak links - the sign of plasticity is keyed to activation phase relative to the inhibition sweep, not plain Hebbian coincidence. Effect: max-margin-like separation of target vs intruding attractors, raising retrieval SNR without global decay. Ported cost: ~2 extra readouts per query at two thresholds, O(2 H R D) per query step - negligible at REFA scale.

**REFA application.** REFA never writes on query steps (is_query gate). Port RIF: on each query, read at two effective thresholds (routing temperatures tau_low, tau_high). Traces retrieved at tau_low but inconsistent with the winning answer are competitors: apply an erase-only delta Delta M = -beta_q r_h k (k^T M - v_hat) to them and sharpen the winner. Predicts and should fix interference accumulation over long streams with repeated queries - directly testable on the distractor and fan-effect tasks.

Sources:
- Norman, Newman, Detre, 'A neural network model of retrieval-induced forgetting', Psychological Review 114:887 (2007), DOI 10.1037/0033-295X.114.4.887
- Anderson, Bjork, Bjork, 'Remembering can cause forgetting: retrieval dynamics in long-term memory', JEP:LMC 20:1063 (1994)

### Cerebellar Marr-Albus capacity math (and CMAC, the original brain-to-ML port) bounds REFA's banks
*status: convergent*

Marr-Albus: mossy-fiber input is expansion-recoded by granule cells (each sampling ~4 inputs) into sparse high-dimensional codes; a Purkinje cell is a perceptron trained by climbing-fiber error via LTD. Two quantitative results deepen this: Brunel et al. (Neuron 2004) showed a sign-constrained (excitatory-only) perceptron has half the Cover capacity and at maximal storage predicts a majority of exactly-silent synapses, matching Purkinje data; Litwin-Kumar et al. (Neuron 2017) showed representation dimension is maximized at small in-degree K ~ 4 with sparsening, explaining conserved anatomy across cerebellum-like circuits including fly Kenyon cells. The ML port is convergent and old - Albus's CMAC (1975) is hashed tile coding, still core RL function approximation - and attention-approximates-SDM (Bricken & Pehlevan, NeurIPS 2021) ties the same architecture to transformers. The value to REFA is the capacity accounting, which says when its banks must fail.

**Math.** Cover/Gardner: unconstrained perceptron stores alpha_c = 2 patterns/synapse; sign-constrained alpha_c = 1 with >= 50% silent (exactly zero) synapses at capacity (Brunel 2004). Expansion: granule-layer dimension maximized at small K with sparse activity (Litwin-Kumar 2017). Applied to REFA: each bank M is an R x D linear associator over L2-normalized keys; error-free capacity = R orthogonal keys (R=16); with random keys, crosstalk ~ (P-1)/R for P stored pairs; the delta rule converges toward the pseudoinverse solution, exact for P <= R. Total clean addressable associations ~ H*R = 128. CMAC: y = sum_{i in tiles(x)} w_i over C overlapping tilings; resolution ~ width/C with memory hashed.

**REFA application.** Publishes REFA's capacity budget: ~128 clean associations at H=8, R=16 - the fan/distractor benchmarks should show a knee there; measure it. To push capacity without VRAM, raise R and sparsify keys top-k (granule-style, ~4 of R active): interference falls as k^2/R while compute stays O(kD). The sign-constraint result warns against forcing nonnegative memories (halves capacity): keep signed M+/M-.

Sources:
- Brunel, Hakim, Isope, Nadal, Barbour, 'Optimal information storage and the distribution of synaptic weights: perceptron versus Purkinje cell', Neuron 43:745 (2004)
- Litwin-Kumar, Harris, Axel, Sompolinsky, Abbott, 'Optimal degrees of synaptic connectivity', Neuron 93:1153 (2017)
- Albus, 'A new approach to manipulator control: the cerebellar model articulation controller (CMAC)', J. Dyn. Sys. Meas. Control 97:220 (1975)
- Bricken & Pehlevan, 'Attention approximates sparse distributed memory', NeurIPS 2021, arXiv:2111.05498

### Splitter cells and clone-structured graphs: context-split addressing for aliased facts
*status: convergent*

Hippocampal splitter cells fire differently on identical spatial trajectories depending on where the animal came from or is going (reviewed/reframed by Duvelle, Grieves & van der Meer, eLife 2023): the hippocampus encodes latent task state, not observables - the biological solution to aliased observations. This has a genuine ML port: clone-structured cognitive graphs (George et al., Nat Commun 2021), a cloned HMM where each observation owns many clone states; EM learning splits an aliased percept into context-specific clones, producing splitter-like context codes, learned maps from aliased sequences, and vicarious evaluation. Convergent as a standalone model, but unintegrated with streaming fast-weight memories: no memory-augmented sequence model allocates context-split addresses for relational facts whose truth depends on context.

**Math.** CSCG: HMM with emission-partitioned states - each observation o owns clones {s_o1..s_oM}, deterministic emission P(o|s) = 1[s in clones(o)]; learn transitions T by Baum-Welch restricted to the partition, complexity O(M^2 E) per step (E = observed transitions), far below a general HMM since emissions are fixed. Inference: belief b_t proportional to (T^T b_{t-1}) elementwise-masked by 1[clones(o_t)] - the same observation activates different clones in different temporal contexts. Capacity: M clones per observation represent up to M distinct contexts per symbol.

**REFA application.** REFA addresses and routes on the event embedding alone, so (s,r) pairs with context-dependent truth alias to one address and overwrite each other. Splitter port: condition addressing on inferred context - address = normalize(W_a [x; workspace]), route = softmax(W_r [x; workspace]/tau) - a two-line change giving clone-like context-split addresses. Test with context-dependent truth tasks (same fact supported in episode A, opposed in B; renewal probes after context switch).

Sources:
- Duvelle, Grieves, van der Meer, 'Temporal context and latent state inference in the hippocampal splitter signal', eLife 12:e82357 (2023) — <https://elifesciences.org/articles/82357>
- George et al., 'Clone-structured graph representations enable flexible learning and vicarious evaluation of cognitive maps', Nature Communications 12:2392 (2021) — <https://www.nature.com/articles/s41467-021-22559-5>

### Global-workspace bottleneck implementations: the upgrade path for REFA's GRU workspace
*status: convergent*

Global-workspace theory has concrete ML implementations absent from both ledgers. Goyal et al. (ICLR 2022) place a small shared workspace between specialist modules: modules compete via top-k attention for write access, then workspace content is broadcast back to all modules; the bandwidth limit itself improves systematic generalization on relational reasoning tasks. VanRullen & Kanai (TiNS 2021) frame GW as a shared multimodal latent trained by cycle-consistent translation between modality encoders, implemented by Devillers, Maytie & VanRullen (2024): semi-supervised alignment through the workspace transfers policies zero-shot across modalities. Status is convergent (2021-2024 ML exists); listed because a GW-shaped bottleneck is the natural upgrade to REFA's single-vector GRUCell and formalizes the seriality needed for conflict arbitration between evidence streams.

**Math.** Write phase: N module outputs provide keys/values; workspace slots (n_s x d) provide queries; A = top-k(softmax(Q K^T / sqrt(d))) with hard k winners (k = 1-2); M <- LN(M + A V). Broadcast phase: each module attends back, h_i <- h_i + softmax(q_i M^T) M. The formal bottleneck: only k coalitions per step get write access, and capacity n_s * d << total module state. Cost O(N n_s d) per step - trivial at REFA scale (N ~ 4 streams, d = 64).

**REFA application.** Replace the GRUCell with a 2-4 slot workspace and top-1 write competition among {event x, z^+, z^-, prediction-error embedding}; broadcast state conditions the router and write gate. Enforces one-evidence-stream-at-a-time revision (serial arbitration on conflicting evidence) and yields an inspectable per-step trace of which stream won access - useful for the conflict and abstention tasks and for debugging revision failures.

Sources:
- Goyal et al., 'Coordination among neural modules through a shared global workspace', ICLR 2022, arXiv:2103.01197
- Devillers, Maytie, VanRullen, 'Semi-supervised multimodal representation learning through a global workspace', 2024 (IEEE TNNLS; arXiv:2306.15711, ID unverified in this sandbox)
- VanRullen & Kanai, 'Deep learning and the global workspace theory', Trends in Neurosciences 44:692 (2021)

### Overfitted-brain hypothesis: dreams as designed corruption, not faithful replay
*status: partial*

Hoel's overfitted-brain hypothesis (Patterns 2021; arXiv:2007.09560): dreams are biologically generated noisy, hallucinatory data - sparse, narratively warped corruptions of waking experience - whose function is to combat overfitting to the day's statistics; the explicit ML analogy is dropout, data augmentation, and domain randomization, and the prediction is that dream deprivation impairs generalization more than memorization. This complements rather than repeats the ledger's CLS/ripple-replay entries: replay there consolidates true episodes with priority ordering; OBH says a portion of replayed content should be deliberately corrupted. ML status: partial - the analogy is explicit and some wake-sleep/generative-replay continual-learning systems inject generator noise, but corruption level as the designed variable OBH implies is not systematically studied in memory-augmented models.

**Math.** Noise injection ~ regularization: for small sigma, E_eps[L(f(x + eps), y)] ~ L(f(x), y) + (sigma^2/2) * ||grad_x f||^2-type penalty (Bishop 1995) - training on corrupted episodes is a Jacobian/curvature penalty steering toward flatter minima. Domain-randomization view: generalization gap shrinks as the support of the augmented distribution covers test-time shifts. The testable dial is corruption rate p*: p -> 0 recovers pure memorization; p too high destroys stored facts - OBH predicts an interior optimum.

**REFA application.** Between streaming episodes, run a cheap dream phase: replay stored event tuples with entity swaps, relation shuffles, and polarity flips as corrupted negatives, training the classifier (memory writes frozen) to answer unknown/opposed correctly on them. Targets a likely REFA failure mode - the classifier overfitting to spurious co-occurrence statistics of the synthetic generator - at zero additional VRAM; sweep corruption rate p to test the predicted interior optimum.

Sources:
- Hoel, 'The overfitted brain: dreams evolved to assist generalization', Patterns 2:100244 (2021); arXiv:2007.09560 — <https://doi.org/10.1016/j.patter.2021.100244>

### Vicarious trial and error / theta cycling: uncertainty-gated serial hypothesis evaluation
*status: partial*

At decision points rats show vicarious trial and error (Redish, Nat Rev Neurosci 2016): hippocampal theta sequences sweep serially down alternative future paths, roughly one option per theta cycle; Kay et al. (Cell 2020) showed representations of the two possible futures alternate at ~8 Hz in constant background cycling. Crucially, VTE is uncertainty-gated: frequent early in learning and at high-conflict choices, vanishing with automatization as control hands off to cached habit. This is distinct from the ledger's replay and phase-precession entries: it is on-line, serial, time-multiplexed hypothesis evaluation with the deliberation budget set by conflict. ML port: rollout planning (MCTS/MuZero) is generically convergent, but the specific pattern - alternation between exactly the competing hypotheses on a fast internal clock, with rollout count annealed to zero as competence grows - is not implemented in streaming memory models.

**Math.** Serial hypothesis multiplexing: per decision, cycles c = 1..C alternate hypotheses; deliberation budget C = C_max * sigma((H(p) - theta)/kappa) with H(p) the entropy of the choice posterior, or the margin |z^+ - z^-| as the gate; habitization sends C -> 0 as visit count/accuracy grows (model-based to model-free arbitration, cf. Daw). Cost: each cycle is one extra query read O(H R D), so expected extra compute E[C] * O(H R D) concentrates on hard queries only - conditional test-time compute without a separate router network.

**REFA application.** On query steps where |z^+ - z^-| < theta, run up to C internal cycles alternating hypothesis-conditioned probes q_+ = normalize(q + W_p e_sup) and q_- likewise, accumulating evidence in the workspace before classifying; gate C by the running margin. Gives the tiny model test-time compute scaling on exactly the contrastive/abstention items where it is uncertain, plus an interpretable per-query deliberation count.

Sources:
- Redish, 'Vicarious trial and error', Nature Reviews Neuroscience 17:147 (2016), DOI 10.1038/nrn.2015.30
- Kay et al., 'Constant sub-second cycling between representations of possible futures in the hippocampus', Cell 180:552 (2020)

## Scout: ML and engineering areas relevant but unmentioned
`ml-scout`

REFA's core update (decay + key-addressed erase + stance write) is mathematically a gated delta rule, which since 2024 has acquired an exact chunkwise-parallel training algorithm (WY representation), production Triton kernels (FLA; adopted in Qwen3-Next/Qwen3.5, Olmo Hybrid), and 2026 refinements (decoupled erase/write GDN-2, curvature-preconditioned DeltaNet) — none cited by either companion document. A parallel theory literature pins down exactly what such recurrences can compute: eigenvalue range [0,1] cannot even do parity, while beta in (0,2) (negative eigenvalues) or Householder products unlock group state-tracking — directly determining REFA's revision-task capacity. Three further unmentioned lanes give the evidence story rigor: semiring provenance (Green-Karvounarakis-Tannen, executable in Scallop with differentiable top-k proofs) is a formal calculus for WHY a conclusion holds; evidential deep learning gives a closed-form Dirichlet head where "unknown" is literally the uncertainty mass K/S, matching REFA's three-class output; and kNN-LM-style semi-parametric interpolation makes every prediction decompose into named memory entries. On engineering: current Triton main dropped RTX 2060 support (CC 8.0+ only; v2.1-v3.2 support 7.0+), SM75 has no native bf16 and no cp.async, and the Python time-loop is kernel-launch-bound, so CUDA-graph capture / chunkwise kernels, not more math, are the speed fix. muP gives near-zero-shot hyperparameter transfer from a 50k-param proxy to the 1.5M target. AbstentionBench (20 datasets) shows abstention does not improve with scale and reasoning fine-tuning actively hurts it — strengthening the case for REFA's architectural (not prompted) unknown class.

**Verification** of load-bearing claims:

- **CONFIRMED** — REFA's update IS a gated delta rule: chunkwise-parallel WY training, mature kernels, and 2026 decoupled-erase variants
  - Sources verified via shallow clones of the official repos (arXiv itself was egress-blocked). fla-org/flash-linear-attention README confirms: DeltaNet chunkwise paper arXiv:2406.06484 ('Parallelizing Linear Transformers with Delta Rule over Sequence Length'); Gated DeltaNet arXiv:2412.06464 ('Gated Delta Networks: Improving Mamba2 with Delta Rule'), and NVlabs/GatedDeltaNet README confirms ICLR '25 acceptance (01/22/2025) plus adoption exactly as claimed: Qwen3-Next (09/10/2025), Qwen3.5 (02/17/2026), Olmo Hybrid (03/12/2026). GDN-2 arXiv:2605.22791 'Gated DeltaNet-2: Decoupling Erase and Write in Linear Attention' (added to FLA 2026-05) and Preconditioned DeltaNet arXiv:2604.21100 'Preconditioned DeltaNet: Curvature-aware Sequence Modeling for Linear Recurrences' (FLA 2026-06, 'ATK preconditioner') both exist and match the characterization; FLA README ships chunk_gdn Triton benchmarks against FlashAttention-2. Test-time regression arXiv:2501.12352 (Wang, Shi, Fox, 'Test-time regression: a unifying framework for designing sequence models with associative memory') exists and does derive the delta rule as one online-regression/gradient step. TWO CORRECTIONS: (1) the stated WY identity is transposed — numerically, prod_{i=1..C}(I - beta_i k_i k_i^T) (in left-to-right order matching S_t = S_{t-1}(I - beta_t k_t k_t^T)) equals I - K^T W, i.e. I - sum_i k_i w_i^T, NOT 'I - W K^T' (with W = T K of shape C x d, W K^T is C x C and dimensionally wrong); T = (I + tril(diag(beta) K K^T, -1))^{-1} diag(beta) is correct and the inverted matrix is indeed C x C unit lower-triangular (verified to machine precision). (2) GDN-2's decoupled gates are channel-wise vectors per the FLA implementation (erase gate b in R^K on the key axis, write gate w in R^V on the value axis; optional allow_neg_eigval multiplies b by 2), not two scalars 'a_t, b_t' as the claim's math writes — the qualitative point (erase and write independently controllable) stands. Complexity O(L C d + L d^2) matmul-dominant vs O(L d^2) sequential is consistent with the paper's algorithm. I could not fetch the 2605.22791/2604.21100 abstracts directly (arXiv blocked); their content is verified from FLA README + code docstrings, which quote the exact titles and mechanisms.
- **CONFIRMED** — State-tracking expressivity: eigenvalue range [0,1] cannot do parity; beta in (0,2) / Householder products unlock it
  - The automl/unlocking_state_tracking repo (cloned; official for arXiv:2411.12537) quotes the full abstract, which matches the claim: finite-precision LRNNs with only positive eigenvalues cannot solve parity; extending DeltaNet/Mamba eigenvalues to [-1,1] enables parity 'without changing the cost of training or inference'; and LRNNs whose transitions are products of (I - v v^T) matrices with eigenvalues in [-1,1] can learn ANY regular language. It also confirms the Sarrof et al. 2024 attribution (failure of diagonal SSMs on parity stems from restricting diagonal transition values to [0,1]); arXiv:2405.17394 is Sarrof, Veitsman, Hahn, 'The Expressive Capacity of State Space Models: A Formal Language Perspective' (NeurIPS 2024). Merrill, Petty, Sabharwal arXiv:2404.08819 'The Illusion of State in State-Space Models' (ICML 2024) is real and does prove SSMs/transformers are in TC0 and cannot solve NC1-hard state tracking (S5 word problem). DeltaProduct arXiv:2502.10297 'DeltaProduct: Improving State-Tracking in Linear RNNs via Householder Products' and RWKV-7 arXiv:2503.14456 'RWKV-7 Goose with Expressive Dynamic State Evolution' both verified via the FLA README paper table; RWKV-7's beyond-TC0 state-tracking/regular-language claim matches that paper. Eigenvalue math verified numerically: with ||k||=1, I - beta k k^T has eigenvalue 1-beta (beta in (0,2) gives (-1,1); beta=2 gives a reflection, eigenvalue -1). ONE NUANCE the synthesis should carry: per the Grazzi abstract, negative REAL eigenvalues suffice for parity (Z_2), but counting modulo 3 requires complex eigenvalues for diagonal transitions — general Z_m and larger groups need products of Householders (DeltaProduct-style) or complex spectrum, not a single beta-in-(0,2) write per token. The claim's math line 'a reflection composes to represent Z_2, Z_m' overstates what one reflection per step gives; Z_m in general needs n_h>1 Householder products.
- **CONFIRMED** — muP / muTransfer: tune hyperparameters on a tiny proxy, transfer zero-shot across width
  - microsoft/mup repo cloned; README verbatim confirms the three desiderata ('Every (pre)activation vector... Θ(1)-sized coordinates'; 'Neural network output should be O(1)'; 'All parameters should be updated as much as possible... without leading to divergence') and that they 'uniquely single out μP'; confirms the Θ(n)-vs-Θ(sqrt(n)) entry-size table for outer-product vs iid matrices; the 1/d (not 1/sqrt(d)) attention scaling (README code comment, with an 8/d backward-compatibility variant); Adam LR of hidden weights scaled by 1/width_mult (fan_in/base_fan_in); the MuReadout output layer; and the coord-check tooling. The GPT-3 detail (6.7B tuned via a ~40M proxy, outperforming the published 6.7B model) is stated in the arXiv:2203.03466 abstract and is accurately characterized (verified from knowledge; abstract fetch was egress-blocked). u-muP arXiv:2407.17465 (Blake et al., 'u-μP: The Unit-Scaled Maximal Update Parametrization', combining muP with unit scaling for low-precision-friendly tensors) is real and correctly characterized. The caveat that transfer is theoretically across WIDTH only (depth/sequence-length transfer empirical at best) is a correct, conservative reading; note the paper does report empirical depth transfer for transformers, so the claim errs on the safe side. The proposed proxy sweep cost argument O(cost(50k) x trials) follows directly.
- **CONFIRMED** — Evidential deep learning / subjective logic: 'unknown' as closed-form Dirichlet uncertainty mass
  - Sensoy, Kaplan, Kandemir, 'Evidential Deep Learning to Quantify Classification Uncertainty' (NeurIPS 2018, arXiv:1806.01768) is real; the cloned dougbrion/pytorch-classification-uncertainty README reproduces the abstract verbatim (subjective logic, Dirichlet on class probabilities, evidence collected by a deterministic net) and confirms the empirical detail exactly as claimed for the expected-MSE loss variant: uncertainty around 1.0 on misclassified/rotated digits vs around 0.2 on correctly classified ones (the digamma/expected-cross-entropy variant gives ~0.7 vs ~0 — the claim cites the MSE figures, which is accurate). README confirms Eq. 4 is the expected cross-entropy under the Dirichlet. Math verified: alpha_k = e_k + 1, S = sum alpha_k, b_k = e_k/S, u = K/S, and sum_k b_k + u = 1 checks numerically; for K=2, u = 2/S. The digamma loss form sum_k y_k(psi(S) - psi(alpha_k)) plus annealed KL(Dir(alpha~)||Dir(1)) with alpha~ = y + (1-y) odot alpha matches the paper's Eq. 4/5. The characterization that 'unknown' is the complement of collected evidence rather than a third softmax class is faithful to the method.
- **CONFIRMED** — Semiring provenance: Green-Karvounarakis-Tannen algebra, executable and differentiable in Scallop
  - scallop-lang/scallop repo cloned; its README states Scallop is 'built on a generalized Provenance Semiring framework' and links exactly the claimed upenn.edu URL for Green, Karvounarakis, Tannen (PODS 2007), confirms configurable semirings for discrete/probabilistic/differentiable reasoning, and shows provenance="difftopkproofs" as the default in the scallopy PyTorch Module example — supporting the claim that top-k proofs are the differentiable provenance backpropagated into an upstream network. Scallop paper (Li, Huang, Naik, PLDI 2023, arXiv:2304.04812) matches. The K-relation semantics (sum over derivations of products over used facts), the universality of N[X] polynomials (any commutative-semiring evaluation factors through them), and the specializations to boolean/why-provenance/counting/access-control/probability are all standard, correct statements of the PODS 2007 results. #P-hardness of exact weighted model counting and top-k proofs as the tractable surrogate are correctly stated; the 'Pr(t) approx sum over top-k proofs of products of input-fact probabilities, with corrections' is a fair description of difftopkproofs semantics. Minor caveat: I verified via the official repo rather than the PODS paper PDF (upenn URL not fetchable through the proxy), but the paper's existence and content are settled literature.

### REFA's update IS a gated delta rule: chunkwise-parallel WY training, mature kernels, and 2026 decoupled-erase variants ⚑
*status: partial*

REFA's per-bank update M <- lambda*M - beta*r_h*k(k^T M) + beta*r_h*k v^T is (up to where decay multiplies the erase term) exactly Gated DeltaNet's recurrence (Yang, Kautz, Hatamizadeh, ICLR'25), now deployed in Qwen3-Next (Sep 2025), Qwen3.5 (Feb 2026) and Olmo Hybrid (Mar 2026). Yang et al. 2024 showed the delta rule, which looks inherently sequential, admits an exact chunkwise-parallel form via the WY representation, so training needs no per-timestep Python loop. The FLA library ships Triton chunk kernels (chunk_gdn) competitive with FlashAttention-2. Two 2026 successors matter for REFA: Gated DeltaNet-2 'Decoupling Erase and Write in Linear Attention' (arXiv:2605.22791) separates the erase strength from the write strength — REFA currently ties both to one gate beta*r_h, so it cannot erase without writing (e.g. retract evidence) or write without erasing; and Preconditioned DeltaNet (arXiv:2604.21100) adds curvature-aware preconditioning of the linear recurrence. Also relevant: the test-time-regression framework (arXiv:2501.12352) derives the delta rule as one online-regression step on loss ||M k - v||^2, giving a principled way to design REFA's write as an optimizer step.

**Math.** Recurrence: S_t = S_{t-1}(I - beta_t k_t k_t^T) + beta_t v_t k_t^T (gated: multiply S_{t-1} by alpha_t). Chunk of length C: prod_{i=1..C}(I - beta_i k_i k_i^T) = I - W K^T with W = T K, T = (I + tril(diag(beta) K K^T, -1))^{-1} diag(beta) (UT/WY transform; the inverse is of a C x C unit lower-triangular matrix). Cost: O(L C d + L d^2) matmul-dominant vs O(L d^2) strictly sequential; the delta write is one gradient step on L(M) = 1/2||v - M^T k||^2 with step beta (test-time regression view). GDN-2: separate gates alpha_erase != beta_write in S_t = S_{t-1}(I - a_t k_t k_t^T) + b_t v_t k_t^T.

**REFA application.** Replace the Python per-timestep loop with chunkwise WY training (exact, testable against the existing float64 equivalence suite): treat (bank h, stance s) as head index, R=16 as key dim, D=64 as value dim. Adopt GDN-2's decoupled gates so a 'retraction' event can erase from M+ without writing, which the revision task directly stresses. FLA's chunk_gdn kernels are importable but assume Ampere+; on the RTX 2060 implement the same chunk math in plain PyTorch matmuls (still removes the T-step loop).

Sources:
- Yang, Wang, Zhang, Kim — Parallelizing Linear Transformers with the Delta Rule over Sequence Length (arXiv:2406.06484) — <https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.06484>
- Yang, Kautz, Hatamizadeh — Gated Delta Networks (ICLR 2025, arXiv:2412.06464); repo verified incl. Qwen3-Next/Qwen3.5/Olmo adoption — <https://github.com/NVlabs/GatedDeltaNet>
- FLA library README (verified 2026-08): GDN-2 arXiv:2605.22791, Preconditioned DeltaNet arXiv:2604.21100, chunk kernels — <https://github.com/fla-org/flash-linear-attention>
- Wang, Shi, Fox — Test-time regression: a unifying framework (arXiv:2501.12352) — <https://arxiv.org/abs/2501.12352>

### State-tracking expressivity: eigenvalue range [0,1] cannot do parity; beta in (0,2) / Householder products unlock it ⚑
*status: absent*

A tight theory now bounds what REFA's recurrence can compute. Merrill, Petty & Sabharwal ('The Illusion of State in State-Space Models', arXiv:2404.08819, ICML'24) prove diagonal SSMs and transformers are in TC0, so they cannot solve NC1-hard state tracking (e.g. permutation composition / S5 word problem) at unbounded length; Sarrof et al. (arXiv:2405.17394) show SSMs with nonnegative real eigenvalues cannot recognize parity. Grazzi, Siems et al. ('Unlocking State-Tracking in Linear RNNs Through Negative Eigenvalues', arXiv:2411.12537, ICLR'25) prove that widening the state-transition eigenvalue range from [0,1] to [-1,1] — for DeltaNet, allowing write strength beta in (0,2) so (I - beta k k^T) has eigenvalue 1-beta in (-1,1) — enables parity and modular counting at no extra cost (GitHub repo description verified). DeltaProduct (arXiv:2502.10297) takes n_h Householder steps per token, giving state transitions that are products of generalized Householder matrices, interpolating between diagonal and dense orthogonal, capturing larger permutation groups. RWKV-7 (arXiv:2503.14456) generalizes the delta rule with vector gating and shows expressivity beyond TC0 (state tracking / regular-language recognition) with constant memory.

**Math.** REFA's transition (per bank, before decay) is I - beta~ k k^T with beta~ = sigmoid(gate)*r_h in (0,1): eigenvalues in (0,1] — provably cannot compute parity of an event stream (products of matrices with nonneg spectrum cannot represent -1). With beta in (0,2): eigenvalue 1-beta in (-1,1); a reflection (beta=2) composes to represent Z_2, Z_m, and with products of n_h Householders, subgroups up to S_n. Complexity frame: diagonal/SSM and transformers in TC0; group word problems for non-solvable groups are NC1-complete; length generalization on parity requires |eigenvalue| reaching -1.

**REFA application.** REFA's revision semantics ('later evidence flips earlier') is a state-tracking demand, e.g. an even/odd chain of retraction events is parity. Concretely: change write_gate to 2*sigmoid(.) so beta in (0,2), and allow negative per-bank decay range if length-extrapolating revision counts matter; test on a parity-of-retractions synthetic task before/after. If deeper tracking is needed (evidence about evidence), one DeltaProduct-style double-Householder write per event is the cheapest upgrade.

Sources:
- Grazzi et al. — Unlocking State-Tracking in Linear RNNs Through Negative Eigenvalues (arXiv:2411.12537); repo automl/unlocking_state_tracking verified via GitHub search — <https://github.com/automl/unlocking_state_tracking>
- Merrill, Petty, Sabharwal — The Illusion of State in State-Space Models (arXiv:2404.08819) — <https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.08819>
- Siems et al. — DeltaProduct (arXiv:2502.10297); title/ID verified via FLA README — <https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.10297>
- Peng et al. — RWKV-7 Goose (arXiv:2503.14456) — <https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.14456>

### muP / muTransfer: tune hyperparameters on a 50k-param proxy, transfer zero-shot to the 5M target ⚑
*status: absent*

Maximal Update Parametrization (Yang & Hu; Tensor Programs V, arXiv:2203.03466) makes optimal hyperparameters (learning rate, init scales, multipliers) stable across model WIDTH: tune a tiny proxy, transfer to the wide model with no re-tuning. Verified from the official README: muP is derived from three desiderata — (1) every preactivation has Theta(1) coordinates throughout training, (2) network output O(1), (3) every parameter updated as much as possible without divergence — and is the unique parametrization satisfying them. Empirically GPT-3 6.7B was tuned via a 40M proxy and beat the original. Practical rules: replace output layer with a 1/width-scaled readout, use 1/d (not 1/sqrt(d)) attention scaling, scale Adam LR of hidden matrices like 1/width, init variance like 1/fan_in. Caveats that matter for REFA: transfer is across width (d_model, memory_rank, num_banks as widths), NOT across depth or sequence length; nonstandard modules (GRUCell workspace, the delta-rule write gate, route temperature) need empirical 'coord checks' (activation scale vs width plots, provided in the mup package) to confirm correct scaling. u-muP (arXiv:2407.17465) combines muP with unit scaling so tensors stay near unit variance — synergistic with fp16-only Turing hardware.

**Math.** Key table (README): for correlated 'outer-product' matrices A (gradients) and iid-init A, entry size of A v is Theta(n) vs Theta(sqrt(n)) for n-dim v with Theta(1) entries. Resulting scalings: W_hidden init Var ~ 1/fan_in; readout multiplier ~ 1/fan_in; Adam LR(W_hidden) ~ 1/width; attention logits q.k/d not q.k/sqrt(d). Consequence: argmin_LR L(width, LR) is approx width-independent, so HP search cost drops from O(cost(5M) x #trials) to O(cost(50k) x #trials).

**REFA application.** Define a base REFA at d_model=16, R=4, H=2; sweep LR, route temperature init, beta-gate init, decay init, and fusion multipliers there (hundreds of cheap runs on the 2060); set_base_shapes then transfers to d=64/R=16/H=8 and beyond. Run mup coord checks specifically on the GRUCell and the M read/write path since fast-weight states are nonstandard under Tensor Programs; validate with the repo's existing multi-seed harness.

Sources:
- Yang, Hu et al. — Tensor Programs V: Tuning Large Neural Networks via Zero-Shot Hyperparameter Transfer (arXiv:2203.03466); README math section verified — <https://github.com/microsoft/mup>
- Blake et al. — u-muP: The Unit-Scaled Maximal Update Parametrization (arXiv:2407.17465) — <https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.17465>

### Evidential deep learning / subjective logic: 'unknown' as closed-form Dirichlet uncertainty mass, not a third softmax class ⚑
*status: partial*

Sensoy, Kaplan & Kandemir (NeurIPS 2018, arXiv:1806.01768; abstract verified) replace softmax with a network that outputs nonnegative EVIDENCE per class, treated as parameters of a Dirichlet over class probabilities (subjective logic). Belief mass and an explicit uncertainty mass come in closed form, so 'I don't know' is not a learned third class competing in a softmax but the complement of total collected evidence — exactly the semantics REFA wants for supported/opposed/unknown. Their loss (expected MSE or expected cross-entropy under the Dirichlet, plus an annealed KL toward the uniform Dirichlet on the misleading part of evidence) drives evidence toward zero off-distribution; the verified README reproduces uncertainty approx 1.0 on misclassified/rotated digits vs approx 0.2 on correct ones. This composes with (does not duplicate) the companion doc's abstention-rewarding proper scoring: EDL is an architectural uncertainty head; proper scoring is a training criterion. Successors (posterior networks, natural posterior nets) refine the density model but the two-class-evidence version is the right minimal fit here.

**Math.** Evidence e_k >= 0 (e.g. softplus of logits), alpha_k = e_k + 1, S = sum_k alpha_k. Belief b_k = e_k / S, uncertainty u = K / S, with sum_k b_k + u = 1 (K = number of classes). Expected prob p_k = alpha_k / S. Loss (Eq.4/5): sum_k y_k (psi(S) - psi(alpha_k)) + lambda_t KL( Dir(alpha~) || Dir(1) ), alpha~ = y + (1-y) odot alpha, lambda_t annealed 0->1. For REFA, K=2 (support, oppose): u = 2/S; predict 'unknown' iff u > threshold; calibration curve = coverage vs risk under u.

**REFA application.** Replace the 3-logit output head with two evidence heads e+ = softplus(g+(workspace, z+)) and e- = softplus(g-(workspace, z-)) fed by the M+ and M- readouts, so evidence mass literally comes from the stance memories; output supported/opposed by b+ vs b-, and 'unknown' when u = 2/S is high. Gives free type-2 calibration metrics (u vs error) for the abstention task with approx zero extra parameters.

Sources:
- Sensoy, Kaplan, Kandemir — Evidential Deep Learning to Quantify Classification Uncertainty (NeurIPS 2018, arXiv:1806.01768); abstract + equations verified via implementation README — <https://github.com/dougbrion/pytorch-classification-uncertainty>
- arXiv:1806.01768 — <https://arxiv.org/abs/1806.01768>

### Semiring provenance: a rigorous algebra for WHY a conclusion holds, executable and differentiable in Scallop ⚑
*status: absent*

Green, Karvounarakis & Tannen (PODS 2007) showed that annotating each database fact with an element of a commutative semiring (K, +, x, 0, 1) and evaluating queries with + over alternative derivations and x over joint use of facts yields, in the free semiring of polynomials N[X], the most general provenance: the polynomial records exactly which facts, combined how, support each conclusion. Specializations recover boolean truth, set-based why-provenance, counting, access control, and probability. Scallop (Li, Huang & Naik, PLDI 2023, arXiv:2304.04812; README verified) makes this executable and DIFFERENTIABLE: its 'difftopkproofs' provenance keeps the top-k proofs (DNF clauses) per derived fact, scores each proof as a product of input-fact probabilities, and backpropagates through the k best proofs into an upstream neural network — precisely a trainable evidence-combination layer. This is the missing formal layer for REFA: the fast-weight banks give fuzzy recall, but a semiring annotation gives auditable provenance (which events, per conclusion) and a principled conflict semantics (support and opposition as separate polynomial annotations rather than a subtraction).

**Math.** K-relation semantics: for query q and tuple t, ann(t in q(D)) = sum over derivations d of t of ( prod over facts f used in d of ann(f) ), in a commutative semiring. N[X] polynomials are universal (any semiring evaluation factors through them). Scallop difftopkproofs: keep top-k DNF clauses; Pr(t) approx sum_{j<=k} prod_{f in proof_j} p_f (with inclusion-exclusion or WMC corrections); gradient d Pr / d p_f flows through proofs containing f. Complexity: exact WMC is #P-hard; top-k proofs is the tractable surrogate, O(k) proofs per fact per rule application.

**REFA application.** Run a tiny semiring shadow ledger next to the banks: each written event gets id x_i; maintain for each (s,r,o) two top-k proof sets (support, oppose) updated on write, giving exact citations for any output and a symbolic cross-check of the neural three-class decision (disagreement = probe set for the confabulation battery). At REFA scale (synthetic streams, thousands of events) this costs kilobytes. Scallop itself can be the reference implementation for the relational tasks.

Sources:
- Green, Karvounarakis, Tannen — Provenance Semirings (PODS 2007); linked from verified Scallop README — <https://repository.upenn.edu/cgi/viewcontent.cgi?article=1022&context=db_research>
- Li, Huang, Naik — Scallop: A Language for Neurosymbolic Programming (arXiv:2304.04812); repo README verified incl. provenance='difftopkproofs' — <https://github.com/scallop-lang/scallop>

### AbstentionBench: abstention does not improve with scale and reasoning fine-tuning makes it WORSE
*status: absent*

AbstentionBench (Kirichenko et al., Meta FAIR, arXiv:2506.09038; README verified) is a holistic abstention benchmark: 20 datasets (3 new underspecified-reasoning sets) across 6 scenarios — underspecified context, unknown answers, stale data, etc. — over 20 open and closed LLMs with human-validated judges for both abstention and correctness. Two verified headline findings: (1) model scale has almost no effect on abstention ability; (2) reasoning fine-tuning HURTS abstention — reasoning models answer over-confidently and rarely abstain, even on unanswerable math/science questions. This is the strongest recent external evidence for REFA's core bet: 'unknown' must be an architectural output backed by memory state (absent/conflicting evidence), not an emergent behavior of scale or chain-of-thought training. It also supplies scenario taxonomy REFA's synthetic abstention tasks should mirror (answer-absent vs underspecified vs stale/superseded — the last one is exactly REFA's revision setting).

**Math.** Task: given query q, decide answer vs abstain; metrics are judge-based abstention recall/precision on unanswerable vs answerable splits (selective-prediction style: risk-coverage). Findings are invariant across parameter scale (no significant trend over model sizes), and fine-tuning for reasoning shifts the operating point toward low abstention recall. For REFA the relevant scoring is proper selective risk: R(tau) = E[err | u <= tau] vs coverage P(u <= tau), with u the uncertainty mass from the evidential head.

**REFA application.** Adopt AbstentionBench's 6-scenario taxonomy as the blueprint for REFA's abstention suite: (a) never-stated facts, (b) underspecified queries (entity exists, relation unqueried), (c) stale/superseded evidence (revision), (d) conflicting evidence, plus risk-coverage curves as the reported metric. Positions REFA as a mechanistic counterexample to the scale-does-not-help result at 1M params.

Sources:
- Kirichenko et al. — AbstentionBench (arXiv:2506.09038); README with 20 datasets / 6 scenarios / findings verified — <https://github.com/facebookresearch/AbstentionBench>

### MoE routing math for sparse top-k bank routing: load-balance loss, router z-loss, expert-choice, Sinkhorn
*status: partial*

REFA's roadmap wants sparse top-k routing over its H banks; the MoE literature has the exact math for keeping such routers healthy at small scale. (1) Auxiliary load-balance loss (Shazeer/Switch): differentiable surrogate pushing the token->expert distribution uniform. (2) Router z-loss (ST-MoE, arXiv:2202.08906; formula verified in the st-moe-pytorch transcription): penalizes large router logits, fixing instability from softmax saturation — important because REFA already learns a route temperature that can saturate. (3) Expert-choice routing (Zhou et al., arXiv:2202.09368): invert the assignment — each expert selects its top-k' tokens — giving perfect load balance by construction and variable compute per token. (4) Sinkhorn/BASE routing (Lewis et al. arXiv:2103.16716; Clark et al. arXiv:2202.01169): treat assignment as entropic optimal transport solved by a few Sinkhorn iterations. Caveat for REFA: MoE balance losses assume interchangeable experts; REFA banks are SEMANTIC (relation-typed), so balance should be a weak regularizer (prevent dead banks) not a hard constraint — expert-choice per batch is the safer mechanism since it cannot starve a bank.

**Math.** Balance loss: L_aux = alpha * H * sum_{i=1..H} f_i * P_i, f_i = fraction of events routed to bank i (top-k hard), P_i = mean_t softmax(router(x_t))_i; minimized (=1) at uniform. z-loss: L_z = (1/B) * sum_{t} ( log sum_{i} exp(x_{t,i}) )^2. Expert choice: score S = softmax(X W_g) in R^{T x H}; each bank i takes top-(k*T/H) events of column S_{:,i} -> perfect balance. Sinkhorn: P* = argmax_{P in U(a,b)} <P, log S> + eps * H(P) via iterative row/col scaling, converges linearly.

**REFA application.** When moving from soft routing to top-k=2 over H=8 (and later H=64 with RAM offload): add L_z (coef about 1e-3) immediately — it stabilizes the learned-temperature router; add weak L_aux (about 1e-2) only to prevent dead banks; consider expert-choice writes (each bank ingests its top events per chunk) which meshes naturally with chunkwise delta-rule training from finding 1.

Sources:
- Zoph et al. — ST-MoE: Designing Stable and Transferable Sparse Expert Models (arXiv:2202.08906); z-loss/balance coefficients verified in st-moe-pytorch README — <https://github.com/lucidrains/st-moe-pytorch>
- Zhou et al. — Mixture-of-Experts with Expert Choice Routing (arXiv:2202.09368) — <https://arxiv.org/abs/2202.09368>
- Lewis et al. — BASE Layers (arXiv:2103.16716); Clark et al. — Unified Scaling Laws for Routed Language Models, Sinkhorn-BASE (arXiv:2202.01169) — <https://arxiv.org/abs/2202.09368>

### RTX 2060 (SM 7.5) engineering facts: Triton main dropped Turing; no native bf16; kernel-launch-bound loops need CUDA graphs
*status: absent*

Verified from Triton READMEs: current main supports 'NVIDIA GPUs (Compute Capability 8.0+)' only, while v2.1.0 through v3.2.0 state 'Compute Capability 7.0+'. So on the RTX 2060 (SM 7.5) you must pin an older Triton (<=3.2, i.e. the version shipped with PyTorch <=2.6-era builds); custom Triton kernels written today against main will not target the machine. SM 7.5 hardware facts: fp16 tensor cores with fp32 accumulate exist; bf16 has NO native compute path (bf16 mma requires SM80) — train in fp16 + GradScaler or fp32, not bf16; cp.async (async global->shared copies) requires SM80, so software-pipelined kernels (num_stages>2) lose their benefit on Turing. The bigger practical lever for REFA's Python time-loop: the model is tiny, so each of the approx 15-20 kernel launches per timestep (about 5-10 us CPU launch overhead each) dwarfs the microseconds of actual compute; T=512 steps means order 50-150 ms of pure launch overhead. Fixes that work on Turing: torch.compile(mode='reduce-overhead') / manual torch.cuda.CUDAGraph capture of the whole fixed-shape T-step loop, or the chunkwise reformulation (finding 1) which turns the loop into approx T/C big matmuls. accelerated-scan (verified benchmarks) shows a fused first-order scan doing seqlen 65536 x 1536 dims in 11.3 ms — the decay part of REFA is exactly its recurrence x_t = gate*x_{t-1} + token.

**Math.** Launch-bound regime: T_total approx T * N_kernels * t_launch when t_compute << t_launch; REFA: 512 * 18 * 7us approx 65 ms/sequence of overhead vs <5 ms compute. CUDA graph replay amortizes launches to one, bounding speedup by N_kernels*t_launch/t_replay, realistically 5-20x for tiny RNN cells. Precision: fp16 has 10 mantissa bits (eps ~ 9.8e-4) vs bf16's 7; on SM75 use fp16 accum-fp32 (tensor core HMMA) + dynamic loss scaling. Chunkwise alternative: O(T/C) launches of O(C*R*D) matmuls.

**REFA application.** Immediate wins on the 2060 without new kernels: (1) keep shapes static and capture the whole forward loop in a CUDA graph (works because REFA's loop has no data-dependent control flow); (2) train fp16+GradScaler, never bf16; (3) pin triton<=3.2/matching torch if writing kernels, and avoid num_stages>2 and tl.dot on bf16; (4) use accelerated-scan for the pure-decay baseline and chunk-WY for the full delta rule.

Sources:
- Triton README at main (CC 8.0+) vs v2.1.0/v3.0-3.2 (CC 7.0+) — both verified directly — <https://github.com/triton-lang/triton>
- accelerated-scan: first-order associative scan kernels + benchmark table (verified) — <https://github.com/proger/accelerated-scan>
- NVIDIA Turing architecture: bf16 mma and cp.async require SM80 (PTX ISA / CUDA C programming guide, established) — <https://docs.nvidia.com/cuda/parallel-thread-execution/>

### kNN-LM semi-parametric interpolation: predictions that decompose into named memory entries (built-in provenance)
*status: absent*

kNN-LM (Khandelwal et al., ICLR 2020; repo verified) interpolates a parametric model with a non-parametric nearest-neighbor distribution over an episodic datastore of (context-key, value) pairs, improving Wikitext-103 perplexity from 18.65 to 15.79 without any retraining — memorizing rare patterns explicitly beats forcing them into parameters. The property that matters for REFA is structural, not the perplexity: the kNN term is a sum over IDENTIFIABLE stored items, so every prediction has an explicit citation set (which entries, with what weights) — provenance for free — and the datastore can be edited (add/delete a fact) with immediate effect and no gradient step. RETRO (Borgeaud et al., arXiv:2112.04426) scales the same idea with chunked cross-attention to a trillion-token store. This is the complementary memory to REFA's fast weights: the banks compress and generalize (lossy, no citations); an episodic log retrieves exactly (lossless, cited). The companion doc's hippocampal store is Hopfield-attention recall; the kNN-LM math adds the principled OUTPUT-level fusion rule and its single scalar knob lambda (or a learned, per-query lambda as in adaptive variants).

**Math.** p(y | x) = lambda * p_kNN(y | x) + (1 - lambda) * p_model(y | x), with p_kNN(y|x) proportional to sum over (k_i, v_i) in N_k(f(x)) of 1[y = v_i] * exp( -d(f(x), k_i) / tau ). Cost: one k-NN query per step over |D| entries (FAISS: O(sqrt(|D|))-ish probes); memory O(|D| * dim). Editing: delete entry i removes its additive term exactly — machine unlearning by subtraction.

**REFA application.** Keep an append-only episodic log of (address-key k_t, event id, polarity) beside the banks (kilobytes at REFA scale). At query time, retrieve top-k log entries by q-key similarity, form a count-based evidence distribution, and interpolate with the bank-derived head: p = lambda*p_log + (1-lambda)*p_bank. The log neighbors ARE the citation set, and give an exact-recall fallback when bank capacity (R=16 keys/bank) saturates.

Sources:
- Khandelwal et al. — Generalization through Memorization: Nearest Neighbor Language Models (ICLR 2020, arXiv:1911.00172); repo verified — <https://github.com/urvashik/knnlm>
- Borgeaud et al. — RETRO (arXiv:2112.04426) — <https://arxiv.org/abs/2112.04426>

### Fast-weight capacity bound and hypernetwork-generated per-relation parameters (HypER)
*status: partial*

Two related capacity facts. (1) Schlag, Irie & Schmidhuber ('Linear Transformers Are Secretly Fast Weight Programmers', arXiv:2102.11174 — the paper that introduced the delta-rule write REFA uses; verified via FLA citation table) show a fast-weight matrix M in R^{R x D} can store at most R associations retrievable without interference (keys must be linearly independent; orthogonal for zero crosstalk). REFA's R=16 means each bank holds at most 16 clean (s,r,o) associations — a hard, testable capacity number per bank (128 across H=8), and the reason routing/top-k matters: routing partitions the key space so capacity adds across banks. Their DPFP kernel expands effective key dimension without parameters. (2) HypER (Balazevic, Allen & Hospedales, ICANN 2019, arXiv:1808.07018; repo verified) generates relation-SPECIFIC parameters from a shared hypernetwork: filters w_r = H z_r from relation embedding z_r, so parameters per relation cost |z| shared dims instead of full per-relation matrices, and unseen-but-similar relations get sensible parameters by embedding proximity. Hypernetworks (Ha et al., arXiv:1609.09106) are the general form: theta_r = g_phi(z_r).

**Math.** Capacity: retrieval z = M^T k; for stored set {(k_i, v_i)}, exact recall for all i requires {k_i} linearly independent -> at most R = rank(M) items; crosstalk error for item j: sum_{i != j} (k_j . k_i) v_i, zero iff orthogonal. Delta rule replaces (not accumulates) the value at a key, keeping interference bounded. HypER params: |z|*d_z + d_z*|w| vs N_rel*|w| (linear in relations -> constant); scoring f(s,r,o) = sigma(vec(e_s * w_r) W) e_o.

**REFA application.** Report bank load vs the R=16 bound as a diagnostic (recall should degrade past 16 distinct keys/bank — a predicted failure curve for the capacity task). Longer term: replace the discrete H=8 banks with hypernetwork-generated per-relation read/write transforms and per-relation (lambda, beta) from relation embeddings — new relations inherit dynamics from similar ones, and 'number of banks' stops being a hard hyperparameter.

Sources:
- Schlag, Irie, Schmidhuber — Linear Transformers Are Secretly Fast Weight Programmers (arXiv:2102.11174); cited as DeltaNet origin in verified FLA table — <https://arxiv.org/abs/2102.11174>
- Balazevic et al. — Hypernetwork Knowledge Graph Embeddings (arXiv:1808.07018); repo verified — <https://github.com/ibalazevic/HypER>
- Ha, Dai, Le — HyperNetworks (arXiv:1609.09106) — <https://arxiv.org/abs/1609.09106>

### Compressive Transformer and Infinity-former: lossy long-term tiers with reconstruction losses, beyond plain exponential decay
*status: partial*

REFA currently forgets only by exponential decay lambda about 0.995, which erases uniformly regardless of importance. Two architectures give principled lossy long-term memory. Compressive Transformer (Rae et al., ICLR 2020, arXiv:1911.05507): instead of discarding old activations, compress them at rate c (e.g. strided conv) into a second memory tier, trained with an attention-reconstruction loss — compressed memories must preserve what the model actually attends to, not pixel-level content; temporal receptive field grows by factor c at fixed cost. Infinity-former (Martins, Marinho & Martins, ACL 2022, arXiv:2109.00301; repo verified): represent unbounded history as a CONTINUOUS signal over [0,1] expanded in N fixed basis functions (RBFs), attend with continuous (Gaussian) attention; memory is O(N) independent of length — unbounded context, bounded state — plus 'sticky memories' that re-allocate basis resolution to frequently-attended regions (importance-weighted forgetting). Both are fragments the companion's Benna-Fusi cascade gestures at, but with concrete trainable losses and exact memory-cost equations.

**Math.** Compressive: old n_m activations -> n_m/c compressed; range approx l*(n_s + n_m + c*n_cm) vs l*(n_s+n_m); reconstruction loss L_cr = || attn(h, old_mem) - attn(h, f_c(old_mem)) ||^2 (attention-content, not autoencoding). Infinity-former: X(t) = B^T psi(t), psi = N RBF basis; continuous attention E[X(t)], t ~ N(mu, sigma^2) per query; memory O(N) for any T; sticky: resample basis centers proportional to past attention mass p(t). REFA tier: M_slow <- M_slow + P_c(evicted component of M), P_c a rank-(R/c) projection trained with read-reconstruction loss.

**REFA application.** Add a per-bank slow tier: when decay would erase mass, project the evicted component of M into a rank-4 'gist' matrix trained so that reads against old queries are preserved (attention-reconstruction, not weight reconstruction). Delayed-evidence and provenance-conflict tasks then distinguish 'forgotten' from 'compressed-but-recoverable' — and this is the natural math for the roadmap's VRAM/RAM bank-residency offload (slow tiers live in RAM).

Sources:
- Rae et al. — Compressive Transformers for Long-Range Sequence Modelling (arXiv:1911.05507) — <https://arxiv.org/abs/1911.05507>
- Martins et al. — Infinity-former: Infinite Memory Transformer (arXiv:2109.00301); repo verified — <https://github.com/deep-spin/infinite-former>

### Quantization math for the 6GB budget: NF4 quantile quantization, GPTQ Hessian rounding — for state and future encoder, not the tiny model
*status: absent*

REFA's 10k-1.5M-param models need no weight quantization (<6MB fp32); the 6GB pressure comes from (a) streaming bank state at scale (B x H x 2 x R x D), (b) the roadmap's RAM-offloaded bank residency, and (c) a future text encoder. The relevant math: QLoRA's NF4 (Dettmers et al., arXiv:2305.14314; abstract verified) is 4-bit quantile quantization — 16 levels placed at equal-probability quantiles of N(0,1), matched to zero-mean normal weight distributions ('information-theoretically optimal' per the paper; strictly, minimal expected quantization error under the Gaussian prior among 4-bit codes with per-block absmax scaling), plus double quantization of the per-64-block scale constants (saves about 0.37 bits/param). GPTQ (Frantar et al., arXiv:2210.17323) does one-shot layer-wise quantization by greedy per-column rounding that minimizes output reconstruction error using the layer Hessian H = 2 X X^T, with Cholesky-based updates of remaining columns. Dettmers & Zettlemoyer's k-bit scaling laws (arXiv:2212.09720) find 4-bit near-universally optimal in accuracy-per-total-bits for inference. Caveat: streaming STATE is not Gaussian-stationary like trained weights; per-chunk absmax int8 for cold banks is the safe first step, NF4 only for truly frozen tiers.

**Math.** NF4 levels: q_i = midpoints of Q_N(0,1) at i/17 quantiles, normalized to [-1,1]; store w_hat = absmax_block * q(round); block 64, double-quant scales in 8-bit -> 4.127 bits/param effective. GPTQ objective per layer: argmin_W_hat ||W X - W_hat X||_2^2; quantize column q, then update remaining: delta_F = -(w_q - quant(w_q)) * [H_F^{-1}]_{:,q} / [H_F^{-1}]_{q,q}. Memory: REFA banks at H=64, R=16, D=64, 2 stances, fp16 = 256 KB/sequence-state; int8 halves it — offload bandwidth (PCIe about 12 GB/s) dominates, not capacity.

**REFA application.** For bank-residency offload: keep hot (top-k routed) banks fp16 in VRAM, cold banks int8 (per-bank absmax) in pinned RAM, dequantize on prefetch — quantization error only touches banks not currently routed to. Reserve NF4/GPTQ for the future frozen text encoder: a 100M-param encoder is about 60MB at 4 bits, leaving the 6GB VRAM almost entirely to batch and state.

Sources:
- Dettmers et al. — QLoRA: Efficient Finetuning of Quantized LLMs (arXiv:2305.14314); NF4/double-quant abstract verified — <https://github.com/artidoro/qlora>
- Frantar et al. — GPTQ (arXiv:2210.17323) — <https://arxiv.org/abs/2210.17323>
- Dettmers, Zettlemoyer — The case for 4-bit precision: k-bit inference scaling laws (arXiv:2212.09720) — <https://arxiv.org/abs/2212.09720>
