## Follow-up (critic-directed): Streaming-sketch and mergeable-summary t
`followup:Streaming-sketch and mergeable-summary t`

The streaming-sketch literature already owns REFA's central object: with the erase term off, each bank M_h = Σ_t λ^{T−t} β_t r_{t,h} k_t v_tᵀ is a decayed linear sketch, and the Li–Nguyen–Woodruff turnstile theorem says linear sketches are the canonical form for any deletion-supporting, mergeable streaming memory. One-sided-error theory supplies certified rather than merely learned inputs for the support/oppose/unknown head: count-min only over-counts, Misra–Gries only under-counts, SpaceSaving only over-counts, Frequent Directions only deflates spectral mass, and Bloom filters have zero false negatives — so 'zero support evidence' and 'no evidence ever written' become provable statements, not classifier opinions. The Agarwal et al. mergeability framework pinpoints exactly which REFA component blocks the roadmap's offload and consolidation: the delta-rule erase term makes the state transition a product of non-commuting contractions, and supersession (order-sensitivity) is provably incompatible with commutative merging — resolvable via a linear shadow bank, forward-decay reparameterization with read-side recency, or FD-style merge-and-shrink. Conservative update turns out to be the counting analogue of REFA's residual write (delta rule = CU with assignment where CU uses max), and recent analyses show a phase transition to asymptotically exact counts below a hypergraph-peeling load threshold. IBLTs give exact full-state enumeration below ~0.81 pairs/cell and O(difference)-cost shard reconciliation — a formal audit/decoding organ; Frequent Directions gives a deterministic, mergeable capacity law for the R×D banks. ML bridgeheads exist (learned frequency estimation, learned/neural Bloom filters, FetchSGD's summed gradient sketches), but nothing in ML treats fast-weight memories as sketches with guarantees, so most findings are building sites. Caveat: this session's web-search budget and external egress were exhausted before verification; classic results are cited from stable knowledge, and the 2022–2024 items (Fusy–Kucherov phase transition, rateless IBLT, listing-guarantee IBLTs) are explicitly flagged as ID-unverified.

**Verification** of load-bearing claims:

- **CONFIRMED** — Count-min one-sided error: certified upper bounds justify the M+/M− polarity split
  - Citation correct: Cormode & Muthukrishnan, J. Algorithms 55(1):58–75, 2005 (DOI 10.1016/j.jalgor.2003.12.001). All stated math matches the paper: d=⌈ln(1/δ)⌉, w=⌈e/ε⌉, pairwise-independent hashes, a_i ≤ â_i always for non-negative updates, Pr[â_i > a_i + ε‖a‖₁] ≤ δ (Thm 1); turnstile median estimator |â−a| ≤ 3ε‖a‖₁ w.p. 1−δ^{1/4} (Thm 2). CountSketch citation correct (Charikar–Chen–Farach-Colton, ICALP 2002 / TCS 312, 2004); the ℓ2-residual-tail form ε‖a_tail(1/ε²)‖₂ is the standard modern restatement, not the paper's original top-k phrasing — accurate but attribution is to the folklore/later analysis. Parameter math verified: w=272 ⇒ ε≈1%, d=3 ⇒ δ=e⁻³≈5%; the '~13KB for both stances' figure assumes 8-byte counters (1632 counters); with 4-byte counters it is ~6.5KB. 'One-sided iff monotone non-negative updates' is correct in this sketch framework. Verified from established knowledge; live fetch impossible this session (egress blocked).
- **PLAUSIBLE** — Conservative update: the delta rule's monotone cousin, with a phase transition to exactness
  - Estan–Varghese SIGCOMM 2002 ('New Directions in Traffic Measurement and Accounting') is real and does introduce conservative update; the stated CU rule, the invariant a_i ≤ â^CU ≤ â^CMS, and one-sided over-count error are all correct. The merge claim was checked and is mathematically sound: since min_j(C₁+C₂)[j] ≥ min_j C₁[j] + min_j C₂[j], summed CU sketches remain valid over-estimates but only at vanilla-CMS error grade. Ben Mazziane–Alouf–Neglia ('A Formal Analysis of the Count-Min Sketch with Conservative Updates', ~2022, Zipf-focused, journal version in Computer Networks) and Fusy–Kucherov phase-transition results (relative error → 0 below a peeling-type load threshold on random streams, Θ(1) above, 2022–2024) both exist to my knowledge and are characterized consistently, but the exact arXiv IDs, venues, and theorem conditions (results are for uniformly random streams, as the claim correctly hedges) could not be re-verified this session — all academic domains blocked at the egress proxy and search budget exhausted. The claim itself flags these as unverified, which is honest. Treat the phase-transition specifics as plausible, not certified.
- **CONFIRMED** — Mergeability theory: the delta-rule erase term is exactly what blocks offload and consolidation
  - All three citations correct: Agarwal–Cormode–Huang–Phillips–Wei–Yi 'Mergeable Summaries' PODS 2012 / ACM TODS 38(4) 2013 (proves linear sketches merge by addition exactly, MG≅SpaceSaving with merged error ≤ (N_A+N_B)/(k+1) via add-counters-then-subtract-(k+1)-st-largest, and mergeable quantile summaries); KLL = Karnin–Lang–Liberty FOCS 2016, arXiv 1603.05346; FetchSGD = Rothchild et al. ICML 2020, arXiv 2007.07682 (Count-Sketched gradients merged by summation at the server). The REFA-side math was checked independently and is correct: with erase off, M_T = Σ_t λ^{T−t} β_t r_t k_t v_tᵀ is linear in per-event increments and hence exactly sum-mergeable; with erase on, the transition Π_t(λI − w_t k_t k_tᵀ) is a product of non-commuting contractions (commuting only for mutually orthogonal or parallel keys), so merge(M(A),M(B)) ≠ M(A⊎B) and order-sensitivity is genuinely incompatible with commutative mergeability. Verified from established knowledge; live fetch impossible this session.
- **CONFIRMED** — IBLT: exact recovery below a peeling threshold — a formal audit/decoding organ
  - Core citations correct: Goodrich–Mitzenmacher 'Invertible Bloom Lookup Tables', Allerton 2011, arXiv 1101.2245 (cells of count/keySum/valueSum with optional hashkeySum; insert/delete/merge are cellwise additions; listEntries peels degree-1 cells; failure probability polynomially small); Eppstein–Goodrich–Uyeda–Varghese 'What's the Difference?' SIGCOMM 2011 (IBF subtraction decodes the symmetric difference at cost O(|AΔB|) independent of |A|,|B|). Threshold math verified against known 2-core peeling constants for random k-uniform hypergraphs: c₃ ≈ 0.818, c₄ ≈ 0.772, k=3 minimizes overhead, m ≈ 1/0.8184 · t ≈ 1.222t. 4096 cells × 16B = 64KB checks out. The recent extensions are real to my knowledge — Bar-Lev–Etzion–Yaakobi 'IBLTs with Listing Guarantees' (~2022+) and Yang–Gilad–Alizadeh 'Practical Rateless Set Reconciliation' SIGCOMM 2024 (Rateless IBLT) — and the claim correctly flags them unverified; the '~1.35t coded cells' overhead figure is consistent with my recollection of the Rateless IBLT paper but could not be re-verified this session (egress blocked), so treat that specific constant as plausible rather than certified.
- **CONFIRMED** — Frequent Directions: a deterministic, mergeable, one-sided capacity law for R×D banks
  - Citations correct: Liberty KDD 2013 (arXiv 1206.0594) and Ghashami–Liberty–Phillips–Woodruff, SIAM J. Computing 45(5), 2016 (arXiv 1501.01711). The stated guarantee matches GLPW: for all k < l, 0 ⪯ AᵀA − BᵀB ⪯ (‖A−A_k‖_F²/(l−k))·I — i.e., genuinely one-sided (BᵀB never overstates AᵀA) with error bounded by the rank-k tail energy over (l−k); the shrink step B ← sqrt(max(Σ²−σ_l²I,0))Vᵀ is the paper's algorithm; space Θ(ld) is proven optimal and GLPW prove mergeability (stack two sketches, re-shrink, bound preserved exactly). Co-occurring Directions is real: Mroueh–Marcheret–Goel, 'Co-Occuring Directions Sketching for Approximate Matrix Multiply', AISTATS 2017, arXiv 1610.07686, with spectral error O(‖A‖_F‖B‖_F/l) for AᵀB — matching the claim (the claim's 'ID unverified' hedge can be resolved to 1610.07686, though I could not re-fetch it this session). The sub-millisecond 32×64 SVD cost estimate is uncontroversial. Verified from established knowledge; live fetch impossible this session (egress blocked).

### Count-min one-sided error: certified upper bounds justify the M+/M− polarity split ⚑
*status: partial*

Count-min sketch never under-counts: for non-negative update streams the point estimate always upper-bounds the true count, exceeding it by more than an epsilon-fraction of total stream mass only with probability delta. Crucially, one-sidedness REQUIRES non-negative monotone updates: signed (turnstile) streams force median estimators with two-sided error, and CountSketch handles signs only at the price of unbiased two-sided l2-tail error. The sketch literature's design rule follows: never mix positive and negative evidence in one min-structure; keep two non-negative sketches and compare at read time. This is exactly REFA's separate M+ (support) and M− (oppose) banks — the capacity/SNR literature treats that split as a heuristic, but sketch theory identifies it as the unique way to preserve certified one-sided semantics per stance. ML has fragments (hashing trick, Bloom embeddings, count-based exploration bonuses) but none exploit the one-sided guarantee for evidential three-way outputs.

**Math.** CMS: d = ceil(ln(1/\delta)) rows, w = ceil(e/\epsilon) counters per row, pairwise-independent h_j; update C[j,h_j(i)] += c with c >= 0; estimate \hat{a}_i = min_j C[j,h_j(i)]. Guarantees: a_i <= \hat{a}_i always, and Pr[\hat{a}_i > a_i + \epsilon ||a||_1] <= \delta. Space O(\epsilon^{-1} log(1/\delta)) counters, O(log(1/\delta)) per op. Signed streams: median estimator gives only |\hat{a}_i − a_i| <= 3\epsilon||a||_1 w.p. 1 − \delta^{1/4}. CountSketch: w = O(1/\epsilon^2), E[\hat{a}_i] = a_i, |\hat{a}_i − a_i| <= \epsilon ||a_{tail(1/\epsilon^2)}||_2 (two-sided, l2-tail-scaled; better on skewed streams). Rule: one-sided error iff updates are monotone non-negative.

**REFA application.** Add per-stance counting CMSs keyed by hash(s,r,o): d=3, w=272 gives \epsilon=1%, \delta≈5% at ~13KB per bank for both stances. Certified semantics for the 3-class head: \hat{s}=0 certifies zero support evidence was ever written; output 'supported' only when \hat{s} − \epsilon N > \hat{o} (upper bound beats upper bound with margin). Free calibration probe: compare head confidence against certified counts.

Sources:
- G. Cormode, S. Muthukrishnan, 'An improved data stream summary: the count-min sketch and its applications', J. Algorithms 55(1):58–75, 2005 — <https://doi.org/10.1016/j.jalgor.2003.12.001>
- M. Charikar, K. Chen, M. Farach-Colton, 'Finding frequent items in data streams', ICALP 2002 / Theoretical Computer Science 312(1), 2004

### Conservative update: the delta rule's monotone cousin, with a phase transition to exactness ⚑
*status: absent*

Estan–Varghese conservative update (CU) increments only the counters currently equal to the minimum — write only where the structure under-represents the key. Error stays one-sided (over-count) and is pointwise <= vanilla CMS; empirically 1.5–4x better. Formal analysis is recent: Ben Mazziane–Alouf–Neglia give error bounds (Zipf case), and Fusy–Kucherov prove a phase transition — for random streams, below a critical load (keys per counter; a hypergraph-peeling-type threshold) CU's relative error tends to 0, i.e. asymptotically EXACT counts, while above it error is Theta(1) [2022–2024 results; IDs unverified this session — egress blocked]. Structurally, CU is 'write the deficit': the counting analogue of the delta rule's residual write k(v − kᵀM)ᵀ, but using max (monotone, never destroys stored mass) where the delta rule uses assignment (destructive erase). Cost: the CU improvement is not mergeable — summing two CU sketches retains only the vanilla CMS guarantee. No ML fast-weight write rule uses CU-style monotone deficit semantics; this is a building site.

**Math.** CU update for (i,+c): \hat{a}_i = min_j C[j,h_j(i)]; then C[j,h_j(i)] <- max(C[j,h_j(i)], \hat{a}_i + c). Invariant a_i <= \hat{a}_i^{CU} <= \hat{a}_i^{CMS}. Deficit form: counter j gains (\hat{a}_i + c − C[j,h_j(i)])^+ — a clipped residual write. REFA delta rule: M <- M + \beta r k(vᵀ − kᵀM), i.e. readout kᵀM <- (1−\beta r)·old + \beta r·vᵀ — assignment-like, can erase. CU = same residual target with max instead of assignment => monotone accumulation, one-sided recall. Phase transition (Fusy–Kucherov): with load \lambda = n_keys/w and k hashes, exists \lambda_c(k) (peeling/core-type threshold) s.t. relative error -> 0 for \lambda < \lambda_c and Theta(1) above. Merge: CU_1 + CU_2 is a valid over-estimate but only CMS-grade.

**REFA application.** Concrete ablation (~20 lines in refa.py): replace the erase term with a monotone clipped deficit write per stance, so support recall is non-decreasing in support evidence (certifiable), destructive cross-fact interference disappears, and supersession is carried by the oppose bank plus recency weighting rather than erasure. Prediction: better retention on contrastive-evidence and distractor tasks at equal parameters; below a routed-load threshold, near-exact recall.

Sources:
- C. Estan, G. Varghese, 'New directions in traffic measurement and accounting', SIGCOMM 2002
- É. Fusy, G. Kucherov, phase-transition and worst-case analyses of count-min with conservative updates, 2022–2024 (arXiv; exact IDs unverified this session)
- Y. Ben Mazziane, S. Alouf, G. Neglia, 'A Formal Analysis of the Count-Min Sketch with Conservative Updates' / journal version in Computer Networks, 2022 (unverified)

### Mergeability theory: the delta-rule erase term is exactly what blocks offload and consolidation ⚑
*status: partial*

Agarwal–Cormode–Huang–Phillips–Wei–Yi formalize mergeability — merge any two summaries, keep both size and error guarantee, closed under arbitrary merge trees — and prove: linear sketches merge by plain addition exactly; Misra–Gries and SpaceSaving (shown isomorphic) merge with a preserved deterministic error bound; mergeable quantile summaries exist (later optimized by KLL). This is precisely the math REFA's bank-residency VRAM/RAM offload and episodic-to-parametric consolidation need and lack. Applied to REFA: without the erase term the bank is a decayed linear sketch, so shards built on different devices or time-slices sum EXACTLY. With the erase term the transition is a product of non-commuting contractions (\lambda I − \beta r k kᵀ): order matters, merge(M(A),M(B)) != M(A ⊎ B), and no merge operator can fix it — supersession (order-sensitivity) is inherently incompatible with commutative mergeability. ML fragment: FetchSGD merges CountSketched gradients across federated clients by summation, evidence that the linear-merge discipline scales; no ML work manages the linearity-vs-order-sensitivity tradeoff for fast-weight memory.

**Math.** Mergeable: forall A,B: merge(S(A),S(B)) is a size-k, error-\epsilon summary of A ⊎ B, closed under arbitrary merge trees. Linear sketch: S(A⊎B) = S(A) + S(B) exactly. MG/SS with k counters: error <= N/(k+1); merge = add counters, subtract the (k+1)-st largest, keep top k — bound preserved (error adds over N_A+N_B). REFA transition: M_t = (\lambda I − \beta_t r_t k_t k_tᵀ) M_{t−1} + \beta_t r_t k_t v_tᵀ. Erase off: M_T = \Sigma_t \lambda^{T−t} \beta_t r_t k_t v_tᵀ — linear in per-event increments, sum-mergeable with zero merge error. Erase on: \Pi_t (\lambda I − w_t k_t k_tᵀ) is non-commuting unless all k_t are mutually orthogonal => order-dependent; commutativity is necessary for mergeability => impossible in general.

**REFA application.** Split the architecture: keep the nonlinear delta-rule bank as the ONLINE path; maintain a parallel linear shadow bank (Hebbian + decay, or forward-decay form) as the MERGEABLE path used for offload, cross-device merge, and consolidation into slow weights, reconciled via an IBLT ledger. Alternative: make writes linear and move supersession to read-time recency weighting. Either way, the roadmap's offload gets a correctness criterion (exact merge equivalence) instead of a hope.

Sources:
- P. K. Agarwal, G. Cormode, Z. Huang, J. M. Phillips, Z. Wei, K. Yi, 'Mergeable Summaries', PODS 2012; ACM TODS 38(4), 2013
- Z. Karnin, K. Lang, E. Liberty, 'Optimal quantile approximation in streams', FOCS 2016 — <https://arxiv.org/abs/1603.05346>
- D. Rothchild et al., 'FetchSGD: Communication-Efficient Federated Learning with Sketching', ICML 2020 — <https://arxiv.org/abs/2007.07682>

### Turnstile streaming ≡ linear sketching: REFA's linear read/write is the canonical form
*status: absent*

Li–Nguyen–Woodruff (STOC 2014) prove that any one-pass turnstile (insertion+deletion) streaming algorithm can be simulated by maintaining a linear sketch Ax with only logarithmic space overhead: within the class of deletion-supporting, mergeable streaming memories, linearity is without loss of generality — not a design choice. Kallaugher–Price later showed the equivalence requires long/adversarial streams (strict separations exist for short streams), so it is a structural guide rather than a straitjacket. Combined with space lower bounds for l1 point queries and heavy hitters, this calibrates what a 16x64 bank can certify and frames the delta rule precisely: it is the deliberate departure from turnstile semantics (commutative signed updates) into order-dependent semantics. No ML work frames fast-weight memories in this canonical-form language; the repo's exact streamed-linear rewrite is unknowingly the canonical member of REFA's complexity class.

**Math.** LNW'14: turnstile algorithm with space S => linear-sketch algorithm maintaining Ax (A fixed, poly-bounded entries) with space O(S · log n). Kallaugher–Price'20: equivalence fails for short streams (separation). Lower bounds: \epsilon-l1 point query / heavy hitters need \Omega(\epsilon^{-1} log(\epsilon n)) bits; CMS matches at O(\epsilon^{-1} log(1/\delta) log n). Calibration heuristic for REFA: bank = R·D = 16·64 = 1024 cells; per-bank resolution floor \epsilon >~ c/1024 of routed l1 evidence mass; H=8 banks with disjoint routed mass multiply capacity by at most H; routing collisions transfer directly into effective \epsilon.

**REFA application.** Theory-backed scaling experiment: per-bank recall error should scale ~1/(R·D) in routed evidence mass, and sparse top-k routing helps only insofar as it decorrelates bank loads — measurable now with the synthetic recall task by sweeping R, D, H. Also elevates the float64-equivalent streamed linear rewrite from optimization to canonical form for everything except the erase term.

Sources:
- Y. Li, H. L. Nguyen, D. P. Woodruff, 'Turnstile streaming algorithms might as well be linear sketches', STOC 2014
- J. Kallaugher, E. Price, 'Separations and equivalence between turnstile streaming and linear sketching', STOC 2020 (arXiv:1905.02358, ID unverified)

### IBLT: exact recovery below a peeling threshold — a formal audit/decoding organ ⚑
*status: absent*

Goodrich–Mitzenmacher invertible Bloom lookup tables store key-value pairs in m cells of (count, keySum, valueSum); insert AND delete are cellwise additions, merge is cellwise addition, and listEntries peels degree-1 cells to recover the ENTIRE stored key-value multiset EXACTLY, w.h.p., whenever the number of stored pairs t is below a load threshold given by random-hypergraph peeling (t <~ 0.81m at k=3 hashes). Above threshold it fails gracefully (partial listing). Because deletion is native, subtracting two IBLTs decodes the symmetric difference at cost proportional to the difference, not the sets (set reconciliation). Recent extensions: worst-case listing-guarantee constructions (Bar-Lev–Etzion–Yaakobi) and rateless IBLTs (Yang–Gilad–Alizadeh, SIGCOMM 2024) that decode t differences from ~1.35t–2t coded cells without knowing t in advance [recent IDs unverified this session]. This is the exact-recovery counterpart to REFA's approximate dense banks — the formal model the audit/decoding path lacks.

**Math.** m cells, k hashes; cell = (count, keySum, valSum[, hashkeySum]); insert/delete: +/- into k cells; merge: cellwise addition. Listing succeeds w.h.p. iff t/m < c_k, the 2-core (peeling) threshold of random k-uniform hypergraphs: c_3 ≈ 0.818, c_4 ≈ 0.772; k=3 near-optimal => m ≈ 1.222·t cells; failure probability polynomially small in m. All ops O(k)=O(1). Reconciliation: IBLT(A) − IBLT(B) lists A\B and B\A when |A \Delta B| is below threshold — cost O(|A \Delta B|), independent of |A|,|B|. Rateless variant: expected ~1.35·t cells to decode t differences, universal in t.

**REFA application.** Shadow ledger per bank: IBLT keyed by h(s,r,o,polarity), value (count, last-step). Gives (1) exact enumeration of everything a bank holds — the audit/decode path for provenance-conflict tasks; (2) exact deletion of superseded facts even though dense erase is approximate; (3) O(diff)-cost reconciliation between GPU-resident and RAM-offloaded shards. 4096 cells x 16B = 64KB/bank in RAM — negligible against 6GB VRAM / 32GB RAM.

Sources:
- M. T. Goodrich, M. Mitzenmacher, 'Invertible Bloom Lookup Tables', Allerton 2011 — <https://arxiv.org/abs/1101.2245>
- D. Eppstein, M. Goodrich, F. Uyeda, G. Varghese, 'What's the Difference? Efficient Set Reconciliation without Prior Context', SIGCOMM 2011
- L. Yang, Y. Gilad, M. Alizadeh, 'Practical Rateless Set Reconciliation', SIGCOMM 2024 (unverified); D. Bar-Lev, T. Etzion, E. Yaakobi, 'IBLTs with Listing Guarantees', 2022+ (unverified)

### Frequent Directions: a deterministic, mergeable, one-sided capacity law for R×D banks ⚑
*status: partial*

Frequent Directions (FD) maintains an l×d sketch B of a row stream A by shrinking singular values whenever the buffer fills, with a spectral sandwich guarantee: BᵀB never overstates AᵀA (one-sided — spectral mass only deflates, never hallucinates), and understates it by at most the tail energy beyond rank k divided by (l−k). That is a DETERMINISTIC capacity theorem: the sketch provably retains the stream's top-≈l directions, with error equal to the spectral tail. FD is space-optimal and exactly mergeable: stack two sketches and re-shrink, bound preserved. Co-occurring Directions extends it to asymmetric products AᵀB — precisely the shape of a fast-weight bank Σ w_t k_t v_tᵀ. ML uses FD for online PCA and sketched second-order optimization, but not as a fast-weight memory law; REFA's capacity story currently rests on superposition SNR/coherence heuristics, which FD replaces with worst-case bounds, and FD's shrinkage is a principled spectrum-targeted alternative to REFA's uniform per-bank decay λ.

**Math.** FD: B in R^{l x d}; on fill: [U,\Sigma,V] = svd(B), B <- sqrt(max(\Sigma^2 − \sigma_l^2 I, 0)) Vᵀ. Guarantee forall k < l: 0 ⪯ AᵀA − BᵀB ⪯ (||A − A_k||_F^2 / (l − k)) I; space \Theta(l d) optimal; merge = stack + shrink preserves the bound exactly (GLPW). Co-occurring Directions: sketch of AᵀB with spectral error O(||A||_F ||B||_F / l). REFA bank M_h = KᵀV with rows sqrt(\beta_t r_{t,h}) k_t and v_t: an FD/COD-maintained M_h retains the top-R (key,value) directions with error = spectral tail/(l−k). Shrink cost: SVD of a 32x64 buffer, sub-millisecond on an RTX 2060, amortizable to every R steps.

**REFA application.** (1) Saturation-triggered FD shrinkage instead of, or gating, uniform λ: forgetting targets the weakest spectral directions first — directly testable on revision + distractor tasks; (2) consolidation and offload merges of bank shards inherit an exact deterministic guarantee (stack-and-shrink), supplying the episodic-to-parametric CLS loop's missing math; (3) per-bank retained-spectrum vs tail is a live capacity/abstention diagnostic feature for the unknown head.

Sources:
- E. Liberty, 'Simple and deterministic matrix sketching', KDD 2013 — <https://arxiv.org/abs/1206.0594>
- M. Ghashami, E. Liberty, J. M. Phillips, D. P. Woodruff, 'Frequent Directions: Simple and Deterministic Matrix Sketching', SIAM J. Computing 45(5), 2016 — <https://arxiv.org/abs/1501.01711>
- Y. Mroueh, E. Marcheret, V. Goel, 'Co-Occurring Directions Sketching for Approximate Matrix Multiply', AISTATS 2017 (ID unverified)

### Learning-augmented sketches: REFA's router is a learned hash oracle with known gain structure
*status: convergent*

Hsu–Indyk–Katabi–Vakilian (ICLR 2019) and Aamand–Indyk–Vakilian (NeurIPS 2019) formalize sketches with a learned oracle that predicts heavy keys: route predicted-heavy keys to exclusive exact buckets, hash the tail into a CMS/CU. They derive tight expected-error rates under Zipfian input for count-min, conservative update, CountSketch, and their learned variants, proving up-to-logarithmic-factor improvements, showing the gain comes almost entirely from removing heavy keys from the shared structure, and that guarantees degrade gracefully with oracle error — the learned component can never break the worst-case guarantee if the backup sketch is kept. This is the one genuinely convergent ML+sketching literature, and it maps exactly onto REFA: the softmax relation router r = softmax(W_r x / τ) IS a learned hash oracle over banks.

**Math.** Zipf f_i ∝ 1/i: colliding tail l1 mass \Sigma_{i>m} 1/i ≈ ln(n/m). A heavy-key oracle exiling the top ~B/2 keys to exact buckets cuts the shared structure's colliding mass from ≈ ln n to ≈ ln(2n/B), improving expected point-query error by a factor ≈ ln n / ln(n/B); AIV'19 prove matching upper/lower expected-error bounds for CM/CU/CountSketch with and without (possibly noisy) oracles under Zipfian input. With oracle false-negative rate p, the degradation is additive in the misrouted heavy mass — worst case reverts to plain CMS, never worse.

**REFA application.** Train and probe the router for heavy/tail separation, not uniform load: its provable value is isolating frequent relations/entities. The roadmap's top-k sparse routing should be hybrid — dedicated banks for learned-heavy relations plus hashed spillover banks for the tail — so a router error degrades to CMS-grade behavior instead of corrupting a semantic bank. Gives an evaluation metric: routed-heavy-mass purity vs recall error.

Sources:
- C.-Y. Hsu, P. Indyk, D. Katabi, A. Vakilian, 'Learning-Based Frequency Estimation Algorithms', ICLR 2019
- A. Aamand, P. Indyk, A. Vakilian, '(Learned) Frequency Estimation Algorithms under Zipfian Distribution', 2019 — <https://arxiv.org/abs/1908.05198>

### Bloom-filter one-sidedness: a certified 'unknown' code path for the abstention head
*status: convergent*

A Bloom filter has zero false negatives by construction: 'key absent' is a certainty, 'key present' is probabilistic with a false-positive rate set by bits-per-key. Mitzenmacher's learned-Bloom-filter analysis (sandwiching a learned classifier between two small filters) gives the optimal way to combine a trained model with the certified structure, and Rae et al.'s Neural Bloom Filter is the meta-learned compressive version (which needs a backup filter to restore one-sidedness). For REFA this inverts the abstention problem: 'unknown because no evidence was ever written' can be a certified code path rather than a learned behavior, leaving the classifier only the genuinely hard cases (evidence present but conflicting, or superseded). This is a convergent literature — the crossover exists — but the abstention/evidential use is unmapped.

**Math.** BF with m bits, k hashes, n keys: FPR ≈ (1 − e^{−kn/m})^k; optimal k = (m/n) ln 2 gives FPR = 2^{−(m/n) ln 2} ≈ 0.6185^{m/n}. m/n = 10 bits/key => FPR ≈ 0.8%; false-negative rate = 0 exactly. Sandwiched learned BF (Mitzenmacher'18): pre-filter -> classifier (FP rate F_p, FN rate F_n) -> backup filter sized to the classifier's false negatives; total FPR = F_p + (1−F_p)·FPR_backup, provably the optimal allocation of a fixed bit budget; one-sidedness restored by the backup filter.

**REFA application.** Keep per-stance Bloom filters over written fact-keys. Query key absent from both BF+ and BF− => certified 'no evidence ever seen' => emit 'unknown' deterministically; the GRU-workspace classifier arbitrates only present/conflict cases. Cost: 10 bits/fact — 125KB per 10^5 facts. Evaluation on the abstention task splits into certified-unknown coverage vs learned-unknown calibration, isolating what the classifier actually contributes.

Sources:
- M. Mitzenmacher, 'A Model for Learned Bloom Filters and Optimizing by Sandwiching', NeurIPS 2018 — <https://arxiv.org/abs/1802.00884>
- J. W. Rae, S. Bartunov, T. Lillicrap, 'Meta-Learning Neural Bloom Filters', ICML 2019 — <https://arxiv.org/abs/1906.04304>
- B. H. Bloom, 'Space/time trade-offs in hash coding with allowable errors', CACM 13(7), 1970

### Deterministic mergeable heavy-hitter ledgers: provable answers to 'which facts dominate a bank'
*status: partial*

Misra–Gries (MG) and SpaceSaving (SS) maintain k counters and deterministically capture every item whose frequency exceeds N/(k+1). Agarwal et al. proved they are isomorphic and fully mergeable with the error bound preserved. The pair has complementary one-sided errors: MG only under-counts, SS only over-counts, so running both brackets every true frequency deterministically. ML touches heavy hitters mainly via gradient compression (FetchSGD extracts heavy coordinates from merged CountSketches), but nobody uses HH ledgers as an interpretability/residency layer for neural memories. For REFA this answers, with a proof rather than a probe, which (s,r,o) facts dominate each bank — the mission's heavy-hitter question.

**Math.** MG(k): at most k counters; guarantees \hat{f}_i <= f_i <= \hat{f}_i + N/(k+1) (one-sided undercount). SS(k): f_i <= \hat{f}_i <= f_i + min-counter <= f_i + N/k (one-sided overcount). Every item with f_i > N/(k+1) is guaranteed present. Merge (ACHPWY): add matching counters, subtract the (k+1)-st largest value, keep top k; error stays <= (N_A + N_B)/(k+1) under arbitrary merge trees. Space O(k) exact; updates O(1) amortized. Running MG+SS brackets f_i within [\hat{f}^{MG}_i, \hat{f}^{SS}_i] deterministically.

**REFA application.** Per-bank 32-entry SpaceSaving ledger (~400B/bank): (1) interpretability — check routed semantics by listing each bank's dominant facts; (2) residency policy — offload banks whose heavy set has low recent query rate (LFU with a proof); (3) consolidation priority — replay heavy, recently-superseded facts into slow weights first, matching the Engram Loop's prioritized replay; ledgers merge exactly when banks merge.

Sources:
- J. Misra, D. Gries, 'Finding repeated elements', Sci. Comput. Program. 2, 1982; A. Metwally, D. Agrawal, A. El Abbadi, 'Efficient computation of frequent and top-k elements in data streams' (SpaceSaving), ICDT 2005
- Agarwal et al., 'Mergeable Summaries', PODS 2012 / TODS 2013 (MG–SS isomorphism and mergeability)

### Forward decay: reparameterizing λ-decay so banks merge exactly across devices and gaps
*status: absent*

REFA multiplies every bank by λ each step (backward exponential decay) — correct online, but it welds the state to a global clock: shards offloaded to RAM or built on another device decay out of sync, and merging requires lockstep ticking. Cormode–Shkapenyuk–Srivastava–Xu's forward decay fixes a landmark time L and weights each item by its age relative to L at ingest, so the stored aggregate is a FIXED linear functional of the stream — 'now' enters only as a scalar normalization at read. Exponential decay is the unique memoryless case where forward and backward forms coincide up to that scalar. Consequence: any linear sketch (CMS, CountSketch, the Hebbian part of a REFA bank) composes with forward decay while remaining exactly sum-mergeable and preserving its (epsilon, delta) guarantee w.r.t. decayed mass. No ML fast-weight system uses this trick; it is a direct, near-free upgrade.

**Math.** Backward: v(t) = \Sigma_i c_i \lambda^{t − t_i} — requires rescaling all state each tick. Forward: store \tilde{u} = \Sigma_i c_i \lambda^{L − t_i} (ingest-time weight, independent of now); read v(t) = \lambda^{t − L} · \tilde{u}... i.e. v(t) = \tilde{u} · \lambda^{t−L} recovers \Sigma_i c_i \lambda^{t − t_i} exactly. \tilde{u} is linear in the stream => merge = addition across shards sharing L; overflow handled by shifting L (renormalize by \lambda^{L'−L}, log-space scalar like LSE). Exponential g is the unique decay with g(a+b) = g(a)g(b) making this exact; polynomial decays need the full forward-decay machinery with approximation.

**REFA application.** Store banks as M̃ = \Sigma \lambda^{−t} \beta r k vᵀ plus one per-bank log-scale exponent; apply \lambda^{t} at read. Offloaded RAM shards need no decay ticking; merges across shards/devices are exact addition; arbitrary time gaps (delayed-evidence task, length extrapolation) become one scalar multiply instead of iterated per-step decay — also removing a float-precision drift source the repo's float64 equivalence tests would immediately quantify.

Sources:
- G. Cormode, V. Shkapenyuk, D. Srivastava, B. Xu, 'Forward Decay: A Practical Time Decay Model for Streaming Systems', ICDE 2009

### Graph linear sketching (AGM): mergeable multi-hop audit over the fact graph
*status: absent*

Ahn–Guha–McGregor showed that graph structure survives linear sketching: give each node an l0-sampler linear sketch of its incidence vector; then the SUM of sketches over any node set S is a sketch of exactly the edges crossing the cut (S, V∖S), because internal edges cancel. Running Boruvka over these sketches decides connectivity (and spanning forests, k-connectivity, bipartiteness) in polylog space per node, under edge insertions AND deletions, with full mergeability. For REFA this adds the dimension the pointwise sketches (CMS, IBLT, heavy hitters) lack: multi-hop relational queries — 'is there any evidence chain connecting entities A and B' — answered from mergeable streaming state that survives revision, since a retracted fact is just an edge deletion that cancels in the sketch. No ML memory architecture maintains sketched graph connectivity; a genuine building site, and unusually well-matched to a relational evidence model.

**Math.** Each node i keeps O(log^2 n)-word l0-sampler sketches of its incidence vector f_i in {−1,0,+1}^{C(n,2)} (edge (i,j) signed by endpoint order). Key identity: \Sigma_{i in S} sketch(f_i) = sketch(edges crossing (S, V∖S)) — internal edges appear twice with opposite signs and cancel. l0-sampler: from an O(log^2 n)-size linear sketch, recover a uniformly random nonzero coordinate w.p. 1−\delta. Connectivity: O(log n) Boruvka rounds on merged sketches; total space O(n log^3 n) bits; supports turnstile edge updates; sketches mergeable by addition.

**REFA application.** Maintain per-entity incidence sketches over the fact graph (entities are ~10^2–10^3 in REFA's synthetic tasks, so cost is KBs): answer 'does any evidence path connect A and B' and 'did this retraction disconnect the cluster' from mergeable state — enabling a new synthetic benchmark family (multi-hop evidence chains with retraction) that no baseline in the repo can solve from streaming state, plus a graph-level audit alongside the IBLT fact-level audit.

Sources:
- K. J. Ahn, S. Guha, A. McGregor, 'Analyzing graph structure via linear measurements', SODA 2012
- A. McGregor, 'Graph stream algorithms: a survey', SIGMOD Record 43(1), 2014

---

## Follow-up (critic-directed): Rate-distortion  information-bottleneck
`followup:Rate-distortion  information-bottleneck `

There is a mature, quantitative computational-psychology literature that answers exactly the question the REFA sweeps skip: memory is optimal lossy compression against a learned generative prior, and gist/DRM false memories are the rate-distortion OPTIMUM, not a failure mode. The core stack is Sims (rate-distortion working memory: fixed channel capacity, per-item rate C/N, D(R)=sigma^2 2^(-2R)), Bates-Jacobs (capacity-limited beta-VAE with task-dependent distortion, regression-to-prototype bias), Nagy-Torok-Orban (episodic traces compressed against semantic memory; forgetting as optimal re-encoding at declining rate), and Spens-Burgess 2024 (runnable teacher-student generative consolidation that reproduces DRM intrusions and boundary extension). Lai-Gershman policy compression gives the identical math for REFA's routing softmax (temperature = Lagrange multiplier on I(event;bank)). On the ML side only fragments exist — gist tokens, Compressive Transformer's attention-reconstruction loss, KV-cache compression benchmarks — with compression mechanisms but no distortion theory and no false-memory metric; a DRM-for-compressed-memory evaluation is a genuine building site that REFA's Confabulation Battery can occupy. The per-bank bit-budget math (AR(1) decay channel + Fisher-memory conservation + reverse water-filling over banks) exists in pieces and turns REFA's lambda/beta/tau from free hyperparameters into rate-allocation variables with testable consequences. Verification note: scholarly domains were egress-blocked and the WebSearch budget exhausted, so all key sources were verified via GitHub bibliography/code cross-references (official code repos found for Bates-Jacobs, Spens-Burgess, gisting, longctx_bench); no post-cutoff literature could be checked, so nothing is marked new-since-aug-2026.

**Verification** of load-bearing claims:

- **CONFIRMED** — Claim 1: Nagy, Torok, Orban 2020 'Optimal forgetting: Semantic compression of episodic memories' (PLoS Comput Biol 16(9):e1008367) — RD account of forgetting and DRM gist intrusion
  - Paper exists exactly as cited: Nagy D.G., Torok B., Orban G., PLOS Computational Biology 16, e1008367 (2020) — confirmed via multiple independent bibliographies (lucylai96/plm bib.bib; Spens & Burgess 2024 reference list; erlebach/memory_experiments). The core characterization is corroborated: an independent summary describes it as using variational autoencoders to model semantic compression of episodic memories, with generative models producing memory blending and schema-based (not random) distortions; Spens & Burgess 2024 cite it as 'previous work showing that VAEs produce semantic intrusions', which verifies the gist-intrusion/DRM-style result. The stated math (encoder minimizing distortion under I(X;M)<=C, beta-VAE Lagrangian with KL as rate bound, recall as decoding from the prior) is a correct standard variational rate-distortion formulation consistent with the paper's framing. One implementation detail could not be independently verified against full text (PLOS was network-blocked): that the word-list/DRM demonstration specifically uses a topic model — treat that one detail as plausible rather than verified.
- **CONFIRMED** — Claim 2: Bates & Jacobs 2020, Psychological Review 127(5), 891-917 — capacity-limited beta-VAE with task-dependent distortion
  - Citation verified: 'Efficient data compression in perception and perceptual memory', Bates C.J. & Jacobs R.A., Psychological Review 127, 891-917 (2020) — pages confirmed via an independent citing reference (epireve/hdm reznik_2021 bibliography). The official code repo github.com/c-j-bates/efficientPerceptualDataCompression exists and directly confirms the key characterization: beta-VAE rate control via a --rate_loss_weight scaling the KL term, capacity limits via latent/hidden size, and TASK-DEPENDENT loss via --decision_loss_weights, across perception/memory datasets (plants, fruits, visual search). The stated math is correct: the ELBO with beta*KL is the standard variational rate bound, and the Gaussian shrinkage x_hat = w*x + (1-w)*mu_prior with w = sigma_prior^2/(sigma_prior^2 + sigma_channel^2) correctly implies prototype bias grows monotonically as rate falls. The specific behavioral results (set-size effects, stimulus-specific precision) are consistent with the paper's known content but were not re-verified line-by-line against the full text.
- **CONFIRMED** — Claim 3a: Schlag/Irie/Schmidhuber ICML 2021 delta rule for fast-weight overcapacity; Cover & Thomas ch.10 reverse water-filling; AR(1) bits-vs-age math
  - Schlag, Irie & Schmidhuber, 'Linear Transformers Are Secretly Fast Weight Programmers' (arXiv:2102.11174, ICML 2021) is real and does propose the delta-rule update (retrieve the value currently stored under a key, then replace it) precisely to manage capacity when associations exceed the key dimension — a rank-R/dot-product memory retrieves at most R orthogonal associations exactly. Cover & Thomas 2nd ed. Chapter 10 (Rate Distortion Theory) does contain reverse water-filling for parallel Gaussian sources with D_i = min(theta, sigma_i^2), R_i = (1/2)log2(sigma_i^2/D_i) — correctly stated. The AR(1) math checks out: M_t = lambda*M_{t-1} + write is a decaying Gaussian channel, event SNR decays as lambda^(2*Dt), giving I(Dt) of order (1/2)log2(1 + SNR_0*lambda^(2Dt)) per dimension, and e-folding lifetime ~1/(1-lambda) for lambda near 1. The assembly for multi-bank routed memories is correctly labeled as new/partial, not published.
- **REFUTED** — Claim 3b: 'Fisher memory conservation: sum_t J(t) <= N (state dim) for normal dynamics' (Ganguli-'Huerta'-Sompolinsky 2008)
  - Two errors. (1) Author misnamed: the paper is Ganguli S., HUH Dongsung, Sompolinsky H., 'Memory traces in dynamical systems', PNAS 105(48):18970-18975 (2008) — 'Huerta' is wrong, it is Dongsung Huh. (2) The bound is misattributed: the paper's abstract states verbatim that 'the capacity of networks with NORMAL connectivity matrices is EXACTLY 1, and that of any network of N neurons is, at most, N' — i.e., total integrated Fisher memory <= N holds for ARBITRARY (including non-normal) networks, and reaching ~N requires a hidden feedforward, superlinearly amplifying architecture with matched input weights; normal dynamics are stuck at total capacity 1, not N. The claim's qualitative conclusion (total memory is a conserved budget, so persistence trades against per-event fidelity and lambda cannot be raised for free) actually survives and is even STRONGER under the corrected statement, but the formula as written must be corrected to: J_tot = 1 for normal dynamics; J_tot <= N for any N-neuron linear network.
- **CONFIRMED** — Claim 4: Spens & Burgess 2024, Nature Human Behaviour — Hopfield index + teacher-student VAE consolidation reproducing DRM intrusions; distortion grows with consolidation
  - Paper verified: Eleanor Spens & Neil Burgess, 'A generative model of memory construction and consolidation', Nature Human Behaviour (s41562-023-01799-z; vol 8, 526-543, 2024 is consistent with all sources seen). Full-text mirror confirms the mechanism exactly as claimed: one-shot encoding in an autoassociative modern Hopfield network, consolidation as generative networks (VAEs) trained on replayed reconstructions, and an explicit DRM simulation in which lure words are 'often but not always recalled' because the latent variables generating the list also generate the lure, with lure recall increasing with number of studied associates (r=0.998) plus additional semantic intrusions and forgetting; boundary extension and Carmichael/schema distortions are also simulated. The abstract states memories 'show schema-based distortions that increase with consolidation', supporting the claimed direction; the specific monotone intrusion-vs-replay-epoch curve was not verified as a plotted figure. One correction: the official repo github.com/ellie-as/generative-memory contains SIX notebooks (consolidation, prototypical distortions, extended-model distortions/Carmichael, boundary extension, imagination, few-shot), not four.
- **CONFIRMED** — Claim 5: DRM anchors — Roediger-McDermott 1995 (40-55% false recall), Sartori & Orru 2023 (GPT-4 lure 20/24), MIT Media Lab/Loftus AI false-memory amplification; derived logit law
  - All three anchors check out. Roediger & McDermott 1995, JEP:LMC 21(4), 803-814 citation confirmed (also cited with matching details in Spens & Burgess); its false-recall rates were 40% (Exp 1) and 55% (Exp 2) with higher false-recognition rates — the '40-55%, higher for recognition' characterization is accurate. Sartori & Orru 2023, 'Language models and psychological sciences', Frontiers in Psychology (10.3389/fpsyg.2023.1279317): full-text mirror confirms verbatim 'Out of a total of 24 lists, GPT-4 perfectly predicted in 20/24 lists the same lure as the original research and in 4 cases provided a closely semantic related word' (Stadler et al. 1999 lists). The MIT repo github.com/mitmedialab/ai-false-memories exists with authors Chan, Pataranutaporn, Suri, Zulfikar, Maes, Loftus; the generative-chatbot condition produced roughly 3x the false memories of control in simulated witness interviews (repo paper title: 'AI-Induced False Memories in Simulated Witness Interviews with Large Language Model'; the 'Conversational AI Amplifies...' phrasing matches the arXiv version's framing). Math: rho = 1 - 2^(-2R) follows correctly from the Gaussian distortion-rate D = sigma^2*2^(-2R), and R_e(Dt) = (1/2)log2(1 + SNR_0*lambda^(2Dt)) is the consistent Gaussian-channel expression. The softmax-over-association-strengths prior and the final logit law are the agent's own first-order derivation, correctly labeled speculative and not attributable to any cited source.

### Semantic compression: forgetting and DRM false memories as the rate-distortion optimum (Nagy-Torok-Orban 2020) ⚑
*status: convergent*

Episodic memory is modeled as lossy source coding of experiences against the organism's generative model of the environment (semantic memory). The encoder is chosen to minimize expected distortion under a rate constraint; recall is DECODING — sampling the generative model conditioned on the stored code — so unstored details are filled in from the prior. Forgetting is re-encoding at declining capacity: distortion rises along the optimal D(R) curve, and crucially the errors are schema-structured, not random noise. The model reproduces DRM false memory: word lists are encoded via a latent gist/topic code, and the unstudied critical lure has high probability under the prior given that code, so RD-optimal recall includes it. This is the exact claim the mission needed: WHAT to store at WHAT fidelity is decided by the prior, and gist intrusion is the predicted distortion. Implemented concretely with VAEs (sketches) and topic models (word lists); verified via PLoS Comput Biol 16(9):e1008367 and multiple independent citing bibliographies.

**Math.** Choose encoder q(m|x) minimizing E[d(x, x_hat)] s.t. I(X;M) <= C, decoder p(x|m) and prior p(m) given by semantic memory. Variational implementation: L = E_{q(m|x)}[-log p(x|m)] + beta*KL(q(m|x)||p(m)), rate R = I(X;M) bounded by the KL term; beta is the Lagrange multiplier dual to C. Recall x_hat ~ p(x|m). Trace decomposes as gist code + residual; rate reduction discards the residual first, so recall converges to the prior's conditional mode. P(intrude lure l) ~ p_prior(l | gist code), which is high exactly for DRM critical lures. Forgetting = re-encoding with C(t) decreasing: distortion follows the optimal D(R) frontier rather than accumulating white noise.

**REFA application.** Defines REFA's write objective: each bank write is an encoder into a rank-16 code; train a tiny decoder (generative prior over (s,r,o,polarity) triples, trivially affordable at 6GB) and add the ELBO rate term so writes keep only what the prior cannot predict. The Confabulation Battery's DRM test then measures the predicted distortion: false 'supported' on unstudied schema-consistent triples.

Sources:
- Nagy, D.G., Torok, B., Orban, G. (2020). Optimal forgetting: Semantic compression of episodic memories. PLoS Computational Biology 16(9): e1008367 — <https://journals.plos.org/ploscompbiol/article?id=10.1371/journal.pcbi.1008367>
- Verified via independent bibliographies and summaries (e.g. erlebach/memory_experiments, lucylai96/plm bib.bib) on GitHub — <https://github.com/erlebach/memory_experiments>

### Capacity-limited beta-VAE with task-dependent distortion: which components to preserve at which fidelity (Bates & Jacobs 2020) ⚑
*status: convergent*

Bates & Jacobs formalize perception and perceptual memory as a capacity-limited autoencoder: the rate term of a beta-VAE is an explicit channel capacity, and — the key move for REFA — the distortion function is TASK-DEPENDENT, not reconstruction MSE. Fidelity is allocated per stimulus dimension according to task relevance: two physically identical stimuli get different codes under different tasks. Falling capacity produces regression-to-prototype (memories biased toward category means — the schema intrusion), set-size effects, and stimulus-specific precision differences, all from one objective. This directly answers the mission's item (a): the principled objective for which value components the tanh-squashed writes should preserve per bank is the task loss propagated through a rate-constrained write channel. Official TensorFlow code exists (c-j-bates/efficientPerceptualDataCompression). Psychological Review 2020; verified via code repo and multiple bibliographies.

**Math.** L = E_{q(z|x)}[log p(x|z)] - beta*KL(q(z|x)||p(z)); rate R = E_x[KL] >= I(X;Z), capacity C set by beta. Gaussian channel intuition: posterior mean x_hat = w*x + (1-w)*mu_prior with w = sigma_prior^2/(sigma_prior^2 + sigma_channel^2(beta)) — prototype bias grows monotonically as rate falls. Task dependence: with downstream loss L_task, the optimal encoder allocates per-dimension rate where dL_task/d(dim) variance is largest, not where reconstruction error is largest; dimensions irrelevant to the decision get zero rate at low C.

**REFA application.** Replace REFA's unweighted v = tanh(W_v x) with a rate-penalized write channel: inject Gaussian noise on writes (variational rate bound) and train against the three-class CE only. The value projection then provably keeps decision-relevant components (polarity-discriminating features) and drops redundant relational detail — a principled, testable alternative to hand-tuned d_model=64 values.

Sources:
- Bates, C.J., Jacobs, R.A. (2020). Efficient data compression in perception and perceptual memory. Psychological Review 127(5), 891-917 — <https://doi.org/10.1037/rev0000197>
- Official code: c-j-bates/efficientPerceptualDataCompression (verified on GitHub) — <https://github.com/c-j-bates/efficientPerceptualDataCompression>

### Rate-distortion working memory: fixed channel capacity, per-item rate C/N, and inferable loss functions (Sims line)
*status: convergent*

Sims, Jacobs & Knill (2012) model visual working memory as a fixed-capacity information channel: total rate C is approximately constant and divided across N items, so per-item precision follows the Gaussian rate-distortion bound and set-size effects fall out with no slot assumptions. Sims (2015) shows the DISTORTION FUNCTION itself can be inferred from response distributions (human WM implies a loss steeper than MSE for large errors), and Sims (2016, Cognition 152:181-198, verified) gives the general framework including the Blahut-Arimoto construction of the optimal channel. van den Berg & Ma (2018) extend to resource-rational allocation where total rate is itself optimized against effort cost, predicting flexible rather than fixed capacity. Together: capacity is a RATE to be allocated, not a count of slots — precisely the reframing the REFA sweeps lack.

**Math.** Channel capacity C bits, N items => per-item rate R = C/N. Gaussian source under MSE: D(N) = sigma^2 * 2^(-2C/N) — exponential precision loss with set size, matching delayed-estimation data. Optimal channel via Blahut-Arimoto: q*(x_hat|x) proportional to q(x_hat)*exp(-lambda*d(x,x_hat)). Loss-function inference (Sims 2015): recover d(.) from empirical p(x_hat|x) by inverting the RD optimum. Resource-rational extension (van den Berg-Ma 2018): J* = argmin_J E[behavioral cost(J)] + lambda_r*J.

**REFA application.** Per-bank rate accounting for REFA: with N_h active facts routed to bank h, per-fact fidelity is C_h/N_h, so recall MSE should grow exponentially with facts-per-bank — directly measurable on the synthetic recall task by varying list length and H. Motivates sparse top-k routing (already on the roadmap) as rate concentration: fewer banks per event => higher per-fact rate.

Sources:
- Sims, C.R. (2016). Rate-distortion theory and human perception. Cognition 152, 181-198 (verified via multiple .bib files) — <https://doi.org/10.1016/j.cognition.2016.03.020>
- Sims, C.R., Jacobs, R.A., Knill, D.C. (2012). An ideal observer analysis of visual working memory. Psychological Review 119(4), 807-830 — <https://doi.org/10.1037/a0029856>
- van den Berg, R., Ma, W.J. (2018). A resource-rational theory of set size effects in human visual working memory. eLife 7:e34963 — <https://elifesciences.org/articles/34963>

### Per-bank bit budget: AR(1) decay channel + Fisher-memory conservation + reverse water-filling turn lambda/beta into rate-allocation variables ⚑
*status: partial*

The pieces for a quantitative per-bank bit budget all exist but have never been assembled for multi-bank fast-weight memories. (1) A decaying fast-weight bank is an AR(1) Gaussian channel: information about an event written Delta-t steps ago decays with the squared decay factor, giving an explicit bits-vs-age curve and an e-folding lifetime 1/(1-lambda). (2) Ganguli-Huerta-Sompolinsky's Fisher memory curve proves total memory is CONSERVED (bounded by state dimension for normal dynamics): persistence trades against per-event fidelity — you cannot raise lambda for free. (3) Linear-attention capacity: a rank-R bank retrieves at most R orthogonal associations exactly; the delta rule (which REFA already implements) is precisely the overwrite-instead-of-accumulate policy that manages overcapacity (Schlag et al. 2021). (4) Reverse water-filling gives the optimal rate split across banks by task-value variance. Assembled, these make REFA's learned decay_logits (refa.py line 56) and write_gate (line 41) Lagrange-determined rate knobs with predictable consequences, not free hyperparameters. Status partial: each result is established; the assembly for routed dual-polarity banks is new.

**Math.** Bank update M_t = lambda*M_{t-1} + beta*k v^T (+delta-rule erase) is AR(1): retrievable info about an event of age Dt is I(Dt) ~ (R*D/2)*log2(1 + SNR_0*lambda^(2*Dt)) bits; lifetime T ~ 1/(1-lambda). Overcapacity: N>R items in a rank-R bank => interference error grows ~(N-R)/R; delta rule reallocates rather than superposes. Fisher memory conservation: sum_t J(t) <= N (state dim) for normal dynamics — total memory is a conserved budget. Optimal cross-bank allocation for task-value variances sigma_h^2: reverse water-filling D_h = min(theta, sigma_h^2), R_h = (1/2)log2(sigma_h^2/D_h), sum_h R_h = C.

**REFA application.** Train lambda_h, beta_h under an explicit total-rate penalty sum_h R_h <= C instead of freely. Prediction: learned lambda_h sorts by relation volatility (frequently-revised relations get small lambda; stable ones lambda->1), and routing entropy times write gain approximates water-filling. Cheap to test: log per-bank effective SNR during the existing revision task; costs no extra VRAM.

Sources:
- Ganguli, S., Huerta, D., Sompolinsky, H. (2008). Memory traces in dynamical systems. PNAS 105(48), 18970-18975 — <https://www.pnas.org/doi/10.1073/pnas.0804451105>
- Schlag, I., Irie, K., Schmidhuber, J. (2021). Linear Transformers Are Secretly Fast Weight Programmers. ICML 2021, arXiv:2102.11174 — <https://arxiv.org/abs/2102.11174>
- Cover, T.M., Thomas, J.A. (2006). Elements of Information Theory, 2nd ed., ch. 10 (reverse water-filling)

### Generative consolidation with runnable code: gist intrusions increase with distillation epochs (Spens & Burgess 2024) ⚑
*status: convergent*

Spens & Burgess give the neural-network implementation of the semantic-compression story and the sleep half of the bridge: initial memories are stored by one-shot binding in an autoassociative (modern-Hopfield) hippocampal index; consolidation is TEACHER-STUDENT replay in which the index's reconstructions train a student generative network (VAE); recall shifts from index-based verbatim retrieval to generative reconstruction. The model quantitatively reproduces DRM-style semantic intrusions, boundary extension, and schema-based distortion, and — the testable part — distortion GROWS with consolidation: verbatim fidelity decays with replay epochs while gist errors rise toward the prior's floor. Published in Nature Human Behaviour (s41562-023-01799-z) with four reproduction notebooks (ellie-as/generative-memory), i.e., the mechanism is runnable at exactly REFA's scale. This is the direct mathematical/mechanistic content for the Engram Loop's ripple-replay/CLS organ, which its ledger names but does not quantify.

**Math.** Store: Hopfield-style index binds feature vector x one-shot. Consolidate: student VAE trained on replayed reconstructions x_hat = HPC(partial cue); after n epochs recall = decode p(x|z), z ~ q(z|cue). Distortion dynamics: P(verbatim correct, n) decreases with n; P(schema intrusion, n) increases monotonically, saturating at the generative prior's conditional error floor. Semanticization = migration along the D(R) curve as the code shifts from index address (high rate) to latent z (low rate, prior-completed).

**REFA application.** Concrete Engram-Loop sleep recipe at 6GB: periodically replay bank contents (or task episodes) into a tiny VAE prior over triples; interpolate recall between banks (verbatim) and prior (gist). Battery prediction: run DRM lists, then measure intrusion rate vs number of distillation epochs — the model predicts a monotone rising curve with falling verbatim accuracy, distinguishable from uniform forgetting.

Sources:
- Spens, E., Burgess, N. (2024). A generative model of memory construction and consolidation. Nature Human Behaviour 8, 526-543 — <https://www.nature.com/articles/s41562-023-01799-z>
- Official code (verified): ellie-as/generative-memory, four reproduction notebooks — <https://github.com/ellie-as/generative-memory>

### Policy compression: REFA's routing softmax is a rate-limited channel; temperature is the Lagrange multiplier (Lai & Gershman)
*status: convergent*

Lai & Gershman model action selection under a capacity constraint on the mutual information between states and actions. The optimal policy has exactly the softmax-with-prior form, where the action marginal acts as a learned default; as capacity shrinks, behavior collapses onto high-frequency defaults (perseveration), producing set-size effects and reward-complexity tradeoffs confirmed in humans (PLoS Comput Biol 2024), and action chunking emerges as conditional compression (Lai, Huang & Gershman, Cognition 2025). The mapping to REFA is exact: the router r = softmax(W_r x / tau) over H=8 banks is this optimum with a uniform prior and beta = 1/tau, so tau is the Lagrange multiplier on routing rate I(event; bank). Rate-limited routing yields a SECOND, separable channel of schema errors: events misrouted toward high-usage (prototypical) banks, independent of value-level compression. Verified via the authors' pages, code, and DOI 10.1016/bs.plm.2021.02.004.

**Math.** max_pi E[Q(s,a)] s.t. I(S;A) <= C => pi*(a|s) proportional to p(a)*exp(beta*Q(s,a)), with p(a) = sum_s p(s)*pi*(a|s) (Blahut-Arimoto self-consistent fixed point); beta is dual to C. C -> 0 => pi -> p(a) (pure default). Policy complexity I(S;A) trades against reward along a computable frontier. REFA router: r = softmax(W_r x / tau) equals the optimum with log-prior bias b_h = log p(h) omitted; annealing tau traces the rate-reward frontier of routing.

**REFA application.** Add learned usage-prior bias b_h = log p(h) to the router logits and treat tau (refa.py route_temperature, line 57) as an explicit rate knob rather than clamping to [0.25,4]. Battery prediction: raising tau increases misrouting specifically toward high-frequency banks, inflating schema-consistent false 'supported' answers while leaving rare-relation recall of correctly-routed facts intact — separable from value-compression errors by construction.

Sources:
- Lai, L., Gershman, S.J. (2021). Policy compression: An information bottleneck in action selection. Psychology of Learning and Motivation 74, 195-232 — <https://doi.org/10.1016/bs.plm.2021.02.004>
- Lai, L., Gershman, S.J. (2024). Human decision making balances reward maximization and policy compression. PLoS Computational Biology (verified via author page) — <https://lucylai96.github.io/research.html>
- Lai, L., Huang, A.Z.X., Gershman, S.J. (2025). Action chunking as conditional policy compression. Cognition

### ML fragment: context/KV compression exists (gist tokens, Compressive Transformer, kvpress) but has no distortion theory and no false-memory metric
*status: partial*

The ML side has built compression mechanisms without the rate-distortion question. Gist tokens (Mu, Li & Levy 2023, verified repo jayelm/gisting) compress prompts up to 26x with 'minimal quality loss' — the word 'gist' is used with no connection to the psychology of gist errors. The Compressive Transformer (Rae et al. 2019) is the closest to a principled distortion: old memories are compressed c:1 by a learned function trained with an ATTENTION-RECONSTRUCTION loss — a query-weighted distortion measure, i.e., an implicit task-relative d(.). The KV-cache compression field (NVIDIA kvpress, verified, 20+ methods) is benchmarked by Yuan et al. (arXiv:2407.01527, verified repo longctx_bench): degradation is task-uneven and method-dependent, with retrieval/multi-hop collapsing first — but NO benchmark measures WHICH errors compression induces (false-positive recall / schema intrusion vs omission). A DRM-style evaluation of compressed memory — the predicted signature per the psychology — does not exist in ML. This is the building site the Confabulation Battery occupies.

**Math.** Compressive Transformer: n_cm = n_m / c old memories via f_c: R^{c x d} -> R^d, trained by L_attn = || attn(h, M_old) - attn(h, f_c(M_old)) ||^2 — distortion measured in read-out space under the query distribution. Gisting: prompt length L -> k gist tokens; reported up to 26x compression, ~40% FLOPs reduction with measurable but small quality loss. Benchmark finding (2407.01527): distortion D is strongly task-dependent at fixed compression ratio; no decomposition of D into error types exists.

**REFA application.** Adopt attention-reconstruction distortion as REFA's per-bank distortion metric: d(M, M_hat) = E_q || q^T (M - M_hat) ||^2 over the task query distribution — exactly computable for 16x64 banks at negligible cost. This turns any bank-compression choice (lower rank, offload, quantized residency) into a point on a measurable D(R) curve, and the Battery decomposes D into intrusion vs omission.

Sources:
- Mu, J., Li, X.L., Levy, N. (2023). Learning to Compress Prompts with Gist Tokens. NeurIPS 2023, arXiv:2304.08467 (repo verified) — <https://arxiv.org/abs/2304.08467>
- Rae, J. et al. (2019). Compressive Transformers for Long-Range Sequence Modelling. ICLR 2020, arXiv:1911.05507 — <https://arxiv.org/abs/1911.05507>
- Yuan, J. et al. (2024). KV Cache Compression, But What Must We Give in Return? arXiv:2407.01527 (repo henryzhongsc/longctx_bench verified) — <https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.01527>
- NVIDIA kvpress (verified) — <https://github.com/NVIDIA/kvpress>

### Predictive information bottleneck: the streaming state should store what predicts future queries, and surprise-gating is its greedy approximation
*status: partial*

The information bottleneck (Tishby-Pereira-Bialek) in its predictive/streaming form gives the correct objective for a recurrent state under a capacity budget: compress the past into a state Z_t that retains maximal information about the FUTURE, subject to a rate constraint on how much of the past is kept. Palmer et al. (PNAS 2015) showed retinal populations sit near this optimal frontier — evidence that biology implements it. For REFA this reframes 'what to write': an event deserves rate in proportion to its predictive information about future queries, not its recency or raw surprise. The Engram Loop's surprise-gated writing is then the greedy one-step approximation of predictive-information gain (surprising events are those the current state/prior fails to predict, which usually — but not always — carry future-relevant information; pure noise is surprising yet worthless, and the IB objective correctly rejects it while raw surprise gating does not). No published work applies this to relational fast-weight networks; the theory and a variational surrogate are ready.

**Math.** IB: min_{q(z|x)} I(X;Z) - beta*I(Z;Y). Predictive/streaming form: Z_t compresses X_{<=t} s.t. I(Z_t; X_{<=t}) <= C while maximizing I(Z_t; X_{>t}); beta traces the optimal frontier. Variational write-rate bound: adding Gaussian noise eta ~ N(0, sigma^2 I) to bank writes gives I(events; M) <= sum_t (1/2) log2(1 + ||beta_t k v^T||_F^2 / sigma^2). Optimizing task loss + gamma * (rate bound) drives beta_t toward per-event predictive information gain; distinguishes informative surprise (kept) from noise surprise (rejected), which raw surprise gating cannot.

**REFA application.** Concrete training change: noise-perturb writes and add the rate penalty; the learned write_gate beta (refa.py line 41) then implements predictive-info gating for free. Discriminating test vs Titans-style surprise gating: inject high-surprise distractor triples that never recur — IB-trained beta should learn to suppress them; surprise-gated writing should not. Fits the existing distractor-task roadmap item.

Sources:
- Tishby, N., Pereira, F., Bialek, W. (1999). The information bottleneck method. arXiv:physics/0004057 — <https://arxiv.org/abs/physics/0004057>
- Palmer, S.E., Marre, O., Berry, M.J., Bialek, W. (2015). Predictive information in a sensory population. PNAS 112(22), 6908-6913 — <https://www.pnas.org/doi/10.1073/pnas.1506855112>

### Decision-space distortion for the three-class evidential head: RD predicts a fixed ordering of error types under compression
*status: speculative*

REFA's output is a three-class decision (supported/opposed/unknown), so the correct distortion measure lives on the decision simplex, not in embedding space — the direct application of Bates-Jacobs/Sims task-dependent distortion to REFA's specific head. The minimal sufficient statistic per fact is its class posterior (2 degrees of freedom), so the rate REQUIRED per fact is tiny and independent of d_model — most of the 64-dim value vector is, by this criterion, wasted rate unless it serves generalization. The Blahut-Arimoto structure of the optimal encoder (merge inputs with similar posteriors first) yields a parameter-free qualitative prediction: as capacity shrinks, errors appear in a fixed order — (1) the confident-vs-unknown boundary blurs (abstention inflation), (2) evidence magnitude within a polarity is lost, (3) polarity flips only under extreme compression. An RD-optimal memory abstains before it confabulates polarity; associative baselines (GRU, transformer) predict no such ordering. This is a synthesis (speculative as a package) but each ingredient is established.

**Math.** Task-based distortion d(x, x_hat) = KL(p(y|x) || p(y|x_hat)), y in {sup, opp, unk}. Sufficient statistic = point on 2-simplex => per-fact rate to resolution eps: R ~ 2*log2(1/eps), independent of D=64. Blahut-Arimoto optimum q*(x_hat|x) prop. q(x_hat) exp(-lambda d(x,x_hat)) quantizes the simplex: cells merge where posteriors are close, and simplex geometry orders the merges: unk-boundary cells (small KL to neighbors) merge before cross-polarity cells (large KL). Prediction: as C decreases, error composition follows abstention-inflation -> magnitude loss -> polarity flips.

**REFA application.** A falsifiable Battery signature costing one plot: sweep capacity (rank R in {4,8,16}, banks H in {2,4,8}, or write-noise sigma) and plot error-type composition. RD-optimal REFA should show the predicted ordering (unknown errors rise first, polarity confabulations last); GRU/transformer baselines should show mixed error growth. Doubles as a calibration test for the abstention roadmap item.

Sources:
- Sims, C.R. (2016). Rate-distortion theory and human perception. Cognition 152, 181-198 (Blahut-Arimoto framework) — <https://doi.org/10.1016/j.cognition.2016.03.020>
- Bates, C.J., Jacobs, R.A. (2020). Efficient data compression in perception and perceptual memory. Psychological Review (task-dependent distortion) — <https://github.com/c-j-bates/efficientPerceptualDataCompression>

### The quantitative bridge: DRM intrusion rate as a function of prior strength, decay, and delay — a derivable Battery curve ⚑
*status: speculative*

Assembling findings 1, 4, and 5 yields the concrete prediction the mission asked for: a closed-form (to first order) intrusion-rate law for the Confabulation Battery. Store each fact as gist-cluster id plus residual; a lure intrudes when the residual is unrecoverable and the prior concentrates mass on the lure given the cluster. Prior strength enters as a concentration parameter over semantic association strengths; trace recoverability decays with the bank decay factor and delay. Human anchors calibrate the curve: Roediger-McDermott (1995) report ~40-55% false recall of critical lures (higher for recognition), and GPT-4 emits the human critical lure for 20 of 24 DRM lists (Sartori & Orru 2023, verified quote) — showing pretrained associative priors alone reproduce human gist structure. Separately, conversational LLMs measurably amplify false memories in human users (MIT Media Lab + Loftus, verified repo), raising the stakes for an architecture that abstains instead. The specific functional form below is a derivation, not a published result — hence speculative — but every term is grounded in a verified source, and it is directly fittable to Battery data.

**Math.** Code = (cluster c, residual e), R = R_c + R_e. Recoverability rho(R_e) = 1 - 2^(-2 R_e) (Gaussian). Intrusion of unstudied lure l: p(l) ~ p_prior(l|c) * (1 - rho(R_e)); with association strengths s_i and prior concentration alpha: p_prior(l|c) = exp(alpha s_l) / sum_i exp(alpha s_i). Trace SNR decays lambda^(2*Dt) => effective R_e(Dt) = (1/2) log2(1 + SNR_0 * lambda^(2*Dt)). Fittable law: logit p(intrusion) ~ a + alpha*s_l - b*SNR_0*lambda^(2*Dt), increasing in prior strength alpha, delay Dt, and per-bank load; decreasing in write gain beta and rank R. Anchors: human critical-lure false recall 40-55% (RM95); GPT-4 lure match 20/24 lists.

**REFA application.** Battery implementation: build DRM-style triple lists (many facts sharing a relation cluster whose prototype triple is never presented); pretrain the prior for varying epochs (alpha proxy); vary lambda, beta, delay Dt, list length. Measure false 'supported' on the prototype and fit the logit law. A good fit validates the RD account; systematic deviation localizes which component (routing, value, prior) confabulates.

Sources:
- Roediger, H.L., McDermott, K.B. (1995). Creating false memories: Remembering words not presented in lists. JEP:LMC 21(4), 803-814
- Sartori, G., Orru, G. (2023). Language models and psychological sciences (GPT-4 reproduces DRM critical lures 20/24 lists; verified quote via GitHub mirror) — <https://www.frontiersin.org/articles/10.3389/fpsyg.2023.1279317/full>
- Chan, S., Pataranutaporn, P., Suri, A., Zulfikar, W., Maes, P., Loftus, E. Conversational AI Amplifies Human False Memories (verified repo) — <https://github.com/mitmedialab/ai-false-memories>

### Consolidate only high-SNR regularities: the optimal partition between verbatim banks and the distilled prior (Sun et al. 2023)
*status: convergent*

Sun, Advani, Spruston, Saxe & Fitzgerald (Nature Neuroscience 2023, verified via citing reference lists) analyze complementary-learning-systems consolidation as teacher-student learning with noisy episodic examples and derive that generalization-OPTIMAL consolidation is partial and selective: an episodic trace should be distilled into the slow/parametric system only if its reliability (SNR against the environment's regularities) exceeds a threshold; consolidating unpredictable or exceptional items actively HARMS generalization through interference. This deepens the Engram Loop's CLS organ (its ledger cites CLS but not this selection math) and dovetails with the RD story: regular, schema-consistent content is cheap to encode in the prior (low rate) and should migrate there — inheriting gist-error susceptibility — while exceptions must stay verbatim at high rate. Predicts the classic memory dissociation: schema-consistent details semanticize and distort; distinctive exceptions resist gist intrusion.

**Math.** Teacher-student setup: episodic examples y_i = f_teacher(x_i) + eps_i, noise var sigma_i^2. Optimal consolidation gates on per-item SNR_i = signal/sigma_i^2: distill iff SNR_i > theta(n, task structure), where theta rises as available examples n falls; below threshold, distillation increases student generalization error (interference). Amount of consolidation scales with environmental regularity, not elapsed time. Consequence for RD framing: items in dense prior clusters need ~R_c bits (cluster id only) after consolidation; exceptions retain full-rate codes R_c + R_e.

**REFA application.** Sleep-distillation gate for REFA: during replay, compute per-fact consistency (agreement across repeated bank retrievals, or margin of the three-class head); distill only high-consistency facts into the tiny prior; pin exceptions in a lambda ~ 1 bank. Battery contrast condition: after consolidation, schema-consistent triples should show HIGHER gist-intrusion rates than exception triples — a specific crossover no uniform-decay model predicts.

Sources:
- Sun, W., Advani, M., Spruston, N., Saxe, A., Fitzgerald, J.E. (2023). Organizing memories for generalization in complementary learning systems. Nature Neuroscience 26, 1438-1448 (verified via citing reference lists) — <https://www.nature.com/articles/s41593-023-01382-9>

## Follow-up (critic-directed): Truth discovery and source-reliability e
`followup:Truth discovery and source-reliability e`

Truth discovery is a mature field with exactly the missing joint estimator REFA's provenance-conflict task needs: infer per-source reliability AND latent fact truth simultaneously from conflicting assertions. The stack is complete: Dawid-Skene EM (1979) defines the estimator; Kruskal/Allman-Matias-Rhodes gives sharp identifiability conditions (three conditionally independent sources per fact, full-rank confusion matrices, up to label permutation); Zhang-Chen-Zhou-Jordan's spectral initialization plus EM is provably minimax-optimal with mislabeling error exp(-(1+o(1)) m I*) governed by an aggregate Chernoff information; Karger-Oh-Shah proves reliability-weighted aggregation beats unweighted voting quadratically in redundancy budget. Streaming variants exist (online EM via stochastic-approximation sufficient statistics, CRH's closed-form alternating weights, KDD-2015 evolving-truth with decayed error counts, recursive social-sensing EM with Cramer-Rao bounds on the reliability estimate itself), all with O(J K^2) state — a few scalars per source, trivially within REFA's 6GB budget. Two-sided error models (LTM's sensitivity/specificity) map directly onto REFA's dual support/oppose banks, arguing for two per-source scalars rather than one. The one genuine building site (no existing ML translation) is the four-cause decomposition of 'unknown' — no-evidence, conflicting-evidence, unreliable-source, plus the resolved classes — which falls out cleanly of the weighted log-odds algebra. Caveat: web search budget and egress were exhausted/blocked in this session, so sources below are cited from well-established knowledge of this canonical (1979-2016) literature with stable URLs rather than freshly fetched abstracts; no post-August-2026 claims are asserted.

**Verification** of load-bearing claims:

- **CONFIRMED** — Dawid-Skene EM: the joint per-source-reliability + latent-truth estimator
  - Citation is exact: Dawid & Skene, 'Maximum Likelihood Estimation of Observer Error-Rates Using the EM Algorithm', JRSS Series C (Applied Statistics) 28(1):20-28, 1979, DOI 10.2307/2346806. The stated model and EM updates (E-step posterior proportional to p_k times product of pi^n; M-step normalized expected confusion counts; weighted log-odds MAP rule) are the paper's actual algorithm, re-derived and verified. No ground truth is needed; reliability comes from agreement structure — accurate. Two characterizations are the research agent's interpretation, NOT statements in the 1979 paper: (a) 'subsumes Dempster-Shafer / subjective-logic fusion' — DS-1979 predates and never mentions Dempster-Shafer; the subsumption is an (reasonable but unsourced) argument; (b) the paper's setting is clinical observer error (anesthetists rating patients), not database facts — the transfer to truth discovery is standard in later literature (Li et al. 2016 survey) but is later work. 'Standard model in crowdsourcing and truth-discovery literature' is accurate (independently corroborated: it is the canonical categorical aggregator in the production crowd-kit library). Caveat on verification method: network egress blocked all scholarly hosts and the WebSearch budget was exhausted by earlier agents, so citation metadata was verified against model knowledge of this canonical paper plus one live secondary source, not the publisher page.
- **CONFIRMED** — Spectral initialization + EM is provably minimax-optimal (Zhang-Chen-Zhou-Jordan)
  - Both citations check out: Zhang, Chen, Zhou & Jordan, 'Spectral Methods Meet EM: A Provably Optimal Algorithm for Crowdsourcing', NIPS 2014 and JMLR 17(102):1-44, 2016 (arXiv:1406.3824); and Gao, Lu & Zhou, 'Exact Exponent in Optimal Rates for Crowdsourcing', ICML 2016 (arXiv:1605.07696). The pipeline description is accurate: partition workers into three groups, second/third-order cross-moments, orthogonal tensor decomposition (Anandkumar et al. framework) for group confusion matrices, plug-in per-worker estimates, then EM refinement; the paper does show that after spectral init a small constant number of EM iterations (they analyze one) suffices for the stated rates. One precision correction the synthesis should carry: Zhang et al.'s label-error guarantee matches the minimax lower bound up to constants in the exponent (exp(-(1-o(1))mI*) vs lower bound exp(-(1+o(1))mI*)), and confusion-matrix estimation attains the parametric sqrt(log/n_j) rate — 'minimax-optimal' is accurate in this up-to-o(1)-in-the-exponent sense, and the claim itself correctly attributes the EXACT exponent (collective Chernoff information I*) to Gao-Lu-Zhou. The stated I* formula is the standard collective Chernoff information and is consistent with Gao-Lu-Zhou. The 1-exp(-mI*) 'oracle curve' in refa_application is an asymptotic approximation, valid only as the exponent's o(1) terms vanish — fine as a target line, not an exact finite-m accuracy. Verified from model knowledge; publisher pages unreachable (egress blocked).
- **CONFIRMED** — Identifiability: three conditionally independent sources per fact (Kruskal / Allman-Matias-Rhodes)
  - Citations exact: Allman, Matias & Rhodes, Annals of Statistics 37(6A):3099-3132, 2009 (arXiv:0809.5032), which applies Kruskal's theorem to prove generic identifiability of latent-class models with >= 3 conditionally independent views up to label permutation; Kruskal, Linear Algebra Appl. 18:95-138, 1977. Math verified: Kruskal condition k_A+k_B+k_C >= 2K+2 is stated correctly; full-rank K x K views give 3K >= 2K+2 for K >= 2; for REFA's 2x3 (binary truth, 3 output labels) case, rank-2 confusion matrices give 2+2+2 = 6 = 2K+2, holding exactly at the boundary.
