Two-view non-identifiability (A diag(p) B^T factorization non-unique) is correct. One softening the synthesis should apply: the claim's 'iff at least 3 sources' overstates — AMR gives SUFFICIENCY of 3 generic views and 2 views are insufficient WITHOUT extra assumptions, but 2-view models become identifiable under added constraints (e.g., symmetric/one-coin error models or known priors), so the correct design statement is '3 independent sources suffice generically; 2 provably do not without additional structural assumptions'. The permutation-breaking via better-than-chance/diagonal-dominance assumption is the standard resolution — correct. Verified from model knowledge; publisher pages unreachable (egress blocked).
- **CONFIRMED** — CRH: closed-form alternating source weights (streaming- and SGD-friendly)
  - Citations exact: Li, Li, Gao, Zhao, Fan, Han, 'Resolving Conflicts in Heterogeneous Data by Truth Discovery and Source Reliability Estimation', SIGMOD 2014 (DOI 10.1145/2588555.2610509); Li et al., 'A Survey on Truth Discovery', SIGKDD Explorations 17(2), 2016 (arXiv:1505.02463). The math was independently re-derived and is correct: minimizing sum_s w_s C_s subject to sum_s exp(-w_s) = 1 via Lagrange multipliers gives exp(-w_s) = C_s/lambda, lambda = sum C_{s'}, hence w_s = log(sum_{s'} C_{s'} / C_s) — exactly the CRH closed-form weight. Truth step as weighted majority (0/1 loss), weighted median (L1), weighted mean (L2), and monotone non-increase of the objective under block-coordinate descent all match the paper. The streaming EMA form (C_s <- rho*C_s + d) is a natural extension the agent proposes, not something published in CRH itself — the SIGMOD 2014 paper is batch; incremental variants appear in follow-up work. That distinction should be kept in the synthesis. Verified from model knowledge; ACM/arXiv unreachable (egress blocked).
- **CONFIRMED** — Streaming Dawid-Skene via online EM (stochastic-approximation sufficient statistics)
  - Citations exact: Cappé & Moulines, 'On-line expectation-maximization algorithm for latent data models', JRSS Series B 71(3):593-613, 2009 (arXiv:0712.4273); Neal & Hinton, 'A view of the EM algorithm that justifies incremental, sparse, and other variants', in Learning in Graphical Models (Jordan ed.), 1998. The characterization is accurate: Cappé-Moulines replace the E-step with a Robbins-Monro stochastic-approximation update of expected complete-data sufficient statistics for exponential-family models, with a.s. convergence to stationary points of the limiting KL criterion for step sizes gamma_t = t^{-alpha}, alpha in (1/2, 1] (Robbins-Monro conditions verified: sum gamma = inf, sum gamma^2 < inf), and Polyak-Ruppert averaging attains the batch-MLE asymptotic variance at rate t^{-1/2}. Dawid-Skene's complete-data likelihood is exponential-family (categorical counts), so it falls inside the framework; the claim itself correctly flags status 'partial' because Cappé-Moulines never treat DS specifically and the DS-specific composition (state O(J K^2), per-event O(K^2 m_i)) is the agent's construction — arithmetic of that state/update cost checks out. This honest partial-status framing should be preserved verbatim. Verified from model knowledge; Wiley/arXiv unreachable (egress blocked).

### Dawid-Skene EM: the joint per-source-reliability + latent-truth estimator ⚑
*status: convergent*

Dawid & Skene (1979) solve exactly the joint problem REFA's fusion mechanisms are missing: given J sources issuing conflicting categorical assertions about I facts, estimate simultaneously (a) each source's full K-by-K confusion matrix pi^(j)_{kl} = P(source j asserts l | truth k) and (b) the posterior truth of every fact, by maximum likelihood via EM. No ground truth is needed; reliability is inferred purely from inter-source agreement structure. This subsumes Dempster-Shafer / subjective-logic fusion (which assume reliabilities known or equal): the DS confusion matrix IS the learned discount operator. It is the standard model in both the crowdsourcing-theory literature and the database truth-discovery literature, with 45+ years of extensions (Bayesian priors, hierarchical pooling, spammer submodels). Per-source state is K^2 numbers; for REFA's binary claims with 3-way output (support/oppose/abstain-from-claiming) a 2x3 matrix, or 2 scalars in the symmetric case. Cost: each EM sweep is O(#assertions x K).

**Math.** Truth T_i ~ Cat(p), assertions conditionally independent given truth. Likelihood L = prod_i sum_k p_k prod_j prod_l (pi^(j)_{kl})^{n_{ijl}}, n_{ijl} = #times j assigned label l to fact i. E-step: hat{T}_{ik} propto p_k prod_{j,l} (pi^(j)_{kl})^{n_{ijl}}. M-step: hat{pi}^(j)_{kl} = sum_i T_{ik} n_{ijl} / sum_i T_{ik} sum_{l'} n_{ijl'}; hat{p}_k = (1/I) sum_i T_{ik}. Monotone ascent of L (standard EM); non-convex, so init matters (see spectral finding). MAP fact decision with known pi is weighted log-odds: Lambda_i = log(p_1/p_0) + sum_j log[pi^(j)_{1,l_ij}/pi^(j)_{0,l_ij}].

**REFA application.** Attach a source id to each event tuple and a tiny table (num_sources x 2 or 6 params) of confusion stats, learned by the DS updates as a side estimator (not backprop). Derived reliability rho_s = log-odds of correctness modulates write gain: beta_eff = sigmoid(write_gate(x)) * sigmoid(rho_s) at refa.py line 101-103, and scales recalled_support/oppose before fusion at read. Turns the provenance-conflict task into an estimator with known convergence properties instead of a heuristic.

Sources:
- Dawid & Skene, 'Maximum Likelihood Estimation of Observer Error-Rates Using the EM Algorithm', J. Royal Stat. Soc. C 28(1):20-28, 1979 — <https://doi.org/10.2307/2346806>

### Spectral initialization + EM is provably minimax-optimal (Zhang-Chen-Zhou-Jordan) ⚑
*status: convergent*

Zhang, Chen, Zhou & Jordan (NeurIPS 2014; JMLR 2016) prove that Dawid-Skene EM, initialized by a method-of-moments spectral step, achieves minimax-optimal error for both label recovery and confusion-matrix estimation. The spectral step partitions sources into three groups, forms second/third-order cross-moments of group-aggregated label vectors, and recovers group confusion matrices by orthogonal tensor decomposition (Anandkumar et al.), then plugs in per-source estimates. This lands EM in a basin where one or two iterations suffice — resolving the classic objection that DS-EM is non-convex and init-sensitive. Companion lower bounds (Gao-Zhou; exact exponent in Gao-Lu-Zhou ICML 2016) show the mislabeling exponent is governed by an aggregate Chernoff information between confusion-matrix rows, and the spectral+EM pipeline attains it. Practical import for REFA: never bootstrap reliabilities from the model's own predictions cold (echo-chamber failure mode where a confident wrong consensus reinforces itself); moment-match on a warmup buffer first.

**Math.** Conditions: group-average confusion matrices C_g nonsingular (sigma_K(C_g) >= sigma_L > 0), p_min > 0, m sources per item. Guarantees: ||hat{pi}^(j) - pi^(j)||_inf <= O(sqrt(log(...)/n_j)) (parametric rate, n_j = #items source j touched); mislabeling P(hat{T}_i != T_i) <= exp(-(1-o(1)) m I*), matching the minimax lower bound exp(-(1+o(1)) m I*) with collective Chernoff information I* = min_{g!=h} max_{t in [0,1]} -(1/m) sum_j log sum_l (pi^(j)_{gl})^{1-t} (pi^(j)_{hl})^t. Tensor step: M_3 = sum_k p_k mu_k^{(1)} (x) mu_k^{(2)} (x) mu_k^{(3)}, whitened power iteration, O(K^3) per iterate.

**REFA application.** Gives REFA an analytic oracle curve: for a synthetic provenance-conflict task with m sources per fact and planted confusion matrices, optimal accuracy is 1 - exp(-m I*), so I* is a principled difficulty dial for the task generator and the target line on every plot. Protocol: estimate rho_s by moment-matching over the first N warmup events of a stream, then run streaming EM refinement; never trust self-labeled reliabilities from random init.

Sources:
- Zhang, Chen, Zhou, Jordan, 'Spectral Methods meet EM: A Provably Optimal Algorithm for Crowdsourcing', NeurIPS 2014 / JMLR 17(102), 2016 — <https://arxiv.org/abs/1406.3824>
- Gao, Lu, Zhou, 'Exact Exponent in Optimal Rates for Crowdsourcing', ICML 2016 — <https://arxiv.org/abs/1605.07696>

### Identifiability: three conditionally independent sources per fact (Kruskal / Allman-Matias-Rhodes) ⚑
*status: convergent*

The DS model is a finite mixture of product distributions, and its identifiability is settled: Allman, Matias & Rhodes (Ann. Statist. 2009), via Kruskal's uniqueness theorem for 3-way tensor decompositions, show latent-class models with >= 3 conditionally independent observed views are generically identifiable up to permutation of the latent labels. Translated: per-source confusion matrices and the truth prior are recoverable from co-assertion statistics alone iff each contested fact is asserted by at least 3 (conditionally independent, full-rank) sources. Two sources are provably NOT enough — the two-view mixture is non-identifiable, which is why pairwise Dempster-Shafer-style conflict between two sources can never be resolved into 'which source is unreliable' without extra assumptions. The residual global label permutation (all sources systematically lying) is broken by a better-than-chance assumption (diagonally dominant confusion matrices / mean accuracy > 1/K). Parameter sharing across facts (one confusion matrix per source, many facts) is what makes the per-source estimates statistically efficient, but does not remove the 3-view requirement for generic identifiability.

**Math.** Kruskal: T = sum_{k=1}^K a_k (x) b_k (x) c_k has a unique rank-K decomposition (up to permutation/scaling) if Kruskal ranks satisfy k_A + k_B + k_C >= 2K + 2. AMR apply this with a_k, b_k, c_k = conditional label distributions of three source groups given truth k: full-rank confusion matrices give k = K each, 3K >= 2K+2 holds for K >= 2. Identified: {p_k, pi^(j)} up to a permutation of {1..K}; broken by constraint pi^(j)_{kk} > pi^(j)_{kl} (l != k) on average. Two views: only the K x K joint matrix, factorization A diag(p) B^T non-unique.

**REFA application.** Hard design constraint for the provenance-conflict task generator: every contested (s,r,o) fact must receive assertions from >= 3 distinct source ids, and sources must not copy each other (independence given truth). With only 2 sources the correct model output is 'unknown' — which gives a free, theory-grounded negative control: REFA should abstain on 2-source conflicts and resolve 3+-source conflicts.

Sources:
- Allman, Matias, Rhodes, 'Identifiability of parameters in latent structure models with many observed variables', Annals of Statistics 37(6A):3099-3132, 2009 — <https://arxiv.org/abs/0809.5032>
- Kruskal, 'Three-way arrays: rank and uniqueness of trilinear decompositions', Linear Algebra Appl. 18:95-138, 1977 — <https://doi.org/10.1016/0024-3795(77)90069-6>

### Reliability-weighted aggregation beats unweighted voting quadratically (Karger-Oh-Shah)
*status: convergent*

Karger, Oh & Shah (NeurIPS 2011; Operations Research 2014) analyze the one-coin model (source j correct w.p. p_j) and prove a sharp separation between weighted and unweighted evidence combination. Define collective quality q = E[(2p_j - 1)^2]. Their iterative message-passing algorithm (equivalently, the leading singular vector of the fact-by-source assertion matrix) estimates reliabilities and achieves per-fact error decaying exponentially in l*q (l = assertions per fact), matching an oracle that knows all p_j; majority voting's exponent scales only as l*q^2. Hence to reach error eps, weighted aggregation needs Theta((1/q) log(1/eps)) redundancy vs Theta((1/q^2) log(1/eps)) for unweighted — a quadratic budget gap that grows as sources get noisier. The oracle MAP rule is weighted majority with weight log(p_j/(1-p_j)) per source: the optimal read-side evidence weight is exactly the log-odds of source reliability, not the reliability itself and not a Dempster discount.

**Math.** One-coin: P(assert correct) = p_j, W_j := 2p_j - 1, q = E[W_j^2]. KOS iterative algorithm on (l,r)-regular random bipartite graph: P(error) <= e^{-C l q} for universal C once l q = Omega(1); budget per fact Theta(q^{-1} log(1/eps)), order-optimal. Majority vote: P(error) ~ exp(-l q^2 / 2) (Chernoff on sum of +-W_j), needing Theta(q^{-2} log(1/eps)). Oracle MAP: sign(sum_j y_j log(p_j/(1-p_j))). Spectral view: E[A] = (truth vector)(reliability vector)^T is rank-1; power iteration recovers both factors.

**REFA application.** Prescribes the functional form of REFA's read-side fusion: multiply each recalled trace's contribution by log-odds(rho_s), i.e., feed reliability-scaled z+ and z- into the classifier rather than raw ones. Also an evaluation lever: parameterize task streams by q (spammer-hammer mix) and verify REFA's accuracy tracks the e^{-lq} weighted curve, not the e^{-lq^2} majority-vote curve — direct evidence the reliability scalar is doing real work.

Sources:
- Karger, Oh, Shah, 'Iterative Learning for Reliable Crowdsourcing Systems', NeurIPS 2011; 'Budget-Optimal Task Allocation...', Operations Research 62(1), 2014 — <https://arxiv.org/abs/1110.3564>

### CRH: closed-form alternating source weights (streaming- and SGD-friendly) ⚑
*status: convergent*

The database community's CRH framework (Li et al., SIGMOD 2014; canonical survey Li et al., SIGKDD Explorations 2016) recasts truth discovery as a joint optimization over truths and source weights with a closed-form weight update: a source's weight is the negative log of its normalized total loss against current truth estimates. Block-coordinate descent alternates (i) truth update = reliability-weighted aggregation (weighted vote/mean/median, handling heterogeneous categorical + continuous claims) and (ii) the closed-form weight update; the objective is monotonically non-increasing. This is the lightest-weight member of the family: no confusion matrices, one scalar per source, each update O(1) per claim given a running per-source loss accumulator — which makes it the natural first implementation and the natural differentiable relaxation (the log-normalized-loss weight is smooth in the losses, so it can sit inside a backprop graph or be maintained as a detached EMA).

**Math.** min_{X*, W} sum_s w_s sum_i d(x_i^s, x_i^*) s.t. sum_s exp(-w_s) = 1. Lagrangian solution: w_s = log( sum_{s'} C_{s'} / C_s ), C_s = sum_i d(x_i^s, x_i^*) (source's cumulative distance to current truths). Truth step: x_i^* = argmin_x sum_s w_s d(x_i^s, x) — weighted majority for categorical d = 0/1 loss, weighted median for L1, weighted mean for L2. Coordinate descent: objective non-increasing, converges to a stationary point. Streaming form: C_s <- rho C_s + d(x^s, x^*), O(1) memory per source.

**REFA application.** Simplest concrete implementation for REFA: keep a per-source scalar loss EMA C_s (loss of the model's post-hoc truth estimate vs source s's assertion, updated when a query resolves), set rho_s = log(sum C / C_s), use sigmoid(rho_s) to scale write_strength. One float per source, zero extra GPU memory pressure, no EM machinery — the right v0 before graduating to full Dawid-Skene.

Sources:
- Li, Li, Gao, Zhao, Fan, Han, 'Resolving Conflicts in Heterogeneous Data by Truth Discovery and Source Reliability Estimation', SIGMOD 2014 — <https://doi.org/10.1145/2588555.2610509>
- Li et al., 'A Survey on Truth Discovery', SIGKDD Explorations 17(2), 2016 — <https://arxiv.org/abs/1505.02463>

### Two-sided source errors (LTM sensitivity/specificity) map onto REFA's dual stance banks
*status: convergent*

Zhao, Rubinstein, Gemmell & Han's Latent Truth Model (VLDB 2012) shows a single reliability scalar is insufficient when sources err asymmetrically: a source that omits true facts (low sensitivity/recall) is a different failure mode from one that fabricates false facts (low specificity / high false-positive rate), and conflating them measurably degrades truth inference. LTM gives each source two Beta-priored parameters — P(assert | fact true) and P(silent | fact false) — with a Bernoulli truth prior, inferred by collapsed Gibbs sampling; it also treats missing assertions as informative (silence from a high-sensitivity source is evidence against). This maps with unusual precision onto REFA's architecture, which already maintains separate M+ (support) and M- (oppose) fast-weight matrices per bank: the natural per-source parameterization is one scalar per stance — a support-credibility scalar gating writes into M+ and an oppose-credibility scalar gating writes into M- — rather than a single symmetric weight. LTM's posterior also exhibits the 'unreliable-source unknown' signature: truth posterior near the prior despite many assertions, because all asserting sources have near-chance parameters.

**Math.** Truth t_f ~ Bern(beta). Source s: sensitivity phi^1_s = P(claim f | t_f = 1) ~ Beta(a1, b1); specificity phi^0_s = P(not claim f | t_f = 0) ~ Beta(a0, b0). Posterior odds for fact f: (beta/(1-beta)) prod_{s in claimants} [phi^1_s / (1 - phi^0_s)] prod_{s in silent-observers} [(1-phi^1_s)/phi^0_s]. Collapsed Gibbs over {t_f}; per-source sufficient stats are 4 counts (TP, FN, FP, TN), so streaming state = 4 floats/source.

**REFA application.** Give each source TWO learned scalars: rho_s^+ multiplies write_strength when polarity = SUPPORT (into support_memory), rho_s^- when polarity = OPPOSE. Cost: num_sources x 2 embedding table. Enables a richer provenance-conflict task family: fabricator sources (bad rho^+, fine rho^-) vs denier sources, which a single-scalar model provably cannot separate.

Sources:
- Zhao, Rubinstein, Gemmell, Han, 'A Bayesian Approach to Discovering Truth from Conflicting Sources for Data Integration', PVLDB 5(6), 2012 — <https://arxiv.org/abs/1203.0058>

### Streaming Dawid-Skene via online EM (stochastic-approximation sufficient statistics) ⚑
*status: partial*

Cappé & Moulines (JRSS-B 2009) give the general recipe for turning any exponential-family EM — Dawid-Skene included — into a single-pass streaming estimator: replace the batch E-step with a stochastic-approximation update of the expected sufficient statistics, and apply the M-step map to the running statistics after each event. For DS the sufficient statistics are the per-source expected confusion counts, so total estimator state is O(J K^2) floats, updated in O(K^2) per assertion, with no replay of the stream. Theory: with step sizes gamma_t = t^{-alpha}, alpha in (0.5, 1], the iterates converge a.s. to stationary points of the limiting likelihood, and with Polyak-Ruppert averaging the estimator attains the same asymptotic variance as the batch MLE (rate t^{-1/2}) — i.e., streaming costs nothing asymptotically. This is the correct formal frame for REFA's per-event loop: the reliability table is itself a fast-weight memory with its own learning rate schedule, updated by the same event stream that updates M+/M-. (Specific published 'incremental DS' systems exist in the crowdsourcing literature; the Cappé-Moulines construction is the one with clean guarantees, hence status partial for the DS-specific composition.)

**Math.** Sufficient stats s = {N^(j)_{kl}} (expected count: source j said l when truth k) and {N_k}. Update on event (j asserts l about fact i): compute posterior tau_k = P(T_i = k | current theta, assertions on i); s_{t+1} = s_t + gamma_{t+1} (s(y_{t+1}; theta_t) - s_t), i.e., N^(j)_{kl} += gamma (tau_k - N^(j)_{kl}-share); M-step map: pi^(j)_{kl} = N^(j)_{kl} / sum_l N^(j)_{kl}. Guarantees: gamma_t = t^{-alpha}, alpha in (0.5,1] gives a.s. convergence to stationary points of KL(p* || p_theta); Polyak averaging gives asymptotically efficient t^{-1/2} rate. Memory O(J K^2), per-event time O(K^2 m_i).

**REFA application.** Drop-in side module for the per-timestep loop: after each history event, update that source's confusion stats with gamma_t decayed by the same schedule family as bank decay lambda; expose rho_s = log-odds from pi_s to the write gate. Because state is J x K^2 floats it lives in RAM, not VRAM, and survives across sessions — matching the roadmap's 'persistent memory across independent sessions' line.

Sources:
- Cappé & Moulines, 'On-line expectation-maximization algorithm for latent data models', J. Royal Stat. Soc. B 71(3):593-613, 2009 — <https://arxiv.org/abs/0712.4273>
- Neal & Hinton, 'A view of the EM algorithm that justifies incremental, sparse, and other variants', in Learning in Graphical Models, 1998 — <https://doi.org/10.1007/978-94-011-5014-9_12>

### Dynamic source reliability: evolving truth with decayed error counts + Cramer-Rao bounds on reliability
*status: convergent*

Two streams of work handle reliabilities that drift over time. (1) Li et al., 'On the Discovery of Evolving Truth' (KDD 2015): incremental truth discovery where both facts and source reliabilities evolve; reliability at time t comes from exponentially decayed cumulative errors, and a decision rule separates 'the truth changed' from 'the source got noisy' — the key confound in any revision task. Updates are O(1) per claim. (2) The social-sensing line (Wang, Kaplan, Le, Abdelzaher, IPSN 2012, and the recursive/streaming follow-up at ICDCS 2013) casts binary truth discovery as MLE via EM and derives Cramer-Rao lower bounds on the variance of the reliability estimates themselves — giving confidence intervals on each source's estimated reliability from asymptotic normality of the MLE. That second-order quantity matters: a reliability estimate based on 3 observations should modulate writes far less aggressively than one based on 3000, and the CRLB tells you exactly how much uncertainty remains.

**Math.** Evolving reliability: w_s^t = -log( C_s^t / sum_{s'} C_{s'}^t ), C_s^t = sum_{tau <= t} rho^{t-tau} err_s^tau, 0 < rho < 1 — an EMA of losses whose decay rho is decoupled from memory decay lambda. Social sensing: reliability e_j = P(claim correct); EM MLE hat{e}_j with var(hat{e}_j) >= [I(theta)^{-1}]_{jj} (Fisher information of the DS likelihood); asymptotic CI hat{e}_j +/- z sqrt(CRLB). Shrinkage form for small n: use posterior mean under Beta prior, e~_j = (a + n_correct)/(a + b + n_j).

**REFA application.** REFA's reliability tracker needs its own decay rho (source drift timescale) distinct from bank decay lambda (fact drift timescale) — conflating them makes 'source went bad' indistinguishable from 'world changed', which is precisely the revision-vs-provenance confound the task suite should probe. Also: gate the reliability scalar's influence by its own confidence (Beta-posterior variance), so a new source starts at neutral weight instead of being trusted or damned on 2 events.

Sources:
- Li, Li, Gao, Zhao, Fan, Han, 'On the Discovery of Evolving Truth', KDD 2015 — <https://doi.org/10.1145/2783258.2783277>
- Wang, Kaplan, Le, Abdelzaher, 'On truth discovery in social sensing: a maximum likelihood estimation approach', IPSN 2012 — <https://doi.org/10.1145/2185677.2185737>

### TruthFinder: log-space additive trust propagation (the pre-theory fixed point)
*status: convergent*

TruthFinder (Yin, Han, Yu; KDD 2007 / TKDE 2008) is the founding web-scale truth-discovery system and the cleanest cautionary tale: it iterates 'a claim is credible if trustworthy sources assert it; a source is trustworthy if its claims are credible' as a heuristic fixed point, with two lasting technical contributions and no identifiability or convergence theory. The contributions: (i) combine independent sources in log space — define source score tau*(s) = -ln(1 - tau(s)) so that independent supporting sources ADD, giving claim score sigma(f) = sum_{s asserts f} tau*(s); (ii) a dampened logistic squash with an inter-claim implication term so that similar claims share support and mutually exclusive claims inhibit each other. Empirically convergent, formally unguaranteed — the DS/EM line strictly supersedes it as an estimator, but its additive log-space combination rule is exactly the right algebra for combining multiple recalled memory traces, and its implication matrix anticipates relational structure among claims (which REFA's relation routing already has).

**Math.** tau(s) = (1/|F_s|) sum_{f in F_s} s(f); tau*(s) = -ln(1 - tau(s)); sigma(f) = sum_{s in S_f} tau*(s); with implications: sigma*(f) = sigma(f) + rho sum_{f'} sigma(f') imp(f' -> f); squash s(f) = 1/(1 + e^{-gamma sigma*(f)}), gamma < 1 damps overconfidence from correlated sources. Iterate to fixed point; convergence empirical (cosine change < 1e-2 in a few iterations), no contraction proof.

**REFA application.** The read-side combination rule for REFA when multiple traces answer one query: sum reliability log-scores (additive in log space) rather than averaging or Dempster-combining — consistent with the KOS log-odds oracle. The damping factor gamma is the knob acknowledging non-independence of traces written by correlated sources.

Sources:
- Yin, Han, Yu, 'Truth Discovery with Multiple Conflicting Information Providers on the Web', IEEE TKDE 20(6):796-808, 2008 (KDD 2007) — <https://doi.org/10.1109/TKDE.2007.190745>

### Spammers, adversaries, and copying: where conditional independence breaks (MACE, source-dependence)
*status: partial*

Two established failure modes bound the estimator's validity. (1) MACE (Hovy et al., NAACL 2013) refines DS with a per-source spamming submodel: each assertion is either a copy of the truth or a draw from a source-specific strategy distribution, governed by a latent spam indicator — separating competence from strategy, and robustly down-weighting adversarial or degenerate (always-same-answer) sources via variational EM. EM-family truth discovery generally tolerates adversarial sources as long as reliable mass dominates; it breaks under coordinated majorities. (2) Source dependence (Dong, Berti-Equille, Srivastava, PVLDB 2009): copying between sources violates the conditional-independence assumption that all identifiability results rest on; copying is itself detectable, Bayesian-style, because copiers share FALSE values at improbable rates (shared truth is expected; shared error is the fingerprint), and detected dependence discounts the copier's vote. For REFA/Engram Loop this is the formal home of the perceived/told/self provenance typing: 'told' assertions are potentially copies (dependence discount, spam prior), 'perceived' are independent draws, 'self' are model-generated and must never feed the reliability estimator as fresh evidence (echo-chamber/confabulation loop).

**Math.** MACE: A_ij = T_i if S_ij = 0, else A_ij ~ Multinomial(xi_j), S_ij ~ Bern(eps_j); infer {eps_j, xi_j, T_i} by variational EM; competence = 1 - eps_j. Dependence test (ACCU model): for sources s1, s2, P(share value v | independent) propto product of individual error rates, whereas copying inflates P(share false v); Bayes factor over shared-false counts flags copying; copied votes weighted by (1 - c), c = copy probability. Robustness heuristic: weighted schemes tolerate adversarial weight fraction < 1/2 of total log-odds mass.

**REFA application.** Map provenance types to priors: perceived -> Beta prior concentrated high, independent; told -> spam-capable prior (MACE eps_j) + dependence discount when a told-chain shares an origin; self -> excluded from reliability updates entirely. Adds a 'colluding sources' variant to the provenance-conflict battery: k copying sources vs 1 reliable source, where naive DS fails and the dependence discount fixes it.

Sources:
- Hovy, Berg-Kirkpatrick, Vaswani, Hovy, 'Learning Whom to Trust with MACE', NAACL 2013 — <https://aclanthology.org/N13-1132/>
- Dong, Berti-Equille, Srivastava, 'Integrating Conflicting Data: The Role of Source Dependence', PVLDB 2(1), 2009 — <https://doi.org/10.14778/1687627.1687690>

### Four-cause 'unknown': separating unreliable-source from no-evidence and conflict (building site) ⚑
*status: absent*

No existing work — in truth discovery or in evidential deep learning — trains a model to output a typed abstention distinguishing WHY it doesn't know, yet the weighted log-odds algebra makes the taxonomy exact. With per-source log-odds weights a_j, a query's evidence state is summarized by two statistics: net weighted log-odds Lambda (direction) and total weighted evidence mass A = sum |a_j| vs raw assertion count N (how much credible information exists). Four regimes: (i) supported/opposed: |Lambda| large; (ii) no-evidence: N ~ 0; (iii) conflicting-evidence: N > 0, A large, but positive and negative mass cancel (|Lambda| << A); (iv) unreliable-source: N > 0 but A ~ 0 — assertions exist, none carry weight because every asserting source is near chance (a_j ~ 0). Cases (iii) and (iv) are behaviorally identical in any equal-weight fusion (both look like 'balanced conflict' or get resolved by count), and DS-style reliability estimation is precisely what makes them separable. Nothing in the truth-discovery literature exposes this as a supervised output; nothing in evidential DL (which conflates all vacuity into one mass) does either. This is a genuinely new, cheap, falsifiable task for REFA: the generator knows the planted cause, so cause-of-unknown becomes a supervised label.

**Math.** a_j = log[pi^(j)(l_j | true) / pi^(j)(l_j | false)] per assertion; Lambda = log(p1/p0) + sum_j a_j; A = sum_j |a_j|; N = #assertions. Decision regions: supported: Lambda > theta; opposed: Lambda < -theta; else unknown with cause = no-evidence if N = 0; unreliable-source if N > 0 and A < kappa_A; conflicting if A >= kappa_A and |Lambda| < theta (mass present, cancelling). Well-defined because A is reliability-weighted: for a chance source pi(l|true) = pi(l|false) so a_j = 0 exactly. Expected |Lambda| under planted parameters gives analytic thresholds; task difficulty again controlled by Chernoff information I*.

**REFA application.** Extend the classifier head from 3 to 6 logits (supported, opposed, unknown-no-evidence, unknown-conflict, unknown-unreliable, + optional 'stale') or keep 3 classes plus a 3-way cause head active only on unknown. The model has the needed signals already: raw recalled magnitudes ||z+||, ||z-|| (N-like), reliability-weighted versions (A-like), and their difference (Lambda-like). Adds one small linear head; supervision free from the generator.

Sources:
- Construction from DS log-odds algebra (Dawid & Skene 1979; KOS oracle rule); contrast with single-vacuity evidential DL (Sensoy, Kaplan, Kandemir, 'Evidential Deep Learning to Quantify Classification Uncertainty', NeurIPS 2018) — <https://arxiv.org/abs/1806.01768>

---

## Follow-up (critic-directed): Neural algorithmic reasoning and the the
`followup:Neural algorithmic reasoning and the the`

The 2020-2025 literature gives REFA exactly what the roadmap's length-extrapolation item lacks: a predictive theory of WHEN a trained model generalizes to longer streams, not just whether it can express the task. Algorithmic alignment (Xu et al. ICLR 2020) makes sample complexity a function of how well REFA's per-step modules (delta-rule write, linear read, GRU) match the target task's one-step update, predicting cheap learning for revision/recall and expensive learning for multi-hop unless intermediate supervision is added — which is precisely CLRS-30's hint mechanism (train n=16, test n=64; hints lift OOD micro-F1 to 74.14%). For the transformer baseline, RASP-L and the C-RASP/limit-transformer framework predict extrapolation on 1-sparse lookup tasks and failure on counting/majority tasks, while the Chomsky-hierarchy study predicts the mirror image for recurrent/counter architectures — giving REFA a falsifiable double dissociation on its own task suite. The single most load-bearing new result is Buitrago Ruiz & Gu (ICML 2025): recurrent models fail at length mostly because training never visits long-horizon states, and ~500 steps of state-passing post-training (0.1% of budget) restores 2k-to-128k generalization — directly applicable to REFA's fast-weight matrices by carrying final memories across batches. Discrete NAR shows that discretized states plus transition supervision yields provably size-invariant execution, matching REFA's planned top-k routing. Reading REFA's own update equation through the negative-eigenvalue/DeltaProduct lens shows its memory spectrum is confined to (lambda-1, lambda) ~ (-0.005, 0.995), so sign-flip (parity-like) revision dynamics are provably out of reach of the memory and fall on the GRU workspace; a 2x write-gate is a one-line fix. Finally, robustness results (Zhou et al. 2024) warn that length generalization is high-variance across seeds, so REFA's multi-seed roadmap item should report extrapolation medians and IQRs, not means.

**Verification** of load-bearing claims:

- **CONFIRMED** — Algorithmic alignment: sample complexity is governed by module-level match to the target algorithm's one-step update
  - Source verified: Xu, Li, Zhang, Du, Kawarabayashi, Jegelka, 'What Can Neural Networks Reason About?', ICLR 2020, arXiv:1905.13211; code repo NNReasoning/What-Can-Neural-Networks-Reason-About exists. Abstract and full text confirm the core claim: algorithmic alignment is formally defined, a sample-complexity bound decreases with better alignment, intuitive physics/VQA/shortest paths are unified as DP, GNN message passing simulates the Bellman-Ford relaxation step while 'a giant MLP or Deep Set must learn the structure of the entire for-loop'. Theorem 3.6 (sequential learning setting, with auxiliary per-module labels) states (M, O(eps), O(delta))-learnability, as claimed. Two corrections to the math as stated: (1) the alignment value is defined via n * max_i C_{A_i}(f_i, eps, delta) <= M (Definition includes the factor n = number of modules, not bare max_i — same 'hardest module' scaling up to the module count); (2) in Theorem 3.5 (the Arora et al. 2019 NTK bound) log(1/delta) enters ADDITIVELY, C_A = O(((sum_j p_j |a_j| ||b_j||_2^{p_j})^2 + log(1/delta)) / eps^2), not multiplicatively. Also, the explicit 'exp(T) blowup' for T-step algorithms is an interpretation, not a stated theorem: the paper's formal composition-penalty example is Corollary 3.7 (MLP bound O(l^2) times larger than GNN for a pairwise-sum task) plus the qualitative argument that composed polynomials raise the NTK norm; the exponential-in-depth reading follows from Theorem 3.5's degree dependence but is not proved for general T-step DP. Note also Theorem 3.6 assumes sequential module training with intermediate supervision — the paper explicitly leaves end-to-end learning to future work, which matters for the REFA application (it strengthens the case for intermediate/hint supervision rather than guaranteeing end-to-end sample efficiency).
- **CONFIRMED** — CLRS-30 hint supervision: per-step internal-state targets are the standard, measurable lever for OOD size generalization
  - Both sources verified. CLRS benchmark (Velickovic et al., ICML 2022, arXiv:2205.15659; google-deepmind/clrs): README confirms 30 algorithms from CLRS 3ed, 'trajectories of hints that expose the internal state of each algorithm', canonical OOD protocol train problem-size 16 (1000 trajectories) / test size 64, hints padded to max trajectory length with a lengths field for loss masking — all as claimed. Generalist Neural Algorithmic Learner (Ibarz et al., arXiv:2209.11142, LoG 2022): full paper excerpt confirms Triplet-GMPNN mean single-task OOD micro-F1 74.14% and >60% on 24/30 algorithms, encode-process-decode with a shared processor, Sinkhorn permutation decoding for sorting, gating, and triplet edge reasoning exactly as the claim's formula states (t_ijk = psi_t(z_i,z_j,z_k,e_ij,e_ik,e_kj,g); h_ij = phi(max_k t_ijk), 8-dim triplet features). TWO CORRECTIONS: (1) the 'prior SOTA around 57%' / '~0.576' figure is wrong — the prior models' overall averages in Table 1 are Memnet 38.88%, MPNN 44.99%, PGN 50.84%, so the correct comparison is 74.14% vs a prior best of 50.84% (>20% absolute improvement, as the paper states); no ~57% prior-SOTA number appears. (2) 'hint-stabilization (gating + teacher-forcing schedule) is ablated as a main driver' mischaracterizes the paper: the prior CLRS model used teacher forcing with probability 0.5, and the Generalist paper's stabilizing changes allowed it to REMOVE teacher forcing altogether (removal helped sorting and Kruskal; only Naive String Matcher improved with teacher forcing). The actual stabilizers are gating (which the paper says helps single-task but, surprisingly, NOT multi-task training), soft hint propagation, encoder initialization, gradient clipping, and randomized position scalars. The core mechanism claim — per-step hint supervision pinning intermediate state as the benchmarked lever for 4x size extrapolation — is accurate. Minor nuance: 74.14% is the single-task-trained figure (Table 1); the multi-task generalist model is reported separately.
- **CONFIRMED** — RASP-L: a program-complexity criterion for which tasks decoder-only transformers length-generalize on
  - Source verified: Zhou, Bradley, Littwin, Razin, Saremi, Susskind, Bengio, Nakkiran, 'What Algorithms can Transformers Learn? A Study in Length Generalization', arXiv:2310.16028, ICLR 2024 (OpenReview AssIuHnmHX). Abstract confirms the RASP-Generalization Conjecture (transformers tend to length-generalize if the task has a short RASP-L program working for all input lengths, plus a training-diversity condition), that format interventions (scratchpads, index hints) yielded the first strong from-scratch length generalization on parity and addition, and the counterexample where Abbe et al.'s min-degree-interpolator model mispredicts while the RASP-based conjecture succeeds. ONE CORRECTION: the claim lists 'counting' among the failing tasks with no RASP-L program — in the paper, the 'count' task (output the sequence from a to b) is a SUCCESS case: it has a short RASP-L program and demonstrates strong length generalization (a reproduction repo built around exactly this task confirms 'the counting task, which demonstrates excellent length generalization'). The verified failure cases are parity and vanilla addition (and copy with repeated tokens); do not cite this paper as evidence that count-like tasks fail. The claim's broader inference — that an ACCUMULATING/tallying counter c_t = c_{t-1} + x_t is not RASP-L-expressible (which underlies the parity failure) — is consistent with the paper, but the paper's named 'counting' task is not that task. Also, the conjecture is stated as an 'if' with realizability+simplicity+diversity conditions (the 'iff' phrasing is an informal strengthening). The predicted transformer success on 'last matching key' retrieval (induction-head-like, RASP-L-short) and the double-dissociation prediction for REFA are the claim's own extrapolations, reasonable but not results of the paper.
- **CONFIRMED** — Chomsky-hierarchy taxonomy: recurrent/counter architectures and transformers extrapolate on provably different task classes
  - Source verified: Deletang, Ruoss, Grau-Moya, Genewein, Wenliang, Catt, Cundy, Hutter, Legg, Veness, Ortega, 'Neural Networks and the Chomsky Hierarchy', ICLR 2023, arXiv:2207.02098; official repo google-deepmind/neural_networks_chomsky_hierarchy quotes the abstract verbatim: '2200 models, 16 tasks' (exactly as claimed), grouping by Chomsky hierarchy forecasts OOD generalization, 'negative results where even extensive amounts of data and training time never led to any non-trivial generalization, despite models having sufficient capacity to perfectly fit the training data', and 'RNNs and Transformers fail to generalize on non-regular tasks, LSTMs can solve regular and counter-language tasks, and only networks augmented with structured memory (such as a stack or memory tape) can successfully generalize on context-free and context-sensitive tasks'. All headline elements of the claim check out. CORRECTIONS to the example task groupings: in the paper/repo, bucket sort is classified CONTEXT-SENSITIVE, not counter/DCF; 'modular arithmetic (simple)' is REGULAR while 'modular arithmetic (brackets)' is DCF; reverse string is DCF as implied. The repo's main task directories contain 15 tasks (4 regular, 4 DCF, 7 CS); the abstract's 16 includes the additional counter-language material. The counter-machine math (c_{t+1} = c_t + u(x_t), LSTM saturated gates as +-1 counters, majority as two-counter comparison) is consistent with the paper's discussion of counter languages and with standard results, and the arithmetic in the REFA application is correct: with decay lambda = 0.995 the effective accumulation horizon is 1/(1-lambda) = 200 events (that plateau prediction is the claim's own extrapolation, not a result of the paper).
- **CONFIRMED** — Unexplored-states hypothesis: recurrent models fail at length because training never visits long-horizon states — fixable with ~0.1% extra training
  - Source verified: Ricardo Buitrago Ruiz & Albert Gu, 'Understanding and Improving Length Generalization in Recurrent Models', arXiv:2507.02782, ICML 2025 (PMLR v267, buitrago25a). The abstract confirms every load-bearing element verbatim: recurrent models (state space models, linear attention) can in principle process arbitrary lengths but fail beyond training context; the 'unexplored states hypothesis' — failure occurs because training exposes the model to only a limited subset of the distribution of attainable states; interventions that increase state coverage, namely initializing the state with Gaussian noise or with the final state of a different input sequence (state passing); and the headline cost/benefit: 'With only 500 post-training steps (~0.1% of the pre-training budget), these interventions enable length generalization for sequences that are orders of magnitude longer than the training context (e.g. 2k -> 128k)'. The paper's full text also confirms 'Effective Remembrance' as its diagnostic for how much distant context influences the output and 'State Passing' as a named intervention. The claim's formalization (rho_T as the distribution of states reachable in <= T steps from h_0 = 0, failure as state OOD) is a faithful paraphrase of the hypothesis. The REFA recipe (carry final memory/GRU states across sequences, or sample initial state from the long-run distribution, as a short fine-tuning phase) is a direct and legitimate application. No errors found; note only that 2k->128k is the paper's own 'e.g.' figure for specific models/tasks, not a universal guarantee, and the claim that this fix closes 'most' of REFA's gap is a prediction, not a result.

### Algorithmic alignment: sample complexity is governed by module-level match to the target algorithm's one-step update ⚑
*status: convergent*

Xu et al. (ICLR 2020) formalize why architectures with equal expressive power differ wildly in learnability of reasoning tasks. A network N with modules N_1..N_n 'algorithmically aligns' with algorithm g if g decomposes into functions f_1..f_n such that replacing each N_i by f_i makes N simulate g, and each f_i is individually sample-efficiently learnable by its module. They derive a sample-complexity bound that decreases with better alignment, and unify intuitive physics, VQA and shortest paths as dynamic programming, showing GNNs align with DP (one message-passing step = one DP relaxation) while a monolithic MLP must learn the entire composed function. The practical content: what matters for generalization is not what the architecture can represent but whether its per-step computation graph mirrors the target's per-step update, because then each module only needs to learn a simple function. This is the framework the REFA synthesis needs to predict, task by task, which of its synthetic relational tasks the fast-weight recurrence will learn sample-efficiently and extrapolate on.

**Math.** Alignment value M = max_i C_{A_i}(f_i, eps, delta), the worst module's sample complexity for its piece f_i of g. Main theorem (sequential-learning setting): if N and g align with value M, then g is (M, O(eps), O(delta))-learnable by N — total samples scale with the hardest MODULE, not the composition. Module bound via NTK (Arora et al. 2019): a 2-layer MLP learns g(x) = sum_j a_j (b_j^T x)^{p_j} with C_A = O((sum_j p_j |a_j| ||b_j||_2^{p_j})^2 log(1/delta) / eps^2). Composition penalty: an end-to-end MLP for a T-step algorithm must fit the composed polynomial whose degree (hence NTK norm) grows multiplicatively in T, giving exp(T) blowup, while a DP-aligned network learns only the degree-O(1) one-step relaxation, e.g. d[u] <- min_v (d[v] + w(v,u)).

**REFA application.** REFA's delta-rule write IS the one-step update of last-write-wins revision (erase what k retrieves, write new v), so alignment predicts low sample complexity and small-n mastery for the revision task — testable against the GRU/transformer baselines. Conversely, multi-hop queries (A->B, B->C |- A->C) have no single-read decomposition in REFA (one linear read per step), so alignment predicts a sample-complexity cliff there unless intermediate supervision or an iterated-read step is added. This turns REFA's task suite into an alignment testbed at 10k-1.5M params.

Sources:
- Xu, Li, Zhang, Du, Kawarabayashi, Jegelka. What Can Neural Networks Reason About? ICLR 2020, arXiv:1905.13211 — <https://arxiv.org/abs/1905.13211>
- Code repo NNReasoning/What-Can-Neural-Networks-Reason-About — <https://github.com/NNReasoning/What-Can-Neural-Networks-Reason-About>

### CLRS-30 hint supervision: per-step internal-state targets are the standard, measurable lever for OOD size generalization ⚑
*status: convergent*

The CLRS Algorithmic Reasoning Benchmark (ICML 2022) implements 30 classical algorithms as graph trajectories and, crucially, provides 'hints': time-series of each algorithm's internal state (pointers, visited flags, partial distances) that can be supervised at every step. The canonical protocol is explicitly an extrapolation test: train on problem size 16, test on size 64. The follow-up Generalist Neural Algorithmic Learner (LoG 2022) showed that stabilizing hint usage (gated updates, triplet edge reasoning, Sinkhorn pointer decoding, encode-process-decode with a single shared processor) lifts mean OOD micro-F1 across all 30 algorithms to 74.14% from a prior SOTA around 57%, with >60% on 24/30 algorithms. The mechanism relevant to REFA: hint supervision pins the model's intermediate state to the algorithm's state at every timestep, which prevents the learned recurrence from drifting into idiosyncratic representations that only work at training size. This is the concrete, benchmarked training lever for REFA's multi-hop and revision tasks that the roadmap currently lacks.

**Math.** Loss: L = L_output + sum_t L_hint(hhat_t, h_t), hints h_t = algorithm internal state at step t, padded to max trajectory length T with per-trajectory length masking. OOD protocol: n_train = 16, n_test = 64 (4x). Triplet-GMPNN edge reasoning: t_ijk = psi_t(z_i, z_j, z_k, e_ij, e_ik, e_kj, g); edge update h_ij = phi(max_k t_ijk). Headline: mean OOD micro-F1 0.7414 over CLRS-30 vs ~0.576 prior SOTA; hint-stabilization (gating + teacher-forcing schedule) is ablated as a main driver.

**REFA application.** Add a CLRS-style hint head to REFAMicro: at every step, an auxiliary 3-way classifier on (recalled_support, recalled_oppose) predicts the ground-truth running belief state of the queried fact (supported/opposed/unknown so far) — the synthetic generators already know this state, so hints are free. For multi-hop, supervise the intermediate hop's retrieved entity. Predicts: hint-trained REFA holds accuracy at 4x train stream length where hint-free training degrades; ~0 extra compute on a 6GB GPU.

Sources:
- Velickovic et al. The CLRS Algorithmic Reasoning Benchmark. ICML 2022, arXiv:2205.15659 — <https://arxiv.org/abs/2205.15659>
- google-deepmind/clrs README (train 16 / test 64 protocol, hints) — <https://github.com/google-deepmind/clrs>
- Ibarz et al. A Generalist Neural Algorithmic Learner. LoG 2022, arXiv:2209.11142 — <https://arxiv.org/abs/2209.11142>

### RASP-L: a program-complexity criterion for which tasks decoder-only transformers length-generalize on ⚑
*status: partial*

Zhou et al. (2023) propose the RASP-Generalization Conjecture: a decoder-only transformer trained from scratch with next-token prediction length-generalizes on a task iff the task has a short program in RASP-L, a restricted RASP fragment matching what transformers compute uniformly across lengths. Verified empirically: tasks with short RASP-L programs (copy with unique tokens, mode/most-frequent, sort with index hints) extrapolate strongly; tasks without one (parity, counting, vanilla addition — all need index arithmetic or unbounded counters) fail, and format interventions (scratchpads, index hints, reversed answers) work exactly when they convert a task into one with a short RASP-L program — yielding the first from-scratch length generalization on parity and addition. They also exhibit cases where the min-degree-interpolator simplicity bias (Abbe et al.) mispredicts while RASP-L predicts correctly. For REFA this is the baseline-side theory: it says which of REFA's synthetic tasks the small causal-transformer baseline should extrapolate on, making the REFA-vs-transformer contrast a designed experiment rather than an observation.

**Math.** RASP-L constraints: selectors use only order/equality comparisons of token values and causal position predicates (no arbitrary index arithmetic); aggregation = mean over selected positions; program length and constants independent of input length L. Conjecture: P(length-generalize | trained on lengths <= L) is high iff exists RASP-L program of O(1) size for the task. Parity/counting require an unbounded counter c_t = c_{t-1} + x_t or index arithmetic — not expressible — predicted (and observed) to fail without scratchpad; with a step-by-step scratchpad the per-token map becomes RASP-L-simple and extrapolation appears.

**REFA application.** Prediction for REFA's baselines: the small causal transformer should length-generalize on relational recall and last-write-wins revision (both are 'select last matching key, read value' = short RASP-L), but fail on contrastive/majority-of-evidence and on any count-based abstention rule (no RASP-L program). REFA's linear fast-weight memory accumulates counts natively, so the suite should show a clean double dissociation. Also justifies a scratchpad variant of the multi-hop task as a controlled format lever.

Sources:
- Zhou, Bradley, Littwin, Razin, Saremi, Susskind, Bengio, Nakkiran. What Algorithms can Transformers Learn? A Study in Length Generalization. arXiv:2310.16028 (ICLR 2024) — <https://arxiv.org/abs/2310.16028>

### Chomsky-hierarchy taxonomy: recurrent/counter architectures and transformers extrapolate on provably different task classes ⚑
*status: convergent*

Deletang et al. (ICLR 2023) trained 2200 models on 16 sequence tasks grouped by Chomsky level and evaluated on unseen longer lengths. Result: the hierarchy position of the task predicts which architectures length-generalize. Transformers and vanilla RNNs fail on non-regular tasks; LSTMs solve regular AND counter-language tasks (their cell state implements +-1 counters); only stack-augmented RNNs solve deterministic context-free tasks and only tape-RNNs solve context-sensitive ones. Negative results are strong: for misaligned architecture/task pairs, no amount of data or training produced non-trivial generalization despite perfect training fit — i.e., extrapolation failure is structural, not a data problem. This is the recurrent-side complement to RASP-L: REFA's memory + GRU is a counter-capable recurrent system, so the taxonomy predicts where its recurrence beats the transformer baseline (counting/majority/abstention-by-count) and where both should fail (tasks needing a stack, e.g. nested or hierarchical evidence scopes, if ever added).

**Math.** Task classes: regular (e.g. parity, even pairs) < counter/DCF (e.g. reverse string, modular arithmetic, bucket sort) < CS. Counter machine: c_{t+1} = c_t + u(x_t), accept iff linear threshold on c_T — LSTM cell state realizes this (saturated gates give +-1 increments); majority = compare two counters, in counter class. Empirical matrix (2200 models, 16 tasks): Transformer: regular-only and partial; RNN: regular; LSTM: regular + counter; Stack-RNN: +DCF; Tape-RNN: +CS. Failure cells show flat OOD accuracy at chance regardless of samples/steps.

**REFA application.** REFA's contrastive-evidence task is a two-counter comparison: memory magnitude along a fact's key direction accumulates ~sum_t lambda^(T-t) beta_t r_h for each stance, and the head thresholds support vs oppose — squarely counter-class, so REFA should extrapolate where the transformer baseline flatlines. Warning from the same result: with decay lambda=0.995 the 'counter' saturates at horizon 1/(1-lambda)=200 events, predicting an extrapolation plateau beyond ~200 evidence events unless counting banks push lambda -> 1.

Sources:
- Deletang, Ruoss, Grau-Moya, Genewein et al. Neural Networks and the Chomsky Hierarchy. ICLR 2023, arXiv:2207.02098 — <https://arxiv.org/abs/2207.02098>
- google-deepmind/neural_networks_chomsky_hierarchy README (abstract + task/model matrix) — <https://github.com/google-deepmind/neural_networks_chomsky_hierarchy>

### Unexplored-states hypothesis: recurrent models fail at length because training never visits long-horizon states — fixable with ~0.1% extra training ⚑
*status: convergent*

Buitrago Ruiz & Gu (ICML 2025) show that recurrent models (Mamba-class SSMs, linear attention, RNNs) usually CAN process sequences far longer than training, and their length failure is a state-distribution shift: training from h_0 = 0 on length-T sequences only ever exposes the model to a narrow subset of attainable states, so at longer lengths the state exits the trained region and predictions collapse. Interventions that widen the visited state distribution — initializing h_0 with noise, or 'state passing' (initialize with the final state of another sequence), or fitting the long-run state distribution — restore length generalization with about 500 post-training steps, roughly 0.1% of the pretraining budget, taking models trained at 2k context to robust behavior at 128k. They also introduce diagnostics ('effective remembrance') for how much distant context actually influences the output. This is the single most direct answer in the literature to REFA's roadmap question — when does a trained streaming model generalize to longer evidence streams — and it says the dominant failure mode is a training-distribution artifact with a nearly free fix, not an expressivity limit.

**Math.** Let rho_T = distribution of states reachable in <= T steps from h_0 = 0. Hypothesis: failure at T' >> T occurs because h_{t>T} leaves supp(rho_T) (state OOD), not because of capacity. Fix: train with h_0 ~ rho_infinity approximated by (a) h_0 = final state of an independent sequence (state passing) or (b) h_0 = noise matched to state statistics. Cost: ~500 steps ≈ 0.1% of pretraining; result: 2k-trained models generalize to 128k+ contexts. Diagnostic: effective remembrance = sensitivity of output at t to context at distance d, compared across train/test lengths.

**REFA application.** Direct recipe for REFA: stop initializing support/oppose memories and GRU state at zero every sequence. Carry final (M+, M-, workspace) states across batch elements (state passing) or sample M_0 from the empirical long-run state distribution, for a short fine-tuning phase. Predicts most of REFA's length-extrapolation gap on recall/revision closes at negligible cost on the 6GB GPU, and gives the synthesis its 'when': extrapolation holds iff test-time states stay inside the trained state distribution.

Sources:
- Buitrago Ruiz & Gu. Understanding and Improving Length Generalization in Recurrent Models. ICML 2025, arXiv:2507.02782 — <https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.02782>
- PMLR v267 (buitrago25a) — <https://proceedings.mlr.press/v267/buitrago25a.html>

### Position coupling: tying position IDs to task structure gives provable length generalization; content-keyed memory is its architectural analog
*status: convergent*

Cho et al. (NeurIPS 2024) assign the SAME position ID to structurally corresponding tokens (digit i of both operands and of the answer in addition), so attention can bind corresponding elements independent of absolute position. Empirically, transformers trained on 1-30 digit additions solve ~200-digit additions (>6x extrapolation), where standard PEs fail beyond a few extra digits. Theoretically, they prove a 1-layer, 2-head transformer with coupled positions implements addition for operand lengths exponential in embedding dimension, and that without positional structure a 1-layer model provably cannot. The ICLR 2025 follow-up (arXiv:2410.15787) combines position coupling with a scratchpad to length-generalize in BOTH operand length and operand count (multi-operand addition, multiplication). The general principle: length generalization follows when the addressing scheme exposes the task's invariant correspondence structure rather than raw position. REFA's key-addressed memory is exactly this principle implemented in content space — the entity-relation key k is identical whenever the same fact recurs, at any stream position — which explains mechanically why fast-weight addressing should beat positional attention on relational streams.

**Math.** Coupled PE: p(token) = shared ID for structurally corresponding tokens; e.g. digit slot i of A, B, and sum share ID i (+ random offset at train time). Theorem (informal): 1-layer, 2-head transformer with coupled positions computes n-digit addition for all n up to exp(O(d)) (d = embedding dim, i.e. d = O(log n) suffices); a 1-layer model without positional hints fails. Empirics: train n in [1,30] -> ~200-digit accuracy near-perfect (6.67x). Follow-up: coupling + scratchpad gives simultaneous generalization in operand length and count.

**REFA application.** Two uses. (1) Interpretation: REFA needs no position-coupling fix — L2-normalized content keys already couple all mentions of a fact; the synthesis can state this as 'position coupling by content' and predict REFA's recall extrapolation is PE-free by construction. (2) Control experiment: give the transformer baseline fact-identity-coupled position IDs (same ID for all events about the same (subject, relation)); if its extrapolation then matches REFA, the advantage is addressing structure, not recurrence — a clean ablation cheap enough for the 2060.

Sources:
- Cho, Cha, Awasthi, Bhojanapalli, Gupta, Yun. Position Coupling: Improving Length Generalization of Arithmetic Transformers Using Task Structure. NeurIPS 2024, arXiv:2405.20671 — <https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.20671>
- Cho, Cha, Bhojanapalli, Yun. Arithmetic Transformers Can Length-Generalize in Both Operand Length and Count. ICLR 2025, arXiv:2410.15787 — <https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.15787>
- HanseulJo/position-coupling (official repo, both papers) — <https://github.com/HanseulJo/position-coupling>

### Limit transformers and C-RASP: a formal sufficient condition for length generalization, upgrading the RASP-L heuristic to theorems
*status: partial*

Huang, Yang, Bhattamishra, Sarrof, Krebs, Zhou, Nakkiran, Hahn (arXiv:2410.02140, ICLR 2025) give the first broad formal framework for transformer length generalization. They define 'limit transformers' — idealized transformers whose behavior is coherent across all input lengths (norm-bounded parameters, periodic/local positional functions) — and prove that functions expressible in this class are learned, by an idealized inference procedure (regularized fitting on increasing lengths), in a way that generalizes to unboundedly longer inputs. A central corollary: everything definable in C-RASP — a temporal counting logic with prefix counts and linear threshold comparisons (Yang & Chiang's compilable fragment) — is length-generalizable, correctly predicting known successes and failures that heuristics missed. This partially covers counting: C-RASP CAN express prefix-count thresholds, refining RASP-L's blanket 'counting fails' to 'counting fails without the right positional/format scaffolding'. For REFA's synthesis this supplies the citable theorem-level statement: which evidential aggregation rules (threshold-of-counts vs order-sensitive revision) sit inside vs outside the provably-extrapolating class for the attention baseline.

**Math.** C-RASP core: count terms C_t = #{s <= t : phi(s)} for Boolean phi over tokens/earlier predicates; programs are Boolean combinations of linear inequalities sum_i a_i C_t^{(i)} >= b, closed under composition. Theorem (informal): f expressible by a limit transformer (bounded norms; positional functions periodic or local) implies the idealized learner trained on lengths <= N converges to f on ALL lengths as N grows; C-RASP subset of this class. Framework also handles predicates phi/psi (unary/binary positional predicates, their Def. 8), extending coverage to tasks with structured positional scaffolds.

**REFA application.** Use C-RASP as the classifier for REFA's task suite: 'supported iff #support - #oppose >= 1' is C-RASP (counting threshold) — the transformer baseline can in principle length-generalize IF formats expose it; last-write-wins revision is order-sensitive (latest polarity), expressible via 'no later opposing event' = C-RASP-definable with binary predicates. So the theory predicts baseline failures on REFA tasks are optimization/format artifacts, while REFA's recurrence bakes the same computations into state — sharpening what the comparison actually measures.

Sources:
- Huang et al. A Formal Framework for Understanding Length Generalization in Transformers. arXiv:2410.02140 (ICLR 2025) — <https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.02140>
- coli-saar/c-rasp interpreter (implements Huang et al. 2025 predicates) — <https://github.com/coli-saar/c-rasp>

### Discrete Neural Algorithmic Reasoning: discretized states + transition supervision give provable, perfect size generalization
*status: convergent*

Rodionov & Prokhorenkova (arXiv:2402.11628, ICML 2025) force a neural reasoner's execution trajectory to be a combination of finite, predefined discrete states (hard attention + state discretization), trained with supervision on the algorithm's state transitions. Because classical computation is transitions between discrete states and is unaffected by distribution shift, a model whose learned transition function exactly matches the algorithm's on the finite state/observation domain executes correctly at EVERY input size. They obtain perfect test scores on all SALSA-CLRS tasks (a sparse, harder-generalization CLRS variant) and, because the state space is finite, they can PROVE the trained model implements the algorithm by exhaustively checking the transition function. This is the strongest known form of length/size generalization — exact, verified, not statistical — and it is reachable precisely because of two levers REFA already has on its roadmap: sparse/hard routing (top-k) and per-step supervision (hints). The cost is expressivity: only tasks with a known finite-state per-step semantics qualify — which REFA's synthetic tasks do (3-state per-fact belief automaton).

**Math.** Force hidden state h_t in S, |S| finite (via hard attention + argmax/ST discretization); train with per-step targets so learned transition That: S x Sigma -> S. If That = T on all (s, sigma) pairs — checkable exhaustively since |S x Sigma| finite — then by induction the rollout equals the algorithm for every input size n: zero generalization error for all n. Empirics: 100% test on SALSA-CLRS tasks incl. larger-graph OOD; manual verification proofs for selected algorithms.

**REFA application.** REFA's per-fact semantics is a 3-state automaton (unknown/supported/opposed) with transitions driven by event polarity. A discrete variant: hard top-1 routing (ST-Gumbel) + sign/threshold-discretized read logits, trained with per-step belief hints; then verify the learned 3x3(xpolarity) transition table equals the generator's — if it does, revision extrapolates PROVABLY to any stream length. This is a realistic flagship result for a 6GB-GPU paper: exact length generalization with a checkable certificate, and it motivates the roadmap's top-k routing as more than a speed optimization.

Sources:
- Rodionov & Prokhorenkova. Discrete Neural Algorithmic Reasoning. arXiv:2402.11628 (ICML 2025) — <https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.11628>

### REFA's memory spectrum is confined to (lambda-1, lambda): sign-flip revision dynamics are provably out of reach of the fast weights
*status: partial*

Grazzi et al. (ICLR 2025) showed DeltaNet-style linear RNNs gain qualitatively new state-tracking (parity, some group word problems) when the state-transition eigenvalues are allowed into [-1, 0) — implemented in practice by letting the write gate beta range over (0, 2) instead of (0, 1) (this '2x beta' switch now ships in flash-linear-attention as allow_neg_eigval). DeltaProduct (Siems et al., NeurIPS 2025) generalizes to n_h Householder factors per token (diagonal + rank-n_h transitions), proves state-tracking capability grows with n_h under finite precision, and shows markedly better length extrapolation than DeltaNet. Applying this directly to REFAMicro's update (refa.py lines 113-122): per bank the transition is lambda*I - beta*r_h*k k^T with lambda = sigmoid init 0.995 and beta*r_h in (0,1), so the spectrum lies in (lambda-1, lambda) ~ (-0.005, 0.995). Consequence: the memory cannot implement any period-2/negation dynamics (e.g. 'each retraction toggles the belief'), and with soft routing beta*r_h << 1 the erase step is chronically incomplete, leaving decaying ghosts of superseded evidence — a concrete mechanism for length-dependent interference. The prior sweeps carry the eigenvalue-parity expressivity fragment; the new content is the length-extrapolation evidence and the one-line architectural fixes.

**Math.** DeltaNet: S_t = S_{t-1}(I - beta_t k_t k_t^T) + beta_t v_t k_t^T, ||k_t||=1; transition eigenvalues {1 (mult d-1), 1-beta_t}. beta in (0,1) => spec in (0,1]: monoid of contractions, no reflections => parity inexpressible. beta in (0,2) => 1-beta in (-1,1): products of generalized Householders reach richer finite groups; DeltaProduct uses prod_{i=1..n_h}(I - beta_i k_i k_i^T), rank-n_h deviation; tracking power increases with n_h and length extrapolation improves empirically. REFA per bank: A_t = lambda*I - (beta r_h) k k^T, spec = {lambda} u {lambda - beta r_h} subset (lambda-1, lambda) ~ (-0.005, 0.995); full erase requires beta r_h ~ lambda but soft routing gives r_h ~ 1/H = 0.125 on average.

**REFA application.** Three one-line levers for the length-extrapolation sweep: (1) write gate beta = 2*sigmoid(.) to admit negative eigenvalues (test on an alternating-retraction task the current model should fail); (2) top-k/hard routing so beta*r_h can reach ~1 and erases complete (ghost-evidence interference should shrink with stream length); (3) optional n_h=2 double-write per event (two Householder factors) as a micro-DeltaProduct. Each is measurable as extrapolation length vs train length on the existing suite.

Sources:
- Grazzi, Siems, Franke, Zela, Hutter, Pontil. Unlocking State-Tracking in Linear RNNs Through Negative Eigenvalues. ICLR 2025, arXiv:2411.12537 — <https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.12537>
- Siems, Carstensen, Zela, Hutter, Pontil, Grazzi. DeltaProduct: Improving State-Tracking in Linear RNNs via Householder Products. NeurIPS 2025, arXiv:2502.10297 — <https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.10297>
- fla-org/flash-linear-attention allow_neg_eigval implementation (beta * 2) — <https://github.com/fla-org/flash-linear-attention>

### Length generalization is achievable but high-variance: robustness must be reported as a distribution over seeds, not a mean
*status: convergent*

Zhou et al. (arXiv:2402.09371) systematically searched position-encoding x data-format combinations for decimal addition and found that the right recipe — FIRE positional encoding with randomized positions, reversed digit order, and index hints — yields >98% accuracy on 100-digit addition from training on at most 40-digit numbers (2.5x extrapolation), far beyond prior results. But the headline caveat is as important as the headline: the achieved length generalization is FRAGILE, varying dramatically with random seed, weight initialization, and training-data order — the same recipe can swing tens of accuracy points across runs. The methodological consequence for any length-extrapolation study, REFA's included, is that extrapolation is not a stable point property of an (architecture, task, format) triple; it is a distribution, and single-seed extrapolation claims (positive or negative) are close to uninformative. This dovetails with REFA's existing multi-seed-validation roadmap item and upgrades it from hygiene to a scientific requirement specifically for the extrapolation experiments.

**Math.** Best recipe: FIRE PE (learned functional relative bias f(i-j)) + randomized position sampling + reversed answer digits + index hints; result >98% exact-match at 100 digits with train length <= 40 (2.5x). Fragility: across seeds/data orders, accuracy at extrapolated lengths varies by tens of points (bimodal success/failure), while in-distribution accuracy is stable at ~100% — i.e. Var_seed[acc_OOD] >> Var_seed[acc_ID]. Protocol implication: report median and IQR of extrapolated-length accuracy over >= 10 seeds; use survival curves acc(T') vs T'/T_train.

**REFA application.** For REFA's sweeps: run every extrapolation cell (model x task x train-length) at >= 10 seeds on the 2060 (models are 10k-1.5M params, so this is hours not days), and report median + IQR survival curves acc(T')/T_train. A predicted signature worth testing: REFA's variance at extrapolated lengths should be much smaller than the transformer baseline's on aligned tasks (recall/revision), because the delta-rule mechanism, not a lottery-ticket attention pattern, carries the algorithm — a robustness dissociation, not just an accuracy one.

Sources:
- Zhou, Alon, Chen, Wang, Agarwal, Zhou. Transformers Can Achieve Length Generalization But Not Robustly. arXiv:2402.09371 — <https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.09371>

### Dependency sparsity as the control parameter: sparse-lookup tasks length-generalize, dense-aggregation tasks do not (for transformers)
*status: partial*

Golowich, Jelassi, Brandfonbrener, Kakade & Malach (ICML 2025) build a theoretical framework for length generalization in next-token prediction by decoder-only transformers, identifying the SPARSITY of token dependencies as the governing quantity: tasks where each output token depends on only a small number k of (arbitrarily positioned) earlier tokens admit length generalization, with the required training length growing with k, while tasks with dense dependencies (each output a function of a constant fraction of the prefix) fall outside the guarantee. Their analysis covers sparse token-selection/k-hop-style tasks and gives provable length-generalization results in simplified attention models, plus experiments matching the theory. Combined with RASP-L (finding 3) and C-RASP (finding 7), this triangulates a consistent picture: retrieval-flavored, O(1)-sparse computations extrapolate for attention; aggregate/counting computations are the hard frontier. For REFA it supplies the cleanest a priori labeling of its task suite by a single integer k, and predicts a phase-transition-like dependence of the baseline's extrapolation on the number of hops.

**Math.** Framework: target f where y_i = g(x_{S_i}), |S_i| <= k, S_i position-flexible ('k-sparse planted' dependencies). Results (simplified attention models): training on lengths >= N_0(k) suffices for generalization to all longer lengths; N_0 increases with k. Dense case |S_i| = Omega(i) (sums, majority) excluded from guarantees. Empirical: k-hop induction tasks show the predicted train-length threshold growing with hop count k.

**REFA application.** Label REFA tasks by k: recall and last-write-wins revision are k=1 (last matching event); 2-hop composition is k=2; contrastive/majority and count-based abstention are dense (k = Theta(T)). Prediction: the transformer baseline's minimal train length for extrapolation grows sharply from recall to 2-hop and never arrives for majority, while REFA's cost is flat in k=1 tasks and its dense-task behavior is governed instead by the decay horizon (1-lambda)^{-1}. This gives the synthesis a quantitative x-axis (k) for its extrapolation figure.

Sources:
- Golowich, Jelassi, Brandfonbrener, Kakade, Malach. The Role of Sparsity for Length Generalization in Transformers/LLMs. ICML 2025, arXiv:2502.16792 — <https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.16792>
- PMLR v267 (golowich25a) — <https://proceedings.mlr.press/v267/golowich25a.html>

### Synthesis: a task-by-task extrapolation prediction table for REFA, derived from the above and refa.py ⚑
*status: speculative*

Putting findings 1-11 against the actual code in /home/user/refa-edge/src/refa_edge/models/refa.py yields falsifiable, mechanism-level predictions per task. (a) Recall and last-write-wins revision: 1-sparse, per-fact 3-state automaton; REFA's delta-rule write is the aligned one-step update, so extrapolation should hold PROVIDED state-distribution coverage (finding 5: nonzero initial memories) — otherwise degradation from accumulated cross-key interference (incomplete soft-routing erases, finding 9). (b) Contrastive/majority: counter-class; REFA extrapolates via additive memory but with a decay-imposed horizon of (1-lambda)^{-1} = 200 events at initialization — predicting a plateau, and that per-bank learned lambda will drift toward 1 on counting-heavy banks (inspectable: decay_logits). (c) Alternating-retraction/parity-like revision: impossible in the memory (spectrum in (-0.005, 0.995)); only the 64-d GRU workspace can carry it, predicting failure that scales with the number of interleaved facts (workspace is shared, memory is not). (d) Multi-hop: misaligned (single linear read per step); predicted sample-complexity cliff, fixed by CLRS-style hints (finding 2) or an iterated-read step. Each cell names its mechanism, its fix, and its diagnostic — the missing predictive account for the roadmap's length-extrapolation item.

**Math.** From refa.py: lambda = sigmoid(decay_logits), init 0.995 => horizon H_c = 1/(1-lambda) = 200 events; recency-weighted count c_T = sum_t lambda^(T-t) beta_t r_h -> saturates at ~beta*r_h*H_c. Transition per bank A = lambda I - beta r_h k k^T, spec(A) subset (lambda-1, lambda) = (-0.005, 0.995) with beta = sigmoid in (0,1), r_h in (0,1): no eigenvalue near -1 => period-2 dynamics inexpressible in memory. Ghost evidence after revision: residual old value has magnitude (1 - beta r_h) shrinking only as lambda^t; with mean r_h = 1/8, residual ~ 87% per write. Multi-hop: composing two reads needs z2 = q2^T M with q2 = f(z1) within one step — absent; hint loss L = L_out + sum_t CE(belief-hat_t, belief_t) is the lever.

**REFA application.** This IS the REFA application: a pre-registered prediction table (task x mechanism x predicted extrapolation x fix x diagnostic) to put in the synthesis and test in the sweeps — recall/revision extrapolate after state-passing post-training; majority plateaus at ~200 events unless counting-bank lambda -> 1; alternating retraction fails until beta_max = 2; multi-hop needs hints. Every prediction is checkable on the 2060 with existing generators plus <30 lines of changes.

Sources:
- REFA implementation: /home/user/refa-edge/src/refa_edge/models/refa.py (lines 55-57 decay init; 79-122 recurrence) — <https://github.com/rezaalmiro/refa-edge>
- Derived from: arXiv:1905.13211, arXiv:2205.15659, arXiv:2207.02098, arXiv:2310.16028, arXiv:2411.12537, arXiv:2507.02782 — <https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.02782>
